“ ’Tis Letitia Grayson, sir. My papa is Sir Humphrey Grayson of Grayson Court, in Gloucestershire. He is afflicted with the gout. I expect you may see him by and by, for I left a note for him, and he would be bound to find it.”

“We await his coming, then,” said Miss Merriot. “It solves the matter. My Peter, bespeak a bedchamber for Miss Grayson.”

A confiding hand was slipped into Kate’s as Mr. Merriot strolled away to the door. “Please will you call me Letty?” said Miss Grayson shyly.
===Chinese===
「哦，她跟我们一起上伦敦去。请问，小姐，我们能不能知道您的芳名？」

「我叫莉蒂西娅·格雷森（Letitia Grayson），先生。我爸爸是格洛斯特郡（Gloucestershire）格雷森府（Grayson Court）的汉弗莱·格雷森爵士。他正害着痛风。我料想你们过不多久就能见着他，因为我给他留了张字条，他准会找得到的。」

「那我们就恭候他大驾光临。」梅里奥特小姐说，「事情这就了了。我说彼得，去给格雷森小姐订一间卧房吧。」

梅里奥特先生朝门口踱去时，一只信赖人的小手悄悄滑进了凯特的手里。「请您叫我莱蒂，好不好？」格雷森小姐怯生生地说。

===Original===
Mr. Merriot made an odd grimace at the panel of the door, and went through into the taproom.

Mine host had barely recovered from his very natural bewilderment at finding that the supposed fugitive was still in his house when there came the sound of a chaise bowling at a rare speed along the road. It drew up at the inn, and in the light of the lamps Mr. Merriot saw his servant jump down. He pursed his lips in a soundless whistle. “This should be papa,” he said pensively. “Your fourth room will be wanted, host.” He went back into the coffee-room to find that Miss Letty was at the window already, peering out.

“Your papa, as I believe,” announced Mr. Merriot.

“I am afraid it is,” agreed Miss Letty. “Yet with the gout plaguing him so much﻿—oh lud! As I live, ’tis Anthony!”
===Chinese===
梅里奥特先生冲着门板做了个古怪的鬼脸，随后穿门而过，进了酒厅。

店主这才刚从那再自然不过的懵懂里缓过神来——他发现那位本已远走高飞的逃婚小姐竟然还在店里——路面上就传来一辆马车疾驰而来的辘辘声，那车速着实少见。马车在客栈门前停住，灯光里，梅里奥特先生瞧见他的仆人跳下车来。他撮起嘴唇，吹了一声无声的口哨。「这该是爸爸驾到了。」他若有所思地说，「你那第四间房要派上用场了，店主。」他回到咖啡室，发现莱蒂小姐早已候在窗前，正向外张望。

「令尊到了，料想没错。」梅里奥特先生通报道。

「我怕就是呢。」莱蒂小姐应道，「可他痛风折磨得那么厉害——哎呀老天！这不是安东尼吗！」

===Original===
Miss Merriot threw her brother a comical look. “And so your desires are fulfilled, child. We are all impatience, Letty.”

Mr. Merriot stood by her chair, and took snuff. The door opened to admit a large gentleman, who came in very leisurely.

“Lud, it’s a mammoth!” said Miss Merriot, for her brother’s private ear.

“Oh, are you jealous?” he retorted.
===Chinese===
梅里奥特小姐朝她哥哥抛去一个滑稽的眼色。「这下您的愿望可算成真了，孩子。咱们个个都等得心急呢，莱蒂。」

梅里奥特先生站在她椅子旁边，嗅了一撮鼻烟。房门开了，进来一位大个子绅士，步态极为从容。

「老天，好一尊庞然大物！」梅里奥特小姐说，这话只说给她哥哥的耳朵听。

「哦，你这是嫉妒啦？」他反唇相讥。

===Original===
The large gentleman paused on the threshold and put up his quizzing-glass, through which he blandly surveyed the room. He was a very large gentleman indeed, with magnificent shoulders and a fine leg. He seemed rather to fill the room; he had certainly a presence, and a personality. He wore a tie wig of plain brown, and carried his hat under his arm. The hilt of his sword peeped out from between the folds of his greatcoat, but in his hand he held a cane.

“The gentleman would appear to be annoyed,” murmured Mr. Merriot, looking at the lines about the newcomer’s mouth and square jowl.

“La, my dear, how can you say so?” marvelled Miss Merriot, seeing the large gentleman’s grey eyes calm and bored. She rose with an air, and swept a curtsey. The gentleman must not be allowed to dominate the room thus. It seemed he had the way of it. “Make your leg, child,” she threw over her shoulder at Peter. “We are under observation.”
===Chinese===
大个子绅士在门槛上站定，举起单片眼镜，透过镜片不动声色地把房间环视了一遍。他确实是个大块头绅士，肩膀魁伟，一条腿也漂亮。他往屋里一站，简直要把整个房间都占满了；他分明自有一股威仪，也自有一种气性。他戴一顶素棕色的系带假发，帽子挟在腋下，大衣褶缝间露出半截剑柄，手里则拄着一根手杖。

「这位绅士像是动气了。」梅里奥特先生喃喃道，眼睛瞧着来客嘴角和方正下颌上的那几道纹路。

「嗳呀，亲爱的，你怎么能这么说？」梅里奥特小姐惊叹道，她分明看见大个子绅士那双灰眼睛又平静又倦怠。她仪态万方地起身，深深行了一个屈膝礼。总不能任由这位绅士这般镇住全场吧。他瞧上去还真有这份本事。「行个礼吧，孩子。」她扭头朝彼得抛下一句，「人家正打量着咱们呢。」

===Original===
The sternness about Sir Anthony’s mouth vanished. He smiled and showed a row of very even white teeth. He bowed with easy grace. “Madam, your most obedient! Sir, yours!”

Mr. Merriot took Miss Letty by the hand. “Permit me to restore to you Miss Grayson, sir,” he said, ignoring an indignant protest from the lady.

Sir Anthony showed no desire to receive Miss Grayson, who looked him defiantly between the eyes. He smiled still, but he did not offer to take her hand. “You should be whipped, Letty,” he said pleasantly.

Miss Grayson flushed. “ ’Deed, sir, and did you bring your cane for that purpose?” she demanded.

“No, my dear, but I should be happy to benefit you that far.”
===Chinese===
安东尼爵士嘴边的严苛之色消散了。他微微一笑，露出一口整整齐齐的白牙，从容优雅地躬了躬身。「小姐，您最恭顺的仆人向您请安！先生，也向您请安！」

梅里奥特先生牵起莱蒂小姐的手。「请容我把格雷森小姐交还给您，先生。」他说，全不理会那位女士愤愤的抗议。

安东尼爵士毫无接下格雷森小姐的意思，而后者正气鼓鼓地直瞪着他的双眼。他仍旧微笑着，却并不伸手去接她。「你该挨顿鞭子才是，莱蒂。」他语气和悦地说。

格雷森小姐涨红了脸。「真的呢，先生，您带这根手杖，就是为了这个用处吗？」她质问道。

「不是，亲爱的，不过能让你受益到这个份上，我也乐意的。」

===Original===
Peter Merriot was amused, and permitted his chuckle to be heard. “Faith, it’s a stern suitor.”

“You are﻿—very rude﻿—and﻿—and﻿—and hateful!” declared Miss Grayson, outraged.

Sir Anthony laid down his cane and his hat, and began to take off his greatcoat. As one who had no further interest in Miss Grayson he took out his snuffbox, unfobbed it, and held it out to Mr. Merriot. His hand was very white and finely shaped, but it looked to have some strength. “Sir,” said he, smiling sleepily for all his grey eyes were alert beneath their rather heavy lids, “you will permit me to thank you on behalf of my friend, Sir Humphrey Grayson, for your services to his daughter.”

Mr. Merriot helped himself to a pinch of snuff. Grey eyes met grey; the humorous look played around Mr. Merriot’s mouth. “Lud, here’s a solemnity!” he said. “I am Miss Grayson’s servant to command.”
===Chinese===
彼得·梅里奥特只觉得有趣，索性让自己的轻笑出了声。「老实说，这位求婚人可真够严厉的。」

「您真是——太无礼了——而且——而且——而且可恨透了！」格雷森小姐义愤填膺地宣布。

安东尼爵士放下手杖和帽子，动手宽大衣。他一副对格雷森小姐再无半分兴趣的样子，掏出鼻烟盒，从表袋上解下来，递向梅里奥特先生。他的手白皙而秀气，看去却颇有几分力气。「先生，」他说，眼角挂着一丝困倦的笑意，微垂的眼睑底下，那双灰眼睛却警醒得很，「请容我代我的朋友汉弗莱·格雷森爵士，为您救助他女儿一事向您致谢。」

梅里奥特先生自己拈了一撮鼻烟。灰眼睛对上灰眼睛；那丝谐谑又在梅里奥特先生嘴角边打转。「老天，这可真郑重其事！」他说，「我听候格雷森小姐差遣。」

===Original===
Miss Grayson forgot her dignity. “Tony, ’twas wonderful! His sword was out in a trice, and I thought he was about to run that odious Markham right through the body, but just as it was too monstrously exciting for words the point seemed to flash upwards and the hilt caught Markham on the chin.” She demonstrated with a small fist to her own pretty chin. “He went down like a stone,” she ended dramatically. Her glance fell on Miss Merriot by the fire. “And Miss Merriot too was splendid, Tony, for she pretended to swoon in Markham’s arms.”

Mr. Merriot looked down at his sister something quizzically. “My dear, I eclipse you,” he murmured. He turned again to Sir Anthony. “Thus we mourn our departed suitor. Now where did you find my man John?” He began to pour wine, and handed one glass to the large gentleman.

“At Stilton,” Sir Anthony replied. “Just before I saw my friend Mr. Markham. He was endeavouring to hide a chaise and horses which﻿—er﻿—aroused my suspicions. He was induced to confide in me.”

Mr. Merriot looked meditatively at that square handsome face. “I wonder why?” he said, for he knew his John.
===Chinese===
格雷森小姐忘了端矜持。「托尼，那可太精彩了！他的剑转眼间就出了鞘，我还当他要把那个讨厌的马卡姆扎个透心凉呢，可就在刺激得没法形容的当口，剑尖仿佛一道闪光向上撩起，剑柄正正磕在马卡姆的下巴上。」她攥起一只小拳头，朝自己俏丽的下巴比划了一下。「他像块石头似的直挺挺栽了下去。」她煞有介事地收了尾。她的目光落到炉边的梅里奥特小姐身上。「梅里奥特小姐也出色极了，托尼，她还假装晕倒在马卡姆怀里呢。」

梅里奥特先生颇为揶揄地低头看了看自己的妹妹。「亲爱的，我把你比下去啦。」他喃喃道。他又转向安东尼爵士。「我们就这样悼念那位去了的求婚人。对了，你在哪儿找到我家约翰（John）的？」他开始斟酒，把其中一杯递给大个子绅士。

「在斯蒂尔顿（Stilton）。」安东尼爵士答道，「就在我见到我那位朋友马卡姆先生之前。他正忙着把一辆马车和几匹马藏起来，那——呃——引起了我的疑心。我略加劝诱，他便向我吐露了实情。」

梅里奥特先生若有所思地端详着那张方正的俊脸。「怪了，他怎么就说了呢？」他道，因为他深知自家那位约翰的为人。

===Original===
A singularly attractive smile crossed Sir Anthony’s face. “My charm of manner, sir, I believe,” he said.

There came a laugh from Miss Merriot. “I begin to have a kindness for the large gentleman,” she remarked to the room at large. “And you met the so dear Mr. Markham, sir?”

“Hardly, madam. I had rather say I saw the so dear Mr. Markham pass me in a cloud of﻿—mud, I believe.”

“I wonder, did he see you?” Miss Merriot’s eyes were bright with laughter.

“I am almost persuaded that he did,” said Sir Anthony.
===Chinese===
一个格外动人的笑意在安东尼爵士脸上一掠而过。「想必是拜我风度的魅力所赐，先生。」他说。

梅里奥特小姐发出一阵笑。「我开始对这位大个子绅士生出好感来了。」她对满屋子的人说道，「这么说，您还见到了那位亲爱透顶的马卡姆先生喽，先生？」

「算不上，小姐。我倒情愿说，我是看见那位亲爱透顶的马卡姆先生裹在一团——泥浆里，从我身边一掠而过的。」

「不知道他瞧见您了没有？」梅里奥特小姐的双眼亮晶晶地含着笑。

「我差不多可以断定，他瞧见了。」安东尼爵士说。

===Original===
“Then I take it we are not to expect his return?” Miss Merriot cocked a knowing eyebrow.

“I hardly think so, madam,” said Sir Anthony placidly.

Miss Merriot looked at Miss Grayson. “Why, child, I like the large gentleman, I protest,” she said. “Pray, sir, have you dined?”

“So far I have not had the time, madam, but I have reason to hope the landlord is preparing dinner for me at this moment.”
===Chinese===
「那依我看，他是不打算回来喽？」梅里奥特小姐心照不宣地挑起一道眉。

「我看是不会回来了，小姐。」安东尼爵士心平气和地说。

梅里奥特小姐看向格雷森小姐。「嗳，孩子，说真的，我喜欢上这位大个子绅士了。」她说，「请问，先生，您用晚饭了吗？」

「到眼下还没腾出工夫呢，小姐，不过我有理由指望，店主这会儿正在替我备晚饭。」

===Original===
Mine host himself came in most opportunely then, with the serving maid behind him, carrying a loaded tray. A fresh cover was laid, a roasted chicken placed before Sir Anthony, and a fresh bottle uncorked.

“You permit, madam?” Sir Anthony bowed towards Miss Merriot.

“Pray, sir, be seated. You will be ravenous.”

“I confess I hate to miss my dinner,” said Sir Anthony, and began to carve the chicken. “There is something of me to maintain, you see,” he added, with a twinkle, and a glance cast down his noble bulk.
===Chinese===
正在这个节骨眼上，店主亲自走了进来，身后跟着端着满载托盘的女仆。新摆了一份餐具，一只烤鸡端到安东尼爵士面前，又开了一瓶新酒。

「您允许吗，小姐？」安东尼爵士朝梅里奥特小姐欠身道。

「快请入座吧，先生。您准是饿坏了。」

「我承认，我最恨错过一顿晚饭。」安东尼爵士说着，动手切起鸡来。「您瞧，我这一身可是要维持的。」他补了一句，眼里闪着戏谑，朝自己堂堂的块头扫了一眼。

===Original===
Miss Grayson cut in on Miss Merriot’s laugh. “Food!” she ejaculated scornfully, and tapped an impatient foot. Sir Anthony paid no heed. “Well, Tony, you are come nigh on a hundred miles to rescue me, as I suppose, and now have you nothing at all to say but that you have missed your dinner?”

“That thought has been absorbing me for the last twenty miles,” said Sir Anthony imperturbably.

“And me in peril!” cried the affronted Miss Grayson.

Sir Anthony raised his eyes from the chicken and looked coolly across at her. “Oh, were you in peril?” he inquired. “I came merely to put an end to an indiscretion, as I thought.”

“Peril! At the hands of such a Monster!” Miss Grayson was indignant. “I wonder, sir, that you need ask.”
===Chinese===
格雷森小姐打断了梅里奥特小姐的笑。「就知道吃！」她轻蔑地迸出一句，不耐烦地跺着脚。安东尼爵士浑不理会。「我说托尼，照我料想，你赶了将近一百英里路来救我，如今除了念叨你误了晚饭，就再没有一句别的话可说了吗？」

「这桩心事已经让我惦记了二十英里了。」安东尼爵士不动声色地说。

「可我还身处险境呢！」受了冒犯的格雷森小姐喊道。

安东尼爵士从鸡上抬起眼来，冷冷地朝她望去。「哦，您有险境吗？」他问道，「依我看，我来不过是来终结一桩轻率行径罢了。」

「险境！落在那样一个魔王手里！」格雷森小姐气愤极了，「先生，亏您还问得出口。」

===Original===
Sir Anthony poured wine for himself and Mr. Merriot. “My dear Letty,” said he, “you have so frequently assured us that Mr. Markham is a model of all the virtues that I did you the honour to respect your judgment.”

Miss Grayson turned scarlet, and looked as though she were about to cry. “You didn’t, Tony! You are just being﻿—disagreeable. And he’s not a model of virtue! He is an odious brute, and﻿—and so are you!”

“Tut, child, the gentleman’s hungry, and will be the better for his chicken,” said Mr. Merriot.

“I am not a child!” flashed Miss Grayson, and was off in a swirl of skirts to Miss Merriot’s side. From the shelter of Miss Merriot’s arm she hurled a tearful defiance. “And I would sooner go to Gretna with that Monster than marry you, Sir Tony!”
===Chinese===
安东尼爵士为自己和梅里奥特先生斟上酒。「亲爱的莱蒂，」他说，「你屡屡向我们担保，马卡姆先生是一切美德的模范，我便敬重你的眼力，信了你的判断。」

格雷森小姐涨得满脸通红，一副眼泪就要夺眶而出的样子。「你才没有呢，托尼！你就是成心——讨人厌。他才不是什么美德的模范！他是个可恶的畜生，你——你也是！」

「好了好了，孩子，这位先生饿了，吃过鸡就缓过来了。」梅里奥特先生说。

「我才不是孩子！」格雷森小姐厉声回敬，裙裾一旋，奔到了梅里奥特小姐身旁。她借着梅里奥特小姐臂弯的庇护，含泪发出挑战。「我宁可跟那个魔王去格雷特纳格林，也不嫁给你，托尼爵士！」

===Original===
Sir Anthony remained unmoved. “My dear Letty, if this piece of absurdity was to escape my attentions, believe me it was not in the least necessary. So far as I am aware I have never asked you to marry me. Nor have I the smallest intention of so doing.”

This pronouncement brought Miss Grayson’s head up from Kate’s shoulder. In round-eyed astonishment she gazed at Sir Anthony, busily engaged with the wing of a chicken.

“I have to suppose,” said Miss Merriot sharply, “that the gentleman is an original.”

Mr. Merriot turned away to hide a laughing face. “These family arrangements﻿—!” he said.
===Chinese===
安东尼爵士不为所动。「亲爱的莱蒂，倘若这套荒唐话是为了躲开我的殷勤，那请相信我，这实在大可不必。据我所知，我从来不曾求您嫁给我，也丝毫没有这个打算。」

这番宣示让格雷森小姐的脑袋从凯特的肩头抬了起来。她瞪圆了眼睛，惊愕地望着正忙着对付一只鸡翅的安东尼爵士。

「我只得认定，」梅里奥特小姐语带尖刻地说，「这位绅士是个奇人。」

梅里奥特先生扭过头去，藏起一张憋笑的脸。「这样的家事安排——！」他说。

===Original===
“But﻿—but Papa says﻿—” began Miss Grayson. “Why, Tony, don’t you *want* to marry me?”

“I do not,” said Sir Anthony.

Miss Grayson blinked, but she did not seem to be offended. “Why don’t you?” she asked with naive curiosity.

At that Sir Anthony looked up, and there was a twinkle in his eyes. “I suppose, Letty, because my taste is at fault.”

“Well!” Miss Grayson digested this in silence. She disengaged herself from Kate’s arm, and went slowly to the table. Sir Anthony rose at her approach, and received one little hand in his large one. “Tony, will you tell Papa?” she asked.
===Chinese===
「可是——可是爸爸说——」格雷森小姐开了腔，「为什么，托尼，难道你*不想*娶我吗？」

「不想。」安东尼爵士说。

格雷森小姐眨了眨眼，却似乎并不见怪。「那为什么不想呢？」她带着天真的好奇问道。

这话惹得安东尼爵士抬起头来，眼里闪过一丝狡黠。「我猜呀，莱蒂，是因为我的品味出了岔子。」

「哦！」格雷森小姐默默消化了这句话。她从凯特的手臂间抽出身来，慢慢走向桌边。她走近时安东尼爵士站起身来，一只小手被他握进了宽大的手掌。「托尼，你会告诉爸爸吗？」她问。

===Original===
“I have told him, my dear.”

“How did he take it?” asked Miss Grayson anxiously.

“Philosophically, child.”

“I am so glad!” said Miss Grayson, with a relieved sigh. “If you don’t want to marry me, Tony, I can go home with a quiet mind. And I can even forgive you for being so disagreeable.”

“And I,” said Sir Anthony, “can finish my dinner.”
===Chinese===
「已经告诉他了，亲爱的。」

「他听了怎么说？」格雷森小姐焦急地问。

「达观得很，孩子。」

「那我可太高兴了！」格雷森小姐说，如释重负地叹了口气，「既然你不想娶我，托尼，我就能安安心心地回家了。就连你方才那么讨人厌，我也可以不计较了。」

「而我呢，」安东尼爵士说，「也能把我的晚饭吃完了。」

## XV Challenge to Mr. Merriot / XV 向梅里奥特先生挑战

===Original===
There was no means of telling what John, that stolid creature, made of the situation. His young master and mistress suspected him of being deeper in the old gentleman’s secrets than they were, but he had never a word to say on the matter. When my lord had made his first startling appearance they came home and told him of it, and awaited some show of surprise. It was not forthcoming. John gave a grunt and said that he had doubted but that the old gentleman would arrive soon. As to the manner of his arrival, John seemed to think it natural enough, and he never failed thereafter to give my lord his title. The old gentleman had greeted him with an affectionate smile, and a hand carelessly outflung. John had looked beneath his brows and said gruffly that the affair of Master Robin must be seen to. He further volunteered the opinion that Robin’s present guise was unseemly. As for Miss Prue, the sooner she was got out of this coil the better. John had a grim way with him, but they had none of them the need ever to stand in doubt of his devotion. Nothing could abate the supreme belief in himself that my lord held, but certainly he used fewer extravagancies with his servant than with his children, and would condescend to listen to John’s disapproving words. But not even John could hope to make much impression on that magnificent mind. My lord waved a hand, and promised ultimate success.

===Chinese===
要说约翰（John）这个闷葫芦般的人物对眼下这局面作何感想，实在无从得知。他的年轻主人与主母疑心，他对老先生的秘密知之比他们还深，可他在这件事上从无半句话。老爷初次惊人露面之后，他们回到家中说与他听，只等着他露出几分惊讶。那惊讶却始终不见踪影。约翰哼了一声，说自己本就疑心老先生不久便到。至于他到来的方式，约翰似乎觉得再自然不过，此后开口再没有一次不尊称老爷的头衔。老先生曾满面慈爱地笑着，漫不经心甩出一只手来招呼他。约翰垂着眼皮觑了觑，粗声粗气地说，罗宾（Robin）少爷的事必须有人料理。他还主动进言，说罗宾眼下这身装扮有失体统。至于普鲁（Prue）小姐，越早把她从这团乱麻里摘出来越好。约翰为人冷峻，可他们谁都不必怀疑他的忠心。什么也削减不了老爷对自己那份无上的信心，不过他对仆人确实比对自己的儿女少摆些排场，也肯屈尊听一听约翰那些不以为然的话。但即便是约翰，也休想在那颗堂堂的头脑上留下多深的印痕。老爷挥一挥手，许诺终将大获全胜。

===Original===
“You’re playing a game I don’t understand, my lord,” John said severely. “It’s more of your playacting, for sure, but why you should do it, sir, I can’t see.”

“I plan a great coup,” my lord assured him. “There must never be aught crude in my actions, John. There has never been. I go warily, and I contrive. Oh, but I contrive a tour de force! Continue to watch over my children!”

“It’s well there’s someone to do it, my lord,” said John. “For it’s little care of them you’d be taking. Masquerades and the like!”

“My John, you are foolish. You lack understanding. My wing is spread over the children, as ever.”

===Chinese===
「老爷，您这是在玩一场我看不懂的把戏，」约翰正色道，「不用说又是您那套演戏，可您为什么要这么干，老爷，我实在看不明白。」

「我筹划的是一桩惊天妙举，」老爷向他担保，「约翰，我的行事里永远不可有一丝粗陋。从来不曾有过。我步步为营，潜心运筹。啊，我运筹的可是一件传世杰作！继续看护好我的孩子们！」

「幸而还有人肯管这事，老爷，」约翰说，「不然您才不会把他们放在心上呢。化装易容那一套名堂！」

「我的约翰啊，你可真傻。你缺乏悟性。我的羽翼一如既往，荫蔽着孩子们。」

===Original===
“There’s this Miss Grayson,” John continued, entirely ignoring his lordship. “Master Robin must needs set his fancy on her. I’d a word or two with Sir Humphrey’s man, and it’s little hope there is that he’d countenance such a marriage.”

The old gentleman half closed his eyes. “He shall countenance it, John. If I were to fail in my claim, which is not possible, he should countenance it. You shall all of you dance to my piping.” He smiled with delight at the thought. In some things he had the mind of a child.

“There’s one that won’t dance to your piping, my lord,” was John’s parting shot. “And that’s Miss Prue’s sleepy gentleman!”

My lord wasted as much as three minutes on the consideration of this announcement, but arrived at the conclusion that there could be no truth in it. He could never be got to doubt his own powers.

===Chinese===
「还有那位格雷森（Grayson）小姐，」约翰全然不睬老爷，径自往下说，「罗宾少爷偏偏要对她动心思。我跟汉弗莱爵士的仆人搭过一两句话，他要是肯容下这样一门亲事，那希望可渺茫得很。」

老先生半阖上双眼。「他会容下的，约翰。纵使我的继承之诉落了空——那是不可能的事——他也得容下。你们所有人，都得跟着我的笛声起舞。」他想到此处，喜形于色。在某些事情上，他有一颗孩童的心。

「有一位可不会跟着您的笛声起舞，老爷，」约翰临去时丢下一句，「那就是普鲁小姐那位睡眼惺忪的绅士！」

老爷为思量这句话足足花去了三分钟，末了断定这话绝无半点实情。要叫他怀疑自己的能耐，那是无论如何办不到的。

===Original===
It was understood in polite circles that Mr. Rensley had had disturbing news from his lawyers regarding the claim. It was soon bruited abroad that these men thought some inquiry should be made into his lordship’s past before he should be positively declared Tremaine of Barham. My lord seemed to be quite content with this decision. He smiled, and put his fingertips together as his habit was, and begged the lawyers to make what enquiries they would. Meanwhile he continued to parade the town.

Mr. Rensley was soon infuriated to find that his supposed cousin’s past was hidden in an obscurity there seemed to be no hope of piercing. Inquiries led precisely nowhere. It was true that a Mr. Challoner had once kept a gaming-house in Munich, and it was believed he had gone thence to Rome. But there could be found no trace of him in that ancient city. Indeed, how should there be? Had these seekers after truth mentioned the name of a German baron who had stayed in Rome some years ago they might have learned something of considerable interest from those who remembered that remarkable gentleman. But the seekers, unfortunately, had never heard of the baron, and they were forced to abandon the search for truth.

My lord was understood now to have two children. He spoke of them enthusiastically upon all occasions. Sir Anthony, hearing him, said humorously: “It seems you are to be congratulated, sir.”

===Chinese===
上流圈子里人人都心领神会：伦斯利（Rensley）先生从他的律师们那里得了关于这桩继承之诉的烦心消息。很快又有风声四起，说这几位律师认为，在正式宣告他为巴勒姆的特雷梅因（Tremaine of Barham）之前，应当先对他的过去做一番查究。老爷对这个决定似乎十分满意。他微微一笑，照老习惯把指尖对在一处，请律师们尽管去查。与此同时，他照旧招摇过市。

伦斯利先生很快便怒不可遏地发现，他这位所谓堂亲的身世，藏在一团似乎毫无指望刺破的迷雾之中。多方查证，处处落空。诚然，从前确有一位查洛纳（Challoner）先生在慕尼黑开过一家赌场，据说他后来去了罗马。可是在那座古城里，他的踪迹半点也寻不见。说实在的，又怎么可能寻得见呢？倘若这些追寻真相的人提起几年前旅居罗马的某位德国男爵的大名，本可以从记得那位非凡绅士的人嘴里，打听到不少顶有意思的事。可惜这几位追寻者压根儿没听说过那位男爵，只得放弃了对真相的搜寻。

如今人人心知，老爷有一双儿女。他在一切场合谈起他们来都眉飞色舞。安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）听见了，诙谐地说：「看来该向您道喜了，先生。」

===Original===
“You have said it!” His lordship turned his compelling gaze upon the immovable large gentleman. “My daughter﻿—my Prudence! A Venus!” He looked soulful. “I say it who should not. She favours her mother, my poor Maria. A statue carved in ivory and rose! A goddess, with a voice of gold! Soon you shall see her,” he promised.

“Egad, we’re agog to, sir!” said Mr. Molyneux, smothering a grin. “And is your son thus godlike too?”

“My little Robin!” sighed his lordship. “He has not the height for it, alack! But he is well enough. To see him in the duello is to say he is incomparable. I pine to clasp them to me once more.”

“And﻿—if one is permitted to ask,” said Sir Anthony, observing a speck of dust on his great cuff through a levelling quizzing-glass. “Where are these two paragons?”

“It is permitted. They stay with a friend in France. I send for them when this business is at an end.”

===Chinese===
「您算说对了！」老爷把那道令人无法抗拒的目光，转向纹丝不动的大块头绅士。「我的女儿——我的普鲁登丝（Prudence）！一位维纳斯！」他一脸深情。「这话本不该由我来说。她长得像她母亲，我可怜的玛丽亚（Maria）。一尊用象牙和玫瑰雕成的塑像！一位生着金嗓子的女神！您很快就可见到她了，」他许诺道。

「哎呀，我们都巴望得很呢，先生！」莫利纽克斯（Molyneux）先生憋着笑说。「令郎也是这般天神一流的人物么？」

「我的小罗宾！」老爷叹道。「可惜他没有那份身高，唉！不过也够好的了。见过他决斗的功夫，便只好说他天下无双。我渴望再一次把他们搂进怀里。」

「还有——若是容许动问，」安东尼爵士说着，透过举得端正的单片眼镜，端详大袖口上的一粒尘埃，「这一对完人现在何处？」

「容许的。他们住在法国一位朋友家里。这桩事了结之后，我便派人去接他们。」

===Original===
“Did you ever see or hear the like?” demanded Sir Raymond Orton when the old gentleman had gone.

“A most remarkable man,” said Sir Anthony, and yawned behind his scented handkerchief.

Prudence herself, encountering my lord at Lady Elton’s rout one evening, was informed that she too should have the felicity of meeting his daughter. She bowed politely, and professed herself to be enchanted by the prospect.

===Chinese===
「你可曾见过或听过这等人物？」老先生走后，雷蒙德·奥顿爵士（Sir Raymond Orton）问道。

「一个极不寻常的人。」安东尼爵士说罢，在洒了香水的手帕后面打了个呵欠。

一天晚上，普鲁登丝本人在埃尔顿夫人（Lady Elton）的盛会上遇见老爷，被告知她也将有幸拜见他的女儿。她客气地欠了欠身，口称对此前景心醉神迷。

===Original===
A new piece of information was very soon passed from mouth to mouth. It appeared that no one could discover whence my lord had sprung when he came to make his claim. It had been supposed that he came from France, but no trace of him could be found either at Calais or on the packet boat. He seemed to have sprung up out of the earth in a manner most mysterious, and he could not be identified with any traveller from France for weeks past.

It was Mr. Devereux who conceived the brilliant notion that my lord had not been in France at all, but even this flash of insight failed to lead anywhere.

Prudence, remembering past traffickings, guessed that my lord had been a passenger on one of those crafts that carefully avoid all ports and King’s ships, but put into land in odd out-of-the-way coves under cover of night. But this she kept to herself.

===Chinese===
不久，一桩新的消息便口口相传开来。原来老爷前来提出继承之诉时，谁也查不出他是打哪儿冒出来的。人们本猜想他从法国来，可无论在加来（Calais）还是在邮船上，都寻不见他的踪迹。他仿佛以最神秘莫测的方式从地底下冒了出来，而且过去数周从法国来的旅客中，没有一个能同他对上号。

头一个想出妙见的是德弗罗（Devereux）先生：老爷压根儿就没去过法国。可就连这一星灵光，到头来也没引出任何结果。

普鲁登丝记起从前几趟走私的勾当，猜到老爷搭乘的是那种小心翼翼避开一切港口与王家船只的小艇，专在夜幕掩护下驶进偏僻冷落的小海湾靠岸。但这话她只烂在自己肚里。

===Original===
Mr. Devereux begged her to say whether or no she credited my lord’s claim. She laughed, and tapped her riding whip against her boot. “Why, sir, it’s not for me to hazard an opinion. But it seems to me that his lordship was born to the part.”

“True, very true,” nodded Mr. Devereux. “Charles was saying only this moment he has more the manner of it than our friend Rensley.”

“He could scarcely have less,” said Prudence dryly.

There was a heavy footstep behind her. By an evil chance Mr. Rensley had entered the room at that instant, and was bearing down upon the group by the fire. He came fresh from a gloomy interview with his lawyer; he was conscious that everywhere his chances were being discussed. And now he entered White’s to hear a young upstart from the country pass disparaging remarks upon himself. He strode therefore straight up to Prudence, and with a look in his eyes not at all pleasant, rapped out: “Who could not have less of what, my fine sir?”

===Chinese===
德弗罗先生央她说一句，她究竟信不信老爷的继承之诉。她笑了，拿马鞭轻轻敲着靴筒。「哎呀，先生，妄下断语可轮不着我。不过依我看，老爷天生就是演这角色的料。」

「对，很对。」德弗罗先生点头称是。「查尔斯（Charles）方才还在说，论气派，他比我们的朋友伦斯利更像回事。」

「他简直不可能再少了。」普鲁登丝干巴巴地说。

她身后响起一阵沉重的脚步声。真是撞了邪运，伦斯利先生恰在此时走进屋来，正朝壁炉边这一群人逼近。他刚同他的律师进行过一场阴郁的会谈；他明知四处都有人在议论他的胜算。如今他走进怀特俱乐部（White’s），偏偏听见一个乡下来的暴发小子贬损自己。于是他径直大步走到普鲁登丝跟前，眼里带着极不友善的神色，厉声喝道：「谁不可能再少了？少的是什么，我漂亮的公子哥儿？」

===Original===
It was evident that Rensley had heard all. Mr. Devereux coughed and gazed at the ceiling, reflecting that it was like Rensley to choose a suckling for his prey.

Prudence turned a little to face Mr. Rensley. There was danger confronting her, as well she knew. She said quietly: “I spoke to Mr. Devereux, sir, I believe.”

“Your words were not meant for my ears I make no doubt,” said Rensley evilly.

Prudence bowed. “You apprehend the matter correctly, sir.”

===Chinese===
伦斯利显然全都听见了。德弗罗先生咳了一声，两眼望着天花板，心想伦斯利正是这路人，专挑乳臭未干的雏儿当猎物。

普鲁登丝微微侧身，面对伦斯利先生。她心里明白，危险正当面逼来。她平静地说：「先生，我想，我方才是在同德弗罗先生讲话。」

「您那些话原就不是说给我听的，这一点我毫不怀疑。」伦斯利不怀好意地说。

普鲁登丝躬了躬身。「先生，您把事情理解得丝毫不差。」

===Original===
There was a certain air of tense expectation in the room. Prudence felt that she was on her trial. God knew how it would end!

Mr. Rensley might well let be now. He looked sullenly at Prudence, and thought that he heard a whisper in the group behind her. There had been too much whispering of late; very badly did Mr. Rensley want to avenge himself on someone. He was not ill pleased to take Prudence for a scapegoat. This young ruffler gave himself insufferable airs: it was time he was taught a lesson. Mr. Rensley spoke more offensively still. “I see, Mr. Merriot, that you don’t care to repeat your words.”

There fell a sudden stillness. “I do not, Mr. Rensley.”

“On what grounds, Mr. Merriot, I wonder?”

“On the grounds, Mr. Rensley, of good manners.”

===Chinese===
屋里弥漫起一股紧张期待的气息。普鲁登丝觉得自己正受着公审。天知道这局面会怎样收场！

伦斯利先生此刻满可以见好就收。他阴沉地瞪着普鲁登丝，觉得听见她身后那一群人里的窃窃私语。近来这种窃窃私语实在太多了；伦斯利先生满腔怨毒，亟欲找个人报复。拿普鲁登丝当替罪羊，他正求之不得。这年少轻狂的浮浪子摆出一副叫人难以容忍的架子：是时候教训教训他了。伦斯利先生的话愈发难听。「我明白了，梅里奥特（Merriot）先生，您是不愿把您的话再说一遍了。」

屋里骤然一静。「是不愿，伦斯利先生。」

「这是什么缘故呢，我倒想请教，梅里奥特先生？」

「缘故嘛，伦斯利先生，在于礼数二字。」

===Original===
Rensley flushed. “In which you think me lacking, eh?”

“I have not told you so, sir.”

“And you don’t think it?”

There was a slight pause. Prudence realized, dismayed, that the group behind her was awaiting curiously her challenge. To conciliate this angry, red-faced man, meant the loss of every man’s good opinion; in a word, it meant social ostracism. A challenge was offered, and it seemed it must be accepted. Pride could not be swallowed. She spoke deliberately. “That question, Mr. Rensley, I prefer to leave unanswered.”

“Afraid, eh?”

===Chinese===
伦斯利涨红了脸。「您是觉得我缺了这个，嗯？」

「我并没有这样说，先生。」

「那您也没有这么想？」

略微停顿了片刻。普鲁登丝懊恼地意识到，身后那一群人正好奇心切地等着她接下战书。向这个怒气冲冲、满脸通红的人委曲求全，就意味着丧失在场每一个男人的好感；一言以蔽之，就意味着被逐出社交圈。战书既已递到面前，看来便非接不可。自尊是咽不下去的。她一字一顿地说：「这个问题，伦斯利先生，我宁愿不予作答。」

「怕了，嗯？」

===Original===
Egad, was she afraid? She thought she was too much her father’s daughter. A cold anger took her in its hold; she looked Rensley full between the eyes. “You become insulting, sir. I take leave to tell you, since you will have it, that your manners belong to the taproom.”

It was out, and did she regret it? She became aware of Mr. Belfort at her elbow, and was conscious of the approval of him and of the others in the circle. No, come what might, the thing had to be, and she regretted nothing.

Mr. Rensley flushed darker still. Sure, the man would have an apoplexy one of these days. “I shall send my friends to wait upon yours, Mr. Merriot.”

“Certainly, sir.” She looked towards Mr. Belfort, who nodded encouragingly. Mr. Devereux smiled wearily, and stepped forward a pace. “Mr. Belfort will act for me, and Mr. Devereux,” she said, and turned to resume her conversation with them.

===Chinese===
天哪，她会怕吗？她心想，自己到底是父亲的女儿。一股冷冷的怒意攫住了她；她直直地望进伦斯利的双眼。「您这是存心辱人了，先生。既然您非要听不可，恕我直言，您这副做派是酒馆里头学来的。」

话既出口，她后悔了么？她觉察到贝尔福特（Belfort）先生就在自己肘边，也察觉到他和圈内众人都心怀赞许。不，无论后事如何，这一步总归要走，她毫无悔意。

伦斯利先生的脸涨成了猪肝色。说真的，这个人早晚有一天要中风。「我会派我的朋友去拜会您的朋友，梅里奥特先生。」

「那是自然，先生。」她朝贝尔福特先生望去，对方鼓励地点了点头。德弗罗先生倦怠地一笑，上前一步。「贝尔福特先生将出任我的副手，还有德弗罗先生。」她说完，转过身去继续同他们叙话。

===Original===
Mr. Rensley bowed stiffly and went out. Belfort clapped Prudence on the shoulder. “Well said, my boy!” he declared. “I knew you’d never swallow that! Gad, it’s a good six months since I’ve acted for anyone. We’ll see some sport now!”

Prudence, her anger evaporating fast, could have found another name for it. “I don’t desire this to come to my sister’s ears, Charles,” she said. “I needn’t warn you, I suppose.”

“Oh, not a word, my dear Merriot, trust me!” promised Mr. Belfort. “He’ll name Markham and Jessup his seconds, I dare swear. You’ll choose swords, I take it? We’ll have the whole affair fixed up as snug and quiet as you please.”

===Chinese===
伦斯利先生僵硬地一躬身，出去了。贝尔福特拍了拍普鲁登丝的肩膀。「说得好，小伙子！」他朗声道，「我就知道你咽不下这口气！老天，我足足有半年没替人当过副手了。这下咱们可要看好戏了！」

普鲁登丝的怒气消退得飞快，对这桩「好戏」，她本可以另起一个名目。「我不愿这事传进我妹妹的耳朵，查尔斯。」她说，「我想，不必我再来叮嘱了吧。」

「噢，一个字也漏不了，我亲爱的梅里奥特，信我！」贝尔福特先生打包票。「我敢说他准会点马卡姆（Markham）和杰瑟普（Jessup）当他的副手。您是要用剑吧，我猜？咱们会把整桩事情安排得要多熨帖安静有多熨帖安静。」

===Original===
Mr. Molyneux spoke disapproval. “Rensley must have taken leave of his senses,” he said in an undertone to Sir Raymond Orton. “A man of his years to challenge a boy to fight! It’s child murder!”

“Oh, it won’t come to that, Molyneux,” was Sir Raymond’s comfortable belief. “He’ll pink him easily enough, and Merriot will lie up for a week or so. Rensley knows better than to make it a killing matter. People are getting damned strict over these duels, you know.”

It was Prudence’s own belief as she walked back to Arlington Street: she had not much fear of death, but the thing as it stood was bad enough. It was true she had considerable knowledge of swordplay, but she knew very well that it was one thing to play with foils and quite another to fight in good earnest a man who was one’s declared enemy.

He was a strong man too, by the looks of him. Maybe she might have something of an advantage in the matter of quickness; sure, she had been taught a trick or two not many knew. The affair was not hopeless, she believed, but she admitted she had small relish for it.

===Chinese===
莫利纽克斯先生大不以为然。「伦斯利准是脑子出了岔子，」他低声对雷蒙德·奥顿爵士说，「他那样年纪的人，竟挑一个少年郎来决斗！这简直是戕害孩童！」

「噢，不至于到那个地步，莫利纽克斯，」雷蒙德爵士笃信不疑。「他会轻轻松松刺他一剑，梅里奥特卧床一星期上下也就好了。伦斯利还不至于糊涂到把这弄成人命案子。你知道，如今人们对决斗这种事苛刻得要命。」

走回阿灵顿街（Arlington Street）的一路，普鲁登丝自己也是这么想的：她不怎么怕死，可事情落到这一步，已经够糟了。诚然，她的剑术颇有造诣，可她心里雪亮：拿花剑过招是一回事，同一个明摆着的仇敌真刀真枪地厮杀，又是另一回事。

何况看那副身板，他还是条壮汉。论身手敏捷，她或许能占几分便宜；不错，她学过一两手没多少人会的绝招。这桩事并非全无胜算，她相信，可她承认自己实在提不起多少兴致。

===Original===
One might tell Robin, of course. Ay, and be swept off to France, or see him throw off his disguise and take her place in the encounter. He was quite equal to it; he lacked her cautiousness. Against flight she resolutely set her face. One would leave a sullied name behind; the large gentleman﻿—well, what of him? She considered the point, and found herself blushing. Oh, she must needs stand well with him? The more fool she!

There was the old gentleman, to be sure, but she could not see how he might be expected to help in this. He could whisk her off, doubtless, as Robin would, and then what lay before? She saw a dark road that way, and turned from it. There was little enough to hope for in staying here in England, when one came to think of it, but﻿—Lord, what ailed her that she must still cling to this masquerade?

She reflected that she had steered her craft into a whirlpool; and discovered an ambition in herself to steer it out again, without assistance. To take Robin into her confidence was to overset all their plans: it was to become, in fact, a nuisance.

It was possible she might be unmasked in this encounter: that had to be considered. A wound, the apothecary﻿—Lord, what a pretty scandal! If the worst came to the worst, and her wits failed her, she believed Mr. Belfort might be taken into her confidence. She had a feeling she could trust him. He could arrange matters so as to preserve her secret. She might appeal to his love of adventure. It was not what she liked, but if no better scheme presented itself it might serve. And one must not forget that there was always the possibility of vanquishing Mr. Rensley.

===Chinese===
当然，可以把这事告诉罗宾。是啊，然后被卷包带走法国，要么就眼看他甩掉伪装，顶替她上阵赴斗。他完全干得出来；他缺的就是她那分谨慎。至于逃走，她是断然不肯的。一走了之便会留下污名；那位大块头绅士——唉，他又算得了什么？她把这层掂了掂，发觉自己脸红了。哦，她就非得在他跟前挣个好印象不可？那她可真够傻的！

当然，还有那位老先生在，可她看不出这一回怎么能指望他搭把手。毫无疑问，他能像罗宾那样把她一卷带走，可那之后等着的是什么？那条路她望去黑黢黢的，便掉头避开了。细想起来，留在这英格兰，可盼的本就不多，可是——主啊，她到底犯了什么毛病，竟还死死抱着这场化装戏不放？

她思忖自己已把这条船驾进了漩涡；又发觉心里生出一股志气，要不用旁人搭手，再把它驾出来。把实情吐给罗宾，便会掀翻他们全盘的计划：说实在的，那只会平添一桩麻烦。

这一场较量里，她兴许会被揭去面具：这一点不得不计较。一处刀伤，一位药剂师——主啊，好一桩漂亮的丑闻！倘若糟到无可再糟，她的机智又告了乏，她想贝尔福特先生或可引为心腹。她自觉信得过他。他能把事情安排得保住她的秘密。她还可以投他所好，动他那颗爱冒险的心。这不合她的心意，可若拿不出更好的章程，也只得如此。况且别忘了：战胜伦斯利先生的可能性，终究是始终存在的。

===Original===
She came home in mood somewhat silent, and Robin railed gaily at her for dreaming of her mountain.

===Chinese===
她回到家中，神情有几分沉默，罗宾便笑呵呵地数落她，说她又梦她那座山去了。

===Original===
To 

 G. R. R.

===Chinese===
致

 G. R. R.

## XIII Encounter at White’s / XIII 怀特俱乐部的一场遭遇
===Original===
Far from exhibiting a disposition to seek any sort of seclusion, such as might be supposed to become a gentleman waiting upon so large a claim, the new Lord Barham showed himself abroad whenever opportunity presented itself. It was quite impossible for anyone living in polite Society to be long ignorant of his lordship’s existence: he was a most prominent gentleman. His stature might lack something in height, for he was after all but a small man, but this was more than compensated for by the overwhelming personality of the man. He had but to enter a room for every eye to turn involuntarily in his direction. It was not in his dress that this distinctiveness lay, though that was always gorgeous; it was not even in his carriage, however haughty that might be. It was thought to lie in the arresting quality of his eyes: if he looked at one, one was straightway conscious of no little magnetism.

Discussion concerning him was rife; his children had to listen to all manner of conjectures and rumours, and derived therefrom some amusement, and some alarm as well.

He had his supporters; in the ranks of the ladies they were numberless. Who, pray, could like that coarse Rensley? The ladies knew nothing of claims, or legal matters, but they were sure this gentleman had all the air of a great man, and was far more fitted to be a Viscount than that odious Rensley.
===Chinese===
新任巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）非但没有半点深居简出的意思——照常理，一位坐候如此重大爵位主张的绅士，似乎合该这样韬光养晦——反倒一有机会便四处露面。但凡身在体面社交界的人，谁也不可能长久不知勋爵大人的存在：他是位顶顶惹眼的绅士。论身量，他或许欠些高度，毕竟他终究只是个小个子男人，可这一点早叫他那压倒一切的人格气派加倍补足了。他只消走进一间屋子，满屋的眼睛便都不由自主地朝他转过去。这份出众并不在他的衣着上，尽管他的衣着向来华丽；甚至也不在他的仪态上，任凭那仪态何等倨傲。人们认为，这出众全在他一双眼睛那慑人心神的气韵：他若看上谁一眼，那人立时便觉出一股不容小觑的磁力。

关于他的议论沸沸扬扬；他的一双儿女不得不听尽各式各样的揣测与传闻，从中得了些乐子，也添了些忧惧。

他自有拥戴他的人；在太太小姐们的队伍里，拥戴者更是数也数不清。试问，谁能喜欢伦斯利（Rensley）那副粗坯相？太太小姐们对爵位主张也好，法律文书也罢，一概一窍不通，可她们拿得准：这位绅士通身上下尽是伟人气派，比起那个可憎的伦斯利来，不知配当多少倍的子爵。
===Original===
Amongst the men opinions were varied. There were those who said he had the look of the Tremaines, and there were others who could see no resemblance. Foremost of these was old Mr. Fontenoy, who had some recollection of the lost Tremaine as a boy. He said that the lad he had known was a frank, impetuous youth, and could by no means have developed into the incorrigible actor this fellow showed himself to be.

But opposed to Mr. Fontenoy stood my Lord Clevedale, that jovial peer, who claimed also to have known young Tremaine. He could very easily imagine that the hotheaded boy might easily change into the present figure as the years went by. He claimed old acquaintance with Lord Barham, and was accepted with rapture. To be sure, the Viscount seemed to remember very little of those bygone days, but then my Lord Clevedale’s memory was also a trifle hazy. It was all so many years ago﻿—thirty at least, his lordship believed, for young Tremaine had run off to the Continent when he was scarce a day more than eighteen.

No one set much store by Mr. Rensley’s stout refusal to acknowledge his supposed cousin. Naturally Rensley would fight. The trouble was to know how to address poor Rensley. One could not have two Viscounts of the same name, but until the lawyers had done ferreting out information, and quibbling over documents the new lord had no claim to any title at all, and Rensley might continue to hold it, as he held the estates and the houses. Yet for some reason﻿—it must again lie in that magnetic eye﻿—the newcomer was everywhere addressed as Lord Barham, while his less forceful relative sank back into undistinguished esquiredom.
===Chinese===
男人中间，看法便各色各样了。有人说他有特雷梅因（Tremaine）家的相貌，也有人看不出半分相像。后一种人里领头的，是老丰特努瓦先生（Mr. Fontenoy），他对失踪的特雷梅因少年时还有些印象。他说，他当年识得的那个小子是个坦率冲动的少年，万万变不成眼前这人这副模样的、不可救药的戏子。

可站在丰特努瓦先生对面的，是克利夫代尔勋爵（Lord Clevedale）——那位快活的爵爷——他也自称识得年轻的特雷梅因。他毫不费力便能想见，那个急性子的小子随着年岁一年年过去，极容易变成眼前这副人物。他自称与巴勒姆勋爵是旧相识，勋爵大人欢天喜地地认下了。诚然，子爵对那些逝去的年月似乎记得甚少，不过克利夫代尔勋爵本人的记性也有几分含糊。那都是好多年前的旧事了——勋爵大人估摸着至少有三十年，因为年轻的特雷梅因刚满十八岁没几天，便跑到欧陆去了。

伦斯利先生咬死不认这位所谓的堂亲，对此没有谁太拿它当回事。伦斯利自然要争。麻烦在于不知道该怎么称呼可怜的伦斯利。同一个名号底下不能有两个子爵，可在律师们搜罗完凭据、为文书咬文嚼字地争出个结果之前，新来的这位勋爵对任何爵衔都还没有名分，伦斯利便尽可以照旧把爵衔攥在手里，一如他攥着那份家业和那些宅邸。然而不知怎的——想必又得归功于那双带磁力的眼睛——这位新来者到处都被人称作巴勒姆勋爵，而那位气派稍逊的亲眷，便退回了无人问津的乡绅行列。
===Original===
It was thought to augur well for the authenticity of my lord’s claim that he made no demand on the estate. An impostor, so it was argued, would have been sure to try to get money advanced him from the lawyers. But his lordship had put forward no such suggestion; nor did he show any desire to oust Rensley from the town house in Grosvenor Square until all should be satisfactorily proved. The ladies thought this showed a sweet disposition in the old gentleman; the gentlemen wagged solemn heads, and did not know what to make of it.

When my lord made his stately way in at the sacred portals of White’s club there were one or two gentlemen muttered darkly of effrontery. But the mutterings died down; my lord became a member of the club. No one quite knew the man responsible for this; it was Sir Anthony Fanshawe who said with a deep chuckle that he believed they might see my lord’s proposer in my lord himself. Several gentlemen were quite indignant when the full force of this suggestion dawned on them, but there was no movement made to eject his lordship. He was accepted, perforce, and it had to be admitted that in spite of some foreign extravagancies of manner, his ton was all that it should be, and his general bearing a fine mixture of stateliness and affability.

But there was no denying the man was a puzzle. No one could remember ever to have heard him announce, point-blank, that he was in very truth what he claimed to be. It was recollected that naturally no one cared to ask him this ticklish question, and this was thought by some to extenuate this omission on his part. But others felt that an honest claimant should have an open way with him. Instead of offering any proof to Rensley, and the world at large, of his identity he seemed content to remain an enigma until the lawyers should have done. Lord Clevedale considered this attitude to be a point in the old gentleman’s favour, but Mr. Fontenoy shook his head, and said it was not at all in keeping with the character of young Tremaine.
===Chinese===
勋爵大人对家业分文不索，人们认为，这倒给他主张的真实添了个吉兆。照一些人的论法，冒牌货必定会使劲想法子从律师手里预支钱财。可勋爵大人连半个字的这种要求都不曾提；在一切水落石出之前，他也毫不想把伦斯利从格罗夫纳广场（Grosvenor Square）的城中宅邸撵出去。太太小姐们认为，这显出老先生一副甜和的好性子；先生们则一本正经地摇着头，不知该把这看作什么。

勋爵大人仪态万方地迈进怀特俱乐部（White's）那神圣的门庭时，有一两位绅士阴阳地咕哝了几句厚颜无耻。可咕哝声渐渐平息了；勋爵大人成了俱乐部会员。谁是经手此事之人，谁也说不真切；是安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）低沉地闷笑一声，说依他之见，诸位不妨到勋爵大人自己身上去找那位入会引荐人。几位绅士等咂摸透这话的全部分量，相当动了气，却没有人起身要把勋爵大人请出去。他被勉力接纳了；而且不得不承认，尽管他的做派带着几分外国式的浮夸，他的风度却无可挑剔，通身的气概更是威严与和蔼的绝妙调和。

可无可否认，这人是桩谜。谁也想不起曾听他直截了当宣布过，自己千真万确就是他自称的那个人。大家回想起来，这样的烫手问题自然无人肯当面问他，于是有人认为，这多少为他这一番缄默作了开脱。可也有人觉得，一个问心无愧的爵位主张人，行事理当坦荡。他不向伦斯利、也不向世人拿出半点证明身份的凭据，反倒安于守着个谜，只等律师们了结一切。克利夫代尔勋爵认为这种态度是老先生的加分处，丰特努瓦先生却直摇头，说这同年轻特雷梅因的性子全不相合。
===Original===
There was some discussion also as to the ticklish point of my lord’s social position while the matter stood in abeyance, but in the end it was decided, no one quite knew how, that he was to be received. In this the ladies may have had something to say, for they frankly doted on his lordship. So the old gentleman paraded the town, and became immersed in social engagements. His children met him almost every day at some house or other, and it was observed that his lordship was developing quite an affection for these young guests of his dear friend, Lady Lowestoft.

Sir Anthony saw fit to twit Prudence on the growing intimacy, one late afternoon at White’s. They were standing in the cardroom, Sir Anthony but just come in, and Prudence having risen from a faro table.

She had her answer ready. “Oh, it’s quite an amusing old roué!” she said, with a startling lack of respect for so near a relative. “He comes to visit my lady, and ogles my poor Kate.”
===Chinese===
至于这桩事悬而未决期间勋爵大人的社交地位这一微妙问题，也起过一番议论，但到头来，谁也说不清是怎么定的，社交界决定接纳他。这里头太太小姐们多半说过话，因为她们对勋爵大人分明宠爱得了不得。于是老先生招摇过市，一头扎进种种应酬里。他的儿女几乎天天在某家某宅同他碰面，大家还看得分明，勋爵大人对他挚友洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft）府上这对年轻客人，正生出相当深的情分。

一天向晚时分，在怀特俱乐部，安东尼爵士拿这份日渐增长的亲厚打趣普鲁登丝（Prudence）。两人正站在牌室里，安东尼爵士才进门，普鲁登丝则刚从一张法罗牌（faro）桌旁起身。

她的回答是现成的。「哦，是位顶有趣的老浪子（roué）！」她说，对这样一位近亲竟大不敬得出奇。「他上门拜望夫人，一个劲儿冲我那可怜的凯特（Kate）抛媚眼。」
===Original===
“And how does Miss Merriot take that?” inquired Sir Anthony, nodding across the room to Mr. Belfort.

“With equanimity, sir. I tell her she’s like to lose her heart to the old gentleman. Pray, is he married, do you know?”

“I should have thought you would be more likely to have the answer to that,” was the unexpected rejoinder.

“I, sir?”
===Chinese===
「那梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）作何感想呢？」安东尼爵士问道，一面朝屋那头的贝尔福特先生（Mr. Belfort）点头示意。

「泰然自若，爵士。我跟她说，她怕要把一颗心丢给老先生了。请问，他可娶过亲，您知道么？」

「我还当这问题的答案多半会在你手里呢。」这答话出人意料。

「我，爵士？」
===Original===
“My Lady Lowestoft should know, surely,” said Sir Anthony in mild surprise.

She bit her lip. Fool, to make so stupid a slip! A sure sign her nerves were not so steady as they had been. She proceeded to smooth over the slip. “Oh, we know he had a wife once,” she said. “But she has been dead these many years. He says nothing of a fresh marriage, but I believe he does not tell my lady all.”

There was a movement behind them. They stood a little in front of the door, and they turned now to see my Lord Barham came in on the arm of Lord March.

“Ah, my dear Fanshawe!” said the old gentleman. “And my young friend Peter Merriot! You behold me fresh from the fatigues of a full hour with my perruquier.” He put up his arm, and surveyed the room through it. “Now where, where is my good friend Clevedale?”
===Chinese===
「洛斯托夫特夫人总该知道呀。」安东尼爵士说着，微露讶异。

她咬了咬嘴唇。蠢材，竟失口失得这样蠢！这是她神经不如先前沉稳的确凿征兆。她随即着手把漏子圆过去。「哦，我们知道他从前有过一位太太。」她说，「只是她去世已经好多年了。再娶的事他只字不提，不过依我看，他并没有把什么事都对夫人说尽。」

他们身后有了动静。两人站的地方离门不过几步，此刻回头，只见巴勒姆勋爵挽着马奇勋爵（Lord March）的手臂走了进来。

「啊，我亲爱的范肖！」老先生说，「还有我的小朋友彼得·梅里奥特（Peter Merriot）！你们瞧我，才同我的假发师（perruquier）足足耗满了一个钟头，方从那份劳顿里脱身出来。」他抬起一条手臂，隔着臂弯环视整间屋子。「这会儿他在哪儿呢，在哪儿，我的好朋友克利夫代尔？」
===Original===
Clevedale himself came up. “Well, Barham, what’s this? You’re half an hour late, and here am I waiting on you.”

My lord flung up his hands. “The perruquier! I crave ten thousand pardons, my dear Thomas! But the exigencies of the perruquier! Had it been anything else in the world the claims of picquet had held me adamant. But adamant, my dear Thomas! My tailor, even, I would despatch to the devil. But a perruquier! You absolve me: you have to absolve me!”

Clevedale laughed. “Gad, what foppery! Oh, I hold you excused. God send I never see you bald. Come off to my table. I’ve held it in the teeth of Molyneux this half-hour.” He bore my lord off to a place near the window.
===Chinese===
克利夫代尔亲自走上前来。「嗨，巴勒姆，这是怎么说？你迟了半个钟头，害我在这儿干等你。」

勋爵大人双手直扬起来。「假发师！我一万个告罪，我亲爱的托马斯（Thomas）！可谁叫这事关假发师呢！换了天底下随便哪一桩旁的差事，皮克牌（picquet）的召唤都足以叫我铁石心肠不为所动。真是铁石心肠，我亲爱的托马斯！连我的裁缝，我都能一股脑儿打发去见魔鬼。可这是假发师呀！你就饶了我吧：你不能不饶我！」

克利夫代尔笑了。「天哪，好一副花花做派！罢了，我算你告过罪了。上帝保佑，可别叫我有朝一日瞧见你秃了头。跟我上我的牌桌来吧。这半个钟头，我一直顶着莫利纽克斯（Molyneux）守住它呢。」他挽起勋爵大人，把人带到靠窗的一个座儿去。
===Original===
“I wonder, doesn’t he find that manner a thought fatiguing to maintain?” said Sir Anthony meditatively.

“Clevedale?” Prudence looked inquiringly.

“No, my innocent: the new Viscount.”

Mr. Belfort came over to them. “Tony, here’s Devereux wants to play at lansquenet, and all the world’s bent on faro. Will you and Merriot join us? The devil’s in Devereux that naught else will do for him. But the poor fellow’s feeling plaguily low today: he’s had bad news, y’know.” Mr. Belfort nodded profoundly. “One must try to cheer him, so I’m pledged to find a four for lansquenet. Always plays lansquenet when he’s in trouble, does Devereux.”
===Chinese===
「我倒纳闷，那样一副做派，维持起来他不嫌有点儿累人么？」安东尼爵士若有所思地说。

「克利夫代尔？」普鲁登丝眼里带着探问。

「不是，我的天真朋友：是新来的子爵。」

贝尔福特先生朝他们走了过来。「托尼（Tony），德弗罗（Devereux）想玩兰斯克内牌（lansquenet），可满世界的人都一门心思扎在法罗牌上。你和梅里奥特（Merriot）肯入我们一伙么？德弗罗那家伙着了魔，别的什么都不依。这位可怜的老兄今天情绪低落得厉害：他得了坏消息，你知道吧。」贝尔福特先生意味深长地点点头。「总得想法子给他打打气，所以我许下愿要凑齐四个人的兰斯克内。德弗罗一有心事就玩兰斯克内，回回如此。」
===Original===
“Pray, what’s the nature of his trouble?” Prudence asked solicitously.

“Oh, cursed bad news, my boy! That old aunt of his from whom he has expectations has rallied, and they say she’ll last another ten years. Poor old Devereux, y’know! Must try and raise his spirits.”

So with this praiseworthy intention they went to play lansquenet with Mr. Devereux.
===Chinese===
「请问，他那桩心事究竟是怎么回事？」普鲁登丝关切地问。

「哦，糟透了的坏消息，我的小伙计！他那位指望着继承遗产的老姑母病情回稳了，据说还能再活上十年。可怜的老德弗罗，你知道的！总得想法子替他提提神。」

于是，怀着这番可嘉的用心，他们去同德弗罗先生玩兰斯克内牌了。
===Original===
There entered a few minutes later Rensley, in company with his friend Mr. Markham. Mr. Markham looked heated; Mr. Rensley was scowling. The truth was he had been somewhat testy with his satellite, and there had been a slight altercation. Mr. Rensley refused curtly an invitation to join a faro party, on the score of his being promised to Markham. The pair sat down to picquet at a table close to Mr. Belfort’s.

It fell to Mr. Markham to deal, while Rensley looked sourly round the room. His glance fell upon my Lord Barham, likewise engaged on picquet. He uttered a strong expletive beneath his breath, and glared angrily. My lord, catching sight of him, waved a white hand, which salutation Mr. Rensley did not return. “Damn the fellow, he’s no more my cousin than you are!” he said, addressing Mr. Markham.

Mr. Markham was still feeling ruffled. Rensley was always quick of temper, and one bore outbursts of anger from a rich viscount. But if Rensley was going to lose his wealth and his title his friend Markham had no intention of bearing his ill-humour with complacency. “Gad, man, let be!” he said shortly. “You’ve said little else for the past hour. Do you take all five cards?”

Rensley sorted his hand rather sullenly, and took time over his discard. A well-known voice smote Mr. Markham’s ears: “Don’t despair, Devereux! She may die of an apoplexy yet!”
===Chinese===
几分钟后伦斯利进来了，同来的还有他的朋友马卡姆先生（Mr. Markham）。马卡姆先生满面涨红；伦斯利先生沉着一张脸。实情是，他方才对他的那位跟班颇发了些脾气，两人起了一场小小的口角。有人邀伦斯利先生入一局法罗牌，他一口回绝了，理由是他已答应陪马卡姆。两人在紧挨着贝尔福特先生的一张桌旁坐下来玩皮克牌。

该着由马卡姆先生发牌，伦斯利则悻悻地扫视牌室。他的目光落到巴勒姆勋爵身上，那位也同样正玩着皮克牌。他压着嗓子迸出一句狠咒骂，怒目而视。勋爵大人瞧见了，挥了挥一只白皙的手，这一番致意，伦斯利先生却没有领受。「让这家伙见鬼去，他哪里是我堂亲，就跟你不我堂亲一个样！」他对马卡姆先生说。

马卡姆先生心里还窝着火。伦斯利向来脾气急，一位有钱的子爵发火，人家也就担待了。可伦斯利若是眼看要丢了财又丢了爵衔，他的朋友马卡姆可没打算再和颜悦色地受他的坏气。「天哪，老兄，算了吧！」他没好气地说。「这过去的一个钟头，你嘴里就没说过别的。五张牌你全要么？」

伦斯利闷闷地理了理一手牌，垫牌时又慢慢磨蹭。一个耳熟的声音敲进马卡姆先生耳朵：「别绝望，德弗罗！她兴许还会害一场中风死掉呢！」
===Original===
Mr. Markham looked sharply round, and found that Mr. Merriot was seated close at hand. He bowed politely, but his brow was black as he faced Rensley again.

Rensley saw, and smiled disagreeably. “Ay, the young sprig from the country’s here, Gregory. Ecod, I believe the lad’s worsted you in some encounter! Eh! man? Now what did he do to you, I wonder?”

“That puppy!” Mr. Markham flushed. “I could break him across my knee!”

“Well, why don’t you?” asked Rensley. “You talk a deal, the Lord knows!”
===Chinese===
马卡姆先生霍地回头，发现梅里奥特先生就坐在近旁。他客气地欠身行了个礼，可再转向伦斯利时，一张脸黑得像锅底。

伦斯利瞧见了，笑得可不怀好意。「是啊，乡下来的那根小嫩芽儿也在这儿呢，格雷戈里（Gregory）。乖乖，我看这小子准是在哪一回交手里叫你吃了瘪！嗯？老兄？我倒纳闷，他到底把你怎么着了？」

「那个小狗崽子！」马卡姆先生涨红了脸。「我能把他搁在膝盖上折成两截！」

「那好啊，你怎么不折呢？」伦斯利问，「你话可说得真不少，老天爷都听着呢！」
===Original===
Markham laid down his cards. “Not to you for much longer, sir, I warn you!” he said.

“Oh, play to my lead, man, play to my lead! Gad, but you’ll admit you’d try the patience of a saint with your prating of having seen that﻿—that impostor somewhere, and not knowing where! Why can’t you think?”

My Lord Barham rose from his table across the room, and stood for a moment talking to March. One or two men gathered around them, after a moment a dice-box was produced, and March cast the dice on the table. Heads were bent over it; there was a laugh, and a murmur of speech, and my Lord Barham swept up the dice.

Mr. Markham chanced that moment to look up. He saw my lord shake back his ruffles, and with eyes growing gradually wider he saw him throw the dice with a curious flick of the wrist.
===Chinese===
马卡姆把牌撂下。「对你，我可忍不了多久了，先生，我警告你！」他说。

「哎呀，照我领的牌出就是了，老兄，照我领的牌出！天哪，可你也得承认，你翻来覆去唠叨什么在哪儿见过那个——那个骗子，偏又想不起在哪儿，这份磨劲连圣人的耐性也要给你磨光！你怎么就不能动动脑子？」

巴勒姆勋爵从屋子那头他的牌桌旁站起身，站着同马奇谈了一会儿。有一两个人围拢过来，片刻之后，有人取出一只骰盅，马奇把骰子掷在桌上。几颗脑袋凑了上去；响起一阵哄笑和一片低语，随后巴勒姆勋爵把骰子一拢收了。

恰在那个当口，马卡姆先生偶然抬起了眼。他看见勋爵大人把花边袖口抖回腕后，接着，他瞪着越睁越大的眼睛，看见勋爵大人手腕古怪地一抖，把骰子掷了出去。
===Original===
Mr. Markham was in the act of dealing, but his hand with three cards in it stayed poised in midair, and he continued to stare across at my lord, his jaw slightly dropped.

“What’s to do now?” demanded Rensley. “Gad, have you remembered,” he added eagerly.

“That man﻿—why, fiend seize it, he’s no more than a common gamester! Of course I know him! Thunder and turf, he’s no viscount. He used to keep a gaming-house in Munich! The instant he cast the dice it all came back to me. Know him! I’ve played in his house a dozen times.”

It seemed the dice had been cast for some special stake only. My lord was coming slowly across the room with March and Clevedale, laughing gently at something March said in his ear. He paused a moment by the lansquenet table, and complimented Sir Anthony on his play. “So few people nowadays understand the art!” he sighed. His smiling glance fell on Rensley’s face. He came to the other table, still leaning on Clevedale’s arm. “My cousin! I salute you!” he said.
===Chinese===
马卡姆先生正发着牌，捏着三张牌的那只手却僵在了半空，他依旧直勾勾盯着屋子那头的勋爵大人，下巴微微耷拉着。

「又出了什么岔子？」伦斯利质问。「天哪，你想起来了？」他又急切地追问。

「那个人——嗐，叫魔鬼抓了去，他不过是个寻常赌棍！我当然认得他！雷劈电打的，他根本不是什么子爵。他从前在慕尼黑（Munich）开赌场！他一掷骰子，我霎时间全想起来了。认得他！我上他场子里玩过十几回呢！」

看来那骰子只是为一桩特别的赌注才掷的。勋爵大人由马奇和克利夫代尔陪着，缓缓穿过牌室走过来，马奇在他耳边说了句什么，引得他低声轻笑。他在兰斯克内牌桌旁略停了停，称许了安东尼爵士的牌品。「如今懂得这门艺术的，可太少了！」他叹道。他含笑的目光落到伦斯利脸上。他依然倚着克利夫代尔的手臂，走到另一张桌前。「我的堂亲！我向你致敬！」他说。
===Original===
Mr. Rensley’s chair scraped along the boards as he sprang up. “Damn it, don’t call me cousin!” he said loudly. “You’re no more than a cursed gamester!”

There fell a sudden hush, for Rensley’s voice carried through the room. Heads were turned; there followed a buzz of whispering. One of his companions fell a little away from my Lord Barham. My lord continued to smile. “Oh!” he said. “Who told you that?”

Markham put down the pack of cards. “I’ve visited the gaming-house you used to keep in Munich,” he said.

My lord looked at him with interest. The whole room awaited breathlessly his reply. It came as a complete surprise to every man there. “Then that must have been where I met you!” he said in the tone of one making an agreeable discovery. “I thought your face familiar from the first.”
===Chinese===
伦斯利先生霍地跳起身，椅子在地板上刮出刺耳的一响。「他妈的，别叫我堂亲！」他高声说，「你不过是个该死的赌棍！」

屋里霎时一片肃静，因为伦斯利的嗓门传遍了整间屋子。人头纷纷转了过来；跟着是一片嗡嗡的耳语。勋爵大人同伴中有一位悄悄从他身边退开了半步。勋爵大人依旧含着笑。「哦！」他说，「这话是谁告诉你的？」

马卡姆把那副牌放了下来。「你从前在慕尼黑开的那家赌场，我进去玩过。」他说。

勋爵大人饶有兴味地望着他。满屋子的人都屏息等他作答。他的回答，让在场每一个人都全然出乎意料。「那么，咱们准是在那儿认识的！」他说，那口气像是得了一个令人愉快的发现。「我头一回见你就觉得你面熟。」
===Original===
At the lansquenet table Sir Anthony gave a low laugh. “Faith, I begin to have a liking for the old gentleman!” he said.

“You admit it, do you?” Mr. Rensley felt his words fall lamely upon expectant ears.

“Admit what?” said my lord, puzzled.

“Why﻿—damme, that you’ve kept a common gaming-house!”
===Chinese===
兰斯克内牌桌那边，安东尼爵士低低笑了。「说真的，我开始喜欢这位老先生了！」他说。

「你认了，是么？」伦斯利先生只觉得自己这句话软塌塌地落在满堂竖起期待的耳朵上。

「认什么？」勋爵大人问，一脸困惑。

「怎么——妈的，认你开过一家不入流的赌场！」
===Original===
My lord’s hand was raised. “No!” he said emphatically, and a sigh went round the room. His next words dispelled relief. “Never in all my life have I kept anything that was common! You insult me by the suggestion.”

There was a low ripple of laughter. People were gathering about that corner of the room, eager to hear what might be the issue.

“No use to play with words, fellow. That won’t serve,” Rensley cried angrily. “Have you kept a gaming-house?”

The old gentleman took snuff. “I have kept at least a dozen, my dear Rensley,” he said, with perfect composure. He looked again towards Mr. Markham. “I am not entirely satisfied,” he mused. “Are you sure you never had lessons in fencing from me, sir?”
===Chinese===
勋爵大人抬起一只手。「不对！」他斩钉截铁地说，满屋子响起一片如释重负的叹声。可他接下来的一句话，把这宽慰驱散得干干净净。「我这一生，从不曾经营过任何不入流的营生！你提这个，分明是辱没我。」

屋里漾起一阵低低的笑声。人们朝屋子那一角围拢过来，都急着听听这事会落个什么下场。

「在字眼儿上耍花枪没有用，小子。那不管用。」伦斯利怒气冲冲地喊道，「你到底开没开过赌场？」

老先生撮了一撮鼻烟。「少说也开过一打了，我亲爱的伦斯利。」他说，神色安然到了极处。他又朝马卡姆先生望去。「我还不算十分满意。」他沉吟道，「您当真没跟我学过剑术么，先生？」
===Original===
There was a gasp. All play was at an end in the cardroom. My Lord March burst out laughing. “Gad, Barham, have you been a fencing-master, too?” he exclaimed.

The old gentleman shut his gold snuffbox with a snap. “My dear March,” he said haughtily, “there is nothing I have not been!” He looked again at Mr. Markham. “Are you quite sure I did not give you lessons in fencing? Let me think a moment! Yes, I had an establishment in Rome once, and﻿—yes, yes, another in Turin!”

“It’s quite possible, no doubt,” sneered Mr. Markham. “I don’t trouble to remember all my fencing instructors.”

“Then of a certainty you are not a pupil of mine,” said my lord. “Me you could never forget. For those whom I taught are masters of fence. It goes without saying. I am incomparable. I have no equal in the art!”
===Chinese===
屋里响起一片倒抽气之声。牌室里一切牌局就此收了场。马奇勋爵放声大笑。「天哪，巴勒姆，难道你还当过击剑师傅？」他失声道。

老先生啪的一声合上金鼻烟盒。「我亲爱的马奇，」他傲然道，「天底下还没有我没当过的行当！」他又看向马卡姆先生。「您当真没跟我学过剑术？容我想一想！对了，我在罗马（Rome）开过一家馆子，还有——对了，对了，都灵（Turin）还有一家！」

「想必大有可能。」马卡姆先生讥诮道，「教过我剑术的师傅多着呢，我犯不着一个个都去记。」

「那么，你断乎不是我的学生了。」勋爵大人说，「我这个人，你是万万忘不掉的。因为我教出来的人，个个都是剑术大师。这自不待言。我无可匹敌。这门艺术里，我没有对手！」
===Original===
Again March broke in. “I’d give something to hear the story of your life, Barham!” he said, hugely entertained.

Rensley flushed. “His name’s not Barham!” he said furiously. “He’s the impostor I always said he was!”

March froze to instant haughtiness. “He has at least the advantage of you in the matter of good manners, Rensley,” he said.

Public opinion veered round in favour of the old gentleman.
===Chinese===
马奇又插了进来。「巴勒姆，你一生的故事，叫我拿点什么来换着听都行！」他说，乐不可支。

伦斯利涨红了脸。「他根本不姓巴勒姆！」他狂怒地喊道，「他正是我一向说的那个骗子！」

马奇霎时冷了下来，架子端得十足。「至少在教养这件事上，他比你占着上风，伦斯利。」他说。

众人的舆论调转了方向，倒向老先生一边。
===Original===
“It’s very, very deplorable,” Mr. Devereux said, with a mournful shake of the head. “But he might be all these damned bourgeois things and still remain Tremaine of Barham.”

“You’re pleased to give him countenance, my lord, but you shall see him exposed!” Rensley snapped.

“But expose me!” cried the old gentleman, and threw wide his arms. “I am here to answer you. Who then am I?”

“Good God, am I to know who you are?” exclaimed Rensley. “But you are not Tremaine! Why, you couldn’t tell me a thing about the family that’s not known to the whole world!”
===Chinese===
「这非常、非常令人痛心。」德弗罗先生说着，悲哀地摇了摇头，「可这些个该死的市侩行当他即便样样干过，也照样不妨碍他是巴勒姆的特雷梅因。」

「大人您乐意抬举他，可您会亲眼看他被戳穿的！」伦斯利厉声道。

「只管来戳穿我呀！」老先生扬声喊道，双臂大大张开。「我就站在这里任你发问。那么，我究竟是谁呢？」

「老天爷，我上哪儿知道你是谁？」伦斯利嚷道，「可你根本不是特雷梅因！嗐，这个家族的事，凡是世人不知道的，你一件也说不出来！」
===Original===
“Ay, that’s a challenge. He must answer that!” whispered Sir Raymond Orton.

“I can at least tell you, cousin, that a portrait of me hangs in the pink salon at Barham. A very damnable likeness of me as a child, taken with my late lamented brother,” said my lord softly.

“A hit!” Mr. Belfort confided to Prudence. “That’s a hit!”

She sat in an attitude of negligent attention, an arm flung over the back of the chair, and her calm face inscrutable. She nodded, and was conscious of Fanshawe’s eyes upon her.
===Chinese===
「是啊，这就是一记挑战。他非答上来不可！」雷蒙德·奥顿爵士（Sir Raymond Orton）低声道。

「我至少可以告诉你，堂亲：巴勒姆宅的粉红客厅里，挂着一幅我的画像。画的是儿时的我，同我那位已故的、令人哀悼的兄长在一处，画得可恶地像。」勋爵大人轻声说。

「打中了！」贝尔福特先生悄悄对普鲁登丝说。「这一下打中了！」

她懒懒地坐着听人说话，一条胳膊甩在椅背上，平静的脸深不可测。她点了点头，同时察觉范肖的眼睛正落在她身上。
===Original===
Rensley banged his fist down on the table. “It’s not the pink salon!” he declared. “There is no pink salon!”

Mr. Belfort was of the opinion that this was a bad check.

“In my day,” said his lordship, undisturbed, “it was pink.”

“Faugh, what do you know of it? You’re trying to brazen it out with a barefaced lie!”
===Chinese===
伦斯利一拳捶在桌上。「那不是粉红客厅！」他断言，「根本没有什么粉红客厅！」

贝尔福特先生的看法是，这一张是张空头支票。

「在我那时候，」勋爵大人不动声色地说，「那客厅是粉红的。」

「啐，你知道个什么？你是想拿一个厚颜无耻的谎话硬撑到底！」
===Original===
Mr. Fontenoy spoke grudgingly. “There was a pink salon,” he said. “Lady Barham used it.”

My lord swept round to face him. “Ah, you remember then?” he said eagerly. “A pink salon in the west wing! There was an oriole window, and my mother’s broidery table set there!” He became rapt in reminiscences.

This produced a sensation. Mr. Belfort thought the old gentleman scored a decided hit there.

Rensley was discomfited for a moment, but recovered.
===Chinese===
丰特努瓦先生不情不愿地开了口。「是有过一间粉红客厅。」他说，「巴勒姆夫人（Lady Barham）用的。」

勋爵大人倏地转身面对他。「啊，这么说你记得！」他急切地说，「西翼的粉红客厅！有一扇凸窗，我母亲的绣桌就摆在那里！」他沉入了悠然的追忆。

这一下引起了一阵轰动。贝尔福特先生认为，老先生这一手是决定性的命中。

伦斯利一时狼狈，但随即缓过劲来。
===Original===
“Oh, you’ve been in the house in your youth! That’s all there is to that. You were a groom there, I dare swear, and you got into the house!”

Mr. Belfort wagged a solemn head. “Ay, that’s a possibility, y’know.”

My lord’s eyes glinted. Very sweet was his voice, dangerously sweet. “It’s more than you can claim to have done, my dear cousin,” he said gently. “I’ll swear you never set foot in it till my brother died!”

Rensley’s jaw dropped; he grew purple in the face. “Damn your impudence!” he spluttered.
===Chinese===
「哦，你年轻时候进过那宅子！这就没什么可说的了。我看八成你是在那儿当马夫，这么才混进宅子里的！」

贝尔福特先生郑重其事地摇了摇头。「是啊，这倒也是一种可能，你知道。」

勋爵大人的眼睛闪了一下。他的嗓音甜极了，甜得危险。「论进那宅子，这可比你能夸口的强了，亲爱的堂亲。」他温温地说，「我敢起誓，我兄长去世之前，你可从来不曾踏进它一步！」

伦斯利的下巴垂了下来；他的脸涨成了紫色。「你这张厚脸皮真该死！」他气急败坏地嚷道。
===Original===
Lord March interposed. “Enough of that. Did you set foot in it, Rensley?”

The old gentleman was indignant. “Certainly he did not!” he said, before Rensley could reply. “There was never a Rensley dared show his face on our land! What had we to do with them?” Almost he snorted.

His daughter’s eyes widened a little; Mr. Belfort sniggered.

Rensley bit back a hot answer. Came a look of cunning into his face. “So you never met me when we were boys, my Lord Barham?” he said.
===Chinese===
马奇勋爵出来打圆场。「够了。你可曾踏进过那宅子，伦斯利？」

老先生愤然作色。「他自然不曾！」不等伦斯利答话，他先开了口，「从来没有哪个伦斯利敢在我们家的地界上露脸！我们同那起子人有什么相干？」他几乎要哼出声来。

他女儿的眼睛微微睁大了；贝尔福特先生嗤嗤地窃笑起来。

伦斯利把一句火爆的回话咽了回去。他脸上浮起一丝狡诈。「这么说，咱们做孩子的时候从没打过照面，巴勒姆勋爵？」他说。
===Original===
“Only once,” said my lord. He dwelt lovingly on a pleasant memory. “How hard I punched your nose then,” he said dreamily.

There was a roar of laughter, hastily suppressed. Mr. Rensley strode to the door. “Don’t think I’ve done with you, my fine gentleman!” he said savagely, and slammed out of the room.

The old gentleman smiled affectionately upon the assembled company. “Very like an encounter I had once with a Margrave,” he said pensively. “I was acting as one of his lackeys at the time.”
===Chinese===
「只照过一回。」勋爵大人说。他津津有味地咂摸着一段愉快的回忆。「那回我把你的鼻子揍得可真狠。」他悠悠地说。

一阵哄堂大笑骤然腾起，又赶忙压了下去。伦斯利先生大步走向门口。「别以为我跟你这事就算完了，我的漂亮绅士！」他狠狠地说，砰的一声摔门而出。

老先生慈爱地朝满座诸公微笑。「这很像我从前同一位边疆伯爵（Margrave）的一场遭遇。」他若有所思地说，「那时候，我正充当他府上的一名*听差*。」
===Original===
“Tare an’ ’ouns, a *lackey*?” gasped Clevedale.

“Certainly,” said my lord, with some hauteur. “Why not? There was a lady in the case.” He smoothed a wrinkle from his satin sleeve. “She was the Margrave’s mistress,” he remarked.

Quite a number of people drew nearer. March thrust his arm in my lord’s, and walked away with him. “Let’s hear that tale, Barham,” he said. “Which Margrave?”
===Chinese===
「天杀的，当*听差*？」克利夫代尔倒抽一口气。

「当然。」勋爵大人说，带着几分傲气，「有何不可？这里头有一位女士呢。」他抚平了缎子袖筒上的一道皱褶。「她就是那位边疆伯爵的情妇。」他说。

相当多的人围拢上来。马奇把胳膊插进勋爵大人的臂弯，同他一道走开了。「给我们讲讲那段故事吧，巴勒姆。」他说，「是哪一位边疆伯爵？」

## XXVIII Exit Miss Merriot / XXVIII 梅里奥特小姐退场

===Original===
Easterly Woods lay but two miles, across country, from my Lady Lowestoft’s house, and John covered the distance swiftly. He came to the house by the river as the lamps were lit, and found my lady waiting in the hall, and Sir Anthony’s chaise in the drive outside. He pulled off his hat and spoke before my lady could open her mouth. “I took Mr. Merriot’s message to Sir Anthony, my lady,” he said in a voice loud enough to carry to the listening lackeys by the door.

My lady’s black eyes snapped. “Yes?” she said. “And he said?”

“I was to tell you, my lady, he would not think of troubling you by coming here since Mr. Merriot was took off. I’ve a note for his man.”

“Bah, it is a mistake the most absurd!” cried my lady. “Mr. Merriot will return at once! Where does Sir Anthony go?”

“He did say, my lady, he would turn off to visit a friend,” John answered. He remembered the mare, and added apologetically: “The mare cast a shoe, my lady, and I made bold to leave her with the smith.”

My lady nodded. Her eyes searched John’s face, but could read nothing therein. “Your mistress is in a sad way,” she informed him, with considerable meaning.

===Chinese===
伊斯特利树林（Easterly Woods）离洛斯托夫特夫人（My Lady Lowestoft）的宅子，抄田间小路不过两英里，约翰（John）不多时便赶完了这段路。掌灯时分，他到了河畔那座宅子，只见夫人已候在大厅里，安东尼爵士（Sir Anthony）的马车停在外头车道上。他摘下帽子，抢在夫人张口之前说上了话。「我把梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）的口信送到了安东尼爵士那儿，夫人。」他说这话的嗓门，提得恰好足以让门边竖着耳朵的听差们个个听清。

夫人那双黑眼睛炯炯一闪。「哦？」她说。「那他说了什么？」

「他吩咐我转告您，夫人，既然梅里奥特先生叫人带走了，他便不来叨扰您了。我这儿有张给他的仆人的便条。」

「呸，这是个最荒唐不过的误会！」夫人叫道。「梅里奥特先生即刻就回来！安东尼爵士上哪儿去？」

「他倒是说了，夫人，他要拐道去访一位朋友。」约翰答道。他记起那匹母马，又抱歉地补上一句：「母马掉了块蹄铁，夫人，我斗胆把她留在蹄铁匠那儿了。」

夫人点了点头。她的眼睛细细察看约翰的脸，却什么也读不出来。「你家小姐这会儿正伤心着呢。」她话里带着十足的深意。

===Original===
“Yes, my lady? Should I give the note to Sir Anthony’s man?”

“Do so at once, of course. Then come to put up a change of clothes for Mr. Merriot. You must take them to him on the instant. To snatch him away in that fashion with never a moment to pack a valise﻿—*affreux*!” She swept round, and went off up the wide stairs.

John stayed but to give Sir Anthony’s note to his man, and followed my lady to Robin’s room. He entered without ceremony and found his young master in coat and breeches, pulling on his top-boots.

“For the love of God, John, will you make him listen to reason?” besought my lady.

Robin’s fair face was set in uncompromising lines. He threw my lady an impatient glance. “Oh enough, ma’am, enough! Do you suppose I shall sit here while my sister’s hailed off under escort?”

===Chinese===
「是么，夫人？那便条要我交给安东尼爵士的仆人么？」

「自然，这就交去。然后去替梅里奥特先生收拾一身换洗衣裳。你必须即刻给他送去。那样把人掳走，连收拾一只行囊的工夫都不给——*affreux*（吓死人）！」她一旋身，一阵风似的上了宽阔的楼梯。

约翰只停下来把安东尼爵士的便条交给了他的仆人，便随着夫人到罗宾（Robin）房里去了。他也不通禀就进了门，只见他家少爷已穿好外衣和马裤，正往上提着长筒马靴。

「看在上帝份上，约翰，你能让他听一听道理么？」夫人恳求道。

罗宾那张俊脸绷出一副决不妥协的轮廓。他不耐烦地朝夫人瞥了一眼。「哦，够了，夫人，够了！您莫非以为，我姐姐被人押着带走，我还能安坐在此地？」

===Original===
John shut the door behind him. “She’s safe, sir.”

Robin’s hands left tugging at his boot. “What?”

“Sir Anthony has her, sir. He’s ridden off with her into Hampshire, and he bid me tell you he would keep her safe.”

My lady gasped. Robin turned in his chair to face John. “Good gad!” he said. “The mountain! But how, man, how?”

John became quite animated. “Sir, you couldn’t have done it better! No, nor my lord either. There’s a coach well on the way to London with two men trussed up inside it, the horses kicked over the traces, and the whole in an uproar.” He laughed at the thought of it.

===Chinese===
约翰随手关上了身后的门。「她安全了，少爷。」

罗宾拉着靴子的两手停了下来。「什么？」

「安东尼爵士把她接过去了，少爷。他带她骑马去了汉普郡（Hampshire），还吩咐我告诉您，他会护她周全。」

夫人倒抽了一口气。罗宾在椅子里转过身来，面对约翰。「我的天哪！」他说。「那座山！可这是怎么办到的，伙计，怎么办到的？」

约翰登时眉飞色舞起来。「少爷，这事就是您亲自动手，也办不到这么漂亮！勋爵大人也一样。这会儿有一辆马车正走在去伦敦的半道上，里头捆着两个五花大绑的家伙，拉车的马尥着蹶子挣乱了缰套，整辆车闹了个底朝天。」他想起那光景，哈哈笑出声来。

===Original===
My lady sat down on the edge of the bed. “Sir Anthony did this?” she said incredulously. “Never!”

“We did it between us, my lady, but ’twas Sir Anthony planned it. Ay, there’s a cool head to be sure! ’Deed I’ve not seen his equal with a horse, Master Robin. It’s a wizard he is.”

“But tell!” ordered my lady, striking her hands together.

Robin’s eyes were bright and questioning. “Let’s have it from the start, John, if you please.”

===Chinese===
夫人在床沿上坐了下来。「这是安东尼爵士干的？」她难以置信地说。「万万想不到！」

「是我们俩合力办成的，夫人，只是谋划出自安东尼爵士。可不，那才叫一颗冷静的头脑！的确，论马上的功夫，我没见过能与他匹敌的，罗宾少爷（Master Robin）。他简直就是个巫师。」

「快讲呀！」夫人命令道，双手一拍。

罗宾的眼睛亮晶晶的，满含探问。「那就劳驾，从头讲起吧，约翰。」

===Original===
“Ay, sir. You’ve to know I was off to my lord the instant those two vultures had Miss Prue off into the coach. Well, I know a way over the fields, and I could get ahead fast enough. I came on Sir Anthony riding down here, and he had the tale out of me.” John smiled. “He wouldn’t have me go to his lordship; he said ’twas his affair. That’s a man I don’t care to cross, Master Robin. He planned it we were to hold the coach up and get Miss Prue safe away. He’d be off with her to his sister, so he said, and I was to get you away this very night, sir. And so I will,” he added, with a touch of truculence.

“Never mind that.” Robin brushed it aside. “Do you tell me Sir Anthony planned to waylay this coach, and make off with a captive of the Law?”

“Oh, he made nothing of that, sir! We was both muffled to the eyes, and Sir Anthony had his sword out. We waited in a bit of a spinney till the coach rounded a bend in the road. Sir Anthony, he said to me, ‘Take them in a charge.’ But there was no doing it his way. Leastways, not for me, and I thought I could ride, so I did. He had the roan under him: you’ll know the horse, sir. Great powerful quarters, and I’ll warrant you he can cover the ground. Sir Anthony was out of the spinney, and thundering straight down upon the coach before I could know what he would be at. ’Deed, and I thought myself he would spear the roan on the shaft of the coach!”

===Chinese===
「是，少爷。您得知道，那两只秃鹫刚把普鲁小姐（Miss Prue）押进马车，我立马就动身去报勋爵大人了。喏，我认得一条穿田越野的近道，脚程抢得够快。我撞见安东尼爵士正骑马往这边来，便把事情原原本本倒给了他。」约翰微微一笑。「他不叫我去找勋爵大人；他说那是他自个儿的事。那样一个人，我可犯不着去招惹，罗宾少爷。他谋划的是：咱们去把马车拦下，平平安安救出普鲁小姐。他说他要带她去他妹妹那儿，而我呢，就在今夜把您接走，少爷。我也定要办到。」他添了一句，带着几分倔头倔脑。

「那个先别管。」罗宾把它撇到一旁。「你是说，安东尼爵士谋划着拦截那辆马车，劫走一名官府拘押的人犯？」

「哦，这个他压根没放在心上，少爷！咱俩都裹得只露出一双眼睛，安东尼爵士也拔剑在手。我们在一小片树林里候着，直等到马车拐过路上的弯道。安东尼爵士对我说：『冲上去收拾他们。』可要照他那个打法，办不来。至少我办不来；我寻思自己还会骑马，便自行其是了。他胯下骑着那匹菊花青马：少爷，那马您认得。一对后躯雄健有力，我敢担保它跑起来快得惊人。我还没弄清他要干什么，安东尼爵士已冲出树林，雷霆一般直扑那辆马车。说实话，连我自个儿都当他要把菊花青马扎死在车辕上呢！

===Original===
My lady blinked. It all seemed so very unlike the indolent Sir Anthony Fanshawe.

“How many men?” demanded Robin.

“Four﻿—if you could call them such, sir. Sir Anthony swerved to the right, and I got the mare round to the left of the coach. I’d a thick ash staff, and that accounted for the coachman. Sir Anthony planned it so that the horses were all startled and plunging; the other man on the box had his hands full with them. Sir Anthony wrenched open the coach door, and out comes one of the vultures, sprawling in the road. Sir Anthony was off the roan in a trice; I brought the mare round to him, and caught his bridle. I can tie up a man quickly and neat myself, sir, as you know, but Miss Prue’s sleepy gentleman beats all, so he does! He had him bound, arms and legs, before you’d time to look round.”

“I make the mountain my compliments,” said Robin. “Lord, I would I had been there! What did Prue do? You won’t tell me she folded her hands.”

===Chinese===
夫人眨了眨眼。这一切，同那个懒散成性的安东尼·范肖爵士实在太不相像了。

「一共几个人？」罗宾追问。

「四个——少爷，倘若那也配称作『人』的话。安东尼爵士向右一拐，我把母马兜到马车左边。我手头有根粗白蜡木棍，一棍便把车夫打发了。安东尼爵士谋划得妙：几匹马全受了惊，直立刨跳；车夫座上另一个家伙，光应付它们就两手不得闲。安东尼爵士一把拽开车门，里头一只秃鹫滚了出来，四仰八叉摔在大路上。安东尼爵士眨眼间翻身下了菊花青马；我把母马兜到他跟前，又一把抓住那匹青马的缰绳。少爷，您是知道的，我自个儿捆人也捆得又快又利索，可普鲁小姐那位懒洋洋的绅士真是没得比，真是没得比！不等您回头看清，他已把那人连胳膊带腿捆了个结实。」

「我向那座山致敬。」罗宾说。「老天，真愿我当时在场！普鲁（Prue）做了什么？你可别告诉我，她只管袖着手干看。」

===Original===
“I will not, sir. I’d seen to it she had her sword-stick with her, and you may lay your life she made use of it. She had the point at the other man’s throat till Sir Anthony jumped in to take his pistol from him, so I heard. There was no more to it. We were off, all three of us, with Miss Prue up before Sir Anthony on the roan. We made for Easterly Woods, and ’twas there I gave the mare up to Miss Prue.”

Robin slowly pulled off his boots again. “Lord!” he said. “And so farewell Peter Merriot! She went willingly?”

“Oh ay, she knew well enough there was no saying him nay then. He told me to bring her woman’s clothes down to my Lady Enderby’s as soon as may be. For, says he, ‘she’s done with this masquerade.’ But first, sir, I must have you away. We’ll have a whole pack of the Watch down on us here when this is known.”

Robin bit one fingertip. “If the mountain﻿—egad, what a man it is!﻿—has borne Prue off there’s naught for me to do. I’ll slip away tonight.”

===Chinese===
「我不会那么说，少爷。我早打点好了，让她随身带着剑杖（sword-stick），您拿性命打赌都成，她准用它了。听说她拿剑尖抵住了另一个家伙的喉咙，直抵到安东尼爵士跳上车去下他的手枪为止。再往后就没什么可说的了。我们三个一道脱身，普鲁小姐坐在前头，同安东尼爵士共骑那匹菊花青马。我们直奔伊斯特利树林，便是在那儿，我把母马交给了普鲁小姐。」

罗宾又慢慢把靴子褪了下来。「老天！」他说。「就这样，别了，彼得·梅里奥特（Peter Merriot）！她是情愿走的？」

「哦是的，她心里透亮，那时候说什么也没法回他的话。他吩咐我尽快把她的女人衣裳送到恩德比夫人（Lady Enderby）府上去。因为，他说：『她这场化装戏演到头了。』不过先得把您送走，少爷。这事一旦张扬出去，一大帮巡丁立刻会砸到咱们头上来。」

罗宾咬着一根指尖。「倘若那座山——天哪，那是何等样的人物！——已把普鲁掳走，那我就无事可做了。我今夜便溜走。」

===Original===
John nodded. “Ay, but get you into your petticoats again now. I’m off to his lordship. It’s odds he’ll have something to say. I’ll take the valise my lady spoke of, to seem as though I were off to Miss Prue in prison.”

“Drive the curricle,” my lady said.

“Ay, my lady. And you’ll bide here, Master Robin, till I bring word from his lordship.”

Robin got up. “Don’t fear me. I make my escape when everyone’s abed. I’ll await your return safe enough.”

===Chinese===
约翰点了点头。「是，不过您这会儿先把裙裳穿回去。我这就动身去勋爵大人那儿。十有八九他有话要吩咐。夫人说的那只行囊我带上，装作我是去监狱给普鲁小姐送东西的。」

「驾那辆双轮轻便马车（curricle）去。」夫人说。

「是，夫人。还有您，罗宾少爷，您且守在这儿，等我带回勋爵大人的话。」

罗宾站起身。「别替我担心。等大家都睡下了，我便脱身。我等您回来，稳当得很。」

===Original===
He and my lady had dinner in lonely state in the big dining-room. In the character of Miss Merriot he affected to be quite overcome; my lady, when dinner was over, insisted that poor Kate should lie down in her boudoir with the hartshorn. She led poor Kate thither, and summoned fat Marthe. Fat Marthe was told that my lady did not desire her servants to sit up late. It was to be understood both she and Miss Merriot had gone early to bed. Marthe signified complete understanding, and rolled out again. My lady and Robin sat and talked over the strange events of the day, and the gilt clock on the mantelpiece ticked over the minutes.

At ten o’clock Robin was restive, listening for John, and he began to tap an impatient foot. Why must he delay, a’God’s name? Marthe came in with hot chocolate, and the news that old Williams had at last taken himself off to bed. The house was very still. Robin went softly away to his chamber, candlestick in hand, and was shut up there for nearly an hour. It was just on eleven when he came back into my lady’s boudoir, and he was dressed in coat and breeches with shining top-boots on his feet, and a sword at his side. He went to the window, and stood looking down the moonlit road, listening.

My lady studied his profile, and when he turned, feeling her gaze upon him, nodded and said: “*Du vrai*, my child, I like you best as a man. I do not think anyone will ever know you for the bold Miss Merriot.”

===Chinese===
他同夫人在大饭厅里冷冷清清、却摆足仪制地用了晚餐。他仍以梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）的身份，装出一副受惊不轻的样子；晚餐用罢，夫人坚持要可怜的凯特（Kate）带着鹿角精嗅盐（hartshorn）到她闺房里躺一躺。她把可怜的凯特领了去，又唤来胖玛尔特（Marthe）。胖玛尔特听得吩咐：夫人不愿自家的仆人熬夜。还要叫大家明白，夫人和梅里奥特小姐都已早早安歇。玛尔特表示全然领会，又滚着身子出去了。夫人同罗宾坐下来，把这一天的种种怪事细细谈论，壁炉架上的镀金座钟嘀嗒嘀嗒，数着时辰。

十点钟时，罗宾已等得焦躁，侧耳听着约翰的动静，一只脚不耐烦地开始点地。看在上帝份上，他还非得这么耽搁不可么？玛尔特端着热巧克力进来，还捎来消息说，老威廉斯（Williams）总算自去睡了。宅子里静极了。罗宾手擎烛台，轻手轻脚地回自己房里去，闭门在里头待了将近一个钟头。将近十一点，他回到夫人的闺房，已换上外衣和马裤，脚上是擦得锃亮的长筒马靴，腰间佩着一柄剑。他走到窗前，伫立着俯望月光洒落的大路，凝神听着。

夫人端详他的侧影；他觉出她的目光落在自己身上，转过身来，她点了点头，说道：「*Du vrai*（说真的），我的孩子，我最喜欢你做男人的模样。我想，再不会有人认得出，你就是那位天不怕地不怕的梅里奥特小姐了。」

===Original===
“You don’t, ma’am?” Robin glanced towards the mirror.

“No, never. I do not know what makes the so great change.” She pondered it. “Miss Merriot was a fair height for a lady, but Master Robin﻿—oh, we must not call him a little man, of course!”

“You spare my feelings, in fact. It may be the neckcloth, and the hair drawn back. I was careful always to affect a dégagé style as Miss Merriot.”

“Well,” said my lady slowly. “Miss Merriot was a dainty piece, but you, my child﻿—you look to be all muscle and﻿—je ne sais quoi.”

“I have my fair share of muscle, ma’am, I believe,” Robin said modestly.

===Chinese===
「是么，夫人？」罗宾朝镜子瞥了一眼。

「不会，永远不会。我也说不清，是什么造出了这么大的变化。」她细细琢磨起来。「论小姐，梅里奥特小姐个子算是高的；可罗宾少爷——哦，咱们自然不能管他叫小个子！」

「您这是顾全我的感受。许是领巾的缘故，加上头发往后一拢。做梅里奥特小姐时，我一向留心装出一副洒脱随性（dégagé）的风致。」

「嗯，」夫人慢悠悠地说。「梅里奥特小姐是个娇滴滴的可人儿，可你，我的孩子——你瞧上去浑身是筋骨，还有——je ne sais quoi（说不出的什么）。」

「我相信，我名下该有的那份筋骨还是有的，夫人。」罗宾谦逊地说。

===Original===
But my lady was right. With her petticoats he cast off all Miss Merriot’s mannerisms. Kate had a tripping step: Robin a clean, swift stride; Kate was languorous: Robin never; Kate fell into charming attitudes: Robin’s every movement was alert and decisive; Kate could adopt a melting siren’s voice: Robin’s speech was crisp, just as his eye was keen where Kate’s was languishing. The truth was he was a consummate actor, and if he played a part he became that part, heart and soul. My Lady Lowestoft had often marvelled at the perfection of his acting, the rigid attention to every little feminine detail, but she doubted whether she had ever appreciated him fully until now, when he threw off his disguise and all its attendant mannerisms.

She was thinking of this when the sound of horses came to her ear. In another minute or two the wheels stopped by the porch.

Robin peered through the windowpane. “This will be John at last. Oh lord, ma’am, it’s the old gentleman himself!”

Marthe was evidently waiting to let in the travellers, for a few seconds later the door of the boudoir opened, and my Lord Barham walked in, point-de-vice as ever, in a scarlet riding coat under his cloak, buff small-clothes, and high top-boots.

===Chinese===
夫人说得不错。一脱下裙裳，他便把梅里奥特小姐的种种作派丢了个干净。凯特的步子是碎步轻移：罗宾的步子却干净利落、迅捷有力；凯特是慵懒倦怠的：罗宾从不如此；凯特会摆出种种娇媚的体态：罗宾的一举一动都警醒果决；凯特能捏出一副缠绵销魂的海妖嗓音：罗宾说话干脆爽利，正如凯特的眼神是脉脉含情的，他的眼神却锐利逼人。事实是，他是位造诣精深的演员，演一个角色，便从心到魂变成那个角色。洛斯托夫特夫人时常叹服他演技的完美，叹服他对每一处女儿家细枝末节的一丝不苟；只是直到此刻，看他把伪装连同附着其上的一切作派尽数卸去，她才疑心，自己从前怕是并没有真正领略透他。

她正想着这些，马蹄声传进了耳朵。又过一两分钟，车轮在门廊旁停了下来。

罗宾眯眼贴着窗玻璃往外望。「这回该是约翰到了。哎呀老天，夫人，是老先生他亲自来了！」

玛尔特显然一直候着给来客开门，因为没过几秒，闺房的门便开了，巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）走了进来，依旧浑身上下 point-de-vice（一丝不苟）：大氅底下是猩红色骑装外套，鹿皮色紧身短裤，足蹬高筒马靴。

===Original===
“Well, Robert!” said my lady.

My lord kissed her hand punctiliously, but without his usual display of rapture. A severe gaze was bent upon his son. “The whole of this affair,” announced my lord in an awful voice, “is deplorable in the extreme. It has been botched and bungled in a manner passing my comprehension.”

John, entering behind my lord, shut the door. “He’s been like this all the way down,” he told Robin. “We’d ha’ been here an hour since, but that he must needs stop to change his clothes,” he added.

“It is not my habit to drive about the country in ball dress,” said my lord crushingly.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，罗伯特（Robert）！」夫人道。

勋爵大人极尽礼数地吻了吻她的手，却没了往常那副欢欣淋漓的做派。一道严峻的目光直射向他儿子。「这整桩事，」勋爵大人以一种令人震慑的声调宣布道，「糟糕得无以复加。办得七零八落、磕磕绊绊，其程度超出了我的理解。」

约翰跟在勋爵大人身后进来，随手关上了门。「他这一路上都是这副样子，」他告诉罗宾。「要不然咱们一个钟头前就到了，偏他非要停下来换身衣裳。」他补上一句。

「穿着舞会的衣裳乘车在乡间跑来跑去，不是我的习惯。」勋爵大人一句话把人堵得死死的。

===Original===
It was quite evident that he was very much put out. Lady Lowestoft patted the couch invitingly. “But sit down, my dear Robert!” she coaxed.

My lord came out of his cloak. “Take it!” he said. John obeyed with a wry smile at Robin. My lord gave his ruffles a twitch, and bent to flick a speck of dust from his shining boots. He then walked to the fireplace, and entirely ignoring my lady’s invitation, stood with his back to it, and proceeded to deliver himself of a terrific denunciation. “Botched and bungled!” he repeated. He appeared to address no one in particular. “Are my schemes so incomplete they need adjustments? Do I leave aught to chance? Am I to be set aside, disregarded, overruled? In a word, am I to be disobeyed?”

His hearers felt that they were not expected to venture a reply. Robin sat down astride a chair, laid his arms along the back of it, propped his chin on them, and waited patiently. My lord’s eyes swept the room. “I am not!” he said, in a tone that made my lady jump guiltily. “At the start of this episode I made my plans. They were beautifully complete. I do myself less than justice: they were perfect! I issued my orders: a child might have comprehended them. Not so my son. Did I ordain that my Prudence should embroil herself in the affair? I did not. Did I inform my son that I desired him to escort Miss Grayson home when all was done? I did not. No one possessing but the smallest knowledge of me could have supposed it possible that I should meditate such a piece of folly! My children chose to set me at naught. They meddled in a plan of *my* making!” The penetrating eye flashed.

Robin sighed, and continued to watch his father; my lady blinked; John, standing still by the door, compressed his lips, and looked at my lord rather as an adult might look upon the tiresome tricks of a small child.

===Chinese===
显而易见，他气得不轻。洛斯托夫特夫人拍了拍长椅，招他落座。「快坐下吧，亲爱的罗伯特！」她哄劝道。

勋爵大人从大氅里脱出身来。「接着！」他说。约翰依言接了，朝罗宾做了个苦笑。勋爵大人扯了扯袖口的褶边，又弯下身，弹去亮靴上的一星尘土。随后他踱到壁炉前，全然不理会夫人的邀请，背对壁炉而立，开始发表一篇雷霆万钧的痛斥。「七零八落、磕磕绊绊！」他又说了一遍。他像不是在冲任何一个特定的人说话。「我的谋划难道残缺不全到需要旁人修订？我可曾把任何事情付与运气？我竟要被人撇在一边、受人漠视、遭人驳回么？一句话，我竟要被人违抗么？」

听训的几位都觉得，谁也别想冒险回嘴。罗宾跨坐在一把椅子上，双臂搭在椅背上，下巴支在臂上，耐心等着。勋爵大人的目光扫过全屋。「我不许！」他说，那声调吓得夫人心虚地一跳。「这桩事端初起之时，我便定下了我的谋划。它们完备得漂亮。这样说还亏待了我自己：它们是完美无缺的！我发布了号令：三岁孩童也能听懂。我儿子却不然。我可曾钦命我的普鲁登丝（Prudence）亲身卷进这件事？我没有。我可曾告知我儿子，说大事办毕之后，我要他护送格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson）回家？我没有。但凡对我有哪怕一星半点了解的人，都决计想不到我会盘算出这样一桩蠢事！我的儿女们偏要把我视若无物。他们插手的，可是*我*的谋划！」那双洞穿一切的眸子闪出光来。

罗宾叹了口气，继续望着他父亲；夫人眨着眼；约翰一动不动立在门边，抿紧了嘴唇，瞧着勋爵大人的神气，倒像个大人瞧着小孩子的淘气把戏。

===Original===
My lord’s accusing gaze rested on each one in turn. “I have a forbearance passing anything one could imagine,” he said amazingly. “Did I, when this came to my astonished, my incredulous ears, give way to my very righteous indignation? I did not. Some slight reproof I may have allowed to pass my lips. Enough, one would say to warn my children that in future they must obey the very letter of my law. The thing was done; the crass error had been perpetrated. To what avail my censure? I held my peace. I said only: ‘Do nothing without word from me. Await my instructions!’ When you came to this place﻿—a measure of which I never approved﻿—I said it. To John, my servant, I said more emphatically still: ‘If aught should befall my children apprise me instantly.’ By John no less than by my children have I been disregarded.”

“Ay, my lord, and I’ve been telling you for the past hour more that I was on my way to you when I met Sir Anthony. If you would but listen﻿—”

My lord flung up a hand. “You interrupt me at every turn! Allow me to speak!” The tone was not that of a request; John looked helplessly at Robin, who held up a finger. It was quite plain to Robin that his father was greatly annoyed to think that anyone but himself had had a hand in the management of the affair.

“I have said I was disregarded,” my lord continued. “It is very true! tragically true! Do you suppose that I had not foreseen the apprehension of my daughter? It is possible you could think I had not made my plans in preparation of this?” He paused a moment. Robin, who had thought precisely this, held his piece. My lord, satisfied that he was not going to venture to speak, swept on. “It was, from the first moment of deviation from my original schemes a contingency to be expected. I expected it. It happens. My daughter is arrested; my servant, not yet lost to all sense of what is due to me, sets off to apprise me of it. He meets Sir Anthony Fanshawe. He should never have done such a thing!”

===Chinese===
