She entertained on the lavish scale, her acquaintances were many, and she had beside quite a small host of admirers.

“You understand, these English consider me in the light of an original,” she exclaimed to Prudence. “I have an instant success, *parole d’honneur*!”

She was off without awaiting the reply, on to another subject. Conversationally she fluttered like a butterfly, here, there, and everywhere. She had much to say of the late executions: there were upflung hands of horror, and some pungent exclamations in the French tongue. She spoke of his Grace of Cumberland, not flatteringly; she had a quick ripple of laughter for his ugly nickname, and the instant after a brimming pair of eyes when she thought how he had earned it. Blood! England must needs reek of it! She gave a shudder. But there must be no more executions: that was decided: no nor risings either. All that was folly; folly the most outrageous. *Peste*, how came the Merriots in so forlorn a galère?﻿ ﻿…

They sat alone at the dinner-table; the lackeys had withdrawn, and even the little black page had been sent away. Prudence answered my lady, since Robin sat silent. “Oh, believe me, ma’am, we ask ourselves! The old gentleman had a maggot in his brain belike. A beau geste, I am persuaded; nothing else.”

===Chinese===
如今她安居在阿灵顿街（Arlington Street）的宅子里：有胖玛尔特（Marthe）照看她，有时兴规矩定下的一只猴子蹲在裙褶里，还有一个黑人小听差替她跑腿。她待客极尽奢靡，相识众多，此外还有小小一队爱慕者。

「您明白吗，这些英国人把我当成一件奇人异士看待，」她向普鲁登丝（Prudence）扬声道。「我一炮而红，*parole d’honneur*！」

她不等回答便又飞走了，扑向另一个话题。谈吐之间她像只蝴蝶，忽东忽西，四处翩跹。关于新近的那些处决，她有许多话可说：惊骇中双手高高扬起，嘴里迸出几句辛辣的法语感叹。她提起坎伯兰公爵殿下（his Grace of Cumberland），言语间毫无恭维；听到他那难听的绰号，她发出一串轻快的笑声，可一想到这绰号是怎么挣来的，转眼便双眼盈泪。血！英格兰非要血气熏天不可！她打了个寒噤。但决不能再有处决了：这是定了的：起义也不许再有。那一切全是愚蠢；愚不可及的愚蠢。*Peste*，梅里奥特家（Merriot）怎么会上了一条这般凄凉的苦役船？……

他们单独坐在餐桌旁；仆役们已经退下，连那个黑人小听差也被打发开了。罗宾（Robin）闷声不响，便由普鲁登丝回答夫人。「哦，请相信我，夫人，我们自己也在纳闷呢！老爷子脑子里怕是钻了蛆虫。依我看，那不过是为着摆一记漂亮的手势；仅此而已。」

===Original===
“But stupid, my child, stupid! There was never a hope. Moreover, we do very well with little fierce George. Bah, why plunge all in disorder for a pretty princeling?”

“He had the right.” Robin spoke sombrely.

“*Quant à ça*, I know nothing of the matter, my little one. You English, you chose for yourselves a foreigner. *Bien!* But you must not turn against him now. No, no, that is not reasonable.”

“By your leave, ma’am, not all chose him.”

===Chinese===
「可是蠢哪，我的孩子，蠢！从来就没有一丝指望。再说，我们同那凶巴巴的小乔治（little fierce George）过得好好的。呸，何必为一个俊俏的小王孙把一切都搅个天翻地覆？」

「他有他的正当权利。」罗宾阴沉地说。

「*Quant à ça*，这桩事我毫不知情，我的小宝贝。你们英国人，自己给自己挑了个外国人。*Bien*！可你们如今不该再倒戈相向。不，不，那可不讲道理。」

「恕我直言，夫人，当年并非人人都选了他。」

===Original===
She flashed a look at him. “Eh, so he had you under his spell, the bonnie prince? But you﻿—no, my cabbage, you are no Jacobite at heart. A spell, no more.”

“Oh, I am nothing at all, ma’am, rest you content. I meddle no more in the affairs of princes.”

“That is wise,” she approved. “This time you escape. Another time﻿—who knows?”

He laughed irresponsibly. “As to that, my lady, I don’t count myself safe as yet.”

===Chinese===
她飞快地瞟他一眼。「诶，这么说，那位俊俏王子（the bonnie prince）把你迷住过？可你——不，我的小卷心菜，你心里并不是詹姆士党人（Jacobite）。不过中了一道魔咒，仅此而已。」

「哦，我什么也不是，夫人，您只管放心。王子们的事，我再也不插手了。」

「这才是聪明。」她嘉许道。「这一回你脱了身。下一回——谁说得准？」

他没心没肺地笑了。「说到那个，夫人，我还没把自己算作安全之列呢。」

===Original===
His sister’s serenity was ruffled momentarily. She looked with some anxiety towards my lady, who bent towards her swiftly, and patted her hand.

“Ah, no more of that! *Au fond*, you do not like to see blood flow, you English. It is thought there has been blood enough: the tide turns. Lie close, and all blows over. I am certain of it﻿—*moi qui te parle!*”

Robin made a face at his sister. “The creature must needs play the mother to me, madam.”

“Madam, behold my little mentor!” Prudence retorted. “Give you my word I have my scoldings from him, and not the old gentleman. ’Tis a waspish tongue, egad.”

===Chinese===
他姐姐的平静一时间被搅起了涟漪。她带着几分忧虑朝夫人望去，夫人忙向她俯过身来，拍了拍她的手。

「啊，这话别再提了！*Au fond*，你们英国人骨子里不喜欢看血流成河。人人都觉得血已经流得够了：风向转了。蛰伏着别动，一切自会平息。这一点我有把握——*moi qui te parle*！」

罗宾朝姐姐做了个鬼脸。「这位夫人偏要给我当起母亲来，太太。」

「太太，您瞧瞧我这位小导师！」普鲁登丝回敬道。「我向您担保，吃训斥的是我——训我的是他，倒不是老爷子。那张嘴可真黄蜂似的毒，天哪。」

===Original===
Talk ran awhile then on the vagaries of Mr. Colney. My lady must needs speculate upon his whereabouts; his dutiful children could not permit themselves to indulge in the optimism of hazarding a guess. Sufficient for them that he had named London as a meeting-place: wherefor behold them here, in all obedience.

My lady professed alarm; Prudence cracked a nut. My lady was urgent to know the nature of Mr. Colney’s business in the late rebellion; her queries were met by a humorous quirk of the eyebrow, and a half shrug of the shoulder. *Eh bien* then, might he with safety show himself in town? Had he not, in effect, been conspicuous up there in the North?

It was Robin who said with a laugh:﻿—“Lud, ma’am, and did you ever know him when he was not conspicuous? It has been dark intrigue for him, here and there﻿—a go-between, as I take it. What does one know of him? Nothing! But I’d wager my last guinea he has his tracks well covered.”

My lady reflected on the likelihood of this, but it was evident that she continued to feel some trepidation at the thought of *ce cher* Robert coming to London, which was, in fact, the lion’s den.

===Chinese===
随后话题转到科尔尼先生（Mr. Colney）的古怪行径上，谈了好一阵。夫人免不得要揣测他如今身在何方；他那对孝顺儿女却不敢纵容自己，乐观到贸然瞎猜的地步。他既指定了伦敦作会合之地，他们于愿已足：瞧，他们便百依百顺地来了。

夫人连声叫忧；普鲁登丝嗑开一颗坚果。夫人急欲知道科尔尼先生在前番叛乱里干的究竟是哪一路营生；她的追问只换来一道戏谑扬起的眉毛和半个耸肩。*Eh bien*，那么，他现身京城可保无事么？他岂不是分明在北方出过十足的风头？

倒是罗宾笑着开了口：——「老天，夫人，您几时见过他不出风头？他干的向来是暗地里的勾当，东一趟西一趟——依我看，是个居中传话的角色。谁了解他些什么？什么也不了解！可我敢拿我最后一枚几尼打赌，他的行踪掩盖得滴水不漏。」

夫人思忖着这话有几分可信，但显然，一想到*ce cher*罗伯特（Robert）要来伦敦——那可是狮穴虎窟——她心头依旧忐忑难安。

===Original===
Prudence smiled. “My lady, he has very often informed us that ‘I contrive’ might well stand for his motto, and, faith, I believe him.”

“ ‘I contrive,’ ” mused my lady. “Yes, that is Robert. But it is the motto of the Tremaines.”

“The more like the old gentleman to appropriate it,” said Robin. “Who are the Tremaines?”

“Oh, one of your old families. They are Viscounts of Barham these many years, you must know. The last one died some few months since, and the new one is only some cousin, I think, of name Rensley.”

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝微微一笑。「夫人，他屡屡告知我们，『我运筹成了』尽可拿来作他的箴言，老实说，我是信他的。」

「『我运筹成了』，」夫人沉吟道。「是啊，这就是罗伯特。可那是特雷梅因家（Tremaine）的箴言。」

「这么着，倒更像老爷子的作为了——顺手借来就用。」罗宾说。「特雷梅因家是什么人家？」

「哦，是你们的老门第之一。要知道，他们家做巴勒姆（Barham）子爵已经有许多年了。上一位几个月前才去世，如今的新爵主，我想不过是他们家的一位堂亲，姓伦斯利（Rensley）。」

===Original===
“Then our poor papa can have his motto,” said Prudence.

She had a mind to learn something of Sir Anthony Fanshawe, and drew the trend of the talk that way. There was no word spoken of Miss Letty and her indiscretion: Sir Anthony had been chance-met on the road﻿—also one Mr. Markham.

My lady wrinkled her brow at the last name; it was plain she did not count Mr. Markham amongst her friends. More closely questioned, she said that he was a man of *mauvais ton*, a great gambler, and received at an astonishing number of houses, for no reason that she could perceive unless it were his friendship with my Lord Barham.

“There you have two people of no great breeding,” ran her peroration. “Have naught to do with either, my children. Both are counted dangerous, and both are rogues. Of that I am convinced.”

===Chinese===
「这么说，咱们可怜的爸爸就能名正言顺地用上这句箴言了。」普鲁登丝说。

她有心探听些安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）的底细，便把话头往那边引。莱蒂小姐（Miss Letty）和她的轻率行径，一个字也没有人提起：安东尼爵士是路上偶遇的——同行的还有一位马卡姆先生（Mr. Markham）。

听到最后那个名字，夫人皱起了眉头；明摆着，她没把马卡姆先生算在朋友之列。再往细里追问，她便说此人*mauvais ton*，是个天大的赌棍，出人意料地受着一长串人家的接待，个中缘由她实在看不出，除非是因为他同巴勒姆勋爵（my Lord Barham）的那份交情。

「这你就得了两个没什么教养的人，」她收尾的宏论如是。「这两个都别沾，我的孩子们。两个都被算作危险人物，两个都是无赖。这一点我深信不疑。」

===Original===
“And Sir Anthony?” said Robin, with a quizzical look at his sister. “Is that another rogue?”

My lady found this infinitely amusing. “The poor Sir Tony! To be sure, a very proper gentleman﻿—well born, rich, handsome﻿—but fie! of an impenetrability. Ah, you English!” She shook her head over the stolidity of the race.

“He displays already a most fatherly interest in my little sister, ma’am,” Robin said solemnly. “We are like to be undone by it.”

“Robin must have his jest, my lady.” Prudence was unruffled. “I believe I am not a novice in the art of simulation. I don’t fear Sir Anthony’s detection.”

===Chinese===
「那么安东尼爵士呢？」罗宾说着，朝姐姐递去一个促狭的眼色。「他是不是又一个无赖？」

夫人觉得这话有趣极了。「可怜的托尼爵士（Sir Tony）！诚然是位十分端正的绅士——出身好，有钱，相貌堂堂——可是呸！他这人全然让人捉摸不透。啊，你们这些英国人！」她为这个民族的木头脑袋直摇头。

「夫人，他对我的小妹妹已经流露出十足的慈父般的关怀了。」罗宾一本正经地说。「我们怕是要败在这上头了。」

「罗宾就爱说笑，夫人。」普鲁登丝不慌不忙。「我相信，在弄虚作假这门艺术里我不是新手。我不怕安东尼爵士看破。」

===Original===
“My dear, he does not see a yard before his own nose, that one,” my lady assured her. “Fear nothing from him. You will meet him at my rout tomorrow. All the world comes.”

There was no more talk then of Sir Anthony, but he came again into Prudence’s mind that night when she made ready to go to bed. She came out of her coat﻿—not without difficulty, for it was of excellent tailoring, and fitted tightly across her shoulders﻿—and stood for a while before the long mirror, seriously surveying herself. A fine straight figure she made: there could be no gainsaying it, but she found herself wondering what Sir Anthony, of the lazy speech and sleepy eyelids, would make of it. She doubted there might be too great a love of the respectable in the gentleman. She placed her hands on her slim hips, and looked, without seeing, into the grey eyes in the mirror. Sir Anthony refused to be banished from her mind.

Respectable! Ay, there was the sneering epithet of a vagabond for an honourable gentleman. It was tiresome of the man, but there was that in his face inspired one with trust, and a disinclination to simulate. One could not imagine the large gentleman descending to trickery and a masquerade. So much the worse for him, then, if he found himself ever in a dangerous corner. One might give the masquerade an ugly sounding name: call it Deceit; no good ring to that. Or call it the pitting of one’s wits against the world’s; that had a better smack.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，他呀，那个人，连自己鼻子前面一码远的东西都瞧不见。」夫人向她打包票。「别怕他什么。明天我的晚会上你就会见到他。全城的人都会来。」

这以后便不再谈起安东尼爵士了，可当晚普鲁登丝预备就寝时，他却又回到了她的心头。她从外套里脱出身来——颇费了些工夫，因为那外套做工一流，肩头收得紧紧的——在长镜前站了片刻，一本正经地打量自己。瞧那身段，挺拔匀称：这没得可说；可她不由得纳闷，那位说话懒洋洋、眼皮睡不醒的安东尼爵士见了会作何感想。她疑心这位绅士身上，对体面二字的癖好未免太深。她把双手按在纤细的胯上，望着镜中那双灰眼睛，视而不见。安东尼爵士赖在她心头，怎么也驱逐不走。

体面！可不是嘛，这是一个流荡子冲着一位正人君子冷笑出来的字眼。这人叫人不痛快，可他脸上偏有那么一股东西，叫人信得过，叫人不想弄虚作假。谁也无法想象那位大块头绅士会自贬身价，去使诈、去演化装戏。那么，万一他哪天落到凶险的角落里，可就更加糟糕了。这场化装戏尽可以给它安上一个难听的名目：叫它欺骗；这可没什么好听的。或者管它叫以一己之智斗天下人之智；这才有点儿滋味。

===Original===
The fine mouth showed a tendency to curl scornfully. One’s wit against the world was well enough; one’s wit against a single fellow creature, not so good. The one was after all a perilous losing game, with all to risk; the other savoured a little of the common imposter. Sir Anthony would be friendly; unpleasant to think that one could show but a false front.

She caught herself up on the thought, turned away from the mirror, and began to untie the lace at her throat. Egad, she was in danger of turning sentimental because a large gentleman looked on her with kindness. A sentimental country, this England: it awoke in one a desire for security.

The neckcloth was tossed on to the table, and a soft chuckle came. Ludicrous to think of security with Mr. Colney for sire. She reflected ruefully that her father was somewhat of a rogue; disreputable even. A gaming house in Frankfort, forsooth! She had a smile for that memory. Hand to mouth days, those, with herself in boy’s clothes, as now. The old gentleman had judged it wisest, and when one remembered some of those who came to the gaming house one had to admit he had reason. A dice box in one pocket, and a pistol in the other, though! Proper training for a girl just coming out of her teens! A mad life, egad, but there had been much to recommend it. One had learned something, after all. Sure, only to live with the old gentleman was an education: one owed him a deal, but if one desired to enter into a life of security his very existence must prove a bar.

===Chinese===
那张姣好的嘴角泛起一丝讥诮的弧度。以一己之智斗天下人之智，倒也罢了；以一己之智斗单独一个人，就不大好了。前者说到底是一场凶险的、注定要输的赌局，押上的是全部身家；后者呢，多少沾了点寻常骗子的味道。安东尼爵士会待人以友；可一想到自己只能给他看一副假面目，就叫人不痛快。

她猛地收住这个念头，从镜前转过身去，动手解开颈间的花边。天哪，只因一位大块头绅士和颜悦色地看她，她就有多愁善感起来的危险了。这英格兰真是个惹人伤感的国度：它唤醒了人心底对安稳的渴望。

领结被抛到桌上，一声轻笑随即溢出。想想看，摊上科尔尼先生这么位生身父亲，还谈什么安稳，实在可笑。她怅然自忖，父亲多少算个无赖；甚至声名狼藉。法兰克福（Frankfort）的一家赌场，好家伙！忆及此事她不禁莞尔。那些日子是过一天算一天的日子，她自己一身男孩装束，一如眼下。老爷子当年断定这样最稳妥，而只要想起赌场里进出的某些客人，谁也得承认他自有道理。只是——一只口袋里装骰子筒，另一只口袋里掖手枪！给一个刚跨出少女年纪的姑娘受的教养，可真够齐整！疯疯癫癫的日子，天哪，可也实在有它的好处。人到底长了不少见识。说真的，单是同老爷子一起过活，就是一堂教养课：欠他的着实不少；可谁若想踏入安稳的人生，他这个人的存在本身就是一道拦路的坎。

===Original===
She perceived in her thoughts a tendency to edge round to the contemplation of Sir Anthony, and judged it time to have done. Dimly she could see difficulties ahead; characteristically she dismissed them with a fatalistic gesture. Time enough to ponder them when they presented themselves.

She pulled the heavy curtains back from the bed, and of habit slipped a little gold-mounted pistol beneath her pillow. She climbed into the big four-poster, and very soon lay lightly asleep. Not the dark future, nor Sir Anthony would be permitted to disturb Prudence’s repose, though fleetingly both might enter into her dreams. After all, one could not be mistress of one’s thoughts in sleep.

===Chinese===
她察觉自己的思绪又在一圈圈蹭回安东尼爵士身上去，便断定该到此为止了。前路隐隐可见重重难处；她照着性子，用一个听天由命的手势把它们挥开。等它们当真找上门来，再细细思量不迟。

她拉开床边厚重的帐幔，照老习惯把一支小小的镶金手枪塞到枕头底下。她爬上那张高大的四柱床，不多时便恬然睡去，呼吸轻浅。任是晦暗的前程，还是安东尼爵士，都不容许来搅扰普鲁登丝的清梦，虽说二者都可能倏忽掠进她的梦里。说到底，人在睡乡里，做不得自己念头的女主人。

## IX Mohocks Abroad / IX 摩霍克匪徒横行

===Original===
It seemed Robin was well satisfied with the night’s work; his sister visited him as he lay sipping his chocolate in bed next morning, and cocked a quizzical eyebrow. Robin smiled sweetly, but volunteered no confidences. He went to call upon Miss Grayson later in the day, but although Letty was delighted to see her dear Kate, she was a little abstracted, and had but a few words to say of the ball. Yes, it had been very amusing; she wished Kate had been there. Yes, she had danced with a number of gentlemen. It was a pity Mr. Merriot had chosen to wear crimson.
Robin went off with a smile playing about the corners of his mouth. He was constrained to drive out visiting with my Lady Lowestoft, and went, smothering a yawn.
Prudence﻿—she was beginning, she thought, to feel more of a man than a woman﻿—strolled round to White’s, and found Mr. Walpole there reading the Spectator. Mr. Walpole was graciously pleased to exchange a few words; he had a small flow of tittle-tattle at his tongue’s tip, and announced his intention of retiring to Strawberry Hill. He protested that these late nights in town were harmful to his constitution. He raised supercilious brows at the sight of Mr. Markham entering the room, and retired once more behind the Spectator.
Mr. Markham bowed to Prudence, and went to write letters at a table against the wall. Prudence stood talking to one Mr. Dendy, and was presently tapped on the shoulder.
===Chinese===
看样子，罗宾（Robin）对昨夜的作为颇为满意；第二天早晨，他正倚在床上啜着巧克力，姐姐到他房里来看他，挑起一边揶揄的眉毛。罗宾甜甜一笑，却一个字也不肯主动招认。当天晚些时候，他去拜会格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson），莱蒂（Letty）见到她亲爱的凯特（Kate）虽然欢喜，却有些心不在焉，谈起舞会也只有寥寥数语。是的，舞会很有意思；她真希望凯特也在场。是的，她同好几位绅士跳了舞。只可惜，梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）偏偏挑了那一身绯红。
罗宾嘴角噙着一丝笑意走开了。他还不得不奉陪洛斯托夫特夫人（My Lady Lowestoft）坐车出门拜客，于是去了，一路上掩着一个个哈欠。
普鲁登丝（Prudence）——她心想，自己已渐渐觉得，与其说像个女人，倒不如说更像个月人了——信步踱到怀特俱乐部（White's），看见沃波尔先生（Mr. Walpole）正在那里读《旁观者》（the Spectator）。沃波尔先生惠然屈尊，肯同他交谈几句；他舌尖上淌着一小股流言碎语，又宣布了自己要退隐到草莓山（Strawberry Hill）去的打算。他连声抱怨，说城里这种熬到深更半夜的日子，对他的身子骨大有妨害。一眼瞥见马卡姆先生（Mr. Markham）走进屋来，他扬起傲慢的眉毛，重新躲回《旁观者》后面去了。
马卡姆先生向普鲁登丝一躬，走到墙边一张桌子跟前去写信。普鲁登丝站着同一位登迪（Dendy）先生说话，不多时，肩膀上被人轻轻拍了一下。

===Original===
“Here’s your man, Devereux!” said the voice of Sir Francis Jollyot.
Mr. Devereux came up with his mincing gait. “ ’Pon my soul, so ’tis!” He swept a leg, flourishing a scented handkerchief. “I am but this instant come from Arlington Street, where they told me you had walked out. I have to beg the honour of your company at a small gathering I have a mind to hold tonight. A little game of Chance, you understand.” He held up a very white finger. “Now don’t, I implore you, *don’t* say me nay, Mr. Merriot!”
Prudence smothered a sigh. “Why, sir, I confess I had purposed to spend this evening with my sister,” she began.
“Oh, come now, Merriot!” expostulated Jollyot jovially, “you must not deny me my revenge!”
===Chinese===
「你要找的人在这儿呢，德弗罗！」弗朗西斯·乔利奥特爵士（Sir Francis Jollyot）的声音说道。
德弗罗先生（Mr. Devereux）迈着扭扭捏捏的碎步走上前来。「我以灵魂起誓，可不是嘛！」他扫腿行了一礼，挥着一方熏了香的手帕。「我方才从阿灵顿街（Arlington Street）过来，他们告诉我，您出门散步去了。今晚我打算办一个小小的聚会，务求赏光，请您作陪。玩一点碰运气的牌戏，您明白的。」他竖起一根雪白的手指。「您可别，我求您了，*千万*别对我说个不字，梅里奥特先生！」
普鲁登丝压下一声叹息。「哎呀，先生，不瞒您说，我本打算今晚陪我妹妹的。」她开口道。
「哦，别这样嘛，梅里奥特！」乔利奥特乐呵呵地劝道，「你可不许不让我报这一箭之仇！」

===Original===
“To be sure, I live in a most devilish outlandish spot,” said Mr. Devereux mournfully. “But you may take a chair: you know you may take a chair. ’Pon rep, sir, I do positively believe an evening spent at home is vastly more fatiguing than a quiet card-party. ’Pon my honour, sir!”
There was nothing for it but to show polite acceptance.
Mr. Devereux was wreathed in smiles. “To tell you the truth, sir, I’ve had a devilish ticklish task to find anyone free tonight,” he said naively. “Fanshawe’s engaged; so’s Barham. Molyneux goes out of town; Selwyn’s in bed with a trifling fever.”
Over against the wall Mr. Markham stopped writing, and raised his head.
===Chinese===
「说实在的，我住的地方偏僻得要死。」德弗罗先生哀声道。「可您可以坐轿椅来呀：您知道，您尽可以坐轿椅来。我拿名声担保，先生，我千真万确地相信，在家消磨一个晚上，比一场安安静静的小牌局累人得多。以我的名誉起誓，先生！」
无可奈何，只得客客气气地应承下来。
德弗罗先生满脸堆笑。「不瞒您说，先生，今晚要寻一个得空的人，真是个要命的难差事。」他天真地说。「范肖（Fanshawe）有约在先；巴勒姆（Barham）也是。莫利纽克斯（Molyneux）出城去了；塞尔温（Selwyn）害着一点小热病，正卧床呢。」
墙那边，马卡姆先生停了笔，抬起头来。

===Original===
“I’m overwhelmed by the honour done me,” said Prudence ironically.
The irony went unperceived. “Not at all, my dear Merriot. Oh, not in the least! I shall see you then, at five? You can take a chair, you know, and be there in a trice.”
“As you say, sir. But I think I have not the pleasure of knowing your address.”
Mr. Devereux simpered elegantly. “Oh, a devilish inconvenient hole, sir! I’ve apartments in Charing Cross.”
===Chinese===
「承蒙如此抬举，真叫我消受不起。」普鲁登丝讥诮地说。
这讥诮没人听出来。「哪里的话，我亲爱的梅里奥特。哦，一点儿也不是！那么五点见，可好？您知道，坐轿椅去，眨眼工夫就到了。」
「就依您说的，先生。只是我想，我还没有这份荣幸，知道您的府上地址。」
德弗罗先生斯斯文文地嫣然假笑。「哦，一个要命的不方便的角落，先生！我在查令十字（Charing Cross）有几间屋子。」

===Original===
“Ah yes, I remember the street now,” Prudence said. “At five o’clock, sir.”
Mr. Devereux beamed upon her, and airily waved one languid hand. “Au revoir, then, my dear Merriot. You will take a chair, and suffer not the least inconvenience in the world. An evening at home﻿—oh no, ecod!” He drifted away on Jollyot’s arm, and the rest of his sentence only reached Prudence as a confused murmur.
Mr. Markham went on with his writing.
Prudence walked slowly back to Arlington Street, and remarked to Robin, on his return, that she was in danger of wearing herself away to skin and bone.
===Chinese===
「啊对了，我如今记起那条街了。」普鲁登丝说。「五点钟，先生。」
德弗罗先生朝她灿然一笑，一只绵软的手轻飘飘一挥。「那么 Au revoir，我亲爱的梅里奥特。您坐轿椅来，绝不会受半点罪。在家待一个晚上——哦不，乖乖！」他挽着乔利奥特的胳膊飘然而去，那句话的后半截传到普鲁登丝耳中，只剩一片含混的咕哝。
马卡姆先生继续写他的信。
普鲁登丝慢慢走回阿灵顿街，等罗宾回来，便对他说，自己眼看就要把自个儿熬得只剩皮包骨头了。

===Original===
Robin was bored. “Heigh-ho, would I were in your shoes! All this female society gives me *mal-à-la-tête*.”
“Give you my word these card-parties and drinking bouts will be the death of me.”
Robin swung an impatient foot. “Does it occur to you, my dear, that events have not transpired precisely as they were planned?” he inquired with a rueful look.
“It has occurred to me many times. We meant to lie close.”
===Chinese===
罗宾腻烦透了。「唉——嗨，我巴不得处在你的位置上！这成天成夜的女人堆，看得我 *mal-à-la-tête*（头昏脑涨）。」
「我向你担保，这些牌局酒局总有一天要了我的命。」
罗宾不耐烦地晃着一只脚。「亲爱的，你可曾想到，事情的走势跟咱们原先盘算的并不完全一样？」他问道，神情颇有些懊恼。
「想到过不知多少回了。咱们原本打算深藏浅出、避人耳目的。」

===Original===
“Oh!” My Lady Lowestoft was arranging flowers in a big bowl. “But the *bon papa* planned it thus, my children. I was told to present you to the world.”
“Egad, we owe it to the old gentleman, do we?” said Robin. “I might have known. But why?”
“*Seulement*, I think he judged it wisest. You escape remark this way. That is true, no?”
“I suppose so. But the impropriety of Prue’s conduct﻿—oh lud, ma’am!”
===Chinese===
「哦！」洛斯托夫特夫人正在往一只大碗里插花。「可这本来就是 *bon papa* 安排下的呀，我的孩子们。我奉了命，要把你们引见给世人。」
「哎呀，原来咱们是奉老先生之命，是么？」罗宾说。「我早该料到的。可这是为什么？」
「*Seulement*（只不过），我想他是判定这样最稳妥。这样一来，你们反倒躲开了众人的注目。这话说得对，不是么？」
「我也这么想。可是普鲁的行止也太不成体统了——哎呀老天，夫人！」

===Original===
“Consider only the impropriety of your own, my child!” chuckled my lady.
“I do, ma’am, often. But as regarding this charming réunion tonight, Mistress Prue, you’ll be pleased to take a chair, and eschew the Burgundy.”
“Behold the little mentor!” Prudence bowed to him. “Rest you content, my Kate.”
The evening was like a dozen other such evenings. There was dinner, and some ribald talk; cards, with the decanter passing from hand to hand, and the candles burning lower and lower in their sockets. Prudence made her excuses soon after midnight. Her host rolled a blear eye towards her, and protested thickly. Prudence was firm, however, and won her way. A sleepy lackey opened the front door for her, and she stepped out into the cool night air.
===Chinese===
「还是只想想你自己的行止成不成体统吧，我的孩子！」夫人咯咯轻笑道。
「我想的，夫人，常常想。不过，今晚这场迷人的 réunion（聚会）嘛，普鲁大小姐，有劳您大驾坐轿椅去，还有，勃艮第酒也请敬而远之。」
「瞧这位小导师！」普鲁登丝朝他一躬。「你就放宽心吧，我的凯特。」
这个晚上同十来个别个的晚上并无二致。先是晚餐，还有几段子下流的笑谈；再是牌局，细颈酒瓶一手传过一手，插在烛座里的蜡烛越烧越短。午夜刚过，普鲁登丝便起身告罪。主人朝她翻起一只惺忪的醉眼，口齿含混地挽留。普鲁登丝却不为所动，终于如愿脱了身。一个睡意惺忪的听差替她打开大门，她一脚踏进清凉的夜气里。

===Original===
The street was deserted, but she knew a chair might be found at Charing Cross, a few score yards away. She swung her cloak over her arm, and walked in that direction, glad of this breath of clean air after the stuffiness of the cardroom.
It may have been that never quite dormant watchfulness in her that warned her of danger. No more than fifty yards up the street she felt it in the air, and checked her pace slightly. There was a shadow crouched in an embrasure in the wall a few steps further on﻿—a shadow that had something of the form of a man. She slid a hand to her sword hilt, loosening the blade in the scabbard. She must walk on: no use turning back now. A little pale, but steady-eyed as ever, she went forward, her fingers closed about the sword hilt.
The shadow moved, and behold! there were two other shadows springing up before her. There was a flash of steel as she wrenched the sword free from the scabbard, and for a moment the shadowy figures held back. The moment’s hesitation was enough to allow her to get her back against the wall, and to take a sure grip on the cloak over her left arm. Then there was a hoarse murmur, and the three rushed in on her with cudgels upraised.
Her rapier swept a circle before her; the foremost man jumped back with a curse, but the fellow to the right sprang in to aim a vicious blow at Prudence’s head. The rapier shot out, and the point struck home. Came a gasp, and a check: the cloak, unerringly thrown, descended smotheringly over the wounded man’s head, and there was at once a tangle of cloth and hot oaths.
===Chinese===
街上空无一人，不过她知道，几十码外的查令十字兴许雇得到轿椅。她把斗篷一甩搭上手臂，朝那个方向走去；在牌室里憋闷了半宿，能呼吸上这口干净的空气，她只觉痛快。
也许是她体内那份从不曾全然沉睡的警觉，向她报出了危险。沿街走出不过五十码，她便从空气里觉察出不对，脚步微微一缓。再往前几步，墙上凹进去的一处壁龛里蹲着一团黑影——一团隐约带些人形的黑影。她一只手滑向剑柄，松了松鞘中的剑。她必须走下去：此刻回头已然无用。她脸色微微发白，眼神却一如既往地镇定，一步一步向前走去，五指扣紧了剑柄。
那团黑影动了，看哪！又有两团黑影在她面前蹿了起来。她猛地把剑拽出剑鞘，一道寒光闪过，那几条影影绰绰的人影迟疑了一瞬。这一瞬的迟疑，已足够让她背抵住墙，把搭在左臂上的斗篷攥得牢牢的。接着一声嘶哑的低语，三条人影高举短棍，一齐朝她扑了上来。
她的剑在身前扫出一个圆弧；最前面那人骂了一声跳开去，右边那条汉子却欺身直进，抡起短棍，照着普鲁登丝的头顶狠狠便砸。剑尖疾刺而出，扎了个正着。一声抽气，一个踉跄：那件斗篷掷得不偏不倚，严严实实罩落下来，蒙住了伤者的头，登时人裹在布里，咒骂得热火朝天。

===Original===
Prudence made lightning use of this momentary diminution in the number of her assailants, parried a blow aimed at her sword arm, sprang sideways a little, and lunged forward the length of her arm. There was a groan, and the sword came away red, while the cudgel fell clattering to earth.
She was breathless and panting; this could not last. Even now the third man had got himself free of the cloak, and was creeping on her with it held in his hand. She guessed he meant to catch at her blade through it, and her heart sank. She thrust shrewdly at the man before her, and staggered under a blow from a cudgel on her left. She was nearly spent, and she knew that a few moments more must end it.
Then, from a little way down the street came a shout, and the sound of a man running. “Hold them, lad, I’m with you!” cried the newcomer, and Prudence recognised the voice of Mr. Belfort.
He fell upon her assailants from the rear, and there was swift and bloody work done. With a howl the man Prudence had first wounded went running off down the street, one hand clipped to his shoulder. His flight was a signal for the other two to follow suit. In another minute the street was empty, save for Prudence and the Honourable Charles.
===Chinese===
普鲁登丝抓住围攻的人数转眼少了一人的时机，出手迅如闪电，格开朝她持剑臂扫来的一棍，侧身一让，手臂送剑直刺出去。一声闷哼，剑抽回来已然见红，那根短棍哐啷啷落了地。
她喘息急促，气都接不上来；这样撑不了多久。就在这时，第三条汉子已经从斗篷里挣脱出来，手里攥着它，正朝她一步一步逼近。她猜到他是想拿它裹缠她的剑刃，心便往下一沉。她朝面前那人狠狠一刺，左边却又挨了一棍，打得她一个趔趄。她的力气已近乎耗尽，心里明白，再撑片刻，就该有个了结了。
就在这时，街头不远处传来一声呐喊，还伴着一个人奔跑的脚步声。「顶住，小子，我来帮你！」新来的人喊道，普鲁登丝听出那是贝尔福特先生（Mr. Belfort）的嗓音。
他从背后扑向那几个袭击她的人，一场又快又见血的厮杀就此展开。普鲁登丝最先刺伤的那人嗥叫一声，一只手死死按住肩膀，顺着街逃了下去。他这一跑便是号令，那两个也跟着撒腿就溜。转眼之间，街上已空无一人，只剩普鲁登丝和尊敬的查尔斯（the Honourable Charles）两个。

===Original===
Mr. Belfort leaned panting on his sword, and laughed hugely. “Gad, see ’em run!” he said. “Hey, are you hurt, lad?”
Prudence was leaning against the wall, dizzy and shaken. The shoulder which had sustained the blow from the cudgel ached sickeningly. With an effort she stood upright. “Naught. A blow on the shoulder, no more.” She swayed, but mastered the threatened faintness, and bent to pick up her cloak. Her hand shook slightly as she wiped her sword in its folds, but she managed to smile. “I have﻿—to thank you﻿—for your prompt assistance,” she said, trying to get her breath. “I rather thought I was sped.”
“Ay, three to one, blister them,” nodded Mr. Belfort. “But white-livered curs, ’pon my soul. Not an ounce of fight in ’em. Here, take my arm.”
Prudence leaned gratefully on it. “Just a momentary breathlessness,” she said. “I am well enough now.”
===Chinese===
贝尔福特先生拄着剑喘气，放声大笑。「天哪，瞧他们跑的！」他说。「嘿，你伤着没有，小子？」
普鲁登丝倚着墙，头晕目眩，浑身发颤。挨了短棍的那边肩膀一阵阵疼得发恶心。她费了一把劲才站直。「没事。肩膀上挨了一下，仅此而已。」她晃了一晃，却压住了那一阵涌上来的晕眩，俯身拾起斗篷。她拿斗篷的褶子擦拭剑身时，手微微发抖，却还是挤出了笑容。「我——得谢谢你——援手来得这样及时。」她说，一面竭力喘匀了气。「方才我还当，自己这条命真要交代了。」
「是啊，三个打一个，这帮遭瘟的。」贝尔福特先生点头道。「不过是些胆小如鼠的孬种，我拿灵魂起誓。他们身上找不出一两的斗志。来，挽着我的胳膊。」
普鲁登丝感激地挽了上去。「不过一时喘不上气罢了。」她说。「我如今已经好多了。」

===Original===
“Gad, it must have been a nasty blow!” said Mr. Belfort. “You are shaken to bits, man. Come home with me; my lodging is nearer than yours.”
“No, no, I thank you!” Prudence said earnestly. “The blow﻿—struck an old wound. I hardly heed it now.”
“Tare an’ ’ouns, but that’s bad!” cried Mr. Belfort. “Really, my dear fellow, you must come to my place and let me look to it.”
“On my honour, sir, it’s less than naught. You may see for yourself I am quite recovered now. I shall not trespass on your hospitality at this hour of night.”
===Chinese===
「天哪，那一棍定是结结实实！」贝尔福特先生说。「你整个人都散架啦，老兄。跟我回家去吧；我的住处比你的近。」
「不，不，多谢你了！」普鲁登丝恳切地说。「那一下——正打在一处旧伤上。这会儿我已经不大觉得了。」
「天杀的，这可糟了！」贝尔福特先生叫道。「说真的，亲爱的伙计，你务必到我那儿去，让我瞧瞧它。」
「以我的名誉担保，先生，这连小事一桩都算不上。您自己也看得出，我此刻已完全复原了。这么深更半夜的，我不便再叨扰您的款待了。」

===Original===
He protested that the night was young yet, but not to all his entreaties would Prudence yield. They walked on together towards Charing Cross, the Honourable Charles still adjuring Prudence at intervals to go home with him. “By gad, sir, these Mohocks become a positive scandal!” he exclaimed. “A gentleman mayn’t walk abroad, damme, without being set upon these days!”
“Mohocks?” Prudence said. “You think they were Mohocks, then?”
“Why, what else? The town’s teeming with ’em. I was set on myself t’other day. Stretched one fellow flat!”
Prudence thought of the words she had caught as she had come up to the embrasure. A rough voice had growled: “This is our man, boys.” She said nothing of this, however, to Mr. Belfort, but assented that without doubt the men had been Mohocks, intent on robbery.
===Chinese===
他分辩说夜还早着呢，可任凭他苦劝再三，普鲁登丝就是不肯松口。两人一道朝查令十字走去，尊敬的查尔斯一路上还是隔三差五地央告普鲁登丝随他回家。「老天作证，先生，这帮摩霍克匪徒（Mohocks）简直成了骇人的祸害！」他高声道。「如今这世道，一个绅士连上街走一走都不成，妈的，动不动就叫人围上一顿好打！」
「摩霍克匪徒？」普鲁登丝说。「那么你认为，他们当真是摩霍克匪徒？」
「不然还能是什么？城里遍地都是这号人。前几天我自己就叫他们围过一回。还撂平了一个家伙呢！」
普鲁登丝想起自己走近那处壁龛时捕到的一句话。一个粗嘎的嗓音当时低吼道：「就是这小子，弟兄们。」不过这话她对贝尔福特先生只字未提，只顺着话头附和，说那几个人无疑就是摩霍克匪徒，图的是劫财。

===Original===
“A good thing ’twas I left Devereux’s rooms directly after you,” said Mr. Belfort. “But that Burgundy, y’know﻿—demned poor stuff, my boy! There was no staying longer. How a man can get drunk on it beats me. Look at me now! Sober as a judge, Peter! Yet there’s poor Devereux almost under the table already.”
They parted company at Charing Cross, where Mr. Belfort saw Prudence solicitously into a chair. She was borne off west to Arlington Street, and set down safely outside my lady’s house.
A light burned still in Robin’s room. Sure, the child would never go to bed until she was come home. She went softly in, and found Robin reading by the light of three candles.
Robin looked up. “My felicitations. You escaped betimes.” His eyes narrowed, and he got up. “Oh? What’s toward, child?” he said sharply, and came across to Prudence’s side.
===Chinese===
「幸亏我在你后头紧接着就离开了德弗罗的寓所。」贝尔福特先生说。「可那勃艮第酒，你是知道的——该死的劣货，我的小子！实在没法多待。一个人怎么能喝那玩意儿喝到醉，我可弄不明白。瞧瞧我！清醒得像个法官，彼得（Peter）！可可怜的德弗罗，已经快钻到桌子底下去了。」
两人在查令十字分了手，贝尔福特先生千关切万叮嘱，看着普鲁登丝坐进轿椅。她被抬着一路往西，到阿灵顿街，在夫人的宅子门外平安落了轿。
罗宾房里还亮着灯。可不是，这孩子不见她回家，是决计不肯上床睡觉的。她轻手轻脚走进去，见罗宾就着三支蜡烛的光亮在读书。
罗宾抬起头来。「恭喜恭喜。你脱身得早。」他眯起眼睛，站起身来。「哦？出什么事了，孩子？」他急促地说，快步走到普鲁登丝身旁。

===Original===
She laughed. “What, do I look a corpse? I was near enough to it. But there are no bones broken, I believe.”
The beautifully curved lips straightened to a thin line; Prudence saw her brother’s eyes keen and anxious. “Be a little plain with me, child. You’ve sustained some hurt?”
“No more than a bruise, I think, but oh, Robin, it hurt!” Again she laughed, but there was a quiver in her voice. “Help me to come out of this coat; ’tis on my left shoulder.”
The shoulder was swiftly bared and an ugly bruise disclosed. There came a soft curse from Robin. “Who did it?”
===Chinese===
她笑了。「怎么，我瞧着像具尸首么？方才离那也差不了多少了。不过我想，骨头一根没断。」
那两片弧线姣好的嘴唇绷成了一条细线；普鲁登丝看见她弟弟的眼神又锐利又焦灼。「跟我明说吧，孩子。你受了伤，对不对？」
「我想不过是一处瘀伤，可是唉，罗宾，疼得厉害！」她又笑了，嗓音里却带着一丝发颤。「帮我脱下这件外衣；伤在左肩上。」
肩头很快袒露出来，露出一片吓人的瘀青。罗宾低低咒了一声。「谁干的？」

===Original===
“Now, how should I know? Charles spoke of the Mohocks.”
Robin was searching on his dressing table for ointment, and came back to her with the pot in his hand. As he smeared the stuff lightly over the bruise, he said remorsefully: “ ’Tis I who was at fault. I should have seen to it you had my lady’s chaise out.”
“Oh, no harm done, as it chances. But there were three of them and I was all but sped. Then Charles came running up, and there was an end of it.” She slipped her shirt up again over her shoulder. “Thanks, child. I would you had seen my swordplay. I am sure it did you credit.” She paused and looked at the guttering candles. Her tone changed, and became serious. “I have a notion they were creatures of Markham’s set on to beat me.”
“Markham’s?” Robin set down the ointment.
===Chinese===
「嗐，我哪儿知道？查尔斯说是摩霍克匪徒。」
罗宾在梳妆台上翻找药膏，手里拿着药膏罐回到她身边。他往瘀青上薄薄地抹着药膏，懊悔地说：「是我的不是。我早该打点好，让你把夫人的马车用上。」
「哦，幸好没出什么大事。可他们有三个，我险些就要交代了。这时查尔斯跑着赶来，事情就算了结了。」她把衬衫重新拉上肩头。「多谢你，孩子。真希望你看见了我的剑法。我敢说，它给你增了光。」她顿了顿，望着那几支快燃尽的蜡烛。她的语气变了，郑重起来。「我有个感觉，他们是马卡姆豢养的人，受了指使来打我一顿的。」
「马卡姆的人？」罗宾放下了药膏罐。

===Original===
“I know of no one else with a grudge against me. They were not common Mohocks.” She told him what she had heard.
He strode to the window and back again, frowning. “I think this is where we make our bow,” he said at last.
“Devil a bit!” was the cheerful response. “For the future I shall remember to take a chaise; that’s all there is to it.”
“I had rather see you safe in France.”
===Chinese===
「我想不出还有谁跟我结着仇。他们不是寻常的摩霍克匪徒。」她把自己听到的那句话讲给他听。
他大步走到窗前，又走回来，眉头紧锁。「我看，咱们到了该鞠躬下台的时候了。」他终于开口道。
「才不呢！」回答得干脆而欢快。「往后我记得乘马车出门就是了；就这么回事。」
「我宁可看你平平安安待在法国。」

===Original===
“I won’t go.”
He raised an eyebrow. “Oh, do you turn stubborn?”
“As a mule. We go down to Richmond with my lady tomorrow, and the Markham may think that I’ve gone into retirement on account of my mauling. He should be satisfied. I await the old gentleman, for I’ve a curiosity to see what his game is.” She got up, and stretched her long limbs, wincing at the pain of her bruised shoulder. “Get you to bed, Robin.” She went out, yawning.
They were gone on the morrow down to my lady’s house at Richmond. My lady was loud in her exclamations of horror at what had befallen Prudence, but Prudence could chuckle now that all was over, the while Robin sat in frowning silence. His petticoats began to irk him.
===Chinese===
「我不去。」
他扬起一边眉毛。「哦，你这是犯起犟起来了？」
「犟得像头骡子。明天我们随夫人去里士满（Richmond），那位马卡姆没准会以为，我挨了这顿打，吓得躲起来隐退了。他该称心如意了。我等着老先生，因为我倒好奇，他葫芦里卖的是什么药。」她站起身来，舒展修长的四肢，牵动肩上的瘀伤，疼得一缩。「上床睡去吧，罗宾。」她打着哈欠出去了。
第二天，他们便下到夫人在里士满的宅子去了。夫人大呼小叫，为普鲁登丝的遭遇连声惊骇；好在一切都已过去，普鲁登丝这会儿也能笑出声来了，罗宾却坐在一旁，皱着眉一言不发。他那一身裙子，开始叫他腻烦了。

===Original===
Mr. Markham heard of the affair at White’s, from the lips of Mr. Belfort. He professed himself all concern, but his friend Lord Barham, drawing him aside, said with a snigger: “So that’s a score settled, eh, my buck?”
“It’s not,” said Mr. Markham curtly, and scowled.
“Gad, I’d give something to know what you have against the young sprig!” said his lordship. “It’s a conceited puppy, ecod! I’ve a mind to give it a trouncing myself.”
Mr. Markham saw Sir Anthony Fanshawe, idly twirling his quizzing-glass, and rather testily requested his noble friend to guard his tongue. Sir Anthony continued blandly to survey the pair. Mr. Markham strode off, rather red about the gills.
===Chinese===
马卡姆先生在怀特俱乐部从贝尔福特先生嘴里听说了这桩事。他满口关切，可他的朋友巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）把他拉到一旁，嗤笑着道：「这么说，一笔账算清了，嗯，好小子？」
「并非如此。」马卡姆先生简慢地说，随即沉下脸来。
「天哪，我情愿出点代价，弄明白你到底跟这棵小嫩苗儿置的什么气！」勋爵大人说。「真是只自负的狗崽子，乖乖！我都想亲手揍它一顿。」
马卡姆先生一眼瞧见安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）正闲闲地捻着他的单片眼镜，便相当没好气地请他那位高贵的朋友把舌头管住。安东尼爵士依旧一团和气，继续打量着这一对。马卡姆先生大步走开了，腮帮子红到了耳根。

===Original===
Sir Anthony turned to Mr. Belfort, standing in a circle of his acquaintances. “Well, Charles, have you been fighting with the devil’s emissaries?” he said genially. “What’s this I hear of Mohocks?”
“Three of them, right in the middle of town, if you please!” said Mr. Belfort. “Thunder an’ turf, but it’s a crying disgrace! I’m saying to Proudie here that measures ought to be taken.”
Sir Anthony took out his snuffbox, and shook back the ruffles from his hand. “Oh, were you attacked?” he inquired.
“Not I. ’Twas young Merriot they set upon, as he came off from Devereux’s last night.”
===Chinese===
安东尼爵士转向正被一圈熟人围在当中的贝尔福特先生。「嘿，查尔斯，你跟魔鬼的使者们干了一仗？」他和和气气地说。「我听人说起的这桩摩霍克匪徒，是怎么回事？」
「三个家伙，就在城正中心，你倒瞧瞧！」贝尔福特先生说。「老天爷在上，这真是奇耻大辱！我正跟这边的普劳迪（Proudie）说，该拿出章程来治一治了。」
安东尼爵士掏出鼻烟盒，把手上的花边褶袖抖开。「哦，遇袭的是你么？」他问道。
「不是我。他们围的是年轻的梅里奥特，昨晚他从德弗罗家出来的时候。」

===Original===
The strong hand paused for a moment in the act of unfobbing the snuffbox. The sleepy eyes did not lift. “Indeed?” said Sir Anthony, and awaited more.
“Three to one, the ruffians, and lucky I chanced along, for the lad’s not over strong in the sword arm, I take it. Game enough, but he was soon blown.”
“He was, was he?” Sir Anthony took snuff in a leisurely fashion. “And﻿—er﻿—was he hurt?”
“A blow on the shoulder. It seemed to knock him pretty well endways. But he said something of an old wound there, which would account for it,” said Belfort, feeling that some excuse was needed.
===Chinese===
那只强壮的手正要往表袋外掏鼻烟盒，动作停顿了一瞬。那双惺忪的眼睛并没有抬起来。「是么？」安东尼爵士说，等着下文。
「三个打一个，那帮恶棍；幸亏我碰巧路过，要依我看，这小子使剑的那条胳膊算不得多强壮。胆气倒是足的，可没多久就喘不上气了。」
「喘了，是么？」安东尼爵士慢条斯理地捻起一撮鼻烟。「那么——呃——他伤了没有？」
「肩膀上挨了一棍。看那样子，把他打得七荤八素。不过他说那地方有处旧伤，这就说得通了。」贝尔福特说，觉得总得替他找些开脱。

===Original===
“Ah, an old wound?” Sir Anthony was politely interested. “Of course. That would, as you say, account for it.”
“There’s naught to be said against the lad’s courage,” Belfort assured him. “Game as a fighting cock, pledge you my word. I was all for taking him off to my lodgings to attend to his shoulder, but no, he’d none of it!”
“He refused to go with you, did he?” Sir Anthony flicked a speck or two of snuff from his sleeve.
“Oh, wouldn’t hear of it! Naught I could say was to any avail. He would be off home, and have no fuss made.”
“Very creditable,” said Sir Anthony, stifling a yawn, and strolled away to meet my Lord March, just come in.
===Chinese===
「啊，一处旧伤？」安东尼爵士客气地显出了兴致。「说的是。那自然，正如你所说，这就说得通了。」
「这小子的胆量，没什么好挑剔的。」贝尔福特向他担保。「勇得像只斗鸡，我拿话与你作保。我一门心思要带他回我的住处，好照看他的肩膀，可他不，他怎么也不肯！」
「他回绝了随你走，是么？」安东尼爵士从袖子上弹掉一两星鼻烟末。
「嗐，连听都不要听！我说什么都是白搭。他一门心思只要回家去，还不许人大惊小怪。」
「很可嘉许。」安东尼爵士说着，忍住一个哈欠，踱开去迎刚进门来的马奇勋爵（Lord March）了。

## III My Lady Lowestoft / III 洛斯托夫特夫人
===Original===
Miss Merriot called “Come in!” to a scratching on the door. Came Mr. Merriot into the big bedroom, and walked across to the fireplace where Kate stood. Mr. Merriot cocked an eyebrow at Kate, and said:﻿—“Well, my dear, and did you kiss her good night?”

Miss Merriot kicked off her shoes, and replied in kind. “What, are you parted from the large gentleman already?”

Mr. Merriot looked into the fire, and a slow smile came, and the suspicion of a blush.

“Lord, child!” said Miss Merriot. “Are you for the mammoth? It’s a most respectable gentleman, my dear.”

Mr. Merriot raised his eyes. “I believe I would not choose to cross him,” he remarked inconsequently. “But I would trust him.”

Miss Merriot began to laugh. “Be a man, my Peter, I implore you.”
===Chinese===
梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）听见门上轻轻一阵搔挠，扬声唤道「进来！」。梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）走进这间大卧房，径直走到凯特（Kate）伫立的壁炉边。梅里奥特先生朝凯特挑起一道眉毛，说道：——「怎么样，亲爱的，你同她吻别道过晚安了么？」

梅里奥特小姐踢掉鞋子，照样回敬。「怎么，你这就同那位大块头绅士分手了？」

梅里奥特先生凝望着炉火，缓缓浮起一丝笑意，颊上还隐隐透出一抹将露未露的红晕。

「老天，孩子！」梅里奥特小姐说。「你相中了那头猛犸？他可是位顶顶体面的绅士呢，亲爱的。」

梅里奥特先生抬起眼来。「我想，我是不愿去同他作对的。」这话答非所问。「可我信得过他。」

梅里奥特小姐笑了起来。「拿出男子汉气概来吧，我的彼得（Peter），我恳求你了。」
===Original===
“Alack!” sighed Mr. Merriot, “I feel all a woman.”

“Oh Prue, my Prue, it’s a Whig with a sober mind! Will you take it to husband?”

“I suppose you will be merry, Robin. Do you imagine me in love on two hours’ acquaintance? Ah, you’re jealous of the gentleman’s inches. Said I not so?”

“My inches, child, stand me in good stead. I believe it’s the small men have the wits. My compliments on the swordplay.”

“At least the old gentleman taught me a trick or two worth the knowing,” placidly said the lady, and pulled up her coat sleeve to show a stained shirt. “The last glass went down my arm,” she said, smiling.

Her brother nodded. “Well, here’s been work enough for an evening,” he remarked. “I await the morrow. Give you good night, child, and pray dream of your mammoth.”
===Chinese===
「呜呼！」梅里奥特先生叹道，「我从头到脚都觉得自己是个女人呢。」

「哦，普鲁（Prue），我的普鲁，这可是一位头脑冷静的辉格党人！你要把他娶作丈夫么？」

「我看你是存心寻开心，罗宾（Robin）。你以为相识两个钟头我就会堕入情网么？啊，你这是嫉妒那位先生的个头。我说中了吧？」

「我的个头，孩子，倒处处帮了我的大忙。依我看，偏偏是小个子男人才有心眼。你那手剑法，我谨致敬意。」

「好歹老先生教过我一两手值得学的招数。」那位女士心平气和地说，一面撩起外衣袖子，露出污渍斑斑的衬衫。「最后那一杯全顺着我的胳膊淌下去了。」她微笑着说。

她弟弟点了点头。「好了，一晚上也尽够忙活的了。」他说，「我就候着明天吧。祝你晚安，孩子，千万要梦见你的猛犸啊。」
===Original===
“In truth I need a mammoth to match me,” said Madam Prudence. “Pray dream of your midget, Robin.”

She went away humming a snatch of an old song. It was apparent to her that her brother frowned upon the morrow, but she had a certain placidity that went well with her inches, and looked upon her world with calm untroubled eyes.

The truth was she was too well used to a precarious position to be easily disturbed, and certainly too used to an exchange of personality with Robin to boggle over her present situation. She had faith in her own wits: these failing her she had a rueful dependence on the ingenuity of her sire. Impossible to tread the paths of his cutting without developing an admiration for the gentleman’s guile. Prudence regarded him with affection, but some irony. She admitted his incomprehensibility with a laugh, but it did not disturb her. She danced to his piping, but it is believed she lacked the adventurous spirit. Now Robin might fume at the mystery with which the father chose to wrap himself about, but Robin enjoyed a chequered career, and had an impish daredevilry that led him into more scrapes than the old gentleman devised. Withal he surveyed the world with a seriousness that Prudence lacked. He had enthusiasms, and saw life as something more than the amusing pageant Prudence thought it.

It seemed he had taken this last, unlucky venture to heart. To be sure, he had had a closer view of it than his sister. She supposed it was his temperament made him enthusiastic for a venture entered into in a spirit of adventure only, and at the father’s bidding. She remembered he had wept after Culloden, with his head in her lap at the old house in Perth﻿—wept in a passion of fury and heartbreak, and dashed away the tears with an oath, and a vow that he hated lost causes. To Prudence it was a matter of indifference whether Stewart Charles or German George sat the throne; she suspected her sire of a like indifference, discounting heroics. They were swept into this rebellion for﻿—God knew what cause; they were entangled in its meshes before they knew it. That was Mr. Colney’s way. He made a fine speech, and it seemed they were all Jacobites. A year before they were entirely French, at Florence; before that there was a certain gaming house at Frankfort, whose proprietor of a sudden swept off his son and daughter to dip fingers in a pie of M. de Saxe’s making.

French, German, Jacobite﻿—it was all one to Prudence. But this England was different. She conceived a fondness for it, and found it homelike. Doubtless it was the mother in her, that big, beautiful, smiling creature who had died at Dieppe when Robin was a child.

She remarked on it to Robin next morning, before their departure for London.
===Chinese===
「说实话，要配我这副身量，还真得有一头猛犸不可。」普鲁登丝（Prudence）小姐说，「罗宾，你呢，千万要梦见你的小不点儿啊。」

她哼着一支旧歌的片段走开了。她看得分明，弟弟对翌日之事蹙着眉头；可她自有一种同她身量相得益彰的恬静，只用平静无扰的双眼打量着她的世界。

实情是，她对悬危的处境早已习惯得太深，不会轻易惊惶；对同罗宾互换人格这档子事，她也照样习惯得太深，断不会为眼下的处境犯难。她对自己的机敏怀有信心；机敏万一靠她不住，她便只得带着几分无可奈何，倚仗老父的那份奇巧。踏着老先生披荆斩棘开出的小路走的人，不可能不对这位绅士的狡黠生出敬佩。普鲁登丝看他，怀着亲情，也带着几分讥诮。她笑着承认他这个人无法参透，但这并不叫她不安。她随他的笛声起舞，不过据信她身上缺少那种冒险精神。罗宾呢，对父亲偏要裹在自己身上的那团神秘，尽可以怨气冲天；可罗宾过的是起伏无常的生涯，一身小鬼似的亡命胆气，闯下的乱子比老先生设计的还多。何况他打量这个世界，还带着一股普鲁登丝所欠缺的郑重。他有他的热忱，在他眼里，人生绝不止是普鲁登丝所以为的那一场逗趣的彩戏。

他似乎把最近这桩倒霉的冒险记挂在心上了。诚然，他比姐姐离它更近，看得更真切。她想，正因他的性子，他才会对一桩只怀着冒险精神、又奉父命才参与的事业热切起来。她记得卡洛登（Culloden）战罢，在珀斯（Perth）的老宅里，他把头伏在她膝上哭过——哭得怒火中烧、肝肠寸断，随即咒骂着抹去眼泪，发誓说他恨透了注定败亡的事业。在普鲁登丝看来，斯图亚特家的查理（Stewart Charles）也好，德国佬乔治（German George）也罢，谁坐上王座都无关痛痒；把英雄做派刨开不算，她疑心老父也怀着一样的漠然。他们被卷进这场叛乱，为的是——天晓得什么事业；不等他们回过神来，就已经缠进它的罗网。科尔尼先生（Mr. Colney）就是这样的路数。他发表一篇漂亮演说，他们便俨然都成了詹姆士党人（Jacobite）。一年之前，他们在佛罗伦萨（Florence）做着彻头彻尾的法国人；再往前，是法兰克福（Frankfort）的一家赌场，其东主忽一日卷起儿子女儿，掳去染指萨克斯先生（M. de Saxe）调制的一块馅饼。

法国人、德国人、詹姆士党人——对普鲁登丝全都一样。可这个英格兰却不同。她对它生出了一份喜爱，觉得它像家一样亲切。毫无疑问，这是她天性中的母性使然——那位高大、美丽、笑吟吟的女人，在罗宾还是个孩童时，就死在了迪耶普（Dieppe）。

次日清晨动身赴伦敦之前，她把这话说给了罗宾听。
===Original===
Robin laughed at her; he was busy with the painting of his face. “Lord, my dear, you’re the very picture of English solidity,” he said. “Do you ride with the mountain?”

“So I believe,” said Miss Prudence. Her eye fell on John, packing away Master Robin’s razors. “La, child, have you shaved? And you with not a hair to your chin!”

This drew a grim smile from the servant. “You’d best have a care, the pair of you,” he said. “We’re off to put our heads in a noose. The gentleman with the sleepy eyes sees things, I’ll warrant you.”

“What, do you shy away from the mountain?” Robin said. “I might engage to run in circles round it.”

The man looked upon his young master with rough affection. “Ay, you’re a cunning one, Master Robin, but the big gentleman’s awake for all you think him so dull.”

Prudence sat saddle-wise across a chair, and leaned her arms on the back of it. Chin in hand she regarded John, and said lazily: “Where’s the old gentleman, John?”
===Chinese===
罗宾笑话她；他正忙着给脸敷粉描画。「老天，亲爱的，你活脱脱是一幅英国式持重的画像。」他说，「你要同那座山并辔而行么？」

「我想是吧。」普鲁登丝小姐说。她的目光落到约翰（John）身上，他正在收拾罗宾少爷的剃刀。「嗳呀，孩子，你倒刮了脸？你下巴上可是一根胡子都不长啊！」

这话引得仆人冷冷一笑。「你们俩最好都留点儿神。」他说，「咱们这是动身把脑袋往绞索里送。那位睡眼惺忪的先生可是什么都看得真切，我敢跟你打保票。」

「怎么，你还要避着那座山走么？」罗宾说，「要我绕着它兜着圈子跑，我都能立下保票。」

仆人带着粗豪的慈爱望着他的小主人。「是啊，你是个机灵鬼，罗宾少爷，可别看你当他呆笨，那位大块头绅士可醒亮着呢。」

普鲁登丝像骑马鞍似的跨坐在一把椅子上，双臂搭在椅背上。她手托下巴，端详着约翰，懒洋洋地说：「老先生在哪儿呢，约翰？」
===Original===
There was no expression in the stolid face. “I’ve lived with him more years than you, Miss Prue, and I don’t take it upon myself to answer that.”

“How long have you lived with him, John?”

“Since before you were born, mistress.”

Robin put down the hare’s foot, and got up. “Ay, you’re devilish close, ain’t you, John? Maybe you know what he’ll be at now?”

“Maybe I do, maybe I don’t,” was all the answer vouchsafed him. “What’s to be your ladyship’s dress today?”

Robin came down to the coffee-room twenty minutes later in a dimity gown and pink ribbons. The hood was cast aside in favour of a straw hat with rosettes, and more ribbons, but Prudence, very sober in fawn breeches, and a coat of claret-coloured cloth, carried a fine mantle over her arm, which was presently put about Madam Robin’s shoulders.
===Chinese===
那张呆板的脸上不见一丝表情。「我跟着他的年头比你的岁数还长，普鲁小姐，这个问题我可不敢斗胆作答。」

「你到底跟着他多少年了，约翰？」

「打您还没出生就跟着了，姑娘。」

罗宾放下那块兔爪粉扑，站起身来。「是啊，你嘴严得邪门，是不是，约翰？兴许你知道他这会儿又要折腾什么了？」

「兴许知道，兴许不知道。」他只得到这样一句答话。「那您大小姐今儿个要穿哪一身？」

二十分钟后，罗宾来到楼下咖啡室，身穿一条细棉布裙，缀着粉色缎带。兜帽弃置一旁，换上了一顶缀着玫瑰花结、又添几条缎带的草帽；普鲁登丝却是一身素净：浅黄褐色的马裤，配一件酒红色呢外衣，臂上搭着一件精美的斗篷，不多时这斗篷便裹上了罗宾小姐的肩头。
===Original===
Miss Letty was agog to be off. They set forward in good time, Robin and the lady seated demurely in the chaise, with the seeming Mr. Merriot and Sir Anthony riding a little way behind, for escort.

There were questions, of course: Prudence was prepared for them and knew no faltering. She spoke of a home in Cumberland﻿—it seemed remote enough﻿—and of the Grand Tour. Sir Anthony had made it: that went without saying. They conversed of foreign towns amicably and safely. Prudence displayed a remarkable knowledge of places; indeed she had the greater part of Europe in her memory, as it were, and an intimate acquaintance with haunts unfrequented by the fairer sex. Once she saw the straight brows rise, and tranquilly awaited developments.

“You’ve seen a vast deal for your years, Master Peter,” said Sir Anthony.

“They number twenty, sir,” she replied. If the truth be told they numbered twenty-six, but she looked a stripling, she knew. “But I lived abroad with my parents some years before my mother’s death. She could not support the English climate.”

Sir Anthony bowed politely, and desired to know where Mr. Merriot might be found in London.

“My sister is to visit my Lady Lowestoft, sir,” Prudence answered. “I am her escort, and I believe her ladyship will give me a lodging. Perhaps you are acquainted with her?”
===Chinese===
莱蒂小姐（Miss Letty）心急火燎，只盼动身。他们及早上了路：罗宾和那位小姐端端正正坐在马车里，那位名义上的梅里奥特先生与安东尼爵士（Sir Anthony）骑马缀在后面不远，权充护卫。

问题自然是免不了的：普鲁登丝早有准备，应答起来毫不含糊。她说起坎伯兰（Cumberland）的一个家——听上去够偏远的——又说起欧陆壮游（Grand Tour）。安东尼爵士自然是游过的：这还用说。他们融洽而稳妥地聊着外邦的城镇。普鲁登丝展露出对各地惊人的熟稔；说真的，她仿佛把大半个欧洲都收在记忆里，对那些女儿家绝少涉足的去处尤其知根知底。有一回，她瞧见那两道平直的眉毛扬了起来，便安然静候下文。

「以您的年纪，见过的世面可真不少啊，彼得（Peter）少爷。」安东尼爵士说。

「二十了，先生。」她答道。说实话是二十六，不过她知道自己瞧上去不过是个半大小子。「只是家母去世之前，我随父母在海外住了好些年。她受不住英国的气候。」

安东尼爵士客气地欠了欠身，又表示想知道在伦敦何处能寻见梅里奥特先生。

「舍妹要去拜望洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft），先生。」普鲁登丝答道，「我一路护送她，我想夫人会赏我一个下榻处的。您或许与她相熟？」
===Original===
“Faith, all the world knows Lady Lowestoft,” said Sir Anthony. “If she denies you, or you grow tired of the petticoats, my dear boy, you may command a lodging with me at any time.”

Prudence flushed in sudden surprise, and looked sideways at the gentleman. This was unexpected; it seemed Sir Anthony was developing a kindness for her. She thanked him gravely, and learned that he owned a house in Clarges Street.

They came to London in the dusk. Prudence sat straight enough in the saddle, but she owned privately to fatigue. It was necessary to restore Miss Letty first to her father, where also they left Sir Anthony. The lateness of the hour was pleaded as an excuse for not entering the house with Miss Letty, but Miss Merriot promised to wait upon her as soon as might be. The chaise drove on to Arlington Street, and drew up at my Lady Lowestoft’s door.

Prudence came down out of the saddle with a sigh of relief. Robin touched her shoulder. “Bravery done, child. Journey’s end now.”

“A halt,” Prudence amended. “No doubt we’ll ha’ done with our travels when we get to heaven.”

My lady’s black page it was that ushered them into my lady’s withdrawing room. This was a spacious apartment, resplendent with gilt and yellow brocade. My lady, it seemed, had a taste for the new French furniture. The page went away to carry Mr. and Miss Merriot’s names to his mistress, and Miss Prudence looked round with a comical grimace. “Faith, it seems my Lady Lowestoft is the same Thérèse de Brûton,” she remarked.
===Chinese===
「说真的，普天之下谁人不知洛斯托夫特夫人。」安东尼爵士说，「万一她拒不收留您，或是您腻烦了这裙裾相伴的差事，我亲爱的孩子，您随时可以吩咐一声，到舍下讨个住处。」

普鲁登丝蓦地一惊，涨红了脸，侧眼打量这位绅士。这可是意料之外；看来安东尼爵士对她正生出一份好感。她郑重谢过他，还得知他在克拉吉斯街（Clarges Street）置有一所宅子。

暮色四合时分，他们到了伦敦。普鲁登丝在马鞍上坐得算是笔直了，私下里却认了疲乏。总得先把莱蒂小姐送还她父亲，他们也把安东尼爵士留在了那里。他们推说时辰已晚，没有随莱蒂小姐进屋，梅里奥特小姐却应承尽早登门拜望。马车继续驶上阿灵顿街（Arlington Street），停在洛斯托夫特夫人的门前。

普鲁登丝翻身下鞍，如释重负地叹了口气。罗宾碰了碰她的肩膀。「好汉戏演完了，孩子。这下可到旅程终点啦。」

「不过是中途歇脚。」普鲁登丝纠正道，「咱们这趟旅程，不用怀疑，得上天堂才算走完呢。」

引他们进夫人起居室的，是夫人的黑人小听差。这是间轩敞的屋子，金饰与黄色织锦华彩夺目。看来夫人偏好新式法国家具。小听差退下去，向女主人通报梅里奥特先生与小姐的名姓；普鲁登丝小姐环顾四周，做了个滑稽的鬼脸。「老实说，看来洛斯托夫特夫人还是当年那位泰蕾丝·德·布吕东（Thérèse de Brûton）。」她说。
===Original===
The door was opened, and swiftly shut again behind a lady who came in with a swirl of a silk gown over an enormous hoop﻿—a lady with black eyes like slits in a thin, vivid face, a powdered wig, and many jewels. She stood with her back to the door, her hand still on the knob, and as she looked sharply from one to the other of her visitors the narrow black eyes narrowed still more, and her face was all alive with laughter. “Eh, but which is the man of you, my little ones?” she demanded.

Prudence made her bow. “So please you, madam.”

My lady came to her with quick jerky steps. “Never! Do I not know thee, my cabbage? Eh, Prue, my dear!” She cast her arms about Prue’s large person, and kissed her on both cheeks. Robin fared the same, but returned the caress with greater alacrity than his flushing sister. Prudence had never a taste for stray kisses.

“And the *bon papa*, my children?” cried my lady, holding a hand of each.

“There, madam, we suppose you to have the advantage of us,” Robin said.

She looked a query, with her head tilted birdlike to one side. “Ah? What’s this? You have no news of him?”
===Chinese===
门开了，又随即在一位夫人背后飞快关上——她旋身而入，绸裙在硕大无朋的裙撑上翻卷一旋。这位夫人生着一双黑眼睛，像两道细缝嵌在瘦削而鲜活的脸上，头戴敷粉假发，周身珠光宝气。她背门而立，一只手还搭在门把上，一面把两位来客挨个儿锐利地打量；那双细窄的黑眼睛愈发眯细，一张脸顿时让笑意盘活了。「诶，你们俩哪一个才是男的呀，我的小宝贝们？」她问。

普鲁登丝行了礼。「悉听尊便，夫人。」

夫人迈着急促的碎步朝她走来。「才不呢！我认不出你么，我的小卷心菜？诶，普鲁，我亲爱的！」她张开双臂搂住普鲁那高大的身躯，在她两颊各亲了一口。罗宾也受了同样的待遇，回吻却比他那位涨红了脸的姐姐爽快得多。普鲁登丝从来就无意领受这飞来的香吻。

「那 *bon papa* 呢，我的孩子们？」夫人扬声道，一手拉住他们每人一只手。

「这嘛，夫人，论消息，我们想是您占了我们的先。」罗宾说。

她把头小鸟似的偏向一侧，摆出一脸探问。「啊？这是怎么说？你们没有他的音讯？」
===Original===
“In truth, madam, we’ve mislaid the old gentleman,” Prudence said. “Or he us.”

My lady burst out laughing again. “I would you had brought him! But that was not to be expected. Yes, he wrote to me. I will tell you﻿—ah, but you are tired! You must sit down. Take the couch, Miss Merriot﻿—*tiens*, that is not a name for my stupid tongue!﻿—Prue, my angel, some chocolate, yes? Marthe shall make it herself: you remember Marthe, no?”

“Egad, is it the same fat Marthe,” Robin said. “I drank her chocolate in Paris, ten years ago!”

“The same, my cabbage, but fatter﻿—oh, of an enormity! you would not believe! To think you should remember, and you a little *gamin*﻿—not more than fourteen years, no? But the wickedness even then! And again in Rome, not?”

“Oh, but it was my Lady Lowestoft, then, at the Legation. We﻿—what were we? Sure, it must have been the Polish gentleman and his two sons. There had been some little fracas at Munich, as I remember.”

This made my lady laugh again. She was off to the door, and sent her page running with orders to Marthe.
===Chinese===
「说实在的，夫人，我们把老先生给弄丢了。」普鲁登丝说，「再不然，就是他把我们给弄丢了。」

夫人又放声大笑起来。「我倒盼着你们真把他带来了呢！可那原就指望不上。是啊，他给我来信了。我这就告诉你们——啊，可是你们累了！快坐下来。梅里奥特小姐，坐那张长榻——*tiens*，这名字不是我这笨舌头叫得来的！——普鲁，我的天使，来点巧克力，好么？让玛尔特（Marthe）亲手去做：你们还记得玛尔特吧？」

「天哪，还是那位胖玛尔特么。」罗宾说，「十年前我在巴黎喝过她做的巧克力！」

「还是她，我的小卷心菜，只是更胖了——哦，胖得没了边儿！你都不敢信！想不到你还记得，你那时可是个小 *gamin*——顶多十四岁，是不是？可那时候就一肚子的坏水了！后来在罗马又见着了，嗯？」

「哦，可那时候在公使馆的，还是洛斯托夫特夫人呢。我们——那会儿我们扮的什么来着？对了，想必是那位波兰绅士和他的两个儿子。我记得在慕尼黑闹出过一场小小的风波。」

这话又逗得夫人大笑。她一阵风似的奔到门边，打发小听差一路小跑去给玛尔特传话。
===Original===
“So the old gentleman wrote to you, madam?” Prudence said. “Did he say he would send us?”

“Say? Robert? *Mon Dieu*, when did he in all his life say what one might so easily comprehend? Be sure it was all a mystery, and no names writ down.”

Prudence chuckled. “Egad, we may be sure of that. But you knew?”

“*A vrai dire.* I might guess﻿—since I too know Robert. Ah, he might count on me, he knew well! It is this rebellion, not?” She sank her voice a little, and her bright eyes were keen as needles.

Robin put a finger to his lips. “To be frank, ma’am, I believe I’m under attainder.”

Her very red lips formed an O, and she wrinkled up her nose. “Chut, chut! He must then put your head in a noose too?”
===Chinese===
「这么说，老先生给您来信了，夫人？」普鲁登丝说，「他说过要把我们打发来么？」

「说？罗伯特（Robert）？*Mon Dieu*，他这辈子何曾说过一句叫人轻易听得懂的话？放心，那准是通篇玄虚，一个名字也不曾落笔。」

普鲁登丝咯咯笑了。「天哪，这一点我们尽可放心。可您事先就认出来了？」

「*A vrai dire*，我猜也猜得出——毕竟我也识得罗伯特。啊，他尽可以指望我，这一点他清楚得很！是为着这场叛乱，对不对？」她把嗓音略微压低，那双明亮的眼睛尖利如针。

罗宾竖起一根手指贴在唇上。「恕我直言，夫人，我恐怕正名列褫夺公权令之下。」

她那两瓣涂得极红的嘴唇撮成一个 O 形，鼻子也皱了起来。「嘘，嘘！这么说，他还非得把你的脑袋也往绞索里送？」
===Original===
“Why, madam, to say sooth we were not loth. Prudence lay snug enough at Perth.”

My lady beamed upon Prudence. “I had thought you in the thick of the fight, my child. It is well. But since it ended, where have you been? *Voyons*, it is many months since it is over, and you are but just come to me!”

There came that bitter look of brooding into Robin’s eyes. It was Prudence who made answer. “Robin was fled to the hills, my lady. I waited snug enough, as he says.”

“To the hills?” My lady leaned a little forward. “With the Prince, no?”

Robin made an impatient movement. The cloud did not lift from his brow. “Some of the time.”

“We heard rumours that he had gone. It is true?”
===Chinese===
「嗳呀，夫人，说句实话，我们那会儿倒也不怎么嫌恶。普鲁登丝在珀斯躲得可安稳了。」

夫人满面含笑地望着普鲁登丝。「我还当你正在厮杀最酣处呢，我的孩子。这样倒好。可自打战事完结，你们一直待在哪儿？*Voyons*，仗打完都好几个月了，你们这才来投我！」

罗宾的眼里浮起了那股阴郁出神的苦涩。答话的是普鲁登丝。「罗宾逃到山里去了，夫人。我么，照他说的，躲得够安稳的。」

「到山里去了？」夫人微微前倾。「是随着王子，是不是？」

罗宾做了个不耐烦的动作。眉宇间的阴云并不散去。「有一阵子是。」

「我们听到传言，说他已经走了。是真的么？」
===Original===
“He’s safe﻿—in France,” Robin said curtly.

“The poor young man! And the *bon papa*? Whither went he?”

“Lud, madam, do you ask us that?” laughed Prudence. “In France, maybe, or maybe in Scotland still. Who knows?”

The door opened, and the page let in fat Marthe, a tray in her hands. It was a very colossus of a woman, of startling girth, and with a smile that seemed to spread all over the full moon of her face. Like her mistress, from one to the other she looked, and was of a sudden smitten with laughter that shook all her frame like a jelly. The tray was set down; she clasped her hands and gasped: “Oh, la-la! To see the little *monsieur habillé en dame*!”

Robin sailed up to her and swept a practised curtsey. “Your memory fails you, Marthe. Behold me﻿—Prudence!”

She gave his arm a playful slap. “*My* memory, *alors*! No, no, m’sieur, you are not yet large enough to be mademoiselle.”
===Chinese===
「他平安无事——在法国。」罗宾简短地说。

「可怜的年轻人！那 *bon papa* 呢？他上哪儿去了？」

「老天，夫人，这还用得着问我们？」普鲁登丝笑道，「兴许在法国，兴许还在苏格兰。谁说得准呢？」

门开了，小听差把胖玛尔特让进来，她手里端着托盘。这女人简直是位巨人，腰围惊人身，一张满月似的圆脸上，笑容仿佛漫过整个脸盘。像她的女主人一样，她把两位来客挨个儿打量，忽然被逗得大笑起来，笑得浑身像团果冻直颤。托盘放下了；她双手一合，喘着气道：「哦，啦啦！竟能亲眼瞧见这小小的 *monsieur habillé en dame*！」

罗宾款款地飘然上前，行了一个演练娴熟的屈膝礼。「你的记性靠不住了，玛尔特。看我——普鲁登丝！」

她顽皮地拍了一下他的胳膊。「*我的*记性，*alors*！不，不，m'sieur，您这身量还没大到能当 mademoiselle 的份上呢。」
===Original===
“Oh, unkind!” Robin lamented, and kissed her roundly.

“Marthe, there is need of secrecy, you understand?” My lady spoke urgently.

“*Bien, madame*; I do not forget.” Marthe put a finger to her lips. “*Tenez*, it must be myself to wait always upon the false mademoiselle. I shall see to it.” She nodded in a businesslike fashion. “John is with you yet?”

“Be very sure of it,” Robin said.

“All goes well, then. No one need suspect. I go to attend to the bedchambers.” She went off with a rolling gait, and was found later in Robin’s room, twitting the solemn manservant.
===Chinese===
「哦，真狠心！」罗宾哀叹一声，结结实实亲了她一大口。

「玛尔特，这事可得严守秘密，你明白么？」夫人的语气急切起来。

「*Bien, madame*，我没有忘。」玛尔特把一根手指抵在唇上。「*Tenez*，往后伺候这位假 mademoiselle 的，务必得由我亲自来。这事我自会料理。」她一副精明干练的神气点了点头。「约翰还跟着你们？」

「这您尽管放心。」罗宾道。

「那就全都顺当了。谁也不必起疑心。我去照应卧室去。」她摇摇摆摆地迈步走了；后来有人在罗宾房里寻见她，正打趣那位板着面孔的男仆。

## XIV My Lord Barham Becomes Mysterious / XIV 巴勒姆勋爵故弄玄虚

===Original===
The old gentleman was left undoubtedly a victor; there could be no gainsaying it. Poor Rensley came off badly from a battle of wits. The world shook its head sadly over the startling disclosures of my lord’s past history, but it was prepared to look indulgently on those shocking lapses. Had his lordship betrayed only the faintest sign of discomfiture, shown the slightest shame, the world might have decided to turn a cold shoulder on him. But my lord was far from showing either shame or discomfiture. So far, indeed, that his attitude was one of pride in his chequered career. He carried all off with a high hand. He said majestically that there was nothing he had not done, and such was the power of the man’s eye that the world began to perceive clearly that he had nothing at all to be ashamed of.

There was also the attitude of my Lord March to be considered. March seemed to be in no doubt of the old gentleman’s identity, and there were few who cared to set themselves up in opposition to my lord. If the newcomer was good enough for March, he was certainly good enough for the rest of the world, but there were one or two farseeing people who began to realize that the new viscount had wormed himself into the graces of society so completely, and so cleverly that it would be quite extraordinarily difficult (in the event of his claim falling to earth) to turn him out without loss of dignity to oneself.

His lordship made not the smallest attempt to conceal his lamentable past: his attitude gave one to understand that whatever he chose to do must of necessity become straightway a creditable performance. In fact, not the lowest of vocations could demean this grand gentleman.

It was considered then that to be sure, if one had the good fortune to be born a Tremaine one might do most things with impunity. Certainly it was a pity to have dragged that noble name in the dust, but after all one had to take into account that the old gentleman had been cast off penniless when little more than a boy. That must stand for his excuse.

As for Rensley, his attack had been ill-judged, and he had taken many shrewder blows than he had dealt. Not a doubt of it that March was right when he said that the old gentleman had the advantage of him in good manners. A number of people remembered that they had said at the outset that there was very little breeding to Rensley, cousin to the Tremaines though he might be. Viscount or no viscount, the old gentleman had great polish, and he showed himself perfectly at ease in the politest of company.

===Chinese===
老先生到头来无疑是个赢家；这一点无可否认。可怜的伦斯利（Rensley）在这场斗智的交锋里败得灰头土脸。对勋爵大人往昔身世那番耸人听闻的披露，社交界痛心地摇头，但已预备好以宽容之眼看待那些骇人的失足。倘若勋爵大人流露过最淡的一丝窘态、显出过最轻的一分羞愧，社交界或许已决意对他报以冷肩。可勋爵大人哪里有一点羞愧或窘迫的意思。岂止没有，他那副姿态，简直是以自己坎坷起伏的生涯为荣。他以强硬手腕，把这一切尽数收了场。他威严地表示，天下没有他未曾做过的事；而此人的目光自有一种威势，世人于是渐渐看得明白：他根本全然没有什么可羞愧的。

还有马奇勋爵（Lord March）的态度也须考虑在内。马奇似乎对老先生的身份毫无疑虑，而肯站出来同勋爵大人作对的人也寥寥无几。这位新来者既然入得了马奇的法眼，自然也入得世上其余人的法眼。不过，也有一两位目光深远之士开始意识到：这位新任子爵钻营进社交界的恩宠，钻得如此彻底、如此巧妙，倘若（他的名分主张一旦落了空）要把他逐出门去，还要不损自己的体面，那可难得非同小可。

勋爵大人丝毫不去掩饰他那可叹的过去：他的姿态叫人领会，凡他选择去做的事，必然立时化作一桩体面的作为。事实上，再低贱的行当也折损不了这位大人物的身份。

于是人们认定，诚然，一个人若有幸生为特雷梅因（Tremaine）家的人，多半的事便都能做了而不受惩处。把那样高贵的姓氏拖进尘泥，固然可惜，可毕竟也得记着：老先生当初被扫地出门、一文不名之时，不过是个半大孩子。这便该算是他的情有可原之处了。

至于伦斯利，他那番进击本就失了算，挨的精明回击远比他自己出手的多。马奇说老先生在风度教养上占了伦斯利的上风，这话毫无疑问是对的。不少人这才记起，自己起初就说过伦斯利没什么教养——尽管他是特雷梅因家的堂亲。也姑不论子爵不子爵，老先生自有十足的儒雅气度，置身最讲究礼数的交际场中照样安然自若。

===Original===
My lord had something to say on the matter himself when he took a dish of Bohea with my Lady Lowestoft next day. He smiled benevolently upon his daughter, leaning over the back of a couch, and said triumphantly: “You saw me! You, my daughter, had the privilege of seeing a master mind at work! I felicitate you.”

Prudence gave her deep chuckle. “I knew a few moments’ dread, sir, I confess.”

He brushed that aside. “Never again make that mistake. I am invincible. Observe the subtlety of my methods! I achieve a miracle.”

My lady gave a piece of angel cake to the monkey nestling at her feet. “You told them, then, *mon cher*? You admitted the past?”

“They hung on my lips,” his lordship said dramatically. “They waited breathlessly to hear what I would say. As always I became the centre, the dominating presence.”

===Chinese===
次日，勋爵大人到洛斯托夫特夫人（my Lady Lowestoft）处同饮一盅武夷茶，也就这事亲自发了几句议论。他慈和地含笑望着倚在长榻背上的女儿，得意洋洋地说：「你亲眼见着了！你，我的女儿，有幸亲见一副大师的头脑运筹帷幄！我向你道贺。」

普鲁登丝（Prudence）发出她那低沉的一阵轻笑。「不瞒您说，先生，我着实担了几分钟的惊。」

他把这话拂到一旁。「往后再别犯这个错。我是攻不破的。瞧瞧我手段何等精妙！我运筹成了一桩奇迹。」

夫人掰了一块天使蛋糕，喂给偎在她脚边的猴子。「这么说，你都对人家摊了牌，*mon cher*？把过去都认了？」

「他们个个吊在我的话头上。」勋爵大人做戏似的说。「他们屏息凝神，单等听我要说什么。我一如既往成了全场中心，成了那压倒一切的存在。」

===Original===
My lady twinkled. “And you said?” she prompted.

“I said, Thérèse, that I had kept a dozen gaming-houses. No other man alive would have dared. But I swayed them﻿—I, Tremaine of Barham!” His admiration of the deed held him silent for a moment, but he went on. “They perceived that I could play the lackey and still keep my prestige. It is true! It is very true.”

My lady gasped. “And they condoned it? They supported you?”

“It was not for them to condone what Tremaine might choose to do,” said my lord, with hauteur. “They applaud me now. I achieve the impossible.”

===Chinese===
夫人眼波一闪。「那你是怎么说的？」她追问。

「我说了，泰蕾丝（Thérèse），我说我开过一打赌场。世上再没有第二个男人敢这么认。可我把他们全慑服了——我，巴勒姆的特雷梅因！」对这桩壮举的自我叹服让他静默了片刻，随即他又接下去。「他们看出来了：我纵然屈尊扮一回跟班，威望也分毫不失。是真的！千真万确。」

夫人倒抽一口气。「他们竟宽宥了？还都拥戴你？」

「特雷梅因愿意做什么，轮不到他们来宽宥。」勋爵大人傲然道。「如今他们向我喝彩了。我把不可能都运筹成了。」

===Original===
“He is a great man,” my lady said to Prudence. “You must admit it.”

“Oh, I do, ma’am, believe me.”

My lord tapped the lid of his snuffbox with one polished fingernail. “Even that large gentleman, that ponderous baronet, that sleepy-eyed Sir Anthony Fanshawe, who looked askance at me﻿—even he concedes me admiration. I win all to my side. It could not be otherwise.”

“Indeed, sir, he said he had begun to conceive a liking for you,” nodded Prudence.

My lord accepted this with a gracious inclination of the head.

===Chinese===
「他是个伟人。」夫人对普鲁登丝说。「你不得不承认。」

「哦，我承认，夫人，真的。」

勋爵大人用一根修得光洁的指甲，轻轻叩着鼻烟盒的盖子。「就连那位大块头绅士，那位沉甸甸的从男爵，那位睡眼惺忪的安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）——当初斜着眼瞧我的——连他也向我表示钦佩了。我把所有人心都赢到了我这一边。事情只能如此。」

「真的，先生，他说他已经渐渐生出对您的好感了呢。」普鲁登丝点头道。

勋爵大人雍容地颔首，领受了这句美言。

===Original===
His daughter continued with a hint of seriousness in her tone. “Yet I think you would be well advised, sir, not to seek too great an intimacy with that same large gentleman.”

“My Prudence, it is he, and all the rest, shall seek intimacy with me,” his lordship said majestically.

“That’s as maybe, sir, but I have some friendship with Sir Anthony, and I say beware!”

He shook his head, but it was more in sorrow than in anger. “Still you do not sufficiently appreciate me,” he said.

“It’s conceivable, sir, you don’t sufficiently appreciate the large gentleman.”

===Chinese===
他女儿接着往下说，语气里透出几分郑重。「不过，依我愚见，先生，您还是不要同那位大块头绅士攀得太深为妙。」

「我的普鲁登丝，该是他，连同其余所有人，来亲近我才对。」勋爵大人威仪堂堂地说。

「那也未见得，先生，可我同安东尼爵士有几分交情，我要说一句：当心！」

他摇了摇头，却是哀矜多于恼怒。「你呀，还是不够赏识我。」他说。

「这倒可以想见，先生，您对那位大块头绅士也不够赏识呢。」

===Original===
My lady smiled. “Ah, my cabbage, you have a too great opinion of ce gros Sir Anthony! He sees no further than the end of his nose.”

“You’re mistaken, ma’am. He sees more than the rest of them put together.” She hesitated. “He watches me. That I know. Something he suspects: not much, but a little.”

My lady looked incredulous. “Not you, my child? But no!”

“Oh, not that! Well, who lives may learn. But I’ve warned you, sir.”

“The little Prudence!” My lord smiled affably. “So cautious!”

===Chinese===
夫人微微一笑。「哎呀，我的小卷心菜，你把这位大块头安东尼爵士瞧得太高啦！他看事情，超不出自己的鼻子尖。」

「您弄错了，夫人。他比旁人统统加在一起看得还多。」她迟疑了一下。「他在盯着我。这我知道。他疑到了一些什么：不多，可有一点儿。」

夫人满脸的不信。「总不见得是你吧，我的孩子？不会的！」

「哦，倒不是那层！唉，来日方长，谁也难说。可我把话提醒到了，先生。」

「小普鲁登丝！」勋爵大人和颜悦色地笑了。「这样小心谨慎！」

===Original===
“You named me Prudence, sir.”

He was inclined to suspect a hitherto unperceived foresight in himself. “And wisely! A premonition. I must surely have known.”

“But to return, *mon ami*!” My lady clasped her hands in her lap. “Society adopts you, then, in spite of all?”

“Again you observe the subtlety of my methods! Consider, my Thérèse! Consider how I become one of the select circle! It is fitting. I am at last in my proper milieu.” He looked kindly at his daughter. “I shall carry you with me, my child. Have no fear. You shall be established﻿—you and Robin.” He became aware of Robin’s absence. “But where is my son? Where is the beautiful Miss Merriot?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
「是您给我起的名字叫普鲁登丝，先生。」

他不由疑心起自己身上一份素来未曾察觉的先见之明。「起得英明！一种预感。我必定早有所知。」

「言归正传，*mon ami*！」夫人把双手交握在膝上。「这么说，任凭出了这许多事，社交界到底还是把你收下了？」

「你又见着我手段的精妙了吧！想想吧，我的泰蕾丝！想想我是怎样跻身进那个精选的圈子！这是相宜的。我终于回到了本属于我的天地。」他慈祥地看了看女儿。「我会带着你们一同上去的，我的孩子。别怕。你们都会被安顿妥当——你，还有罗宾（Robin）。」他这才觉察罗宾不在跟前。「可我的儿子在哪儿？美丽的梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）在哪儿？」他问道。

===Original===
“The rogue’s gone off to sit with his lady love,” answered Prudence.

He looked incredulous. “You tell me he entrusts his secret to a woman? No, no, do not say so, my daughter! Robin is my son, and he has sense﻿—a little.”

“I don’t say it, sir. The lady knows naught. Robin﻿—heigh ho, he must needs fall for a pair of brown eyes!” She told him of the encounter with Miss Letty and Mr. Markham on the road to Gretna Green.

He was pleased to approve. “I embrace you, my child. The hilt to the chin! Myself or Robin taught you that trick. You do me credit, *enfin*! I permit myself to take pride in you. Who is the lady? Eh, but the little Robin inherits something of my disposition!”

“It’s a Miss Grayson, sir, and an heiress as I believe. A pretty brown-eyed chit.”

===Chinese===
「那小坏蛋找他的心上人厮坐去了。」普鲁登丝答道。

他一脸不能相信。「你是跟我说，他把秘密托付给了一个女人？不，不，别这么说，我的女儿！罗宾是我的儿子，他有头脑——就那么一点儿。」

「我没这么说，先生。那位小姐什么都不知道。罗宾呀——嗨嗬，他偏就逃不过一双褐色的眼睛！」她把去格雷特纳格林（Gretna Green）路上同莱蒂小姐（Miss Letty）和马卡姆先生（Mr. Markham）遭遇的那一段讲给他听。

他大表赞许。「我拥抱你，我的孩子。剑柄磕下巴！那一手是我或者罗宾教你的。你到底给我争了光，*enfin*！我容许自己为你骄傲一回。那位小姐是谁？嗳，小罗宾分明承袭了我几分脾性！」

「是位格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson），先生，据我所知还是位女继承人。个俊俏的褐眼睛小妞儿。」

===Original===
The old gentleman’s eyes became intent. “Grayson?” he repeated. “Grayson, my daughter?”

“Do you know the name, sir?”

He put the tips of his fingers together and gazed abstractedly before him. There was no reading what lay in his mind. “Grayson!” he said softly.

“You know something of Sir Humphrey, sir?”

His remarkable eyes travelled to her face. “My child, there are few people of whom I do not know something,” he announced, and took his stately leave of them.

===Chinese===
老先生的目光专注起来。「格雷森？」他重复道。「格雷森，我的女儿？」

「您认得这个姓氏么，先生？」

他把指尖对了对，出神地望着面前。他心里盘算着什么，无从窥测。「格雷森！」他轻声说。

「汉弗莱爵士（Sir Humphrey）的事，您知道一些？」

他那双不同凡响的眼睛转到她脸上。「我的孩子，这世上我不略知一二的人，可没有几个。」他宣布道，随即对他们雍容郑重地告辞而去。

===Original===
Prudence saw no necessity to mention the matter to her brother, but to John, whom she found arranging pots of powder and paint in Robin’s chamber, she said: “The old gentleman’s mysterious over Miss Grayson, John. Is Sir Humphrey a friend of his boyhood?”

John could not take it upon himself to answer.

“Ay, you know more than you’ll admit, don’t you?” said Prudence.

John set down one of the pots with a snap. “I’ll say this, Miss Prue: I don’t understand the game he’s playing now!”

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝觉得不必把这桩事提给弟弟，可她撞见约翰（John）正在罗宾的卧房里归置一盒盒脂粉油彩，便对他说道：「老先生为着格雷森小姐神神秘秘的，约翰。汉弗莱爵士是他少年时的朋友么？」

这事约翰可不敢自作主张来回答。

「嗳，你知道的比你肯认的多，是不是？」普鲁登丝说。

约翰啪的一声搁下一只粉盒。「我只能说这一句，普鲁小姐：他眼下玩的这一手，我瞧不懂了！」

===Original===
“Why, when have any of us understood him?”

The man compressed his lips, and seemed to regret his outburst. He could vouchsafe no more.

Robin came in a while later, a vision in cherry stripes, and a lace fichu. “The Markham hangs about Letty still,” he said abruptly. “She meets him at houses here and there. I’m to gather he tries to ingratiate himself once more.”

Prudence raised her brows. “She’s ill-watched then,” she said.

===Chinese===
「嗐，咱们又有哪一天弄懂过他？」

这人抿紧了嘴唇，像是懊悔方才的失言。他再不肯多赐一言了。

过了些时候，罗宾回来了，一身樱桃红条纹，配一条蕾丝小披巾，艳光四射。「那马卡姆还在莱蒂跟前打转。」他没头没脑地说。「她在各处宅子里同他碰面。据我揣想，他是想再度讨好她。」

普鲁登丝扬起眉毛。「这么说，她可受看管得不严。」她说。

===Original===
“As to that, the aunt plays euchre, and it’s supposed none but the most reputable have admission to these private parties. He’s all conciliation from what she says.”

“Lord, has she a mind to play the fool again?”

Robin looked scornful. “Oh, is it a jest?”

“One of your own, child. I take a leaf from your book.” She laughed. “Or does she dream of the Black Domino?”

“God knows. The Markham has no hope of her now.”

===Chinese===
「说到这个，那位姨妈打她的尤克牌，况且据说，凡进这些私人聚会的，无一不是最清白体面的人。照她的说法，他如今百般赔小心。」

「老天，她莫非又想再犯一回傻？」

罗宾一脸不屑。「哦，这是说笑话么？」

「你自家惯说的那一种罢了，孩子。我这是跟你学的。」她笑道。「要不然，莫非她还在梦里惦记那位黑衣多米诺（Black Domino，假面舞会上黑披风面具的装束）？」

「天晓得。马卡姆这会儿是没指望了。」

===Original===
“A persistent gentleman, faith.”

“I take it the man goes in danger of a debtor’s prison. He’s deeply involved, as I hear, but was used to hang upon Rensley. Now, if the old gentleman ousts Rensley, he’ll have need of a fresh patron, or a rich bride. It’s a dangerous dog.”

He had set his finger on the very marrow of it. Mr. Markham saw himself in some need of relief. The turf played him false, and the cards went badly. It seemed to him that his noble friend’s sun was setting fast: Mr. Rensley had that day been informed by a grave man of law that no fault could be found with my Lord Barham’s papers. If it could not be proved that my lord had stolen them from the real owner he must stand acknowledged Tremaine of Barham. There was, then, an end to a profitable friendship. Mr. Markham had small hope of a happy issue. Mr. Rensley, in a moment of impetuosity, had named the sum he would be glad to give the man who should prove my lord an impostor, but although Mr. Markham would be willing to perform that office for him he could not at present see the means of doing it.

His recollection of the gaming-house at Munich had led nowhere. Mr. Rensley had had some biting words to say of it. Mr. Rensley was in a very bad temper over the whole affair, and his manner to his friend was such that Mr. Markham began to conceive a positive dislike for him.

===Chinese===
「好一位锲而不舍的绅士，真的。」

「照我看，这人随时有进债务人监狱的危险。据我所闻，他债台高筑，往常全吊在伦斯利身上。如今老先生若是把伦斯利挤了出去，他就得另寻一位新靠山，不然就讨一位有钱的新娘。这是条危险的狗。」

他这一指头，正按在这桩事的骨髓上。马卡姆先生自觉亟需一条出路。赛马场坑他，牌运也不济。在他看来，他那位显贵朋友的日头正在飞快西沉：伦斯利先生当天已由一位神情肃穆的法律界人士告知，巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）的文书证件挑不出一丝差错。倘若不能证实勋爵大人是把这些证件从真正的主人手里偷来的，他就只得被承认是巴勒姆的特雷梅因了。这样，一段有利可图的交情便到了头。马卡姆先生对圆满的结局没抱多大指望。伦斯利先生在一时性起之下，曾报出一个数目，乐意付给那能证实勋爵大人是个冒牌货的人；马卡姆先生虽然甘愿为他效这番劳，眼下却看不出有什么办到的门道。

他对慕尼黑（Munich）那家赌场的记忆，追来追去也没追出个名堂。伦斯利先生为这事说过几句刻薄话。伦斯利先生为这整桩事心绪坏得很，对他这位朋友又是那副嘴脸，马卡姆先生于是开始当真生出对他的厌恶来。

## XI My Lord Barham in Arlington Street / XI 巴勒姆勋爵在阿灵顿街

===Original===
When the black page announced my Lord Barham next morning both Mr. and Miss Merriot were with my lady in the morning-room. My lord was ushered in, very point-device, with laced gloves, and a muff of miniver, and a long beribboned cane. The muff and the cane were given into the page’s charge; the door closed behind this diminutive person, and my lord spread wide his arms. “My children!” he exclaimed. “Behold me returned to you!”

His children maintained an admirable composure. “Like Jonah cast up out of the whale’s belly,” said Robin.

My lord was not in the least put out of countenance by this coolness. “My son!” He swooped upon Robin. “Perfect! To the last detail! My Prudence!”

Prudence submitted to a fervent embrace. “Well, sir, how do you do?” she said, smiling. “We perceive you are returned to us, but we don’t understand the manner of it.”

===Chinese===
次日早晨，当那黑肤侍童通禀巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）驾到时，梅里奥特先生和梅里奥特小姐（Mr. and Miss Merriot）正陪着夫人在早客室里。勋爵大人被引了进来，一身行头考究入微：戴着饰蕾丝的手套，携一只白鼬皮手筒，拄一根缀满缎带的长手杖。手筒和手杖交由侍童拿管；房门在这小个子身影背后合上，勋爵大人张开双臂。「我的孩子们！」他欢呼道，「瞧啊，我回到你们身边来了！」

他的孩子们端出一副令人叹服的镇定。「倒像从鲸鱼肚子里被吐出来的约拿（Jonah）。」罗宾（Robin）说。

这般冷淡丝毫没有叫勋爵大人面露窘色。「我的儿子！」他一个箭步扑向罗宾。「妙极了！妙到最后一处细节！我的普鲁登丝！」

普鲁登丝（Prudence）由着他热烈拥抱了一回。「嗳，先生，您一向可好？」她含笑说。「我们看得出您回到我们身边了，只是不明白这是怎么个来龙去脉。」

===Original===
He struck an attitude. “But do you not know? I am Tremaine. Tremaine of Barham!”

“Lud!” said Robin. “You don’t say so, sir!”

He was hurt. “Ah, you do not believe in me! You doubt me, in effect!”

“Well, sir”﻿—Prudence sat on the arm of Robin’s chair, and gently swung one booted leg to and fro﻿—“We’ve seen you as Mr. Colney; we’ve seen you as Mr. Daughtry; we’ve even seen you as the Prince Vanilov. You cannot altogether blame us.”

===Chinese===
他昂然摆出一个亮相的姿势。「你们竟不知道么？我是特雷梅因（Tremaine）。巴勒姆的特雷梅因！」

「老天！」罗宾说。「您不会是说着玩的吧，先生！」

他面露受伤之色。「啊，你们不信我！说到底，你们是在疑心我！」

「唉，先生，」普鲁登丝坐上罗宾椅子的扶手，一条穿靴子的腿轻轻来回荡着，「我们见过您做科尔尼先生（Mr. Colney），见过您做多尔蒂先生（Mr. Daughtry），甚至见过您做瓦尼洛夫亲王（Prince Vanilov）。这可实在怪不得我们。」

===Original===
My lord abandoned his attitude, and took snuff. “I shall show you,” he promised. “Do not doubt that this time I surpass myself.”

“We don’t doubt that, sir.”

My lady said on a gurgling laugh: “But what will you be at, *mon cher*? What madness?”

“I am Tremaine of Barham,” reiterated his lordship, with dignity. “Almost I had forgot it, but I come now into my own. You must have known”﻿—he addressed the room at large﻿—“you who have watched me, that there was more to me than a mere wandering gamester!”

“Faith, we thought it just devilry, sir,” Prudence chuckled.

===Chinese===
勋爵大人收起姿势，拈了一撮鼻烟。「我会让你们瞧的，」他许诺道。「不必怀疑，这一回我要超乎我自己的水准。」

「这我们倒不怀疑，先生。」

夫人咯咯笑着说：「可你这回又想干什么呀，*mon cher*？又是什么疯主意？」

「我是巴勒姆的特雷梅因，」勋爵大人庄重地重申道。「我几乎把这忘了，可如今我要认回我自己的名分了。你们本当看得出来」——他向满屋子的人发话——「你们这些冷眼看着我的人，本当看得出来，我这个人，可不止是个四处游荡的赌徒！」

「老实说，我们只当那全是您使的鬼花样呢，先生。」普鲁登丝轻笑道。

===Original===
“You do not appreciate me,” said my lord sadly, and sat him down by the table. “You lack soul, my children. Yes, you lack soul.”

“I concede you all my admiration, sir,” said Prudence.

“You shall concede me more still. You shall recognise a master mind in me, my Prudence. We come to the end of our travels.”

“Tyburn way,” said Robin, and laughed. “Egad, sir, you’ve a maggot in your head to venture on such a piece of folly!”

===Chinese===
「你们不懂得赏识我，」勋爵大人怏怏然说，在桌旁落了座。「你们缺一份灵性，我的孩子们。是的，缺一份灵性。」

「我甘愿奉上我全部的仰慕，先生。」普鲁登丝说。

「你还得再让给我一些呢。你们要认出，我身上有一副大师的头脑，我的普鲁登丝。我们的漂泊到头了。」

「走的是往泰伯恩（Tyburn，伦敦的绞刑场）去的那条道。」罗宾说着，大笑起来。「乖乖，先生，您脑瓜里准又钻进了一条怪蛆虫，竟敢来冒这么一出荒唐险！」

===Original===
The old gentleman’s eyes glinted. “Do my schemes go awry, then? Do I fail in what I undertake to do, Robin my son?”

“You don’t, sir, I’m willing to admit, but you break fresh ground now, and I believe you don’t know the obstacles. This is England.”

“Robin acquires geography!” My lord smiled gently. “It is the land of my birth. I am come home, *enfin*. I am Tremaine of Barham.”

“And pray what are we, sir?” inquired Robin, with interest.

===Chinese===
老先生的眼睛倏地一亮。「这么说，我的谋划落过空？我着手办的事，失败过不成，我的儿子罗宾？」

「没有，先生，这个我乐于承认，可您如今破的是新地，我料想您并不晓得路上有什么拦阻。这里是英格兰。」

「罗宾学起地理来了！」勋爵大人温然一笑。「这是我生于斯的土地。我回到家了，*enfin*。我是巴勒姆的特雷梅因。」

「那请问，我们算什么呢，先生？」罗宾兴致盎然地问。

===Original===
“At present, *mes enfants*, you are Mr. and Miss Merriot. I compliment you. It is admirable. I see that you inherit a part of my genius.” He kissed his fingertips to them. “When I have made all secure you are the Honourable Robin, and the Honourable Prudence Tremaine.”

“Of Barham,” interpolated Prudence.

He looked at her affectionately. “For you, my beautiful Prue, I plan a great marriage,” he informed her magnificently.

“A Royal Prince, belike?” said Prudence, unimpressed.

===Chinese===
「眼下嘛，*mes enfants*，你们是梅里奥特先生和梅里奥特小姐。我恭维你们一句：演得妙极了。我看得出，你们继承了我的一部分天纵之才。」他朝他们飞了个指尖吻。「等我理妥一切，你们便是尊贵的罗宾·特雷梅因和尊贵的普鲁登丝·特雷梅因了。」

「巴勒姆的。」普鲁登丝插话道。

他慈爱地望着她。「至于你，我美丽的普鲁（Prue），我给你筹划了一门了不得的亲事。」他以雍容的气派晓谕她。

「没准儿是位王室亲王？」普鲁登丝说，不为所动。

===Original===
“I will choose from an older house than this of Hanover,” my lord said grandly. “Have no fear.”

Robin looked at his sister. “My dear, what to do?” he said helplessly.

“Leave all to me!” commanded my lord. “I do not make mistakes.”

“Except in the matter of Royal Princes,” said Robin, with meaning.

===Chinese===
「我要从比当今汉诺威（Hanover）更古老的门第里挑，」勋爵大人好大的气派。「放一百个心。」

罗宾看看姐姐。「亲爱的，这可怎么好？」他一筹莫展地说。

「统统交给我！」勋爵大人吩咐道。「我这个人，是从不出错的。」

「只是在王室亲王那桩事上出过一回。」罗宾意有所指地说。

===Original===
“Bah! I forget all that!” The past was consigned to perdition with a snap of thin fingers. “It might have chanced otherwise. I seized opportunity, as ever. Do you blame me for the Rebellion’s failure?”

Prudence shook her head. “Ah, sir, you should have been put at the head of it,” she mourned. “The Prince would be at St. James’s today then.”

My lord was forcibly struck by this view of the case. “My child, you have intuition,” he said seriously. “You are right. Yes, beyond all doubt you are right.” He sat lost in meditation, planning, they knew, great deeds that might have been.

They exchanged glances. My lady sat by the window, chin in hand, raptly gazing upon the old gentleman out of her narrow eyes. There was nothing to do but to wait for him to come out of his trance. Robin sat back in his chair with a shrug of fatalism; his sister continued to sling one booted leg.

===Chinese===
「呸！那些我全忘了！」往昔随着瘦削手指的一记捻响，被打发进了地狱。「兴许机缘一转，成败便另当别论。我照例抓住了机会。叛乱之败，难道也怪到我不成？」

普鲁登丝摇了摇头。「唉，先生，当初就该让您领头才是。」她痛惜道。「那样的话，王子如今早已坐进圣詹姆斯宫（St. James's）了。」

这番见解给了勋爵大人当胸一击。「我的孩子，你有直觉。」他正色道。「你说得对。是的，毫无疑问，你说得对。」他坐在那里出神冥想，他们知道，他正筹划着那些本可成就的千秋伟业。

姐弟俩交换了一个眼色。夫人坐在窗边，手托下巴，眯起那双细窄的眼睛，痴痴地打量着老先生。无可奈何，只得等他自己从迷离状态里回过神来。罗宾往椅背上一靠，听天由命地耸了耸肩；他姐姐继续晃着一条穿靴子的腿。

===Original===
My lord looked up. “Dreams!” he waved them aside. “Dreams! I am a great man,” he said simply.

“You are, sir,” agreed Prudence. “But we should like to know what you plan now.”

“I have done with plans and plots,” he told her. “I am Tremaine of Barham.”

There seemed to be no hope of getting anything more out of him. But Prudence persevered. “So you have told us, sir. But can you prove it to the satisfaction of Mr. Rensley?”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人抬起头来。「梦！」他一挥手把它们挥散。「都是梦！我是个伟人。」他平平淡淡地说。

「您是，先生。」普鲁登丝附和道。「不过我们倒想知道，您眼下作何打算。」

「计谋筹策那一套，我已洗手不干了。」他告诉她。「我是巴勒姆的特雷梅因。」

看来休想再从他嘴里问出什么了。可普鲁登丝不肯罢休。「您是这么对我们说了，先生。可您能证实这件事，证到叫伦斯利先生（Mr. Rensley）心服口服么？」

===Original===
“If Rensley becomes a nuisance, Rensley must go,” my lord declared, with resolution.

“Murder, sir?”

“He will disappear. I shall see to it. It need not worry you. I arrange all for the best.”

“I wonder whether Mr. Rensley will see it in that light?” said Prudence. “Does he acknowledge you, sir?”

“No,” admitted his lordship. “But he fears me. Believe it, he fears me!”

===Chinese===
「伦斯利若是碍事，伦斯利就得消失。」勋爵大人斩钉截铁地宣布。

「谋杀么，先生？」

「他会消失的。我自有安排。这事用不着你们操心。我样样都安排到最好。」

「不知伦斯利先生会不会也这么看？」普鲁登丝说。「他承认您么，先生？」

「不承认。」勋爵大人供认不讳。「可他怕我。相信我，他怕我！」

===Original===
Robin had been sitting with closed eyes, but he opened them now. “I grant you this much, sir: you are to be feared.”

“My Robin!” My lord flung out a hand to him. “You begin to know me then!”

“I’ve a very lively fear of you myself,” said Robin frankly. “Give me audience a moment!”

“Speak, my son. I listen. I am all attention.”

Robin looked at his fingertips. “Well, sir, the matter stands thus: we’ve a mind to turn respectable, Prue and I.” He raised his eyes. His father’s expression was one of courteous interest. “I admit we don’t see our way clear. We wait on you. To be candid, sir, you pushed us into the late Rebellion, and it is for you to extricate us now. I’ve no desire to adorn Tyburn Tree. We came to London under your direction; we stayed for you here, according to the plan. True, you have come as you promised you would, but in a guise that bids fair to compromise us more deeply still. We don’t desert you: faith, we can’t, unless we choose to go abroad again. But we’ve an ambition to settle in England. We look to you.”

===Chinese===
罗宾一直阖眼坐着，这时睁开了眼睛。「这一点我且认了，先生：您确乎叫人畏惧。」
