Robin leaned out to speak to Sir Humphrey. “I bring her back to you again, sir. I daresay she will tell you more than I am permitted to know. I don’t need to ask you to be kind to her.”

Sir Humphrey sighed. “Another scrape! I have to thank you once more, ma’am.”

“There is not the necessity, sir. We happened to chance that way; we had been visiting at Barnet. Take her in, sir: she’s worn out, and, I believe, has suffered much. Drive on, John.”

“You will not enter? A glass of wine﻿—?”

“I thank you, sir, but it grows late, and we must hurry back to my Lady Lowestoft’s. You’re ready, my Peter?”

===Chinese===
罗宾探出身来同汉弗莱爵士说话。「我又把她给您送回来了，先生。我敢说，她会告诉您的，比我获准知道的还多。请您善待她这话，就不劳我多嘴了。」

汉弗莱爵士叹了口气。「又闯了一回祸！我须得再次向您道谢，小姐。」

「不必如此，先生。我们只是恰巧打那边经过；我们在巴尼特（Barnet）访友来着。带她进去吧，先生：她累坏了，而且我相信，受了大罪。赶车吧，约翰。」

「不进来坐坐？来一杯酒——？」

「多谢您，先生，只是天色晚了，我们还须赶回洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft）府上去。你好了么，我的彼得？」

===Original===
Letty, clinging to her father’s arm, watched the chaise roll away down the street, with the neat figure of Mr. Merriot riding behind it. She heaved a deep sigh, and whispered urgently: “Papa, papa, I must speak with you alone! Send Aunt to bed!”

Miss Grayson the elder was in a severe bustle. “Letitia, you pass all bounds! Come within doors, for heaven’s sake, brother. You will explain yourself, Letitia, if you please. How came you to be lost in the gardens, and where, pray, have you been?”

They stood now in the lit hall of the house. Letty shook her head wearily, and cast an appealing, urgent glance up into her father’s face. His mood was of annoyance at this fresh escapade, but he read such lingering horror in his daughter’s brown eyes that he silenced his sister. “I will have a talk with Letitia myself, sister, with your leave. Come into the library, child: you will be the better for a glass of Madeira.”

===Chinese===
莱蒂挽着父亲的手臂，望着马车辘辘沿街远去，梅里奥特先生利落的身影骑行在车后。她深深叹了口气，急切地低声道：「爸爸，爸爸，我必须单独同您说话！快打发姑母去睡！」

大格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson the elder）正气急败坏地忙乱着。「莉蒂西娅，你简直无法无天！看在老天的份上，进屋里来吧，哥哥。请你把话说个明白，莉蒂西娅。你怎么会在花园里走失的？还有，请问，你这些时候到底在哪儿？」

此时他们已站在宅中灯火通明的大厅里。莱蒂倦怠地摇了摇头，向父亲脸上投去恳求而急切的一瞥。他因这新的一场胡闹本怀着一腔恼怒，可他从女儿棕色的眼睛里读出那样一种挥之不去的惊怖，便喝止了妹妹。「容我说一句，妹妹，我要亲自同莉蒂西娅谈一谈。到书房来吧，孩子：喝一杯马德拉酒（Madeira），你会好受些。」

===Original===
Miss Grayson was affronted. “As Letitia’s chaperon, brother, I feel I have the right to know more of this!” she declared.

“So you shall, Cordelia, but later. Do not let us forget that I am Letty’s father.”

Hearing that note in her brother’s voice Miss Grayson thought it as well to retire. She sniffed loudly, and saying that she hoped Sir Humphrey would read his erring child a sharp lesson, flounced off up the stairs to her own apartment.

Sir Humphrey took Letty into the library, where a fire burned still. With austere kindliness he forbade all attempt at explanation until she should have swallowed some wine. This was soon brought by a curious servant. Letty was obliged to drink, and her father had the satisfaction of seeing some of the colour return to her pale cheeks.

===Chinese===
格雷森小姐觉得大受冒犯。「作为莉蒂西娅的陪护人，哥哥，我认为我有权知道更多内情！」她宣称道。

「你会知道的，考狄利娅（Cordelia），不过得晚些。咱们可别忘了，我才是莱蒂的父亲。」

听出哥哥话音里的那股调子，格雷森小姐觉得还是识趣退下的好。她响亮地哼了一声鼻子，撂下一句盼着汉弗莱爵士好好训一训这个不省心的孩子，便气冲冲地摆着裙裾上了楼，回自己房里去了。

汉弗莱爵士把莱蒂带进书房，炉火犹自燃着。他带着严厉的慈爱，不许她张口解释半个字，直到她把酒咽下肚去。一个满腹好奇的仆人很快把酒端了来。莱蒂只得喝了，看着她苍白的双颊重又透出几分血色，做父亲的心满意足。

===Original===
She put back her cloak, and with quivering fingers pulled the letter from the bosom of her gown. “Take it, Papa! Take it, and burn it!” she said in a voice of strong agitation.

Surprised he received the paper, and unfolded it. An exclamation broke from him; he stood with the letter in his hand, staring down at his daughter. “How came you by this?”

Her tired eyelids fluttered upwards. “Mr. Markham had it.”

“That scoundrel! He gave it to you?” Sir Humphrey’s voice was sharp with anxiety. “Good God, child, don’t tell me﻿—” He broke off, afraid to put his dread into words.

===Chinese===
她把外氅向后一褪，用颤抖的手指从胸衣里抽出那封信。「拿去，爸爸！拿去，把它烧了！」她的声音激动得厉害。

他诧异地接过那纸，展开来看。一声惊呼从他口中迸出；他手执那封信立在原地，低头瞪视着女儿。「你从哪儿得来的这个？」

她疲倦的眼皮颤动着抬了起来。「在马卡姆先生手里。」

「那个恶棍！是他给你的？」汉弗莱爵士的嗓音因焦灼而发尖。「我的上帝，孩子，别告诉我——」他顿住话头，不敢把心底的恐惧化作言语。

===Original===
“He said﻿—he said he would expose you unless I would elope with him again. I could not think of a way out.” She clasped her hands nervously in her lap. “He said if I told you he would publish the letter. There seemed to be nothing I could do. I was to fly with him tonight: I did not want to, papa! I have been so miserable! We reached as far as to Finchley Common, and then﻿—” She stopped, and after a moment’s hesitation leaned forward in her chair. “Papa, if I tell you the truth, will you promise to keep it secret? I am bound to divulge nothing, but I must tell you. He could not have meant me not to tell you. If I don’t you could never understand. But you must keep it secret, papa, or I may not tell you!”

He put the letter into the fire, and watched it shrivel, and burn. “Hush, child! My poor girl, you suffer for my folly, but that villain imposed on you. There was not enough in that paper to send me to the gallows.”

“Was there not?” she had but a faint interest in it now. “I did not know. But you do not promise, papa! you do not promise!”

===Chinese===
「他说——他说，除非我再同他私奔，他就要把您揭发出来。我想不出半点脱身之法。」她紧张地把双手在膝上紧紧交握。「他说，倘若我告诉了您，他就把这封信公之于众。我似乎无路可走。我今晚本要随他出走：我并不愿意，爸爸！我一直痛苦极了！我们一路走到芬奇利公地（Finchley Common），然后——」她停住了，迟疑片刻，又从椅子里向前倾过身子。「爸爸，倘若我把真相告诉您，您能答应保守秘密么？我受人之托，什么也不可泄露，可我必须告诉您。他总不至于存心连您也瞒着。我若不说，您就永远无法明白。可您必须保密，爸爸，不然我就不能告诉您了！」

他把信投进火里，看着它蜷曲、烧尽。「嘘，孩子！我可怜的姑娘，你是在替我的糊涂受罪，可那恶棍蒙骗了你。那张纸上的东西，还不足以送我上绞架。」

「是么？」她如今对这事只余一缕淡淡的心思。「我原先不知道。可您还没有答应我，爸爸！您还没有答应！」

===Original===
He sat down beside her and took her hand. “What is this secret? You won’t tell without my promise? Why then, I must give it you. Don’t keep aught back from me, Letty!”

“I must go back so far,” she said hurriedly. “As far as to the masked ball my Lady Dorling gave. You remember?” The whole story came tumbling out, and ended with the Unknown’s reappearance this evening.

Sir Humphrey was thunderstruck. A gasp escaped him at the tale of the duel; he put a quick question or two, and seemed to be almost incredulous. When his daughter came to the end he rose up from his chair, and took a turn about the room, his hands linked behind his back. “Markham dead!” he ejaculated several times. “Good God, the scandal!”

===Chinese===
他在她身边坐下，握住她的手。「这秘密究竟是什么？没有我的许诺你就不肯说？那么好吧，我这就许诺给你。什么都别瞒我，莱蒂！」

「我得从好远的地方讲起。」她急急地说。「一直讲回多林夫人（Lady Dorling）办的那场假面舞会。您还记得么？」整个故事滔滔倾泻而出，末了讲到那位无名氏今夜的再度现身。

汉弗莱爵士如遭雷击。听到决斗一节，他不禁倒抽一口冷气；急急问了一两个问题，几乎难以置信。待女儿讲到结尾，他从椅中起身，双手交扣在背后，在屋里踱了一个来回。「马卡姆死了！」他一连几遍脱口惊呼。「我的上帝，这桩丑闻！」

===Original===
“I know, I know, but I could not help it, papa!”

“No, it has been my fault,” he said sadly. “And but for this strange masked man you would be in a bad case now. We must brave it out. But have you no notion who your preserver may be? If he knows you, you must surely know him!”

“I do not, papa. He is not like any man of our acquaintance.” A blush flooded her cheeks. “Papa﻿ ﻿…”

He observed her heightened colour. “Well, child?”

===Chinese===
「我知道，我知道，可我没有法子呀，爸爸！」

「不，这都是我的过错。」他悲伤地说。「若不是这个古怪的蒙面人，你此刻早已身陷绝境。咱们只能硬着头皮顶过去。可你那位救命恩人究竟是谁，你就没有半点头绪么？他既认得你，你必定也认得他呀！」

「我不认得，爸爸。他同我们相识的随便哪一位都不像。」红晕涌上她的双颊。「爸爸……」

他瞧见她红透了的脸色。「怎么，孩子？」

===Original===
She looked frankly up at him. “I do not know his name, papa, nor anything about him, but I am going to marry him. He said﻿—he said that next time he came it would be for me.”

Sir Humphrey did not know what reply to make. At last he said: “That is for the tomorrow, Letitia. We must know something more of him. But certainly, provided his birth be respectable, he deserves to win you. I look forward to the day when I may have the honour of taking his hand.”

Whereupon Miss Letty promptly cast herself into his arms, and burst into tears.

===Chinese===
她坦然抬眼望着他。「我不知道他的名字，爸爸，关于他我什么都不知道，可我要嫁给他。他说过——他说过，下一回他再来，便是为了我。」

汉弗莱爵士不知该作何答语。他终于开口：「那是往后的事，莉蒂西娅。咱们还须对他多知道些底细。不过可以断言，只要他出身清白体面，他便配赢得你。我盼着有朝一日，能有幸同他握手。」

闻言，莱蒂小姐当即扑进他怀里，放声大哭起来。

## XVII Sad Falling Out of Friends / XVII 朋友反目

===Original===
By the afternoon the news was all over town that Fanshawe had wounded Rensley in a duel that had taken place that morning in Galliano’s rooms, of all places in the world. Every sort of tale was told. Fanshawe had taken leave of his senses and struck Rensley across the face with his glove: no, it was Rensley struck Fanshawe; faith, it must have been that way, for everyone knew that it was not like Fanshawe to pick a quarrel. The affair had sprung up out of a clear sky: there had been some raillery which Rensley took exception to, and Fanshawe had carried it too far.

Mr. Belfort heard it from my Lord Kestrel, and was thunderstruck. My lord told it him between chuckles and with many embellishments, and described, with gesture, the thrust that had put Rensley out of action for many weeks to come. Mr. Belfort went hurrying off to confer with Mr. Devereux, whom he found writing execrable verse to a lady of uncertain morals, and bore him off straight to Arlington Street.

My lady laughed when the message was brought to Prudence, but Robin looked queerly, and showed a desire to inquire further into the need for a private conference. Prudence said lightly that it was some matter concerning a horse, and escaped before Robin could read the trouble in her face. He had the uncanny knack of it.

===Chinese===
到了下午，消息已传遍全城：范肖（Fanshawe）在一场决斗中伤了伦斯利（Rensley），而那场决斗，天底下的地方千千万，偏偏是当天上午在加利亚诺（Galliano）的寓所里进行的。什么样的说法都有。有说范肖失了心智，拿手套甩了伦斯利一脸；不对，是伦斯利甩了范肖；说真的，准是这么回事，因为人人心知肚明，范肖不是那号寻衅闹事的人。这场风波平地而起：先是几句玩笑话，伦斯利听着刺耳，范肖又把玩笑开过了头。

贝尔福特先生（Mr. Belfort）从凯斯特雷尔勋爵（Lord Kestrel）嘴里听说了这事，登时如遭雷击。勋爵大人一边咯咯直笑一边讲给他听，还添了好多枝叶，又连说带比画地描述了那一剑——就是叫伦斯利往后一连好几个星期动弹不得的那一剑。贝尔福特先生急急忙忙去找德弗罗先生（Mr. Devereux）商议，只见他正伏案给一位风评可疑的女士写一首糟糕透顶的诗，便当即把他架走，直奔阿灵顿街（Arlington Street）。

传话送到普鲁登丝（Prudence）这里时，夫人笑了，罗宾（Robin）的神色却有些古怪，露出想再追问追问何以必须密谈的意思。普鲁登丝轻描淡写地说，不过是关于一匹马的一点事，趁罗宾还没从她脸上读出破绽，便抽身走掉了。他就有这么一股邪门的本事。

===Original===
She found Mr. Belfort looking portentous, and Mr. Devereux melancholy. “Why, Charles, what ails you?” she asked. It seemed to her that there was no one but herself had the right to look solemn.

“My dear fellow, it’s the devil of a business,” Belfort said severely. “A most disgraceful affair, ’pon my soul!”

Mr. Devereux shook his head. “Very, very disgraceful,” he echoed.

“Lud, sir, you horrify me! What’s toward?”

“Rensley,” said Belfort, “has committed a﻿—damme, a cursed breach of etiquette! You can’t meet the man, Peter. Can he, Dev?”

Mr. Devereux was of the opinion that it would be impossible.

===Chinese===
她见到贝尔福特先生一脸煞有介事，德弗罗先生满面愁云。「哎呀，查尔斯（Charles），你们这是怎么了？」她问道。在她看来，普天之下便只有她本人，才有资格摆出这副郑重其事的脸色。

「亲爱的伙计，这可是桩糟透了的勾当。」贝尔福特正色道。「一桩顶顶丢脸的事，我以灵魂起誓！」

德弗罗先生摇了摇头。「丢脸，太丢脸了。」他跟着叹道。

「哎呀老天，先生，你们吓坏我了！究竟出了什么事？」

「伦斯利，」贝尔福特说，「干下了——妈的，一桩可恶之至的失礼！你不能同这个人交手了，彼得（Peter）。你说是不是，德夫（Dev）？」

德弗罗先生的意见是，那已万万不可能。

===Original===
A flush sprang up in Prudence’s cheeks. It was of sudden, overwhelming relief, but Mr. Belfort took it to betoken anger. “Ay, Peter my boy, I knew you’d take it hard, but positively you can’t meet the man after such a slight.”

“Very shocking business,” Mr. Devereux said mournfully. “Can’t understand it at all.”

Prudence had command of herself again. If she must not fight it seemed safe enough to protest a little, as was proper. “But pray let me hear what it is!” she said. “I don’t draw back from an encounter, Charles, be sure.”

“It’s Rensley has drawn back,” Mr. Belfort said, still with awful solemnity.

“Not drawn back, Bel. You couldn’t say he had drawn back,” protested Mr. Devereux.

“It’s the same thing, Dev. He can’t meet Peter tomorrow, and I say it’s a cursed insult. I shall tell Jessup our man won’t fight.”

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝双颊腾地飞红。那是一阵突如其来、铺天盖地的宽慰，贝尔福特先生却把它当成了怒意。「是啊，我的彼得小子，我就知道你咽不下这口气，可吃了这样一记怠慢，你实在万万不能再同此人交手了。」

「骇人听闻，实在是骇人听闻。」德弗罗先生凄凄惶惶地说。「怎么想也想不明白。」

普鲁登丝重新管束住了自己。既然不让她打了，那么依着体统稍稍抗争几句，看来是再安全不过的了。「可求你们让我听听，这究竟是怎么回事呀！」她说。「我不会临阵退缩的，查尔斯，你放心。」

「打退堂鼓的可是伦斯利。」贝尔福特先生说，脸上依旧挂着那副吓人的郑重。

「算不上退堂鼓，贝尔（Bel）。你可不能说他打了退堂鼓。」德弗罗先生提出异议。

「还不是一码事，德夫。他明天没法同彼得交手了，我说这就是一记可恶的羞辱。我要去告诉杰瑟普（Jessup），咱们的人不打了。」

===Original===
“Has Rensley fled the country?” demanded Prudence.

“Worse, my dear boy!”

“Not worse, Bel! Hardly worse! Plaguey unfortunate happening.”

Mr. Belfort laid an impressive finger on Prudence’s shoulder. “He’s offered us a damned slight, Peter. It can’t be swallowed. Take my word for it, there can be no meeting.”

“Why, Charles, you mystify me! Let me know what this slight is, I beg of you.”

“He has fought another man this morning,” said Mr. Belfort, and stood back to observe the effect of this terrific pronouncement.

===Chinese===
「伦斯利逃出国境了？」普鲁登丝追问。

「比那更糟，我亲爱的伙计！」

「不是更糟，贝尔！哪儿就更糟了！不过是遭瘟的不凑巧罢了。」

贝尔福特先生把一根意味深长的手指按在普鲁登丝肩上。「他给了咱们一记混账的羞辱，彼得。这口气咽不下去。信我的话，这一场决斗是万万打不成了。」

「哎呀查尔斯，你把我弄糊涂了！这羞辱究竟是什么，求你说给我听吧。」

「他今天早上同另一个人决斗过了。」贝尔福特先生说罢退后一步，等着看这句石破天惊的宣告效果如何。

===Original===
Prudence was all honest incredulity. “You tell me he has met someone else in a duel?” she cried. It seemed to be a positive dispensation of a kindly Providence, but it would not do to let the gentleman suspect she felt this. She affected anger. “He sets me aside, you would tell me! It’s for some later quarrel? You call it a slight! You’re moderate, Charles!”

“Devilish irregular,” said Mr. Devereux. “I was monstrous shocked when I heard of it, give you my word. They say there’s a tendon cut in his sword arm that won’t heal this many a day. Quite impossible to meet him.”

“But apart from that, Dev﻿—apart from that, mind you, I would not have our man swallow such a cursed piece of rudeness,” Mr. Belfort reminded him. “Our quarrel came first, demm it!” A frown marred the cherubic look in his face. “And what’s more, Dev, Fanshawe knew it!”

“Fanshawe!” the exclamation broke from Prudence, who stood staring.

“Fanshawe himself,” nodded Belfort. “And I saw him this morning, and somehow or other the thing slipped out, and I told him you were to meet Rensley.”

“But﻿—you say Fanshawe is the man who fought Rensley?”

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝满心都是老老实实的难以置信。「你是说，他同别人另约了一场决斗？」她叫道。这简直是仁慈上苍的特意安排，只是万万不能让这两位绅士察觉她的如释重负。她装出一副恼怒的样子。「照你们的意思，他竟把我撇在了一边！就为了后来才结下的什么新仇？这还只算一记怠慢！查尔斯，你们可真宽厚啊！」

「无法无天，太没章法了。」德弗罗先生说。「刚听说这事时，我惊骇得不轻，我向你担保。据说他持剑那条胳膊里割断了一根筋腱，好多天里都长不拢。要同他交手是全然不可能的了。」

「可撇开这个不谈，德夫——你听着，撇开这个不谈，我也不愿咱们的人咽下这样一段可恶的无礼。」贝尔福特先生提醒他。「咱们的过节在先，该死的！」一道眉头拧起，破坏了他那张天使般的圆脸。「更有甚者，德夫，范肖分明知道这茬！」

「范肖！」这一声惊呼从普鲁登丝口中迸了出来，她直挺挺地站在那里发怔。

「正是范肖本人。」贝尔福特点头道。「今儿早上我见着他，不知怎么一来，话头漏了出去，我对他说了你要同伦斯利交手。」

「可是——你是说，同伦斯利决斗的就是范肖？」

===Original===
“You may well ask, Peter. Fanshawe it was. Found our man at Galliano’s, and forced a quarrel on him.”

“Carslake tells me it all began as a jest, Bel,” pleaded Mr. Devereux.

“Jest or no, Dev, the man had no business to meet Fanshawe till our little affair was settled. And so I shall tell Jessup.”

“But why did Sir Anthony﻿—?”

“Ah, that’s the question,” nodded Belfort. “I don’t know, but they do say he told Rensley he was a poltroon, and struck him in the face with his glove. Kestrel﻿—he was there, y’know﻿—will have it Tony was out for a fight from the first, but Orton thinks it all sprang up out of naught.”

An idea struck Mr. Devereux. “ ’Pon my soul, Merriot, you might call Fanshawe out, so you might!”

===Chinese===
「你这一问问得好，彼得。正是范肖。他在加利亚诺的寓所里寻着了咱们那位，硬生生把一场争端强加给了他。」

「卡斯莱克（Carslake）告诉我，这整桩事起头不过是句玩笑，贝尔。」德弗罗先生代为缓颊道。

「玩笑也罢不玩笑也罢，德夫，在咱们这桩小小的过节了结之前，那人压根儿就不该去同范肖约斗。这话我也要对杰瑟普说。」

「可安东尼爵士到底为什么——？」

「啊，问题就在这儿。」贝尔福特点头道。「我不知道，不过人家确实在说，他骂伦斯利是懦夫，还拿手套甩了他的脸。凯斯特雷尔——你知道，他当时在场——一口咬定托尼（Tony）从头就是存心寻一场架打，奥顿（Orton）却认为这一切纯属无端而起。」

德弗罗先生灵光一现。「我以灵魂起誓，梅里奥特（Merriot），你满可以去向范肖下战书，当真满可以！」

===Original===
Prudence laughed, and shook her head. “Oh, hold me excused! I count Sir Anthony very much my friend, in spite of this day’s work.”

Mr. Belfort pondered it. “I don’t see that, Dev. No, I don’t see that he can do that. But as for meeting Rensley after this, it’s not to be thought of. Mind that, Peter! Not to be thought of!”

Prudence assumed an air of hesitation, and made some demur. It seemed safe. She was sternly overruled, but Mr. Devereux said it did her credit. He went off with Mr. Belfort to wait upon Mr. Rensley’s seconds.

Prudence was left to make what she might of it. On the face of it, it looked as though the large gentleman had once more scared away the wolf. But why? That gave food for serious reflection. What did he suspect, forsooth? Or had he merely a mind to interpose on behalf of a boy for whom he had some kindness? She could not think he had pierced her disguise; faith, it was too good for that, surely! She went upstairs to Robin, and gave him the full sum of it.

Robin threw her a straight look under his lashes. “I’m to understand you had it in mind to meet Rensley with never a word to me?”

“Just, child. Don’t eat me!”

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝笑了，摇了摇头。「哦，这个我敬谢不敏！任凭他今天干的这一手，我仍把安东尼爵士（Sir Anthony）当作顶要好的朋友。」

贝尔福特先生把这话掂量了一番。「这个我看不出行，德夫。不，我看不出他能这么办。可这之后再同伦斯利交手，那是想也不用想了。记住这话，彼得！想也不用想！」

普鲁登丝装出一副迟疑的模样，推辞了几句。看来是安全的。她遭到斩钉截铁的驳回，德弗罗先生却说，这足为她的品格增光。他随贝尔福特先生一道，拜会伦斯利先生的副手们去了。

普鲁登丝被撇下来，独自咂摸这桩事。表面上看来，那位大块头绅士又是一次吓退了豺狼。可这是为什么？这可大有认真思量的余地。他究竟起了什么疑心呢？还是说，他不过是有心替一个他颇为怜爱的少年郎出头？她想不出他怎么能看穿她的伪装；说真的，她这套戏法妙到毫巅，哪儿是那么容易识破的！她上楼到罗宾那里，把事情始末原原本本告诉了他。

罗宾从睫毛底下直直地射来一眼。「我是否可以这样理解：你打算去同伦斯利交手，对我竟一个字也不透？」

「正是，孩子。可别一口吃了我！」

===Original===
“I’m more likely to beat you. You must be mad indeed!”

She perceived him to be in something of a rage, and made haste to divert him. “I’ve to thank Sir Anthony, for my deliverance. What have you to say to that?”

“You’re of opinion he has your secret? You must have been mighty indiscreet!”

“Not a whit. I’ve given not the smallest reason for him to suspect me, I swear. Unless﻿—” She broke off, frowning. “There was the little matter of staying with him at Wych End. No more.”

Robin shrugged that aside. “I hold to my opinion. But if he suspects﻿—why, it seems he’s a mind to keep his counsel.”

“It’s a comfortable belief, child. Give you joy of it. I dine with him tomorrow. Be sure, I step warily.”

===Chinese===
「我看更想揍你一顿。你准是疯了！」

她看出他正窝着一腔火，赶紧设法岔开。「我这次脱身，还得谢谢安东尼爵士。你对此有何高见？」

「你是认为，他掌握了你的秘密？那你先前必定失检得厉害！」

「一丝一毫也没有。我发誓，我没给过他半点起疑的因头。除非——」她皱着眉顿住了。「也就是在威奇恩德（Wych End）同他一起住过的那一小段。仅此而已。」

罗宾耸耸肩，把这话撇在一边。「我坚持我的看法。不过他若是起了疑——咦，看来他倒有意守口如瓶。」

「这是个叫人安稳的想法，孩子。恭喜你有这份福气。我明天还同他一起用晚餐呢。放心，我脚下步步留神。」

===Original===
In another part of the town there was a gentleman quite as shocked as Mr. Belfort over the morning’s happenings, and infinitely more enraged. Mr. Markham went off to Grosvenor Square, and found his friend Rensley abed, and very sore.

Mr. Markham broke out with a “What’s to do now, a’ God’s name?”

Rensley lay staring at the bedpost, and said only:﻿—“Fanshawe forced the quarrel on to me.”

“God’s life, were you not pledged to Merriot?”

“Oh ay, you’re mighty anxious to see him trounced, aren’t you?”

“To hell with that!” All Mr. Markham’s flattering deference towards his friend was fast departing. “Here’s Belfort and Devereux mighty haughty﻿—damme, they’ve reason!﻿—and say their man won’t fight. And Jessup and I have to make your excuses for you, and look a pair of fools! You make us ridiculous, Rensley, curse it!”

===Chinese===
在城里的另一头，也有这么一位绅士，对早晨的变故同贝尔福特先生一般震惊，怒火却不知要盛上多少倍。马卡姆先生（Mr. Markham）赶到格罗夫纳广场（Grosvenor Square），发现他的朋友伦斯利卧在床上，浑身疼得厉害。

马卡姆先生劈头就嚷：「这又是闹的哪一出，看在上帝份上？」

伦斯利躺着，两眼死盯住床柱，只说了一句：「这场争端，是范肖硬强加给我的。」

「老天在上，你不是同梅里奥特有约在先么？」

「哦是啊，你一心巴望看他挨一顿狠揍，不是么？」

「去他的！」马卡姆先生对这位朋友昔日满口的谄媚恭敬，此刻正飞快地散了个干净。「贝尔福特和德弗罗趾高气扬——妈的，人家有理由！——说他们的人不打了。害得杰瑟普和我只好替你告罪，眼睁睁做了一对傻瓜！你把咱们搞成了笑柄，伦斯利，该死！」

===Original===
Mr. Rensley received this in silence.

“Burn it, you must needs spoil all!” Markham said in disgust. “What madness took you?”

“I tell you it was forced on me!” Rensley exploded.

“Forced be damned! You were pledged to meet Merriot, and Fanshawe must have known it.”

Mr. Rensley raised himself on his sound elbow. “What, you’d have me swallow a blow in the face, would you? Ay, I make no doubt you’d take it!”

“Oh, I’ll leave you!” Markham said, and swung round on his heel.

===Chinese===
伦斯利先生默默受下了这一顿数落。

「见鬼，你偏要搅黄一切！」马卡姆厌恶地说。「你到底是犯了什么疯病？」

「我告诉你，那是强加给我的！」伦斯利炸了。

「强加个鬼！你同梅里奥特约斗在先，范肖不可能不知道。」

伦斯利先生用没受伤的那条胳膊肘撑起半身。「怎么，你要我生生咽下一记耳光，是么？好啊，不用问，换了你准会受着！」

「哦，我这就走，不奉陪了！」马卡姆说着，咯噔一个转身。

===Original===
“It’s little enough help you’ve ever been to me, sure!” sneered Rensley. “Your Munich gaming-houses!”

“It’s little help you’ll have from me in the future!” Mr. Markham cried, and left his friend fuming.

He was let out of the house by a solemn lackey, who had spent the morning discussing his master’s freak below-stairs. He walked down the steps, and became aware of a shabby gentleman, hesitating by the railings. He looked with casual interest, wondering what this individual wanted.

The shabby gentleman accosted him. “Your pardon, sir, but does my Lord Barham live here?”

Mr. Markham gave a short laugh. “There’s certainly a man within calls himself Lord Barham,” he said.

The shabby gentleman looked a little puzzled. “It’s﻿—it’s a small man, with a hook nose,” he ventured. “That’s the man I want to see, sir.”

===Chinese===
「说起来，你从前帮过我的忙实在少得可怜！」伦斯利讥笑道。「你那慕尼黑的赌场！」

「往后你也休想再从我这里得到半点帮衬！」马卡姆先生嚷道，撇下他的朋友在那儿干冒烟。

送他出宅的是一名神情肃穆的听差，这听差一上午都在楼下同人议论主人家这桩出格的变故。他走下台阶，觉察到一位衣衫寒酸的绅士正在栏杆旁踌躇。他带着三分闲散的好奇打量，纳闷此人究竟想做什么。

那寒酸绅士上前搭话。「劳驾，先生，巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）可是住在这儿？」

马卡姆先生短促地笑了一声。「这宅子里倒确实住着一个自称巴勒姆勋爵的人。」他说。

寒酸绅士显出几分困惑。「是位——是位小个子，生个鹰钩鼻。」他试探着说。「我要见的就是这位，先生。」

===Original===
Mr. Markham paused, and his eyes took in the stranger more thoroughly. There was an air of mystery about the man, and some slight savour of nervousness. If this was one of my lord’s late associates it was quite possible that something might be gathered from him of no little importance. “I’m a friend of Lord Barham,” said Markham, in a tone meant to inspire confidence. “Do me the favour of stepping up to my rooms with me.”

The stranger seemed to shrink into himself; hurriedly he declined the honour: he desired to see my lord, and none other.

Mr. Markham’s suspicions were thoroughly aroused. He took the stranger by the elbow, and abandoning the conciliatory tone, said unpleasantly:﻿—“Ay, you’re in a mighty hurry to be off, aren’t you? Now what should the likes of you have to say to Lord Barharn that no one else may hear?”

The stranger tried to break away. “Nothing, sir, I assure you! A matter for my lord’s private ear! I beg you won’t detain me.”

“Ay, but I’ve a mind to know something more of you, my friend,” said Mr. Markham, retaining his hold. “You look to me as though you have information to sell. I know something of this Barham, you see.”

The stranger disclaimed quickly, shooting a swift, scared glance up and down the road. Markham’s suspicions grew, and he drew a bow at a venture. “I believe you’re some damned Jacobite, skulking in hiding,” he said.

===Chinese===
马卡姆先生顿了顿，把来人从头到脚更仔细地打量了一遍。这人周身透着一股神秘气，还隐隐带着些紧张的意味。倘若此公是勋爵大人旧日的同伙，那么从他身上探出些分量不轻的东西来，大有可能。「我是巴勒姆勋爵的朋友。」马卡姆用一种存心叫人放心的口吻说。「赏个脸，随我上我的寓所走一遭吧。」

那生客仿佛整个人往里一缩；他慌忙辞谢了这份抬举：他要见的是勋爵大人本人，旁人一概不干。

马卡姆先生的疑心彻底被勾了起来。他一把攥住生客的胳膊肘，撇开那套哄劝的调子，恶声恶气地说：——「是啊，你急得恨不能马上脱身，是不是？倒说说，你们这号人有什么话要对巴勒姆勋爵讲，竟不能叫旁人听见？」

生客挣扎着想脱身。「没什么，先生，我向您保证！是一桩只入勋爵大人私耳的事！求您别扣住我。」

「是么，可我偏想再多知道一点你的底细，我的朋友。」马卡姆先生说，手上仍不松开。「依我看，你这副模样，像是揣着情报要卖。你要知道，这位巴勒姆的事，我略知一二。」

生客急忙否认，一边朝马路两头飞快地溜了一道惶惶的目光。马卡姆的疑心更重了，便姑且放出一箭试他一试。「我看你是个藏头露尾的詹姆士党人（Jacobite），该死的。」他说。

===Original===
There was the faintest start, and a fresh movement to be free. Markham’s grip tightened on the arm he held, and he began to walk down the square, taking the stranger with him. The stranger protested in a high voice of alarm; his vehement oaths that he was no Jacobite left Mr. Markham unmoved. Markham said:﻿—“If you’ve information for sale about Lord Barham you can go free for aught I care. If not﻿—why, we’ll see what the law will get out of you!”

The protestations died away; the stranger went sullenly beside Mr. Markham until the house where Markham lodged was reached. He was ushered into his host’s rooms, and told to sit down. On either side of the table they sat, the stranger holding his battered hat between his hands, and stealing furtive glances towards the door.

“Now then, fellow, I’m a friend of Lord Barham’s, and I’ll hear what you have to say.”

“If you’re a friend of his, you’d best let me see him,” the other said sulkily. “His lordship won’t desire to have me given up. I can tell too much.”

“Why, what should Barham care for aught you could say?”

“Ask him!” the man replied. “I’m ready to sell his lordship what I hold, but if you, who say you’re a friend of his, are fool enough to give me up, I’ll disclose all I know, and then where will his fine lordship be?”

===Chinese===
生客几不可察地一悸，又重新挣了一挣。马卡姆把攥着的那条胳膊攥得更紧，拽着生客沿广场走去。生客惊惶地尖着嗓子抗议；他慷慨激昂地赌咒发誓，说自己绝非詹姆士党人，马卡姆先生却全然不为所动。马卡姆说：——「你若真有关于巴勒姆勋爵的情报要卖，那你走你的，与我无干。若没有——哼，咱们就瞧瞧法律能从你嘴里掏出些什么！」

抗议声渐渐息了；生客闷闷地走在马卡姆先生身边，一直走到马卡姆寄宿的宅子跟前。他被引进主人的屋里，被吩咐坐下。两人隔着桌子对面而坐，生客两手捧着他那顶破帽子，眼睛不时朝门口偷偷瞟去。

「好了，汉子，我是巴勒姆勋爵的朋友，你有什么话，我听着便是。」

「你要当真是他的朋友，顶好还是让我见他。」对方绷着脸说。「勋爵大人不会愿意把我交出去的。我知道的事，太多了。」

「嗐，你能说出个什么，叫巴勒姆拿它当回事？」

「去问他呀！」那人答道。「我手里的东西，我乐意卖给勋爵大人，可你既然自称他的朋友，要是蠢到把我交出去，我就把我知道的一股脑儿抖落出来，到那时，他那位好勋爵大人还往哪儿摆？」

===Original===
“You’ll give up what you hold to me, my man.”

“If you’re a friend of his,” the stranger insisted, “you dare not hand me over to the law. Take me to Lord Barham.”

“You mistake, fellow,” said Markham cruelly. “I am a friend of the other Lord Barham.”

“I don’t know what you’re talking of. The man I mean is a little man, with bright eyes, and a soft-spoken manner. I saw him riding in a fine coach the other day, and I was told it was Lord Barham.”

In a few words Mr. Markham let him know the true state of affairs. He watched closely the effect, and saw again the furtive look around for a means of escape.

“So now, fellow, you perceive into what trap you have fallen. Faith you’re a bad plotter! I make no doubt your Barham would pay well for the information you hold, because he dare not give you up. But make you no doubt that you’ll get little enough from me. I’ve naught to fear from handing you over to the law. You deserve to hang, but I’m kind. If your information’s worth something I’ll give you twenty guineas to help you out of the country. If you’re stubborn﻿—why, we’ll see what the law-officers have to say to you.”

===Chinese===
「你手里的东西，交到我手上便是，老兄。」

「你要真是他的朋友，」生客咬定不放，「你就绝不敢把我送官。带我去见巴勒姆勋爵。」

「你弄错了，汉子。」马卡姆冷酷地说。「我是另一位巴勒姆勋爵的朋友。」

「我不知道你在说些什么。我说的那个人，是个小个子，眼睛亮亮的，说话斯斯文文。前几天我瞧见他坐着一辆讲究的马车兜风，人家告诉我，那就是巴勒姆勋爵。」

三言两语，马卡姆先生便叫他明白了事情的原委。他紧盯着这番话的效力，又看见那双贼溜溜的眼睛四下逡巡，寻思着脱身之计。

「这下你明白了，汉子，你落进了怎样一个圈套。老实说，你搞阴谋的手段可真蹩脚！我不怀疑，你那位巴勒姆会为你手里的情报出个大价钱，因为他不敢把你交出去。可你也别怀疑，从我这里你能捞到的好处少得可怜。把你送官，我没什么好怕的。你这样的本该吃绞刑，不过我心肠好。你的情报若真值几个钱，我给你二十几尼，助你滚出这个国家。你要是犯犟——哼，咱们就瞧瞧执法官们怎么发落你。」

===Original===
The stranger attempted to bluster and disclaim, but it was plain he had some fears. Mr. Markham bore with this awhile, but arose at last with a significant word of calling to his servant. Bluster turned to a whine; there was produced at last a folded letter from an inner pocket upon which Mr. Markham pounced with some eagerness.

He read some half a dozen finely inscribed lines addressed to no less a person than my Lord George Murray, concerning certain hopes of drawing in two gentlemen to the Rebellion whose names were only indicated by an initial; and came at last to the signature. The name of Colney conveyed nothing to Mr. Markham, but the stranger said sulkily:﻿—“That’s the name of the man I saw out driving. He who calls himself my Lord Barham.”

“How came you by this letter?”

The stranger said evasively that it had fallen into his hands. He saw no reason to tell Mr. Markham that he had stolen it along with some others of little or no importance, in the vague belief that they might be of use to him. Fortunately, Mr. Markham’s interest in the manner of the letter’s acquisition was but fleeting, and he inquired no further, but sat frowning down at that elegant signature.

“Can you prove this Colney to be indeed the man you say he is?”

The stranger answered in some alarm that of a certainty he could prove nothing, since he could not, for obvious reasons, come into the open. Mr. Markham’s dissatisfaction grew. “I don’t see what use this is. I believe I’d better give you up.”

===Chinese===
生客先还想虚张声势、百般抵赖，可他心里存着几分怕，那是明摆着的。马卡姆先生耐着性子由他闹了一阵，末了站起身来，意味深长地吐出一句要唤他的仆人来。虚声恫吓顿时变成了哼哼哀告；生客终于从贴身口袋里掏出一封折好的信，马卡姆先生相当急切地一把抢了过去。

信上是五六行字迹娟秀的话，抬头不是别人，正是乔治·默里勋爵（Lord George Murray），内容是拉拢两位绅士投身叛乱的若干指望，两人的姓名只以一个首字母暗示；他终于读到了落款。科尔尼（Colney）这个名字没让马卡姆先生得出任何头绪，生客却绷着脸说：——「这就是我那天瞧见出门兜风的那人的名字。那个自称巴勒姆勋爵的。」

「这封信怎么落到你手里的？」

生客支支吾吾地说，它是偶然落到自己手里的。他曾偷了这封信，连同另外几封不甚要紧的，模模糊糊指望着它们日后或能派上用场——这个缘故，他看不出有什么要告诉马卡姆先生的。所幸马卡姆先生对信的来路只是一时兴起的兴趣，转瞬即逝，没有再追问，只坐在那里，皱着眉端详那个风雅的签名。

「你能不能证明，这个科尔尼当真是你所说的那个人？」

生客颇为惊慌地回答说，他自然什么都证明不了，因为——不言自明的原因——他不能抛头露面。马卡姆先生的不满愈发重了。「我看这东西顶什么用。我看我还是把你交出去的好。」

===Original===
It was pointed out to him, with some haste, that an inquiry set on foot regarding the movements of Mr. Colney must inevitably lead to the present claimant of the Barham title. Mr. Markham sat pondering it, and began to see his way. The letter was hidden away in a pocket and twenty guineas changed hands. Mr. Markham thought it would be as well for the stranger to leave the country: he preferred that no one save himself should know of this letter. There was some expostulation and some tearful pleading, but upon fresh mention of the law-officers the stranger took a hurried leave of his host, and left him to his reflections.

These were weighty enough. It was Mr. Markham’s first impulse to go with the document to Rensley, who had said he would give as much as ten thousand pounds to the man who should expose his cousin. But Rensley was a mean dog: there could be no trusting him. Rensley had, moreover, used some very cutting expressions to his friend lately; there had been a falling out, and it would not be displeasing to Mr. Markham to do his friend Rensley an ill turn.

His thoughts came round to another market for his document. Ecod, he had naught against the pseudo-Lord Barham, and his lordship seemed to be an openhanded old gentleman. Of a surety it would not do to act precipitately in this matter, but the alternative of taking his document to Barham must be fully considered. Rensley would be bound to pay well for this precious paper, but would not my lord be bound to pay better? This led to a fresh thought: once sold to Rensley, there was an end to the business, so far as Mr. Markham was concerned. But he could dimly perceive years ahead if he made Barham his man. Barham would buy the paper, true, but even that would not make Barham feel quite safe. He could surely be further bled on fear of disclosure. True there could be nothing proved without written evidence, but Mr. Markham could imagine that even a verbal accusation with the paper safely destroyed would prove mighty uncomfortable for my lord. Mr. Markham believed that he might easily hold my lord under his thumb for a good many years to come. He licked his lips at this thought, but decided that the thing must be earnestly weighed. He would sleep on it.

===Chinese===
对方连忙向他指出：但凡着手去查科尔尼先生的行踪，就必然查到如今主张巴勒姆头衔的这位请求人头上。马卡姆先生坐着盘算了一阵，渐渐看出了一条道儿。那封信被藏进口袋，二十几尼也换了主人。马卡姆先生想，这生客还是离开这个国家为好：这封信，他但愿除自己之外再无人知晓。生客又抗争了一番，又眼泪汪汪地哀告了一阵，可执法官三个字再度一提，他便匆匆向主人告辞，撇下他独自去想心事了。

这心事分量着实不轻。马卡姆先生的头一个冲动，是揣上这份文书去找伦斯利——此人说过，谁若揭穿他的堂亲，他肯出到一万英镑。可伦斯利是条卑劣的狗：万万信不得。何况伦斯利近来对他的朋友用过些极刻薄的话；两人已然反了目，给他的朋友伦斯利暗中使个绊子，马卡姆先生只会觉得称心。

他的念头又转到了这份文书的另一个买主身上。乖乖，他同那位冒牌的巴勒姆勋爵无冤无仇，况且这位勋爵大人瞧着还是位出手阔绰的老先生。诚然，此事不宜操之过急，可把文书送到巴勒姆手里这另一条路，总得细细掂量。伦斯利为这张宝贝纸片固然肯出大价钱，可勋爵大人岂不注定肯出得更多？这一想又引出一层新思：一旦卖给了伦斯利，这桩买卖对马卡姆先生来说便一了百了。可若把巴勒姆攥成自己的人，他隐约望见的前头便是好些个年头。巴勒姆自会把纸片买下，不错，可即便如此，巴勒姆也未必觉得稳当。只消拿告发二字悬着，往后准还能接着榨他的血。不错，没有字据，什么也证明不了；可马卡姆先生想得出来，哪怕把纸片毁得干干净净，单凭一张口头指控，也够叫勋爵大人老大不自在的。马卡姆先生相信，把勋爵大人捏在自己拇指底下，轻轻松松便能捏上好些年。想到这里他咂了咂嘴唇，却又拿定主意，此事必须郑重权衡。且睡上一夜，再作计较。

## V Sir Humphrey Grayson Waits Upon Mr. Merriot / V 汉弗莱·格雷森爵士拜访梅里奥特先生

===Original===
The morrow brought Sir Humphrey Grayson early in the forenoon to wait upon Mr. Merriot. The message was brought Prudence in my Lady Lowestoft’s boudoir, where she sat in converse with her hostess. The exigencies of his toilet still kept Robin above stairs; his sister had left him to the lacing of his corsets, an operation conducted by John and accompanied by some of the young gentleman’s choicer oaths.

My lady, upon the news of Sir Humphrey’s call being brought, was all agog with curiosity. She had no notion the Merriots held other acquaintance than herself in town, and desired to be told how they were known to Sir Humphrey, who, to be sure, led something of the life of a recluse.

Prudence mentally consigned Sir Humphrey to perdition: it seemed he would be an added complication. The fewer people to know of Miss Letty’s escapade the better for that sprightly lady, but Prudence reflected that there were mysteries and secrets of her own enough to keep close without the addition of another’s. She evaded my lady’s questions. She claimed no acquaintance with Sir Humphrey, but believed Sir Anthony Fanshawe had solicited his kindness on her behalf. My lady was left to make what she could of this; Prudence went downstairs to the room looking out on to the street that was used for morning callers.

There arose at her entrance a tall thin gentleman with stooping shoulders and a limp. He wore the powdered wig of Fashion, but neglected to paint his face. The brown eyes, not unlike Miss Letty’s own, held some trouble. He had the look of a man prematurely aged by ill-health.
===Chinese===
次日一大早，汉弗莱·格雷森爵士（Sir Humphrey Grayson）便前来拜访梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）。口信送到时，普鲁登丝（Prudence）正坐在洛斯托夫特夫人（my Lady Lowestoft）的小客厅里陪女主人闲话。罗宾（Robin）还被梳妆这道必要工序拘在楼上：姐姐把他撂给了系紧身胸衣的活计——这道工序由约翰（John）操办，还伴着这位年轻先生几句格外精选的咒骂。

汉弗莱爵士到访的消息一送到，夫人顿时好奇心大发。她全然想不到梅里奥特家的人在城里除了她还另有相识，便央着人家告诉她，他们是怎么认识汉弗莱爵士的——要知道，这位爵士过的多少算是半隐居的日子。

普鲁登丝在心里把汉弗莱爵士咒下了地狱：看来他又要平添一重乱子。知道莱蒂小姐（Miss Letty）那桩荒唐事的人越少，对那位活泼的姑娘越有好处；可普鲁登丝转念一想，她自己那一堆谜团和秘密就已经够守口如瓶的了，实在犯不着再添上别人的一桩。她把夫人的盘问搪塞了过去。她自称与汉弗莱爵士素不相识，不过猜想许是安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）替她在爵士面前说了好话。夫人只好自行琢磨这套说辞；普鲁登丝则下楼进了那间临街、专用来接待上午来访客人的屋子。

她一进门，一位瘦高的绅士便起身相迎，只见他双肩佝偻，走路微跛。他戴着一顶时兴的扑粉假发，脸上却不曾涂粉施朱。那双棕色的眼睛——与莱蒂小姐的那双颇有几分相像——含着几分忧色。他一脸久病早衰的模样。

===Original===
The gentleman bowed to Mr. Merriot, leaning the while on his cane. Mr. Merriot returned the bow and was swift to pull forward a chair for the visitor. “Sir Humphrey Grayson, I believe? Sir, you honour me. Will you not be seated?”

A certain grimness about Sir Humphrey’s mouth vanished as his glance took in Mr. Merriot. The young gentleman had a great air of Fashion, but practised what Sir Humphrey had come to believe an old-fashioned courtesy towards the elder generation. He took the chair offered, with a passing reference to a gouty foot. There was a slight squaring of the bent shoulders: it was evident this elderly gentleman had little relish for his visit. “Mr. Merriot, I believe you must know the reason of my being here,” he said bluntly. “Let me be plain with you. My daughter has put me in your debt.”

A stiff-backed old man; one must perforce pity the hurt to his pride. Prudence made swift answer. “Why, sir, I protest, there is no need for such talk! Do me the favour of letting a very trifling service be forgot!”

There were further signs of thaw. “Bear with me, Mr. Merriot. You must do me the honour of accepting my very heartfelt thanks for your rescue of my daughter.”

“Why, sir, there is nothing to all this. My part was played but a bare half-hour before Sir Anthony came upon us. He would have settled the business as quickly had I let be. Pray let us not speak of it! I am happy to have been of service to Miss Grayson. Or thank my sister, sir, whose quicker wits devised the little plot.”
===Chinese===
那位绅士拄着手杖，向梅里奥特先生欠身一礼。梅里奥特先生还了一礼，忙不迭为访客把椅子拉上前来。「想必这位就是汉弗莱·格雷森爵士了？爵士，您肯光临，实在是抬举我了。您不请坐么？」

汉弗莱爵士唇边那抹冷硬之色，在目光扫过梅里奥特先生时消散了。这位年轻绅士一身十足的时髦气派，对长者却行着一套汉弗莱爵士渐已认作老派的谦恭礼数。他在让出的椅子上落了座，顺口提了一句自己那只得痛风的脚。佝偻的双肩微微一挺：显然，这位老先生对这趟造访提不起多少兴致。「梅里奥特先生，想必您知道我为何登门。」他直截了当地说。「容我跟你打开天窗说亮话。小女让我欠下了你的恩情。」

好一位脊背僵直的老人；他这份自尊所受的创伤，着实叫人不能不同情。普鲁登丝敏捷地答道：「哎呀爵士，您何出此言！区区一件微不足道的小事，就请赏脸让它过去了吧！」

坚冰又消了几分。「请容我这一回，梅里奥特先生。你搭救了小女，务请赏光，接受我发自肺腑的谢意。」

「哎呀爵士，这实在算不得什么。我演的那点戏码，前后不过半个钟头，安东尼爵士便赶到了。倘若我不插手，他照样能一口气了结此事。求您千万别再提了！能为格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson）效劳，我深感荣幸。要谢就谢我妹妹吧，爵士，是她的急智想出了那条小计策。」

===Original===
Sir Humphrey permitted himself to smile, and to incline his head. “I do indeed desire to render my thanks to Miss Merriot. My foolish daughter can talk of naught else but that same plot. At least allow me to compliment you on a tricksy piece of swordplay.”

Prudence gave her rich chuckle. “An old ruse, sir, but useful. I trust Miss Grayson finds herself none the worse for her adventure?”

“Rest assured, sir, my daughter is incorrigible.” But a reluctant smile went with the words.

“Why, sir, it’s a child, after all, with a child’s desire for a romantic venture.”

“It might have led to a most damaging scandal, Mr. Merriot.”
===Chinese===
汉弗莱爵士这才容自己露出一笑，颔首致意。「我的确切望向梅里奥特小姐当面道谢。我家那傻姑娘旁的一概不谈，满口都是那同一条计策。至少，请容我夸你一句，好一手机巧的剑法。」

普鲁登丝发出她那串爽朗的低笑。「不过是条老掉牙的计策罢了，爵士，倒还管用。但愿格雷森小姐经过这场历险，安好如初？」

「放一百个心，先生，小女是无药可救了。」话虽如此，一丝不由自主的笑意却随话浮了上来。

「哎呀爵士，她到底还是个孩子，怀着孩子家对浪漫奇遇的渴望罢了。」

「那可是险些酿成一桩害人不浅的大丑闻哪，梅里奥特先生。」

===Original===
Prudence discerned some anxiety in Sir Humphrey’s eye, and made haste to reassure. “All fear of that must be at an end with you, sir; of that I am certain. None save Sir Anthony and ourselves can know aught of the matter.”

There was again a bow. “My daughter should count herself fortunate in meeting so discreet a friend in her trouble,” said Sir Humphrey.

This punctilious grandeur became oppressive. Prudence conceived the happy thought of sending a message up to Robin. Sir Humphrey professed himself all desire to lay his compliments before Miss Merriot. Black Pompey was sent running to Robin’s chamber, and in a little while Robin came, all powdered and patched and scented; a fair vision in pale blue taffety. No girl, Prudence thought, could appear lovelier.

There was a curtsey, a few gliding steps towards Sir Humphrey, and a delicate hand held out. Sir Humphrey bowed low over it, and a faint crease crept between Prudence’s brows. It seemed to her unseemly that the old courtier should kiss her graceless brother’s hand. She met Robin’s dancing eyes of mischief with a look of some reproof. Robin sank into a chair with a billow of stiff silks. “Sir Humphrey, this is too kind in you, I protest! Miss Letty spoke of your love of seclusion. There was no need for this visit. No, no, sir, you shall not thank me for the other night’s work!” A fan was spread, but a laughing pair of eyes showed above it. “Spare my blushes, sir! Conceive me fainting in the arms of the Markham! Oh lud!”
===Chinese===
普鲁登丝瞧出汉弗莱爵士眼里含着几分焦灼，忙宽慰道：「这方面的忧惧，您尽可以到此为止了，爵士；这一点我敢担保。除了安东尼爵士和我们几个，此事再无一人能够知晓分毫。」

又是一躬。「小女遭难之际，能遇上这样一位谨慎周到的朋友，实在该知足了。」汉弗莱爵士说。

这般一丝不苟的堂皇做派渐渐叫人吃不消。普鲁登丝灵机一动，想出往楼上给罗宾递话的好主意。汉弗莱爵士忙不迭表白，自己一心想向梅里奥特小姐当面致意。黑仆庞培（Black Pompey）被打发去罗宾的房间跑腿，不多时罗宾便来了，发上扑了粉，脸上贴了俏斑，周身熏了香；一身淡蓝塔夫绸，好一位姣好的人儿。普鲁登丝心想，天底下没有哪个姑娘能比这更标致了。

先是一个屈膝礼，又滑着碎步向汉弗莱爵士趋近几步，伸出一只纤纤素手。汉弗莱爵士深深俯首去吻那只手，普鲁登丝的眉心却悄悄蹙起一道细纹。在她看来，这位老廷臣竟去吻她那顽劣弟弟的手，实在不成体统。她迎上罗宾那双顾盼闪亮的促狭眼睛，递过去几分责备的眼色。罗宾携着一波挺括绸裙的浪涌，袅袅坐进椅中。「汉弗莱爵士，您也忒客气了，叫我怎么当得起！莱蒂小姐还说起过您深居简出的习性呢。您原不必屈尊来这一趟的。不，不，爵士，那天晚上的事，您可千万别谢我！」折扇刷地展开，扇沿上方却露出一双盈盈的笑眼。「饶了我这点脸面吧，爵士！想想看，我竟晕倒在那位马卡姆（Markham）先生的怀里！哎呀老天！」

===Original===
Prudence might retire into the background; Robin had the situation well in hand. She sat down on the window seat, and was at leisure to admire her brother’s adroitness. For some reason he seemed bent upon the captivation of Sir Humphrey. Prudence could guess the reason. Faith, more complications brewing. But it was unseemly again that Robin should ogle so elderly a gentleman. Lord, what a clever tongue the child had!

Indeed, talk ran merrily between the two in the middle of the room. Robin seemed to have the knack of inducing a stiff-necked sire to unbend. Within ten minutes he might count Sir Humphrey very much his friend, and dare even to touch lightly on the subject of Miss Letitia’s indiscretion. There came no rebuff: only a word or two sufficient to show the worried state of Sir Humphrey’s mind.

Robin put by the fan of painted chicken-skin. With a pretty air of coaxing and of deference he cooed softly: “An impertinence in me to speak of the matter at all, dear Sir Humphrey. Forgive me!” He was assured of Sir Humphrey’s forgiveness, nay, more, of his attention. “Well, well!” Madam Kate smiled confidentially upon him. “I own to some few years more than the child can boast, I believe. Perhaps I may whisper a word or two.”
===Chinese===
普鲁登丝乐得退到后头去；这场面罗宾拿捏得稳稳当当。她在窗座上坐下，从从容容地欣赏起弟弟的机巧来。不知怎的，他像是打定了主意要迷住汉弗莱爵士。个中缘由，普鲁登丝猜得出几分。说真的，又有乱子在酝酿了。只是罗宾冲着这么大年纪的一位绅士抛起媚眼来，同样不成体统。老天，这孩子一张嘴可真真厉害！

果然，屋子当中那两个人谈得兴高采烈。罗宾似乎自有诀窍，能哄得一位倔头倔脑的老父卸下架子。不出十分钟，他已可把汉弗莱爵士算作要好的朋友，甚至敢轻轻触碰一下莉蒂西娅（Letitia）小姐轻率行径这个话头。倒也没挨抢白：只一两句话，便足以显出汉弗莱爵士心里忧烦到了什么地步。

罗宾收起那柄彩绘鸡皮折扇，带着三分撒娇、七分恭谨的俏模样，柔声细气地说道：「这件事本就轮不到我多嘴，亲爱的汉弗莱爵士。恕我冒失！」他随即得到保证：汉弗莱爵士不怪罪他，非但如此，还全神贯注了起来。「哎呀，哎呀！」凯特小姐（Madam Kate）含着会心的微笑望向他。「我想，自己总比那孩子痴长几岁。兴许我能凑近耳边进上一两句忠言。」

===Original===
Sir Humphrey begged the favour of Miss Merriot’s advice. Prudence, by the window, was forgot. There was no doing anything; she could but sit by while Robin became as outrageous as the fit prompted him. Lud, but they were plunging deeper and deeper into the morass!

Robin was dropping dulcet words of advice into a father’s ear. Let him not coop Miss Letty up so close; sure, it was a high-spirited child only in need of a little amusement. Too young?﻿—Oh, fie, never think it! Take her out into the world; let her make her curtsey to Society. By no means take her back to Gloucestershire; that were fatal. So it went on somewhat to Prudence’s amusement. Robin had a mind to pursue the acquaintance, then? Snared by a pair of pansy-brown eyes, ecod!

The amusement fled before the next words. Sir Humphrey made bold to solicit Miss Merriot’s kindness for his daughter. His sister was, perhaps, not an enlivening companion for so frivolous a child as his Letitia. He should think himself more than ever in her debt if Miss Merriot would take Letty a little under her wing.
===Chinese===
汉弗莱爵士恳请梅里奥特小姐不吝赐教。坐在窗边的普鲁登丝被忘了个干净。事已至此，无可奈何，她只得坐在一旁，眼看罗宾兴之所至，愈发口没遮拦。老天，他们可正往那片泥潭里越陷越深哪！

罗宾正把甜滋滋的劝告往一位父亲的耳朵里滴。别把莱蒂小姐关得那么严实；说真的，那不过是个心气活泼的孩子，只需要一点儿消遣罢了。年纪太轻？——呸，快别这么想！领她出来见见世面；让她向上流社会行屈膝礼正式亮相。千万莫带她回格洛斯特郡（Gloucestershire）去；那可就是致命的了。这般絮絮说下去，倒叫普鲁登丝看得有几分好笑。这么说，罗宾是有意把这份结交续下去了？被一双三色堇般的棕色眼睛勾住了魂儿，乖乖！

下一句话把这点好笑驱得无影无踪。汉弗莱爵士大着胆子，请求梅里奥特小姐对他的女儿多加垂怜。孩子的姑母——他自己的胞妹——兴许算不上个有趣的伴儿，陪不了他家莉蒂西娅这样轻浮贪玩的孩子。倘若梅里奥特小姐肯把莱蒂收在翼下略加照拂，他更要觉得自己欠下天大的人情了。

===Original===
“Now how to escape that?” thought Prudence.

But it seemed that Robin had no desire to escape the imposition. There were professions of the utmost willingness; he pledged himself to wait upon Miss Grayson the very next day.

“The rogue!” thought Prudence, and said it aloud as soon as Sir Humphrey had taken his ceremonious leave of them.
===Chinese===
「这下可怎么脱身？」普鲁登丝暗想。

可罗宾似乎压根儿无意推脱这桩强加的差事。他满口万般情愿；还一口应承下，明日一早就去拜访格雷森小姐。

「这个小滑头！」普鲁登丝心里想着；汉弗莱爵士一对他们这儿郑重其事地告辞离去，她便把这话说了出口。

===Original===
Robin laughed, and dropped a mock curtsey. Surely the devil was in the boy today.

“Lord, child, let us be serious. What are you pledged to now?”

“To be a friend to the little dark beauty. I’m all alacrity.”

“It’s evident.” His sister spoke dryly. “I believe it won’t serve, Robin.”

Robin raised one mobile eyebrow. “What’s this? You’ve nervous qualms, my Peter? Faith, I thought there were no nerves in you. I stand in no danger of discovery that I can see.”
===Chinese===
罗宾哈哈一笑，行了个假模假式的屈膝礼。今儿这小子准是魔鬼附了体。

「老天，孩子，咱们说点正经的吧。你这回应承下什么差事了？」

「做那位小黑美人的朋友呀。我满心踊跃着呢。」

「这我看得出来。」姐姐的口气干巴巴的。「我看这行不通，罗宾。」

罗宾挑起一边灵活的眉毛。「这是怎么说？你害起怯来了，我的彼得（Peter）？说真的，我还当你浑身上下没有一根神经呢。依我看，我丝毫没有败露的危险。」

===Original===
“None, child. You’re incomparable,” Prudence said frankly. “You’ve more female graces than ever I could lay claim to, even in my rightful petticoats. I believe my sense of propriety is offended.”

Came a flash into the blue eyes, and a head thrown up a little. “Oh, do you doubt me? *Merci du compliment!*”

Prudence was unmoved. “Ay, that’s the old gentleman in you. It’s a fine gesture.”

The chin came down; the mouth tightened a moment, then relaxed into a laugh. “You’d enrage a saint, Prue. Well, let us have it.”

Memories of the night’s reflections chased one another across Prudence’s mind. “It’s trickery. You become an impostor.”
===Chinese===
「没有危险，孩子。你是举世无双。」普鲁登丝坦言道，「你这一身女儿家的风姿，比我穿回自己名正言顺的裙装时还要多。我看哪，是我的体统之心受了冒犯。」

那双蓝眼睛里倏地闪过一道光，脑袋微微一扬。「噢，你竟信不过我？多谢你这番夸奖了！」

普鲁登丝不为所动。「是啊，你身上那位老先生的劲儿又冒上来了。好一记漂亮姿态。」

下巴落了下来；嘴抿紧了一瞬，随即松开，化为一笑。「你真能把圣人气得暴跳如雷，普鲁。好吧，说来听听。」

昨夜种种思虑的记忆，在普鲁登丝脑中你追我赶地翻涌。「这是欺诈。你正在变成一个骗子。」

===Original===
“I became one when I entered first into these damned uncomfortable clothes, child. Are you answered?”

Impossible to put those hazy ideas into verbal form. “I suppose so,” said Prudence slowly. “Do you know, I begin to dislike myself?”

Robin looked at her, then put an arm about her waist. “Well, say the word. I’ll take you to France, and we’ll ha’ done with all this.”

“You’re a dear, Robin. No, I chose this road, and we’ll stay.”

“I’ve a notion it may lead to some end. Play it out, my dear. Trickery it is, but we harm none.” Prudence looked sceptical. “Oh, you are thinking of the Grayson child! Never doubt me.”
===Chinese===
「打我头一回套上这身该死又难受的衣裳起，我就已经是个骗子了，孩子。这下你可得到答复了？」

那些朦朦胧胧的想法，实在没法变成言语。「大概是吧。」普鲁登丝缓缓说道，「你可知道，我都开始讨厌我自己了？」

罗宾望了望她，随后伸臂揽住她的腰。「这样吧，你发句话。我这就带你去法国，咱们把这一切统统撂开手。」

「你真是个贴心人，罗宾。不，这条路是我自己挑的，咱们就待下去。」

「我有种预感，这条路兴许能通向个什么结局。把它演到底吧，亲爱的。说它是欺诈也无妨，可咱们不害人。」普鲁登丝一脸狐疑。「噢，你是在惦记格雷森家那孩子！千万别信不过我。」

===Original===
“I don’t doubt you. But she thinks you are a woman, and there are things she may say you should not be hearing.”

“Do you think I cannot stop her? ’Tis I shall lead the talk. Be at rest, Prue.”

“And if she discovers the truth?”

“I don’t fear that.”

There seemed no more to be said. “We brave it to the end, then. Well, I’m content.”
===Chinese===
「我不是信不过你。可她当你是位女子，有些话她兴许会说出口，却是你不该听的。」

「你当我拦不住她么？话头自有我来引着。把心搁回肚子里吧，普鲁。」

「那她若是发现了真相呢？」

「这我不怕。」

看来再没有什么可说的了。「那咱们就硬着头皮撑到底吧。好吧，我心甘情愿。」

## X Sudden and Startling Appearance of the Old Gentleman / X 老先生的突然现身

===Original===
At Richmond, in the pleasant house with its gardens running down to the river, Sir Anthony was one of my Lady Lowestoft’s visitors. He rode out to pay a morning call, and was fortunate enough to find my lady and her two guests at home.

Sir Anthony indicated Prudence’s stiff shoulder with a movement of his quizzing-glass. “So you must needs go brawling about our streets, little man?”

There was a quick contraction of Robin’s brows. He looked up to find the sleepy gaze upon him, and straightway achieved a shudder. “Oh pray, sir, don’t speak of it!”

“You should keep him closer tied to your apron strings, ma’am,” said Sir Anthony, and began to talk of the state of the roads. But upon my lady’s going out of the room he broke off to say: “Have you any idea that it was Markham’s men set upon you, young man?”

===Chinese===
在里士满那座宜人的宅子里——花园一路漫到河边——安东尼·范肖爵士是洛斯托夫特夫人的访客之一。他骑马出城来做一次晨间拜访，运气颇佳，正赶上夫人和她的两位客人都在家。

安东尼爵士用单片眼镜朝普鲁登丝僵直的肩膀一指。「怎么，偏要跑到我们的街上去打架生事么，小个子？」

罗宾的眉头倏地一蹙。他抬起眼来，正撞上那副惺忪的睡眼落在他身上，当即打了个寒噤。「哎呀求您了，先生，快别提这事！」

「您该把他拴在您的围裙带子上，拴得再紧些，夫人。」安东尼爵士说罢，便谈起路况来。可等夫人一走出房门，他便断了话头：「你可曾想到，暗算你的是马卡姆的人，年轻人？」

===Original===
“Some little suspicion of it,” Prudence admitted. “I shall be more wary in the future.”

“It’s a vengeful creature.” Sir Anthony crossed one leg over the other. “I believe you would do well not to go abroad unaccompanied at night,” he said, and fell to twirling his eyeglass by its ribbon.

He presently took leave of them, and rode off back to town. Robin said with a laugh: “Oh, it’s all solicitude! The benevolent mammoth!”

“Lord, must you still be jeering!” Prudence demanded and left him rather abruptly.

===Chinese===
「多少有些疑心。」普鲁登丝承认道。「往后我会多加提防。」

「那是个记仇的角色。」安东尼爵士翘起二郎腿。「依我看，你夜里最好莫要独自出门。」他说着，捻着绶带转起了眼镜。

不多时他便向众人告辞，骑马回城去了。罗宾笑道：「嗬，全是关怀体贴！好一头仁慈的猛犸！」

「天哪，你还非得这么冷嘲热讽不可么！」普鲁登丝呵斥一句，颇突兀地丢下他走了。

===Original===
They returned to Arlington Street at the end of the week, arriving on the day of her Grace of Queensberry’s rout, whither they were bidden. They went in state in my lady’s town chariot, and my lady regaled them on the way with some highly entertaining details of my Lord March’s private life.

Her Grace’s salons were large enough to accommodate even the crowd that assembled at her house that evening. There were bright lights in sparkling chandeliers, and many heavy scented flowers, and over all the hum of gay chatter. Her Grace stood at the head of the stairs to receive her guests, and had the felicity of knowing that my Lord March, her son, was adorning the rout with his unaccustomed presence.

My lord was in excellent spirits, and stayed for at least an hour in the big withdrawing rooms. After having done his duty there so nobly, he retired to the cardrooms for a spell, in search of a little relaxation.

Robin’s elderly admirer found him out, and showed an ardent desire to know more of him. Prudence left him, murmuring compliments into one bashful ear.

===Chinese===
周末他们回到阿灵顿街，抵达那日恰逢昆斯伯里公爵夫人（Duchess of Queensberry）殿下开盛大晚会，他们也在受邀之列。他们坐进夫人的自备马车，摆足排场地前往；一路上，夫人拿马奇勋爵私生活里的几桩趣闻细节给他们助兴。

殿下的厅堂够宽敞，纵然那晚群集她府上的大批宾客也尽容得下。晶莹的枝形吊灯上灯火煌煌，浓香馥郁的花卉摆了许多，满堂之上是一派欢声絮语的嗡鸣。殿下立在楼梯口迎客，并有幸得知，她的儿子马奇勋爵竟一反常态地亲临，为这场晚会增色。

马奇勋爵兴致极佳，在大客厅里足足流连了一个钟头。在那里如此漂亮地尽了义务之后，他便退入牌室小待一阵，寻些松快。

罗宾那位上了年纪的倾慕者把他寻了出来，热切地表示渴望对他多些了解。普鲁登丝丢下他走了，临去还对着一只害臊的耳朵低声奉承了几句。

===Original===
It was quite late in the evening when there came a slight stir about the doorway, and Prudence had returned to Robin’s side, ousting the elderly beau. She stood now behind his chair, Sir Raymond Orton a few paces from her, and my Lady Lowestoft, laughing immoderately at something Mr. Selwyn was saying to her, not far distant.

Some late comer, it appeared, was arriving; a knot of ladies gathered near the door gave way, and Prudence could enjoy a clear view.

Two gentlemen came in, and stood for a moment looking round. One of these was my Lord March; the other was a slight, elderly gentleman with arresting grey eyes, a nose inclined to be aquiline, and thin, smiling lips. He was magnificently attired in puce satin, with embroidered waistcoat. His wig must surely have come straight from Paris; his shoes, with their jewelled buckles, had preposterous high red heels to them; the cut of his coat spoke the most fashionable tailor of the day in every line. There was the hint of a diamond in the lace at his throat, and on his breast he displayed several scintillating foreign orders. He stood very much at his ease, his head slightly inclined to hear what my Lord March was saying, and one thin white hand delicately raising a pinch of snuff to a finely chiselled nostril.

Prudence’s hand found Robin’s shoulder, and gripped hard. Robin looked up, and she felt him stiffen.

===Chinese===
夜已颇深，门口起了一阵轻微的骚动，此时普鲁登丝已回到罗宾身边，把那位老纨绔挤到了一旁。她此刻立在他椅后，雷蒙德·奥顿爵士离她数步之遥；不远处，洛斯托夫特夫人正被塞尔温先生（Mr. Selwyn）说的什么话逗得笑不可抑。

看来是有位迟来的客人驾到；聚在门边的一小簇女士让开了去，普鲁登丝得以将门口望个分明。

两位先生走了进来，站定片刻，环顾四周。其中一位是马奇勋爵；另一位是位清瘦的老先生，生着一双摄人的灰眼睛，一只略带鹰钩的鼻子，两片含笑的薄唇。他衣冠华贵，一身紫褐色的缎面，配一件绣花马甲。他的假发必是直接从巴黎运来的；他的鞋缀着珠宝鞋扣，红鞋跟高得离谱；他外套的剪裁，每一道线条都在诉说当世最时髦的裁缝。喉头花边里隐约闪着一粒钻石，胸前则佩着几枚熠熠生辉的外国勋章。他神态从容之至地立着，微微侧首听马奇勋爵说话，一只瘦削的白手优雅地将一撮鼻烟送向雕刻般精致的鼻孔。

普鲁登丝的手寻到罗宾的肩头，狠狠攥紧。罗宾抬起头来，她觉出他整个人都僵了。

===Original===
The old gentleman’s eyes travelled slowly round the room the while he listened to my Lord March; rested a moment on Miss Merriot’s face, and passed on. Her Grace of Queensberry came forward to welcome the newcomer, and he bent with great courtliness over her hand.

Robin turned in his chair. “I am dreaming. I must be dreaming. Even he could not dare﻿—”

Prudence was shaking with suppressed laughter. “Oh, it’s the old gentleman himself, never fear! Lud, might we not have expected something after this fashion?”

“Arm-in-arm with March﻿—covered with jewels﻿—all his misbegotten orders﻿—gad, it beats all! And who the devil does he pretend to be now?” Robin sat fuming; he could not admire this last freak of his sire. “Of course, we’re sped now,” he said in a voice of gloomy conviction. “This will land us all at Tyburn.”

===Chinese===
老先生一边听马奇勋爵说话，一边缓缓环视满堂；目光在梅里奥特小姐脸上略停一停，便移了过去。昆斯伯里公爵夫人上前欢迎这位新客，他极有宫廷气度地俯身吻了她的手。

罗宾在椅中转过身来。「我在做梦。我准是在做梦。就算是他，也不敢——」

普鲁登丝憋着笑，浑身直抖。「嗐，正是老先生本人，别怕！哎呀老天，咱们难道还料不到会是这么一出么？」

「跟马奇挽着胳膊——浑身珠光宝气——挂满他那些来路不正的勋章——天哪，这可真是盖了帽了！他这会儿又见鬼地冒充起谁来？」罗宾气鼓鼓地坐着；他父亲大人这最新一出胡闹，他实在无从欣赏。「当然，我们这下全栽了，」他用一种阴郁而笃定的声气说。「这会把我们统统送到泰伯恩去。」

===Original===
“Oh, my dear, he’s incomparable! You have to admit it.” Prudence saw Mr. Molyneux advancing, and hailed him. “Pray, sir, who is the magnificent stranger but just arrived?”

“What, don’t you know?” cried Mr. Molyneux, shocked. “Ah, to be sure, you’ve been out of town this last week. That stranger is the greatest romance we’ve known since Peterson ran off with Miss Carslake.” He laughed at Robin. “All the ladies are in ecstasies over it, I assure you. It appears, you see, that the grand gentleman is the lost Viscount. One thought such things only happened in fairy tales.”

Robin sank back in his chair; seeing him incapable of speech Prudence said faintly: “Indeed, sir? And﻿—and who is the lost Viscount?”

“Fie, fie, what ignorance! And the thing’s the jest of town!﻿—but you have been at Richmond: I forget that. Why, none but Tremaine, my dear boy, of course!﻿—Tremaine of Barham! Surely you must know that!”

===Chinese===
「嗐，亲爱的，他真是无与伦比！你不得不认。」普鲁登丝望见莫利纽克斯先生走近，便招呼他。「请问先生，方才到的那位气派生客是哪一位？」

「怎么，你们不知道？」莫利纽克斯先生大感意外地叫道。「啊，对了，你们上礼拜不在城里。那位生客，是自打彼得森（Peterson）携卡斯莱克小姐（Miss Carslake）私奔以来我们见识过的最大一桩韵事。」他冲着罗宾发笑。「我向你担保，女士们个个为之如痴如狂。你瞧，据说这位气派的先生便是那位失踪的子爵。谁都以为这种事只有童话里才有呢。」

罗宾瘫回椅中；见他已然语塞，普鲁登丝有气无力地说：「当真么，先生？那——那失踪的子爵又是谁？」

「啧啧，何等地孤陋寡闻！这事可是全城的笑谈！——不过你们一直待在里士满：我忘了这一层。嗐，除了特雷梅因还能是谁，我亲爱的孩子，当然！——巴勒姆的特雷梅因！这个你们总该如何道了吧！」

===Original===
Some dim recollection of my Lady Lowestoft’s talk flitted across Prudence’s memory. “I didn’t know there was a lost Tremaine, sir,” she said.

“Good Gad, not know of the Barham claim?” This was Mr. Belfort, who had wandered up to them. “Why, this is the lost black sheep appeared to filch the title from Rensley. It’s a famous jest, and Rensley’s as sour as a lemon over it.” He laughed delightedly at the thought of the deposed lord’s discomfiture.

“But what’s his claim?” persisted Prudence.

“Oh, that! To be sure, no one remembered his existence in the least, but it seems he’s a brother of old Barham, who died a month or two back. Odd, a’n’t it? I never heard of any brother, but it was all rather before my time, of course. Anyway, Cloverly was telling me he has all the papers to prove he’s the man, and a fine romantic story it all is. A jolt for Rensley, though!”

===Chinese===
洛斯托夫特夫人闲谈里的什么，模模糊糊掠过普鲁登丝的记忆。「我不知道特雷梅因家还失踪过一位呢，先生。」她说。

「老天，竟不知道巴勒姆爵位之争？」说话的是贝尔福特先生，他正逛到他们跟前。「嗐，这便是那位失踪的忤逆之子现身，要从伦斯利手里把爵位窃走喽。这是桩鼎鼎大名的笑谈，伦斯利为此酸得像只柠檬。」想到那位被挤下宝座的老爷的狼狈，他乐不可支地笑了。

「可他凭什么主张这爵位？」普鲁登丝追问不休。

「哦，那个呀！说来，他的存在本来就没有一个人记得分毫，不过看来他是老巴勒姆的兄弟，老巴勒姆一两个月前刚过世。怪事，不是么？我从来没听说过有什么兄弟，不过那当然都远在我记事之前了。总之，克洛弗利（Cloverly）告诉我，他握有全套文书，足以证明他正是其人，整个儿是一桩顶浪漫的故事。不过，对伦斯利可是一记闷棍！」

===Original===
“Does Rensley acknowledge him?” Prudence found strength enough to inquire.

“As to that, Rensley’s lying low, I take it, but I believe he told Farnborough he was sure his cousin was dead, and that this man had stolen the papers. But Rensley would take that tone, y’know.” Mr. Belfort perceived a friend close by, and was off to greet him.

“And what do you make of that?” said Prudence calmly in her brother’s ear.

Robin shook his head. “It’s the most consummate piece of impertinent daring﻿—gad, it beats our masquerade!”

===Chinese===
「伦斯利认他么？」普鲁登丝这才攒足力气问了一句。

「说到这个，我看伦斯利是在避风头，不过我相信他跟法恩伯勒（Farnborough）说过，他拿准了他堂兄已死，这人偷了那些文书。可伦斯利自然会是这个腔调，你懂的。」贝尔福特先生瞥见近旁有位朋友，便赶着寒暄去了。

「这事你怎么看？」普鲁登丝平静地附在弟弟耳边说。

罗宾摇了摇头。「这是登峰造极的一出胆大妄为——天哪，把咱们的化装把戏都比下去了！」

===Original===
“But how can he carry it off? And for how long?”

“And why?” Robin demanded. “It’s senseless! *Why?*”

“Oh, the old love of a fine dramatic gesture. Don’t we know it? It’s to rank with the time he played the French Ambassador in Madrid. And he came off safe from that.”

“But this﻿—this is England!” Robin said. “*Cordieu*, will you but look at him now?”

===Chinese===
「可他怎么圆得了场？又能撑到几时？」

「还有，图什么？」罗宾质问。「这毫无道理！*为什么？*」

「嗐，还不是那老脾气，偏爱漂亮的戏剧性排场。咱们还不清楚么？这一出，足以与他当年在马德里扮演法国大使那一遭并驾齐驱。那一遭他都可保无虞，全身而退。」

「可这——这是英国！」罗宾说。「*Cordieu*，你且瞧瞧他眼下这副光景！」

===Original===
The magnificent gentleman was bowing before Miss Gunning. Well they knew that flourish of a laced handkerchief. Egad, but he had all the airs of a Viscount, or of a Duke for that matter. A large figure came up with him; the new Lord Barham gave Sir Anthony Fanshawe two fingers to clasp. Sir Anthony stayed but to speak a few words, and then walked leisurely away.

Came a gasp from my Lady Lowestoft’s direction. My lady sprang up. “*Mon cher* Robert!” she cried, and held out her hands. Volubly she explained to Mr. Selwyn that this dear gentleman had long been known to her.

“Thérèse!” My Lord Barham kissed both her hands. “I have the supreme felicity to find you!”

“Faith, it’s an ecstatic old gentleman!” The voice came from behind Prudence. Sir Anthony Fanshawe had come round the room to her side.

===Chinese===
那位气派的先生正朝冈宁小姐（Miss Gunning）躬身行礼。花边手帕那一甩，他们再熟悉不过。乖乖，他那份子爵派头十足十，说句实话，便是公爵派头也使得。一个高大的身影同他一道走上前来；新任巴勒姆勋爵伸出两根指头给安东尼·范肖爵士一握。安东尼爵士只留下说了几句话，便从容地踱开了。

洛斯托夫特夫人那边传来一声抽气。夫人一跃而起。「*Mon cher* 罗伯特！」她唤道，伸出双手。她滔滔不绝地向塞尔温先生解释，这位亲爱的先生与她乃是多年的旧识。

「泰蕾丝！」巴勒姆勋爵吻了她的双手。「得与您重逢，真是无上之喜！」

「说真的，好一位心花怒放的老先生！」声音从普鲁登丝背后传来。安东尼·范肖爵士已绕过半个厅堂，来到了她身旁。

===Original===
“I’m to understand it’s a lost viscount, or some such matter?” Prudence took snuff with an air of unconcern.

“Quite so. The last of the Tremaines, it appears. Offspring don’t so far materialise.”

My Lady Lowestoft was bearing down upon them with a hand on Lord Barham’s arm. “*Mon cher*, I must present to you some dear young friends of mine,” they heard her say. “It is a Mr. and a Miss Merriot, who are staying with me for a space.”

“I am enchanted to meet a friend of my Thérèse!” his lordship declared, and was straightway presented to Miss Merriot.

===Chinese===
「照这么说，是位失踪的子爵，或是诸如此类的名堂？」普鲁登丝一副漠不关心的神气，取了一撮鼻烟。

「正是如此。看来是特雷梅因家硕果仅存的一位。子嗣嘛，至今还未见冒头。」

洛斯托夫特夫人一只手搭在巴勒姆勋爵臂上，朝他们一路逼了过来。「*Mon cher*，我必须给您引见两位我亲爱的年轻朋友，」他们听得她说。「是一位梅里奥特先生和一位梅里奥特小姐，眼下正在舍间小住。」

「能结识我泰蕾丝的朋友，真是不胜荣宠！」勋爵大人宣称道，随即被引见给梅里奥特小姐。

===Original===
Robin arose, and spread out his skirts; as he rose from the curtsey he extended a hand right regally, and gazed limpidly into the face of his sire.

My lord bowed deeply over the hand, and, looking up, bestowed a glance of admiration upon Miss Merriot’s fair countenance. “But charming!” he said. “Charming, I protest!”

It was Prudence’s turn now, and she made my lord a leg. Deep down in the grey eyes the twinkle lurked. “I am honoured, sir,” she said.

My lord bowed slightly, as became a man of his years and rank, and smiled with delight upon Mr. Merriot. Indeed, a most affable old gentleman. He turned to compliment my lady on having two such enchanting friends to stay with her, and promised himself the pleasure of waiting upon her in the morning. With yet another bow to Miss Merriot he walked away with my lady on his arm.

===Chinese===
罗宾起身离座，展开裙裾；从屈膝礼中直起身时，他十足王者气派地伸出一只手，清澈无邪地凝望进父亲大人的脸。

勋爵大人深深俯身吻那只手，抬起头来，将欣赏的一瞥赐予梅里奥特小姐姣好的面容。「真是迷人！」他说。「迷人，我敢断言！」

这回轮到普鲁登丝了，她向勋爵大人行了个鞠躬礼。那双灰眼睛深处，潜藏着一丝笑意。「深感荣幸，先生。」她说。

勋爵大人微微欠身——正合他这等年纪身份的礼数——对梅里奥特先生欣然一笑。的确，一位和蔼可亲之至的老先生。他转过身去恭维夫人，说府上竟有两位这般迷人的朋友作客，并自许了明朝登门拜望之乐。又向梅里奥特小姐一躬之后，他便挽着夫人的手臂走开了。

===Original===
“I am entirely overpowered,” complained Sir Anthony, and sat him down beside Robin.

Robin tilted his head speculatively. “Something of a foreign air,” he mused. “Do you agree, sir?”

“Something of an oppressive air I find it,” answered Sir Anthony, with a chuckle.

“My lady seems to know him very well,” remarked Prudence, and went away to glean what information she could.

===Chinese===
「我算是彻底被压服了。」安东尼爵士抱怨道，在罗宾身旁坐了下来。

罗宾若有所思地侧了侧头。「颇有几分外国派头，」他沉吟道。「您以为呢，先生？」

「依我看，倒颇有几分压得人透不过气来的派头。」安东尼爵士含笑答道。

「夫人跟他好像是旧识得很。」普鲁登丝评了一句，便走开去搜罗她能搜罗的消息了。

===Original===
Accounts varied, but it seemed my lord had quarrelled violently in his youth with his father, and taken himself off to France with a lowbred bride of his own choosing. Since that day he had never been heard of, until suddenly, soon after the death of his elder brother, he descended on the town in a blaze of magnificence. Prudence expressed surprise that he had not shown himself upon the death of his father, but the answer to that was ready. There were rumours that there had been little love lost between the brothers: the remarkable gentleman had chosen to remain in obscurity.

She could obtain no more certain information, and returned with her gleanings to Robin. My Lady Lowestoft was ready to go home; they greeted her proposal with relief, and were borne off under her wing. My Lord Barham, seeing them go, waved his hand, and said: “*A demain!*” most gallantly.

Not until they were safe inside the coach did my lady give way to the mirth that was consuming her. But then she lay back against the padded cushions and laughed till the tears ran down her painted cheeks.

Robin regarded her gloomily. “Ay, it’s a rare jest, ma’am.”

===Chinese===
说法各有出入，但大意是：勋爵大人年少时与父亲激烈反目，携一位自己挑中的低门第新娘去了法国。自那日起便再无音讯，直到前不久——他兄长过世之后不久——他忽然光华夺目地降临本城。普鲁登丝表示诧异，他父亲去世时他怎么竟不现身，这个疑问自有现成的答案。有传言说兄弟俩素来不睦：这位非同寻常的先生便宁可埋名隐姓。

再切实的消息她便打听不到了，只得带着收获回到罗宾身边。洛斯托夫特夫人准备回府；他们如释重负地迎奉这一提议，被她收在羽翼底下带走了。巴勒姆勋爵见他们离去，挥了挥手，极潇洒地说了一声：「*A demain*！」

直到安然坐进马车，夫人才放任了那几乎要将她烧穿的欢笑。只见她仰靠在软垫上，笑得眼泪顺着敷粉的双颊直往下淌。

罗宾阴郁地望着她。「唉，真是场难得的笑谈，夫人。」

===Original===
“It is﻿—it is altogether *magnifique*!” she gasped. “It is a coup the most superb! Not even I dreamed of anything so superb!” She sat up and dabbed at her eyes. “*Voyons*, was there ever such a man? I myself am ready to believe him to be Lord Barham. What an air! What effrontery! *Mon Dieu, mon Dieu*, I have not been in such an agony of laughter since he stole the Margrave’s mistress!”

“That’s a tale I don’t know,” said Prudence. “I perceive that a hurried flight to France awaits us.”

“But no, but no! Why, my cabbage? He proves himself the lost vicomte, and who is to know more?”

“Oh, it’s simple!” said Robin dryly. “But there is always the possibility of the true viscount’s appearance.”

===Chinese===
「这是——这是彻头彻尾的*magnifique*！」她喘着气说。「这是最漂亮的一击！连我做梦也梦不到这么漂亮的！」她坐直身子，按了按眼睛。「*Voyons*，天底下可曾有过这样的男人？连我自己都乐于相信他就是巴勒姆勋爵了。多好的气派！多厚的脸皮！*Mon Dieu, mon Dieu*，打从他偷走边疆伯爵（Margrave）的情妇那回起，我就没这么笑得死去活来过！」

「这段故事我可没听过，」普鲁登丝说。「我看明白了，一场奔逃法国正等着咱们。」

「不，不！怎么这样想，我的小卷心菜？他证明了自己就是那位失踪的子爵，还有谁能知道得更多？」

「哦，简单得很！」罗宾干巴巴地说。「可真子爵总有露面的可能。」

===Original===
“How, my child? We see the *bon papa* with all the papers. The real viscount is dead, of course! How else could Robert have the papers?”

“Good God, ma’am, do you put it above the old gentleman to steal them from a live man?”

“There’s more to it than that.” Prudence’s calm voice broke in. “A counterfeit for a day, a week, a month is very well, but even the old gentleman can’t maintain it forever. Rensley won’t be satisfied with a few documents. There’ll be traps set, and others of no one’s setting into which he is bound to fall. Consider, ma’am, what it means suddenly to become an English peer with estates, and a large fortune! The thing’s not so easily done, I believe.”

“There’s also the little matter of the late fracas in the North,” said Robin. “Certain, he discards the black wig, and the French accent, but there must be information out against him.”

“My children, I have faith in him!” her ladyship declared. “He is as I have said﻿—*magnifique*!”

===Chinese===
「怎么会呢，我的孩子？*bon papa* 手里全套文书都在。真正的子爵当然死了！不然罗伯特哪来的文书？」

「老天，夫人，您以为从活人手里把它们偷来，就超出老先生的本事了吗？」

「事情不止这一层。」普鲁登丝平静的嗓音插了进来。「冒充得了一天、一个礼拜、一个月都好说，可就连老先生也无法永远撑持下去。伦斯利不会看几份文书便心满意足。会有人设下圈套，还有些并非谁所设、他自己也注定要栽进去的圈套。想想吧，夫人，一夕之间变成一位有封地、有大宗家产的英国贵族，这意味着什么！这种事，我看没那么容易办到。」

「还有北边新近那场乱子这桩小事，」罗宾说。「没错，他撇掉了黑色假发和法国口音，可官府必定已立了告发他的案子。」

「我的孩子们，我对他有信心！」夫人宣称。「他就是我说的那样——*magnifique*！」

## VIII The Black Domino / VIII 黑衣多米诺

===Original===
My Lady Lowestoft stole up to the door of Prudence’s chamber, threw a swift glance round to see that no one was by, and went in, firmly shutting the door behind her. Prudence sat before her dressing table, haresfoot in hand. She looked round to see who came in so unceremoniously. “Fie!” she said, and turned back to the mirror.

“My reputation if anyone saw me!” said my lady, and sat down in a swirl of purple silk. She carried a strip of velvet in one hand, and a purple domino hung from her shoulders. She put up the velvet to her face. “So! Am not I intrigante, my dear?”

“Very, ma’am. You always are, masked or not.”

“So they say,” nodded my lady. “Oh, la-la! we’re very fine tonight, not?”

Prudence smoothed the crimson silk sleeve of her coat, and smiled a little. “My pièce de résistance, ma’am.”

===Chinese===
洛斯托夫特夫人（My Lady Lowestoft）蹑手蹑脚摸到普鲁登丝（Prudence）卧房门口，先飞快地四下一瞥，确信左右无人，这才闪身进屋，随手把门紧紧关上。普鲁登丝正坐在梳妆台前，手里执着一柄兔足粉扑。听见有人这般不客气地闯进来，她回头看看是谁。「啧啧！」她说着，便转回身去照镜子。

「要是叫人瞧见，我的名声可就完啦！」夫人说着坐了下来，一身紫绸衣衫旋出一圈涟漪。她一手拿着一条天鹅绒，一件紫衣多米诺披垂在肩头。她把那条天鹅绒举到脸前。「瞧！我岂不是个 intrigante（弄阴谋的女人）么，亲爱的？」

「像极了，夫人。您一向都是，戴不戴面具全一个样。」

「人家都这么说。」夫人点头道。「哦啦啦！咱们今晚可真俏丽，不是么？」

普鲁登丝抚平外衣的绯红绸袖，微微一笑。「这是我的 pièce de résistance（压箱行头），夫人。」

===Original===
“Oh, you look very well. That goes without saying. But what a wardrobe! The *bon papa* finds himself in affluent circumstances now?”

“Up and down, my lady. There seemed to be money enough when I saw him last.” Prudence pressed a patch on to her cheek with expert fingers. “Are you for setting forward? I’ll go see if Robin’s dressed.” She picked up the crimson domino from the bed, and her mask and hat with it, and went out.

Robin’s voice desired to know who it was that scratched on the door. Prudence answered, and heard him say: “Oh, enter, my dear.”

She went in humming a snatch of song. It died on her lips at what she saw, and she shut the door rather quickly. In place of the lady she expected to find there stood in the middle of the room a slim, lithe young figure in satin small-clothes, and a cambric shirt. The fair hair was powdered thickly, and tied back with a black ribbon in the neck; the white throat was hidden by a lace neckcloth which fell under the chin in deep ruffles down the shirt front. If Robin made a pretty girl, he was beyond doubt a very handsome young man.

“Robin, are you mad?” said Prudence quietly.

===Chinese===
「哦，你气色好得很，这自不必说。可这行头也太齐全了！那位 *bon papa*（好爸爸）如今手头宽绰起来了？」

「时好时坏，夫人。上回见着他的时候，钱像是还够使。」普鲁登丝以娴熟的手指往脸颊上贴了一枚美人贴。「这就动身么？我去瞧瞧罗宾（Robin）装扮好了没有。」她从床上拾起那件绯红多米诺，连同面具和帽子一并拿上，出去了。

门里传来罗宾的声音，问是谁在挠门。普鲁登丝应了，听他说：「哦，进来吧，亲爱的。」

她哼着一支歌的片断走了进去。一望见眼前的景象，歌声便在她唇边咽住，她颇为利索地带上了房门。屋子正中站着的，不是她料想中的那位小姐，而是一个纤细灵巧的年轻身影，穿着缎面短裤和细麻纱衬衫。一头浅发扑了厚厚的发粉，用一根黑丝带在颈后拢住；雪白的咽喉掩在一条蕾丝领巾底下，领巾在颏下翻出深深的褶边，一路铺到衬衫前襟。罗宾扮姑娘已是好看，做回年轻男子，毫无疑问更是仪表堂堂。

「罗宾，你疯了吗？」普鲁登丝轻声说。

===Original===
In the background, shaking out the folds of an elegant coat, John growled: “Ay, you may well ask, mistress. It’s taken leave of his senses he has.”

Robin laughed out. “My poor John! I shall be the death of you yet.”

“You’ll be the death of yourself, sir, and well you know it.”

Prudence came further into the room. “What mischief now?”

“Madam Prude! I salute you. No mischief, nor any madness either.”

===Chinese===
屋子那头，约翰（John）正抖开一件风雅外衣的褶子，闷声闷气地咕哝：「可不是，您这一问问得对，小姐。他这是魂儿彻底离了窍啦。」

罗宾放声大笑。「我可怜的约翰！我早晚得吓死你。」

「早晚送命的是您自己，少爷，这个您心里也透亮。」

普鲁登丝往屋里又走了几步。「这回又耍什么花样？」

「道学小姐普鲁！我向您致敬。不闹鬼，也没发疯。」

===Original===
“I’m not so sure. Pray will you be serious?”

He held the mask over his eyes. “What, shall I be known?”

“There’s to be an unmasking at supper. What then?”

“At the supper hour﻿—farewell, Robin!” He blew an imaginary kiss from the tips of his fingers, and tossed the mask on to a chair. “Don’t play the spoil sport, sister mine.”

She shrugged. “It’s to jeopardise your life for a pair of brown eyes.”

“It’s to play with fire for the sake of romance, and when have we done aught else? Get you into a hoop and petticoats, and play with your mountain.”

===Chinese===
「这个我可不敢担保。求您正经一点，行不行？」

他把面具举到眼睛前面。「怎么，还要叫人认出来不成？」

「晚宴时可要摘面具的。到那时怎么办？」

「到了晚宴时分——罗宾就此别过！」他从指尖上吹出一记虚幻的飞吻，随手把面具抛到一张椅子上。「别扫兴嘛，我的好妹妹。」

她耸了耸肩。「这是拿性命去换一双棕色眼睛。」

「这是为着浪漫玩火，可咱们哪一天干的不是这一桩？你倒去穿上裙撑和衬裙，陪你那座山玩去。」

===Original===
“Ah now, will you ha’ done, sir?” John put down the coat, scowling. “You’ll do no such thing, Miss Prue!”

“Not I. Robin, one single mischance, and you’re sped.”

“My dear, you grow fearful of shadows. Let be. Tomorrow I shall be again the demure Miss Merriot.”

Prudence knew too well that demon of perversity to attempt further argument. My Lady Lowestoft’s voice begged permission to enter. Prudence turned, and opened the door. “Oh, come in, ma’am, here’s a piece of mad folly for you to see.”

My lady came in all curiosity, and gave a little shriek of laughter at sight of Robin. “*Mon Dieu, mon Dieu*, you *vaurien*!” she said. “This is to go a-wooing, no? Don’t tell me! Me, I know well!”

===Chinese===
「哎哎，您就少说两句吧，少爷？」约翰放下外衣，沉着脸。「这种事您可干不得，普鲁（Prue）小姐！」

「我才不干。罗宾，只要出一星半点岔子，你就没命了。」

「亲爱的，你越来越怕影子了。由它去吧。到了明天，我又是那个文文静静的梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）了。」

那股别扭到底的邪魔性子，普鲁登丝知之已深，便不再试图多争。门外响起洛斯托夫特夫人的声音，请求准许进来。普鲁登丝转身开了门。「哦，进来吧，夫人，这儿有一桩疯癫的把戏给您看。」

夫人满怀好奇地进来，一见到罗宾便尖着嗓子笑出声来。「*Mon Dieu, mon Dieu*，你这个 *vaurien*！」她说。「这是去求爱呢，不是么？别想瞒我！我呀，我清楚得很！」

===Original===
“It’s to run a thousand dangers,” said Prudence. “The devil’s in him, I believe.”

Robin was busy fixing a diamond pin in the lace at his throat. “I pledge you my word I run no risk, Prue. The waistcoat, John.”

“Hé, but this is adventure!” cried my lady, her eyes sparkling. “You are anxious, my Prue? But no! Who should suspect? He may vanish before the unmasking, and Marthe shall be on the watch to let him into the house. It will all go well, I promise you.”

“Madam, you’re a jewel!” Robin told her, struggling into his coat. He shook out his ruffles, and gave his neckcloth a twist. “I am myself again.”

My lady surveyed him critically. “*Du vrai*, you are a very pretty young man,” she said. “*N’est-ce pas*, Prudence?”

===Chinese===
「这是去闯千般危险。」普鲁登丝说。「我看魔鬼准附在他身上了。」

罗宾正忙着往喉头的蕾丝上别一枚钻石饰针。「我向您担保，我一点风险也冒不着，普鲁。马甲，约翰。」

「Hé，这可真是一场奇遇！」夫人大声嚷道，两眼放光。「你发愁了，我的普鲁？用不着！谁会起疑心？揭面具之前他尽管溜走，玛尔特（Marthe）守在那儿放他进屋就是。一切都会顺顺当当，我向你担保。」

「夫人，您真是个宝！」罗宾一面往外套里挣一面说。他抖开褶边，把领巾拧了一道。「我又是我啦。」

夫人挑剔地上下打量他。「*Du vrai*，你真是个标致的年轻人。」她说。「*N'est-ce pas*，普鲁登丝？」

===Original===
“Something undersized,” amended Prudence, with her slow smile.

“Prue can only admire a mammoth, ma’am,” said Robin. His eyes ran over his sister’s large frame. “Well, perhaps she has reason.”

Thus it was that midway through the evening a slight gentleman in a black domino begged my Lady Dorling to present him to a little lady in a pink domino, seated against the wall by an austere spinster.

Lady Dorling said laughingly: “What shall I call you, sir, for indeed the mask baffles me?”

White teeth showed in a dazzling smile. “You shall say that I am *l’Inconnu*, madam.”

She was delighted. “Miss Pink Domino should feel Romance at hand on such an introduction. Why, it’s the little Grayson child.” She led the Black Domino up to the Pink one, and smilingly said: “My dear, may I present a partner to you for the minuet? He has no name that I can find﻿—only *l’Inconnu*. See if he will tell you more.” She rustled away on the words, leaving Miss Letty looking wonderingly up at the unknown.

===Chinese===
「就是块头小了点儿。」普鲁登丝修正道，脸上慢慢漾开笑意。

「普鲁眼里只容得下一头猛犸，夫人。」罗宾说。他的目光把妹妹高大的身架扫了一遍。「唔，也难怪她。」

于是，晚会的半途，一位身披黑衣多米诺（Black Domino）的清瘦绅士求多林夫人（Lady Dorling）引见一位粉衣多米诺的小个子小姐——她正倚墙坐着，身边是一位神情严正的老小姐。

多林夫人笑着说：「我该怎么称呼您呢，先生？这面具实在叫我猜不透。」

一口白牙在一记耀眼的微笑里露了出来。「您就说，我是 *l'Inconnu*（无名氏），夫人。」

她乐开了怀。「这样的引见，粉衣多米诺小姐该觉得浪漫已到了手边。哎呀，这不是格雷森（Grayson）家的小姑娘么。」她把黑衣多米诺领到粉衣多米诺跟前，笑吟吟地说：「亲爱的，我可不可以给你引见一位跳小步舞的舞伴？我实在找不出他的名姓——只有一个 *l'Inconnu*。你瞧瞧他肯不肯对你多说一点。」话音未落她便窸窸窣窣地走开了，撇下莱蒂小姐（Miss Letty）仰着一张惊奇的小脸，望着这位无名氏。

===Original===
He stood bowing deeply before her, one hand holding a point-edged tricorne over his heart, the other laid lightly on the hilt of his dress-sword. The black domino fell all about him in silken folds; the velvet mask through which his eyes glittered strangely baffled recognition.

Miss Letty made her curtsey, still gazing into the unknown’s face.

“Mademoiselle will bestow her hand on me for this dance?”

There was something faintly familiar in the elusive voice. “I may go, Aunt?”

The elder Miss Grayson gave reluctant consent. Masked balls, where strange gentlemen with fanciful sobriquets might claim introductions were not to her taste, but there was no help for it. Miss Letty went away on the Unknown’s arm.

“I have an odd feeling I know you, sir,” she confided, looking up with a child’s smile. “Please tell me, do I?”

===Chinese===
他朝她深深一躬，一手把一顶棱边尖挺的三角帽端在心口，另一只手轻轻搭在礼装佩剑的剑柄上。黑衣多米诺周身垂下绸缎似的褶纹；那双眼睛隔着天鹅绒面具灼灼闪光，偏偏教人无从辨认。

莱蒂小姐行了屈膝礼，目光仍盯着无名氏的脸。

「Mademoiselle（小姐）可肯把手赏给我，同跳这一支？」

那飘忽难辨的嗓音里有点什么，隐隐叫人耳熟。「我可以去么，姑妈？」

年长的格雷森小姐（Miss Grayson）勉勉强强地点头应允。假面舞会上，素不相识的绅士凭着花哨的名号便可讨引见——这本不合她的口味，可也无可奈何。莱蒂小姐便挽着无名氏的手臂走开了。

「我心里有种说不出的奇怪感觉，好像认识您，先生。」她仰起脸，带着孩子气的笑吐露道。「求您告诉我，是不是？」

===Original===
He shook his head; she thought his smile intriguing beyond words. “How should you know *l’Inconnu*, mademoiselle?”

This was Romance indeed. “But you know me, do you not?” They were dancing now, and she asked the question as she sank to the curtsey.

They came together again. “Ah, that is another matter entirely,” said the Black Domino.

She pouted. “And you won’t tell me! So many people I’ve guessed; oh, at once! There is Tony, for instance.”

She nodded towards a massive figure in a grey domino. “There is no mistaking him, to be sure. And I think I know which is Mr. Merriot. I thought that lady in the blue domino was his sister, but of that I am not sure. Do you know, sir?”

“No, mademoiselle, but then I do not want to know. I am content to have found Miss Grayson.”

===Chinese===
他摇了摇头；她只觉他的微笑迷人得无以言表。「您怎么会认识 *l'Inconnu* 呢，mademoiselle？」

这可真是浪漫了。「可您认识我，是不是？」这会儿两人已翩然起舞，她一边沉身行礼，一边把这句问话问了出口。

两人又交汇到一处。「啊，那就完全是另一回事了。」黑衣多米诺说。

她噘起了嘴。「您偏不肯告诉我！多少人我都猜中了；哦，一下就猜中！比方说那一位，就是托尼（Tony）。」

她朝一个身披灰衣多米诺的魁伟身影点了点头。「那一位是断然错不了的，真的。我还当自己认出了哪位是梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）。我原以为穿蓝衣多米诺的那位女士是他妹妹，只是这一点我没把握。您知道么，先生？」

「不知道，mademoiselle，不过我原也不想知道。找着了格雷森小姐，我便心满意足了。」

===Original===
She blushed, and turned away her head.

“I offend Miss Grayson?” the Unknown said softly.

No, she was not offended. Only﻿—only it was so very strange not to know who he was.

“My name you would not know if I told it,” he said. “Why spoil a perfect hour?”

Her lips were a little parted. “A perfect hour!” she echoed. “Is it perfect, sir?”

“For me at least, Letitia.”

===Chinese===
她红了脸，把头扭向一旁。

「我惹格雷森小姐不快了么？」无名氏柔声问。

不，她没有着恼。只是——只是不知道他是谁，这滋味实在太过奇妙。

「我的名字，即便说了，你也不会知道。」他说。「何苦搅坏一个完美的钟点呢？」

她的双唇微微启开。「一个完美的钟点！」她应声道。「当真完美么，先生？」

「至少于我是完美的，莉蒂西娅（Letitia）。」

===Original===
“But﻿—but you must not call me by my name!” she said. Yet she did not sound angry.

“Nor tell you that I came only to dance with you?”

“D-did you, sir?”

He nodded. “But, of course. Didn’t you guess it, Letitia?”

“No, oh no! How should I? And﻿—and you use my name again, sir.”

===Chinese===
「可是——可是您不该叫我的名字呀！」她说。话音里却听不出恼意。

「也不告诉你，我此来专为同你共舞？」

「真——真的么，先生？」

他点了点头。「自然是真的。你难道没猜到么，莉蒂西娅？」

「没有，哦没有！我怎么会猜到？还有——还有您又叫我的名字了，先生。」

===Original===
“But then it is such a pretty name,” he pleaded. “Make me free of it for one night!”

“It is like an adventure,” she said. Behind the mask her eyes were like stars.

“An adventure, or a dream.” He led her out of the dance, away to an alcove behind great pots of flowers.

“Not a dream! Oh no, for then I should wake up, and I do not want to. I want to see your face at the unmasking.”

“You won’t see it, Letitia; I shall remain the Unknown.”

She sat down on the couch placed in the alcove. “But you will have to unmask, won’t you? Everyone must.”

===Chinese===
「可这名字实在动听啊。」他恳求道。「就许我用它这一个晚上吧！」

「这真像一场奇遇。」她说。面具后面，她的眼睛亮得像星星。

「一场奇遇，或者一场梦。」他领她出了舞池，走到几大盆花木后面的一处凹室。

「不要是梦！哦不，是梦我就该醒了，我可不想醒。我想在揭面具的时候瞧瞧您的脸。」

「你瞧不见的，莉蒂西娅；我仍旧做我的无名氏。」

她在凹室里安置的长榻上坐下。「可是到时候您总得摘面具的，对不对？人人都得摘。」

===Original===
He smiled, and shook his head. “To unmask would be to kill Romance, Letitia.”

She was doubtful. “Would it? But how shall I know you again if I do not see your face tonight?”

“Ah, but will you want to know me again? Or will you not regret the perfect hour?”

“No, I am sure I shall not. And of course I shall want to know you again. Shall you not want to know me?”

“Always, but I have you in my dreams, Letitia.”

She blushed adorably. “Do you know, that is the very prettiest thing anyone has ever said to me,” she confided. “But I would like﻿—I mean, I do not want to live only in your dreams. Shan’t you wait upon my papa?”

===Chinese===
他微笑着摇头。「一摘面具，浪漫便死了，莉蒂西娅。」

她将信将疑。「会么？可要是今晚见不着您的脸，往后我凭什么认出您呢？」

「啊，可你还愿意再认出我么？还是说，你不会惋惜这个完美的钟点？」

「不会，我敢说不会。而且我当然愿意再认出您。您难道不愿意认出我么？」

「无时无刻不愿意，何况我的梦里已经有了你，莉蒂西娅。」

她红晕上颊，娇俏动人。「您可不知道，这是人们对我讲过的最最动听的一句话。」她吐露道。「可是我倒愿意——我是说，我不想只活在您的梦里。您不肯来拜见我爸爸么？」

===Original===
The white teeth showed again in a smile of some mischief. “*L’Inconnu* never waits upon papa,” he said. “You will remember me only as a Black Domino.”

Her face fell. “I shan’t see you again?”

“Yes, you will see me﻿—perhaps.”

“And know you?”

He hesitated; then laughed, and stretched out his hand. “When you see that ring again, Letitia, you will know that I have come once more.”

She looked down at the ring on his little finger, a curious piece of wrought gold in a fantastic design. “Only by that?”

===Chinese===
白牙又露了出来，含着一丝促狭的笑意。「*L'Inconnu* 从不拜见什么爸爸。」他说。「你只消把我记成一位黑衣多米诺。」

她的脸垮了下来。「我再也见不着您了？」

「见得着——兴许。」

「也认得出您？」

他迟疑了一瞬；随即笑了，伸出手来。「等你再见到这枚戒指，莉蒂西娅，你便知道我又来了。」

她低头看他小指上的戒指，一件奇巧的金工，花样繁复。「只凭这个？」

===Original===
“Only by that.”

“But﻿—” she considered awhile. “You might forget to wear it,” she pointed out.

“I shall not forget.”

She sighed. “It is all so mysterious. I fear perhaps it is just a game, and I shan’t ever see you again.”

He quoted a Spanish proverb.

“Oh, are you foreign?” she exclaimed, as though that explained all.

===Chinese===
「只凭这个。」

「可是——」她思量了一阵。「您兴许会忘了戴它。」她指出。

「我不会忘。」

她叹了口气。「这一切太神秘了。我只怕这不过是一场游戏，我再也无法见到您。」

他引了一句西班牙谚语。

「哦，您是外国人么？」她惊呼道，仿佛这一下全都解释通了。

===Original===
“No, child, but I have been much in foreign lands.”

“How exciting!” she said. “Tell me about it.”

But a large figure stood in the entrance to the alcove, and a pleasant voice said: “Mistress Pink Domino, will you give your hand to a Grey one?”

*L’Inconnu* came to his feet, and bowed gracefully. “I surrender you,” he said. “But only for a little while.”

Sir Anthony held out his arm to Miss Grayson, and looked curiously at the Black Domino.

===Chinese===
「不是，孩子，不过我在异邦游历过很久。」

「多带劲呀！」她说。「讲给我听吧。」

可是一个大块头的身影已立在凹室门口，一个悦耳的嗓音说道：「粉衣多米诺小姐，可肯把手交给一件灰衣的？」

*L'Inconnu* 起身，风度翩翩地一躬。「我把你交出去。」他说。「不过只此片刻。」

安东尼爵士（Sir Anthony）向格雷森小姐递出手臂，好奇地打量黑衣多米诺。

===Original===
Miss Grayson went reluctantly, saying over her shoulder: “I believe you will disappear.”

“I shall claim you again, be very sure.”

“Who in the fiend’s name may that be?” said Sir Anthony.

“I don’t know, Tony. He is just called *l’Inconnu*, but he knows me and I have a feeling I have met him. You don’t know, either?”

“I haven’t a notion, my dear. I am not quite sure that I approve of unknown gentlemen.”

Her eyes pleaded. “Oh, don’t, don’t tell Aunt, Tony!” she begged. “Truly, I am not being indiscreet.”

===Chinese===
格雷森小姐老大不情愿地随他去，回头说道：「我看您是要消失了。」

「我保管再来讨回你，你只管放心。」

「那究竟是谁，撞了鬼了？」安东尼爵士说。

「我不知道，托尼。人家只管他叫 *l'Inconnu*，可他认得我，我总觉得从前见过他。您也不知道？」

「一点头绪也没有，亲爱的。来历不明的绅士，我可不是十分赞许。」

她的眼里满是恳求。「哦，别，千万别告诉姑妈，托尼！」她央求道。「真的，我并没有不知轻重。」

===Original===
“You don’t contemplate an elopement with the mysterious stranger?” he asked teasingly.

“Tony!”

“I beg your pardon,” he bowed solemnly.

“That was prodigiously ill-natured, Tony.”

“Never say so, my dear.”

“I have a very good mind not to dance with you now.”

===Chinese===
「你没打算同那位神秘的生客私奔吧？」他打趣道。

「托尼！」

「恕我失言。」他一本正经地躬了躬身。

「这话刻薄透了，托尼。」

「千万别这么说，亲爱的。」

「我大有想头，现在干脆不同您跳了。」

===Original===
She was conducted promptly to an antechamber, where there were refreshments spread. “An excellent mind,” said Sir Anthony. “I was never a good dancer. A glass of ratafie?”

She laughed. “It’s too bad of you, Tony!”

“My dear, it would be worse if I stood up with you, I assure you. My forte lies in fetching food and drink for my partners.”

She sat down, perforce. “Well, a little ratafie, then. I do not intend to go near Aunt again all the evening. She may scold as much as she likes afterwards.”
