Sir Anthony poured two glasses of wine. “She’s absorbed in euchre, child; you need have no fear. I drink to your very good health.”

===Chinese===
她随即被引进一间前厅，那里摆开了茶点。「极好的主意。」安东尼爵士说。「我跳舞从来就不成。来一杯拉塔菲亚甜酒（ratafie）？」

她笑了。「您真坏，托尼！」

「亲爱的，我要是真下场陪你跳，那才更糟，我向你担保。我的看家本事，是替舞伴张罗吃喝。」

她只得坐下。「那么，就一点拉塔菲亚甜酒。今晚我打定主意再也不挨近姑妈了。回头她爱怎么数落，随她数落去。」

安东尼爵士斟了两杯酒。「她玩尤克牌（euchre）正入神呢，孩子；你尽管放心。敬你一杯，祝你康健。」

===Original===
Letty sipped at the wine, and dimpled haughtily. “You might drink to my eyes, Tony.”

“No doubt I might,” he said, but showed no disposition to do so.

Letty looked meditatively up at him. “I wonder whether you will *ever* say pretty things?” she said, aggrieved.

“Not to you, minx.”

“I know that. But to someone else?”

“My dear, I doubt I haven’t the aptitude for it. I will tell you if ever I discover it in myself.”

===Chinese===
莱蒂抿了一口酒，傲然浮出一对笑靥。「您倒可以敬我的眼睛一杯，托尼。」

「我无疑是可以的。」他说，却全然没有这个意思。

莱蒂若有所思地仰头望着他。「真不知您到底*有没有*说过一句动听话？」她委屈地说。

「对你可不会，小淘气。」

「这我知道。可对别人呢？」

「亲爱的，我怕我天生没这份才能。哪天我发现自己身上有了，一定奉告。」

===Original===
“I don’t suppose you will. Tell me, I mean,” said Letty with a flash of insight.

“There’s no knowing. I’m to understand your ear’s been tickled with pretty speeches tonight?”

She spread out her fan, and began to trace the pattern on it with one rosy-tipped finger. “I shan’t tell you that, Tony.”

“You need not.” Sir Anthony smiled a little. “It leaps to the intelligence.”

“But don’t you think, Tony,” said Letty sweetly, “that it would be very wonderful if no one had said pretty things to me?”

Sir Anthony regarded her calmly. “You bid fair to become a rare handful,” he remarked. “And that is all the compliment you’ll have from me.”

===Chinese===
「我看不会的——我是说，您不会来告诉我。」莱蒂灵光一闪，说道。

「这可难说。我是不是可以理解为，今晚有甜言蜜语搔着你的耳朵了？」

她摊开折扇，用一根玫瑰色指尖循着扇面花纹描画。「这个我偏不告诉您，托尼。」

「用不着你说。」安东尼爵士微微一笑。「明眼人一望而知。」

「不过您想呀，托尼，」莱蒂甜甜地说，「要是竟没有一个人对我说过动听话，那岂不是天大的奇事？」

安东尼爵士平静地端详她。「你眼看要出落成一个难得的磨人精了。」他评道。「这便是你能从我这里领到的全部恭维。」

===Original===
“I am very glad I am not going to marry you,” said Letty frankly. “You would not suit me at all. Perhaps you’ll marry my dear Miss Merriot instead.”

“Withhold your felicitations awhile,” he replied. “The event is not imminent.”

“I expect you’re agog to be off to claim her hand for the dance,” nodded Miss Letty sapiently.

Sir Anthony set down his empty glass. “I shall have to curb my impatience, then,” he said. “She’s not here.”

“Oh, is she not? I quite thought that was she in the blue domino. Who told you?”

“My Lady Lowestoft. She is kept at home with the migraine, as I believe.”

===Chinese===
「我真高兴，幸好我要嫁的不是您。」莱蒂坦率地说。「您压根儿合不上我的意。兴许您可以转而娶我亲爱的梅里奥特小姐。」

「且慢道贺。」他答道。「这桩事眼下还遥遥无期。」

「我猜您正心痒难耐，急着去讨她的手同跳呢。」莱蒂小姐自作聪明地点头道。

安东尼爵士放下空杯子。「那我只好按捺住这份心焦了。」他说。「她今晚没来。」

「哦，没来么？我还满以为穿蓝衣多米诺的就是她呢。谁跟您说的？」

「洛斯托夫特夫人。据我所知，梅里奥特小姐叫偏头痛困在家里了。」

===Original===
Letty was all concern. “Oh, poor Miss Merriot! But Mr. Merriot is here, isn’t he? In the crimson domino? Yes, I thought so.”

“To say truth, it was he set me on your track. He told me he had sought you for the minuet only to find you spirited away by a man in a black domino.”

This brought the Unknown back to mind. “I would like to return to the ballroom, please,” said Letty decidedly.

But it was Mr. Merriot who claimed her hand, and led her into the quadrille. Letty went with a good grace, but looked eagerly about her. The Unknown was nowhere to be seen, yet at the end of the dance he seemed to spring up out of the ground, as it were, and stood confronting Mr. Merriot with that tantalising smile curling his lips. “The lady is promised to me,” he said; there was a faint note of mockery in his voice.

“On the contrary,” said Prudence. “The lady is mine.”

Really, a masked ball was a most fascinating entertainment. Miss Letty clasped her hands in the folds of her domino, and waited breathlessly.

===Chinese===
莱蒂满心关切。「哦，可怜的梅里奥特小姐！可梅里奥特先生来了呀，是不是？穿绯红多米诺的那位？嗯，我就知道。」

「说实话，正是他把我引到你的踪迹上来的。他告诉我，他本要寻你跳小步舞，却发现你叫一个黑衣多米诺的男人神不知鬼不觉地带走了。」

这话又把无名氏带回她心里。「我想回舞厅去，劳驾。」莱蒂斩钉截铁地说。

可讨她手的却是梅里奥特先生，领着她跳起了四对方舞。莱蒂大大方方地随他去，眼睛却急切地四下张望。无名氏哪里也不见踪影，可舞曲一终，他却仿佛从地底里冒出来似的，站在了梅里奥特先生对面，唇边挂着那抹撩人的笑。「这位小姐可是许给了我。」他说；嗓音里带着一丝淡淡的讥诮。

「恰恰相反，」普鲁登丝说，「这位小姐是我的。」

说真的，假面舞会真是一种迷死人的娱乐。莱蒂小姐在多米诺的褶裥里绞紧双手，屏息等待。

===Original===
The hilt of a sword was thrust slightly forward. “Why, I would meet you for the honour of holding her hand,” said the Unknown. “But she shall choose.” He turned, and offered an arm. “Madam, will you walk?”

She looked beseechingly at Prudence. “Mr. Merriot, I have to choose *l’Inconnu* because I am a female, and they say the silly creatures love a mystery.”

Prudence laughed and bowed. “I retire from the lists, then, cruel Pink Domino.”

“Besides,” said Letty coaxingly, “your crimson and my rose go vilely together, sir!” She threw a smile over her shoulder as she went off, threading her way through the throng of people.

“Bereft, my Peter?”

Prudence started, and turned to face Sir Anthony, standing at her elbow, “Robbed, sir, by a man in a black domino. I chose the wrong colour, and Miss Grayson won’t stay to clash with my crimson.”

===Chinese===
一柄剑的剑柄微微向前一送。「好啊，为了握她手的这份荣幸，我尽可以同你会上一会。」无名氏说。「不过，还是让她来挑。」他转过身，递出一条手臂。「小姐，可肯移步？」

她哀求似的望向普鲁登丝。「梅里奥特先生，我非选 *l'Inconnu* 不可，谁叫我是个女人家呢——人家都说，这号蠢东西天生爱神秘。」

普鲁登丝笑着欠身。「那我便退出赛场了，狠心的粉衣多米诺。」

「再说了，」莱蒂好声好气地哄道，「您的绯红同我的玫瑰红搁在一处，配得可难看死了，先生！」她走开时回头抛下一笑，从攒动的人群里穿行而去。

「丢了心尖儿了，我的彼得（Peter）？」

普鲁登丝一惊，转过身来，正对着立在她肘边的安东尼爵士。「遭了劫，爵士，叫一个黑衣多米诺的男人劫的。我颜色挑错了，格雷森小姐不肯留下来，同我的绯红打架。」

===Original===
“So the mysterious stranger filches the lady from you. Too bad, my dear boy. Come and drown your sorrows in claret.”

Out on the terrace, under a starry sky, the Unknown raised Miss Grayson’s hand to his lips, and held it there a long moment. She shivered a little, and her eyes widened.

“Take off the mask!” He spoke little above a whisper. “Oh﻿—no!” she said, and drew her hand away.

“Ah, don’t deny me!” An arm slid round her shoulders, and deft fingers sought the mask’s string over her ear.

“You﻿—you must not!” Letty said faintly, and put up her hand to stop his against her hair.

But the string was untied, and the mask fell. Her hand was caught and held; she lay back against the Unknown’s shoulder, and felt his other hand gently forcing up her chin.

===Chinese===
「这么说，神秘的生客把你到手的小姐拐走了。真糟心，我亲爱的孩子。来，喝杯红葡萄酒浇浇愁。」

露台上，星空下，无名氏抬起格雷森小姐的手送到唇边，久久停驻。她微微一颤，睁大了眼睛。

「摘下面具吧！」他的声音只比耳语高一丝。「哦——不！」她说，把手抽了回去。

「啊，别拒绝我！」一条手臂滑上她的肩头，灵巧的手指去寻她耳畔面具的系带。

「您——您不可以！」莱蒂轻得几乎听不见地说，抬起手去挡那只正探向她发间的手。

可系带终究解开了，面具滑落。她的手被捉住、握牢；她后仰靠在无名氏的肩上，觉出他另一只手正轻轻把她的下巴托起来。

===Original===
It must surely be a mad dream from which she would awaken soon. She looked up and saw only glittering eyes behind the blackness of the mask, and the hint of a smile in the moonlight. The arm tightened about her shoulders; the hand beneath her chin pressed more insistently, and the Unknown bent his head till his lips found hers.

The spell held for a moment; then she quivered, and made a fluttering movement to be free. The Unknown sank on one knee, and lifted the hem of her gown to kiss. “Forgive me!” he said. “I may never have the chance again, Letitia.”

She stood poised for flight, but his words kept her still. Half timidly she stretched down her hand to him. “Oh, do not!” she said. “I think we are both mad tonight.”

He came to his feet, and stood holding her hands between his. “But you will remember.”

“I shall see you again?” It was a forlorn petition.

===Chinese===
这必定是一场疯狂的梦，她很快就会从中醒来。她仰起眼，只看见面具的黑影后灼灼的双眼，和月光里一丝若有若无的笑意。那条手臂把她肩头搂得更紧；托着她下巴的手愈发不容分说，无名氏低下头去，直到唇瓣寻到了她的。

魔咒镇了她片刻；随后她一个哆嗦，扑扇着要挣脱。无名氏单膝跪下，捧起她的裙裾一吻。「原谅我！」他说。「往后未必再有这样的机会了，莉蒂西娅。」

她亭亭立着，作势欲逃，他的话却把她钉在原地。她半带羞怯地朝他伸下手去。「哦，别这样！」她说。「我看咱们今晚都疯了。」

他站起身来，把她的双手拢在自己两手之间。「可你会记住的。」

「我还能再见您么？」一声凄惶的祈求。

===Original===
“Who can say? This I promise: if ever you are in danger, or in need of a champion you will see me, for I shall come to you then.” He stood for a moment, silhouetted by the silver light against the deep blue sky; then once more he bent, and, turning her hand upwards, pressed a kiss into the palm. “*Adieu, ma belle*; you will not forget.”

He moved swiftly to the low parapet that walled the terrace in; looked over an instant, and placing his hand on the top, vaulted lightly over, down into the silent garden a few feet below.

She ran forward, and peered over the low wall. There was no one in sight, but she thought she heard an echo of his adieu borne back to her on a soft wind.

===Chinese===
「谁说得准呢？这个我应承你：一朝你身临险境，或是需要一位护花使者，你就会见到我，因为那时我必到你身边来。」他伫立片刻，深蓝天幕衬着银辉，勾出他的剪影；随后他再度俯下身，把她的一只手翻向上，将一吻印进掌心。「*Adieu, ma belle*；你不会忘的。」

他疾步走向围住露台的矮女墙；往下一望，转瞬手掌按上墙顶，轻轻一个撑跃，翻落到几尺之下寂静的花园里。

她奔上前去，隔着矮墙张望。四下不见人影，可她仿佛听见他那一声道别的回音，乘着一缕柔风，飘回她耳边。

## XXIII The Fight by Moonlight / XXIII 月光下的搏斗

===Original===

Such a romantic venture as an elopement from Vauxhall Gardens should have delighted Miss Letty, in love with excitement, but alack! she performed her part sadly, in a spirit very different from that in which she had run away with this same gentleman so short a while back. Then it had been done with daredevilry, and in expectation of romance; now it was done with a heavy heart dwelling on a Black Domino with an elusive, tantalizing smile.

Miss Letty had to admit she was reaping the reward of past folly. Ruefully she reflected that if she had never allowed herself to become dazzled in the first place by Mr. Markham’s wiles and compliments she would not now have been in a situation so gloomy and hopeless.

She had not been able to think of a way out of the difficulty. Her ideas of law and treason were very vague; she thought that incriminating letter of her father’s so fraught with danger that she dared do nothing but what Mr. Markham told her, for fear of what awful things might happen. Before she could take any measures against him she must have the letter safe. She thought she might perhaps be able to steal it from him while he slept, for in spite of his talk of heading straight for Scotland she knew very well that he must break the journey sooner or later. It was a forlorn hope, and failing it she could shoot him, she supposed, if only there were a pistol to her hand.

She had never visited Vauxhall with so little pleasure before; it was as though the brilliantly-lit gardens, all a-hum with festivity, were a place of execution. It was easy﻿—wretchedly easy, she thought﻿—to slip away from the rest of her party. She drew her cloak around her, and hurried away down a walk lit by lanterns to the appointed meeting place. Mr. Markham was there, and he showed relief at seeing her, and took her hand. It was withdrawn. “I may be forced to marry you,” said Letty acidly, “but at least you shan’t touch me till then.”

===Chinese===

从沃克斯豪尔花园（Vauxhall Gardens）私奔，这样一桩罗曼蒂克的奇遇，本该叫迷恋刺激的莱蒂（Letty）小姐欢喜不尽，可惜唉！她这场戏却演得恹恹不乐，那份心境，同短短时日之前随这位绅士出走时全然两样。那一回，是揣着亡命之徒的泼天胆气、怀着对罗曼史的满心期盼做下的；这一回，却捧着一颗沉甸甸的心，只顾惦念那位黑衣多米诺（Black Domino），和他那抹捉摸不定、逗人心痒的微笑。

莱蒂小姐不得不承认，自己这是在收割昔日糊涂种下的果报。她悔恨地想，倘若当初不曾先叫马卡姆（Markham）先生的伎俩和奉承迷花了眼，如今断不至于陷入这般阴惨无望的境地。

她想不出一条脱身之计。她脑子里对法律和叛国的概念模模糊糊；只觉得父亲那封招祸的信凶险万分，除了照马卡姆先生的吩咐行事，她什么也不敢做，生怕发生什么可怕的祸事。要对他采取任何手段，先得把那封信稳稳拿到手。她想，兴许能趁他睡着时把信偷到手——尽管他口口声声说要直奔苏格兰，她心里明白，这趟路他迟早得中途歇下。这不过是个渺茫的指望；指望落了空，她还可以开枪打死他——她盘算着——只要手边有把手枪就好。

她从前游沃克斯豪尔，从没有一回这样兴致索然；那灯彩辉煌、欢声鼎沸的花园，在她眼里竟同一座刑场无异。从同行的一班伙伴中间悄悄溜开，实在容易——容易得可悲，她想。她把斗篷往身上一裹，匆匆沿一条灯笼映照的小径，赶到约定的会面地点。马卡姆先生已在等候，见了她面露宽慰，便伸手来握她的手。那只手抽了回去。「我或许免不了要嫁给你，」莱蒂尖刻地说，「可在那之前，你休想碰我一碰。」

===Original===

It was no part of Mr. Markham’s plan to goad her to rebellion. He begged her pardon, and led her swiftly away down the winding walks till they came to an entrance to the gardens. He told her then to pull the hood over her head. She obeyed listlessly, and in a very short time found herself seated in a post-chaise beside her hated lover.

She drew far into her own corner. “You might at least ride beside the coach!” she said. “Can you not see how much I detest you?”

He had her safe at last; he cared nothing for her whims; he could even afford to be generous. “Bear with me, my dear. I won’t plague you with talk.”

“You had much better not,” said Letty, “for I should certainly not answer.”

===Chinese===

激得她奋起反抗，全不在马卡姆先生的计划之内。他赔了个不是，领着她快步顺着蜿蜒的小径走开，一直来到花园的一处出入口。这时他才吩咐她把兜帽罩上头。她恹恹地依了，不多时，便发觉自己已坐进一辆驿车（post-chaise），挨在她恨之入骨的情郎身边。

她深深缩进自己那边的角落里。「你大可以骑马傍着车走嘛！」她说。「难道你看不出我有多厌恶你？」

她到底稳稳到手了；她这些小性子他一概不放在心上；他甚至大度得起。「担待着点儿，亲爱的。我不拿话来烦你。」

「你最好还是省了，」莱蒂说，「反正我必定一字也不答。」

===Original===

This was not a very promising beginning, Mr. Markham thought. When a haughty shoulder was resolutely turned on him he decided that Miss Letitia needed a lesson. His fingers itched to slap her, but he controlled the desire, remembering that there was a lifetime ahead in which to tame a refractory wife. Frightened for her father’s safety as she undoubtedly was she was yet quite capable of raising a disturbance if he tried her too far. So he sat back in his own corner and meditated with some satisfaction on the excellence of his plans, and the delightful time to come.

Letty’s thoughts were not so pleasant. The only food for comfort she could find lay in the pistol holster beside her. There was a weapon in it, large and clumsy for her little hands, but still a weapon.

Mr. Markham observed the direction of her glance and smiled grimly. “Ay, you’re a violent piece, aren’t you? You’d shoot me if you had the chance, I’ll lay my life. The pistol’s not loaded. Yes, there’s another my side, but it’s in the same state. The only loaded pistol, my dear, lies snug in my pocket and there it will stay.”

Letty vouchsafed no answer. She gave herself up to the concoction of a plan to get that gun away from her bridegroom. She could evolve nothing but the haziest of schemes, and involuntarily her thoughts drifted on to the contemplation of the impossible. This time there was no large Tony to come after her. She had left no note of farewell, and it would be hours perhaps before her father knew of her flight. Even then he could have no means of knowing whither she had gone. There were no quickwitted Merriots either, and, worst of all, no stranger in a Black Domino.

Well, she was a great fool to think of the Unknown, who was in all probability nothing but a young buck bent on amusing himself at the expense of a silly chit. Once tied up to this monster at her side she had best banish the Unknown entirely from her thoughts: he could no longer be of avail.

===Chinese===

这个开头可不大吉利，马卡姆先生想。等一只傲慢的肩膀斩钉截铁地掉转过去，他便断定，莉蒂西娅（Letitia）小姐需要受一回教训。他的手指发痒，想掴她一记耳光，却把这股欲望按捺住了，心想来日方长，尽有工夫慢慢驯服一个桀骜的老婆。她为父亲的安危担惊受怕，这毋庸置疑；可他若把她逼得太狠，她照样全然闹得起一场风波。于是他往自己那角一靠，带着几分称意，细细回味起自己计划的周全，和往后那些美妙的日子。

莱蒂的心思可没这般受用。她能寻到的唯一一点安慰，就在身旁的手枪套里。那里头搁着一件家伙，对她那双小手来说又大又笨，但到底是件家伙。

马卡姆先生觑出了她目光的去向，狞笑一声。「是啊，你是个烈性子的小东西，对不对？只要有机会，你就会一枪崩了我，我敢拿性命打赌。那把枪没上弹药。不错，我身边还有一把，也是一样的空膛。唯一上了膛的枪，亲爱的，正舒舒服服躺在我口袋里，而且它就待在那儿不挪窝。」

莱蒂不屑搭腔。她一门心思盘算起一条计策，想把那把枪从她这位新郎官手里弄走。可想来想去，只盘算出个雾里看花的轮廓，思绪便不由自主地飘去想那些不可能的事了。这一回，再没有大块头的托尼（Tony）追上来了。她没留下只言片语的告别信，父亲或许要过好几个钟头才会发觉她出走了。即便发觉了，他也无从知道她去向何方。也没有心思机敏的梅里奥特（Merriot）兄妹，而最糟的是，再没有那位身披黑衣多米诺的陌生人了。

唉，她惦记那位无名氏（the Unknown），实在是个天大的傻瓜——十有八九，那人不过是个纨绔子弟，一心拿一个傻丫头取乐、寻自己的开心罢了。一旦同身旁这头怪物拴在一处，她最好把无名氏从心底彻底逐出去：他再也使不上力了。

===Original===

She looked miserably out of the window at the tall houses slipping by. There were flambeaux at a few of the doors, but a bright moon cast a silver light over all, and made lamps superfluous. They were travelling at a prodigious speed; to be sure, Mr. Markham meant to lose no time in putting London well behind him. In a very short while, so it seemed to poor Letty, the houses grew further and further apart, and at length stopped altogether. She had very little idea of where they were: on that other journey north she had noticed nothing. She saw a heath soon, dotted over with clumps of bushes, casting long black shadows in the moonlight and some tall larch trees stretching up to a sapphire sky. There was nothing else to be seen, and Letty had never felt less in the mood for admiring the beauties of Nature. She pulled her cloak closer about her still, and looked down at her hands, clasped tightly in her lap. She would not cry, however hard a fight it might be to keep the tears back. The monster beside her should not have that satisfaction.

On went the coach, bumping and jolting over the bad road. The pace had slackened somewhat: one could not drive hell-for-leather along the highways of England; they were not in a state for such usage.

They had been passing through this desolate heath some way when she thought she caught the sound of horses galloping: horses other than the ones that were drawing her to her doom. Scarcely had her ears been made aware of this sound than there came a thunder of hoofs, a shout, a lurch, and a medley of confused noises as the coach was pulled quickly to a standstill. A pistol shot sounded; there was a yell of terror from the box, and at the same moment the glass in the window by Mr. Markham was shattered by a blow with something made of metal, and a small gold-mounted pistol held by a slim white hand pointed straight at Mr. Markham’s heart.

===Chinese===

她凄凄惶惶地望着窗外掠过的一幢幢高楼。有几户门前燃着火把，可一轮明月把银辉洒遍四野，灯火已属多余。车子跑得飞快；不必说，马卡姆先生是要争分夺秒，把伦敦远远甩在身后。在可怜的莱蒂感觉里，不多时，房屋便越来越稀疏，到后来干脆绝了迹。她几乎说不清自己身在何处：上回北去的一路，她什么也不曾留意。不多时她便望见一片荒原，一丛丛灌木点缀其上，在月光下投出长长的黑影，还有几株高高的落叶松，直插蓝宝石色的夜空。此外再没什么可看的了，而莱蒂也从没有比此刻更无心赏玩大自然的美景。她把斗篷又往身上拢紧了些，低头望着紧紧交握在膝头的双手。她不要哭，无论忍住泪水是一场多么难打的硬仗。身旁那头怪物，休想称了这份心。

马车一路前行，在坎坷的路面上颠簸震摇。车速放缓了些：在英格兰的大道上，谁也无法撒开了拼命狂奔；这些大路经不起那等用法。

他们穿过这片荒凉的原野已有一程，她忽觉捕捉到疾驰的马蹄声：那不是拉她赴厄运的这几匹马发出的。她耳朵才觉察这声音，霎时间便蹄声如雷，一声呼喝，车身猛地一歪，跟着好一阵纷乱的响动，马车被急急勒停。一声枪响；车夫座上爆出一声骇叫，与此同时，马卡姆先生手边车窗的玻璃被什么金属物件砸得粉碎，一支由纤细白皙的手握着的小巧金饰手枪，直指着马卡姆先生的心口。

===Original===

It had all happened so quickly that Markham, no less than Letitia, was taken quite by surprise. From the moment of the horses being pulled up to the moment of the breaking glass there had been no more time than sufficed to sit up exclaiming: “What’s toward?” Before Markham could pull the pistol from his pocket he was covered, and had perforce to sit perfectly still, glaring at that deadly barrel.

Letty’s heart beat fast. It was a highwayman, beyond all doubt, but she was not in the least afraid. Nothing could be worse than her elopement, and she was inclined to think that it would be better to be killed by a highwayman than to be married to Mr. Markham. If fortune smiled Mr. Markham might be killed, which would be an excellent thing. She sat up all agog with excitement, and stared through the broken glass at the man who held that pistol.

He was speaking. “Put up your hands!”

The voice made Letty jump, so oddly familiar was it. She leaned forward, trying to see the horseman’s face. There was a black mask over his eyes, and a tricorne was pulled low over his brow. He was a slight man, as far as she could see for the many-caped greatcoat that enveloped him. A wild hope sprang up in her breast: she peered at the stranger’s right hand, holding the pistol just inside the window. There was a glint of gold on the little finger. The hand moved a fraction, and the moonlight caught a ring, cunningly wrought.

===Chinese===

这一切来得太快，马卡姆同莉蒂西娅一样，被杀了个措手不及。从马被勒停到玻璃迸碎，中间只容得他坐直身子惊呼一声：「出了什么事？」不等马卡姆从口袋里拔枪，枪口已罩定了他，他只得一动不动地僵坐着，怒瞪那截要命的枪管。

莱蒂的心怦怦直跳。这是个拦路强盗，毋庸置疑，可她丝毫也不害怕。再没有比她这场私奔更糟的事了，她甚至倾向于认为，死在强盗手里，也比嫁给马卡姆先生来得强。倘若命运垂青，没准儿死的会是马卡姆先生，那可真是件大好事。她坐直了身子，满腔兴奋按捺不住，隔着砸碎的玻璃，直盯住那个持枪的人。

他开口了。「举起手来！」

这嗓音听得莱蒂猛地一惊——熟稔得蹊跷。她探身向前，想看清骑马人的脸。他双眼上蒙着黑色面罩，一顶三角帽（tricorne）低压在额前。隔着那件层层披肩、裹住全身的大衣，她只能看出他是个瘦削的人。一个狂野的希望在她胸中腾起：她眯眼细看那陌生人的右手，那只手握着手枪，正抵在车窗内。小指上有一点金光微闪。那手稍动了一动，月光正照上一枚戒指，做工精巧入微。

===Original===

“The Unknown!” Letty gasped, and began to tremble with excitement, relief, and a queer glad sensation she had never known till now.

“Hands up!” The voice was sharp and compelling. There was nothing for Markham to do but to raise his arms above his head. Inwardly he was cursing: this meant not only delay, but loss of all the money he had brought with him.

“Madam,”﻿—the Unknown was speaking to Letty, but he did not take his eyes from Markham’s face﻿—“oblige me by searching this gentleman’s pockets for a pistol.”

Letty pulled herself together. He spoke as to a stranger: she was not to know him then. Oh, here was romance indeed! Romance, and a rescue such as she had not dreamed to be possible. She pushed back her cloak, and with hands that shook, but with a businesslike determination in her small face, dived into the pocket nearest her. There was nothing there. She stretched an arm across Mr. Markham, taking care not to obtrude herself between his person and the Unknown’s pistol, and felt in the right-hand pocket. As her fingers closed round the butt of a pistol she felt Mr. Markham’s hard breathing; and guessed his impotent fury. With a little laugh caught in her throat she pulled out the weapon. “I have it, sir! I’ll take care of it!”

===Chinese===

「无名氏！」莱蒂倒抽一口气，激动、宽慰，还有一种她直到此刻才领教的奇妙欢喜，一齐涌上心头，教她轻轻颤抖起来。

「把手举起来！」那嗓音尖利而逼人。马卡姆无可奈何，只得双臂举过头顶。他心里正破口大骂：这一来非但误了行程，随身带来的全部银钱也要付诸流水。

「小姐，」——无名氏这话是对莱蒂说的，目光却须臾不离马卡姆的脸——「劳您驾，搜一搜这位先生的口袋，看有没有手枪。」

莱蒂定住了神。他说话的口气，像是对一个陌生人：这就是说，她不该认得他。哦，这才是罗曼史呢！罗曼史，外加一场她做梦也不曾想到可能的搭救。她拨开斗篷，双手抖着，小小的脸上却是一副干练的决断，一头探进离她最近的口袋。里头空无一物。她把一条手臂横过马卡姆先生身前，留神不把自己插到他的身子和无名氏的枪口之间，去掏右边那只口袋。指尖合拢在枪柄上时，她觉出马卡姆先生粗重的呼吸，也猜到了他无处发泄的狂怒。一声笑噎在喉咙里，她把那件家伙抽了出来。「找着了，先生！我会看好它的！」

===Original===

She saw the flash of white teeth. “Bravo, madam! Hold fast to it. Sir, be pleased to come down!”

The chafing, fidgeting horse was pulled back; the Unknown bent gracefully in the saddle, and his hand left the bridle to swing open the coach door. Letty sat grasping the pistol, and pointing it at Mr. Markham. Her eyes were bright, and her pretty mouth was set tightly. Mr. Markham took one look, with a vague notion of wresting the pistol from her, but decided that the further he got from a weapon held in such determined but inexpert hands the better. He jumped down on to the road, just as the Unknown sprang lightly from the saddle.

“You damned footpad!” Mr. Markham exploded. “By God, I’ll have you hunted down for this! You cowardly fools there, why didn’t you fire?” He had flung round angrily to look at the men on the box, and saw soon enough the reason for their inanition. In spite of that first shot no one seemed to be hurt, but the two men on the box sat huddled together, staring with popping eyes at the long barrel of a pistol held by a second horseman, who had them covered. The man on one of the leaders sat as still as the fretting horse would let him, and his gaze was as fixed and as fearful as his companion’s. On the road lay a heavy blunderbuss: there had evidently been no time to fire the cumbersome weapon, and it had been surrendered immediately. This second horseman was masked as well and greatcoated. Letty peeping out, could see only the line of a square jaw, and a stocky silhouette. He did not appear to be much interested in what his companion was about, but kept his head and his pistol turned towards the box of the coach.

The Unknown had flung off his greatcoat. “Oh, what an unkind spirit!” he mocked in answer to Mr. Markham. “But I’m generous: I offer you a fight, a fair fight, when I might shoot you like the dog you are. Come, where’s your sword, sir? Here’s the gracious moon to light us, and witnesses enough to see fair play!”

===Chinese===

她看见一口白牙倏然一闪。「好极了，小姐！握牢了别撒手。先生，请您赏光下车！」

那匹焦躁打转的马被勒退了几步；无名氏在鞍上优雅地一俯身，手离了笼头，顺势甩开车门。莱蒂坐在原处，攥着手枪，枪口指着马卡姆先生。她两眼放光，那张俏嘴抿得紧紧的。马卡姆先生看了一眼，起过一阵模糊的念头，想一把把枪夺下来，随即认定：这枪握在这样一只决心十足却全无章法的手里，他离得越远越好。他跳下车，落到路上，恰在此时，无名氏轻捷地翻身离鞍。

「你这该死的路劫贼！」马卡姆先生炸了。「上帝作证，我要叫人把你缉拿到案！你们那上头的胆小蠢货，为什么不开枪？」他怒冲冲地扭头去看车夫座上的人，很快就看清了他们呆若木鸡的缘故。头一枪虽已放响，却似乎无人受伤，只是车座上那两个人挤作一团，眼睛鼓突，呆瞪着第二个骑马人握着的长枪管——那杆枪正罩着他们。骑在前导双马之一上的那个，能挨着那匹躁马坐多稳便坐多稳，目光同他的同伴一样发直、一样惊怖。路上躺着一杆沉甸甸的大口径短铳（blunderbuss）：显然是来不及放这笨重家伙一响，便立刻缴了械。这第二个骑马人同样蒙着面罩、穿着大衣。莱蒂向外偷觑，只能望见一副方下巴的线条，和一个敦实的侧影。他似乎不大在意他同伴的所作所为，只把脑袋和手枪一直对着车夫座。

无名氏已把大衣甩脱。「哎呀，好一副不厚道的心肠！」他讥笑着回敬马卡姆先生。「可我心肠宽厚：我本可以像毙一条狗似的毙了你——你原就是条狗——却偏要请你打一场，一场公平的仗。来吧，先生，你的剑在哪里？这轮月亮多么和善，肯替我们照明，证人也尽够主持公道！」

===Original===

“Fight a damned cutthroat robber?” cried Markham. “If I’d a cane you should taste of it!”

The Unknown laughed merrily. “Should I, sir? Should I indeed? Keep him covered, madam!”

“I am!” avowed Letty, grasping her pistol tighter than ever.

The Unknown’s weapon was laid aside with his cloak. The plain buff coat he wore followed it, and the scabbard of his sword. “Come, sir, come! Will you not fight for the privilege of keeping the lady and the riches? Or shall I fleece you of all? What, must I call you coward?” Off came the heavy riding boots, and the elegant flowered waistcoat. He stood straight in the moonlight, a lithe figure in a white shirt, with fair hair caught in the nape of his neck, and a strip of black velvet hiding the upper part of his face. A naked sword was in his hand; he shook it in the air, and the steel flashed in the moonlight. “A fair duel, sir, and you are the larger man! Faint heart!” Again he laughed. “If I kill you the lady goes free but if you kill me you win all! Shall I rob you as you stand, or will you cross swords with me? Yours is the choice.”

“You kill me, you miserable little dwarf?” Markham cried. “You’ll fight, will you? You’re tired of life! Hand down my sword, girl, this instant! By God, I’ll teach you a lesson, you impudent dog!” He began to strip off his coat as he spoke, and kicked the buckled shoes from his feet.

===Chinese===

「同你这该死的杀人越货的强盗决斗？」马卡姆嚷道。「要是我手里有根手杖，先叫你尝尝它的滋味！」

无名氏快活地笑了。「是么，先生？当真么？拿枪看住他，小姐！」

「正看着呢！」莱蒂一字一顿地说，把手枪攥得比先前更紧。

无名氏把手枪搁在一边，同斗篷放在一处。他身上那件素净的米黄牛皮衣也随之脱下，还有他的剑鞘。「来吧，先生，来吧！你竟不愿为着保住这位小姐连同这份家财的权利一战么？还是要我把你搜刮个干净？怎么，非要我唤你一声懦夫不成？」沉重的马靴蹬脱下来，那件讲究的绣花马甲也脱了下来。他挺立在月光里，一身白衬衫衬出个轻捷的身形，金发在颈后束着，一条黑丝绒蒙住上半张脸。手中一柄出鞘的剑；他朝空中一振，剑刃在月光下寒光闪闪。「公平决斗，先生，何况你块头更大！胆小鬼！」他又笑了。「我杀了你，小姐便得自由；你杀了我，便赢得一切！是要我就这样站在这儿把你抢光呢，还是你肯同我剑锋相向？选择权在你。」

「你杀我，你这可怜巴巴的小矮子？」马卡姆嚷道。「你要打，是么？你活腻味了！把我的剑递下来，丫头，立刻！上帝作证，我要好好教训教训你，你这放肆的狗东西！」他一边说，一边动手脱外衣，把带扣的鞋从脚上踢蹬下去。

===Original===

The Unknown came to the coach door, and reached up a hand for the sword, and spread his fingers a moment for Letty to see the ring.

“I know! Oh, I know!” she whispered, looking down into the face that had haunted her dreams for so many nights past. “Kill him, oh, please kill him!”

“I will,” he promised, and took the sword from her trembling hold.

Mr. Markham stood ready now and snatched his rapier from the Unknown’s hand. “You asked for this!” he snarled. “You’ll regret it too late. I’m not a novice with the small sword! On guard!”

There was the briefest of salutes, and the blades rang together. Markham lunged in quarte; Letty had a moment’s sick apprehension and shut her eyes. They flew open the next instant, to see the Unknown disengaging adroitly.

===Chinese===

无名氏走到车门边，向上伸手来接剑，手指张开了一瞬，好教莱蒂看见那枚戒指。

「我知道！哦，我知道！」她悄声说，低头凝望这张多少个夜晚萦绕她梦魂的脸。「杀了他，哦，求你杀了他！」

「我会的。」他应许道，从她颤颤的握持里接过剑。

马卡姆先生此刻已准备停当，从无名氏手中一把夺过自己的剑。「这是你自己讨的！」他狞声说。「等你后悔可就晚了。使小剑（the small sword）我不是生手！预备！」

双方极简短地一举剑致意，剑刃随即铿然相击。马卡姆一记第四式（quarte）刺出；莱蒂心头掠过一阵令人作呕的惊悸，闭上了眼。下一瞬又霍然睁开，只见无名氏已轻巧地脱出纠缠。

===Original===

There was no sound on all this deserted heath but the scrape of steel; no movement save of those two figures on the grass, fighting sternly, desperately, with lives at stake.

The silver moonlight flooded the scene, and tinted it with an unreal ghostliness, glinting along the blue-grey blades, and touching the fair head of the Unknown, and the dark head of Markham.

To Letty, standing in the doorway of the coach, it was as a dream. Her wide eyes never left the graceful figure of the masked man; they followed every lightning thrust, and every dexterous parry. He was slight and small indeed, but he seemed to be made of wires, so agile were his movements, so unerring and untiring his arm. To see both men one must feel him to be hopelessly overweighted. Markham had the advantage in height, in reach, and in strength; he was a good swordsman besides, with a quick eye and a steady wrist. He had once killed a man in a duel, Letty knew.

But even to her, ignorant of swordplay, it was plain that the smaller and the lighter man had a wizard’s cunning with the rapier. His style was quite different from Markham’s; he was a miracle of swift grace and neat footwork, with a wrist like flexible steel, and eyes like a hawk’s to descry an opening. Fascinated, Letty followed the quick thrust and parry, and she saw the smile still on the Unknown’s lips.

There was a scuffle of blades; Letty’s hands flew to her mouth to press back an involuntary cry; Markham had lunged forward savagely, and for an awful moment Letty thought that his point must go home. But there was a swift parry, and barely had Markham recovered than the Unknown’s sword flashed forward. Forte touched foible, and Letty saw Markham disengage quickly.

===Chinese===

这片荒原上万籁俱寂，唯有钢铁的刮擦；再无半点动静，只有草地上那两条身影，凛然苦斗、以命相搏。

银色的月光漫过这片所在，给它染上一层虚幻的鬼气：在蓝灰的剑刃上流转生辉，映着无名氏的金发，也映着马卡姆的黑发。

在伫立车门口的莱蒂眼里，这一切恍如一梦。她一双睁大的眼睛，须臾不曾离开那蒙面人翩然的身影；追着每一记疾如闪电的突刺、每一招灵巧的格挡。他确实瘦小，可他整个人仿佛是钢丝拧成的：身法那般轻捷，那条手臂那般稳、准、不知疲倦。把两人放在一处看，任谁都要觉得他毫无指望地落了下风。马卡姆占了身高的便宜、臂展的便宜、力气的便宜；况且他剑术不弱，眼疾腕稳。他从前在决斗里杀过一个人，莱蒂是知道的。

可是哪怕她对剑术一窍不通，也看得分明：那个子较矮、身量较轻的一个，使起剑来自有巫师般的狡黠。他的路数同马卡姆全然两样；他简直是迅疾优美与干净步法的奇迹，腕子如柔韧的精钢，一双眼睛如鹰隼般觅出破绽。莱蒂看得入迷，追随着那一来一往的疾刺巧格，还看见无名氏唇边依旧噙着笑意。

剑刃一阵缠绞；莱蒂双手飞快掩上嘴，压下一声不由自主的惊呼；马卡姆凶猛地扑身直刺，有那么一刻可怕的工夫，莱蒂只道他的剑尖必已中的。却是一记疾如旋风的格挡，马卡姆脚跟尚未站稳，无名氏的剑已电光般递出。剑身强部（forte）搭上剑身弱部（foible），莱蒂只见马卡姆仓促抽剑脱开。

===Original===

She threw a glance round at the second masked man, and saw him intent too on the strange duel. And the pistol in his hand was pointing no longer at the men on the box: it covered Mr. Markham. John would have no compunction in shooting if aught befell his young master.

His lesser height and strength did not seem to discompose the Unknown; he showed no signs of tiring; he was fighting still with the same force and cunning; he even seemed to be pressing his opponent. There was a parry, and, it seemed to Letty, two simultaneous lunges. Mr. Markham thought he had found an opening, but as he lunged the Unknown’s sword shot out in a time-thrust quicker than the eye could follow, took Markham’s foible in a flickering parry, and passed on without a check to the heart. It was all over in the flash of an eyelid; dimly Letty realized that she had seen a marvellous piece of swordplay. The Unknown sprang back, gasping for breath; Markham seemed to crumple where he stood, and fell heavily to the ground.

Letty’s eyes rested on him, full of horror and amazement. Only an instant back he seemed on the point of killing his opponent, and now there he lay, a dark heap on the ground.

The Unknown was on his knee beside him, shutting him from Letty’s view; she stood still, clinging now to the frame of the door. After a minute the Unknown rose, and came to the coach. He was no longer smiling, and Letty saw the sweat glistening on his brow. She held out her hands to be helped from the coach. He put up his, and she sprang lightly down.

===Chinese===

她回眸瞥了一眼那第二个蒙面人，见他也在凝神注视这场奇异的决斗。而他手里的枪，已不再指着车夫座上的人：它罩住了马卡姆先生。万一他年轻的主人遭了什么不测，约翰（John）开枪不会有半分迟疑。

身高与力气的欠缺，似乎全未搅乱无名氏的心神；他不见半点疲态；仍以同样的劲道与狡黠接战；甚至仿佛还在步步进逼。一记格挡之后，在莱蒂看来，竟是两剑同时递出。马卡姆先生自以为觅得了破绽，可他刚一刺出，无名氏的剑已激射而出——一记时机突刺（time-thrust），快得目光难追，颤腕一格，搭上马卡姆的剑尖弱部，随即去势不减，直贯心口。一切都在一眨眼间了结；莱蒂恍恍惚惚地意识到，自己方才目睹了一场妙绝的剑技。无名氏跃身疾退，喘息不已；马卡姆却像当场散了架，颓然瘫软，重重栽倒在地。

莱蒂的目光停在他身上，满是惊骇与错愕。不过片刻之前，他还眼看就要取了对手的性命，此刻却躺在那里，成了地上一团黑影。

无名氏单膝跪在他身旁，把那番惨状挡在莱蒂的视线之外；她怔怔站着，此刻正紧抓着车门框。过了一分钟，无名氏起身，向马车走来。他脸上已没了笑意，莱蒂看见他额上的汗珠莹莹闪光。她伸出双手，请他扶她下车。他也举手来接，她便轻巧地跳了下来。

===Original===

“It’s over,” he said. “He was a villain, but he fought well.” He turned, and bent to pick up Markham’s coat. In a moment he had a paper in his hands, and bent his head to inspect it. He turned, and gave it to Letty. “Destroy that, Letitia. You know what it is.”

She hid it in the bosom of her gown. “Oh, thank you! thank you!” she whispered.

He held out his hand. “Remember that I am a highwayman!” he said. “Give me the pearls you wear. I will return them to you very soon now. Can you trust me?”

She unclasped the string. “Trust you! Oh, must you ask?”

He shook his head, smiling faintly, and held out his hand again. She put hers into it, and he bent to kiss it. “I shall come again,” he promised. “And next time you know what I shall demand.”

===Chinese===

「结束了。」他说。「他是个恶棍，可剑使得不坏。」他转过身，俯身拾起马卡姆的外衣。转眼间，他手里已多了一张纸，他低头端详。随即他回过身，把纸递给莱蒂。「把它毁掉，莉蒂西娅。你知道它是什么。」

她把纸藏进裙袍的胸襟里。「哦，谢谢你！谢谢你！」她悄声说。

他伸出手来。「记住，我是个拦路强盗！」他说。「把你戴的珍珠给我。要不了一会儿，我就把它们还给你。你能信得过我么？」

她解开搭扣，褪下珠串。「信你！哦，这还用问么？」

他摇了摇头，浅浅一笑，又伸出手来。她把手放进他掌心，他俯身吻了吻它。「我会再来的。」他许诺道。「下一回，你知道我会讨的是什么。」

===Original===

She nodded; her eyes were shining; she knew neither hesitation nor bashfulness. He would come to claim her; if he chose he might ride off with her over his saddle now.

He had pulled on his boots, and was struggling into his coat. In another few minutes he had leaped into the saddle again, and was bowing low over the horse’s withers. The fair hair was touched to silver by the moonlight; a jewel at his throat winked; and behind the mask Letty thought she saw his eyes gleaming blue. “*Au revoir, ma belle!*” he said, and straightened in the saddle. “Drive the lady back to town!” he said curtly to the coachman. There was a quick word in a strange tongue for the man with the pistol; the restless horse was wheeled about, the three-cornered hat was waved once to Letty. Then the horse bounded forward, across the heath; the pace quickened to a gallop, and in a few moments both riders had disappeared over the brow of a little hill.

Miss Letty rubbed her eyes; it was so like a dream, so unreal, that she began to doubt her senses. But the pearls were gone from her neck, and a few paces distant a dark figure lay on the ground﻿—a figure that had once been Gregory Markham.

===Chinese===

她点了点头；两眼灼灼生辉；她既不知踌躇，也不知羞怯。他自会再来讨她；他若乐意，此刻便可以把她横在鞍前，掳她同去。

他已重新蹬上马靴，正费力地往身上套外衣。再过几分钟，他已翻身跃上马鞍，在马耆甲上方深深俯身行礼。月光把他的金发染成银色；喉头一粒宝石眨眼般闪烁；面罩后面，莱蒂觉得自己看见他的双眼泛着蓝光。「*Au revoir, ma belle!*」他说罢，在鞍上坐直了身子。「把这位小姐送回城去！」他对车夫简短地说。又用一种奇怪的语言，对那持枪的汉子匆匆交代了一句；那匹躁动不宁的马被拨转过来，三角帽朝莱蒂挥了一挥。随即马儿一跃向前，横越荒原；速度渐催渐快，成了疾驰，不多时，两个骑手便翻过一座小丘的脊线，消失无踪。

莱蒂小姐揉了揉眼睛；这一切太像一场梦，太不真切了，她开始怀疑起自己的耳目。可她颈上的珍珠确已不在，几步开外的地上，横着一团黑影——那身形，曾经是格雷戈里（Gregory）·马卡姆。

## XXXI The Honourable Robin Tremaine / XXXI 尊贵的罗宾·特雷梅因

===Original===
People flocked to offer their congratulations to my Lord Barham, and to tell him how delighted they were that his claim﻿—which they had always felt to be true﻿—had been successfully proved. He received these visitors with his usual smile, and deprecated the suggestion that he had made a most handsome settlement on his cousin Rensley. How this news got about no one knew, for certainly Rensley said nothing about it. Rensley went abroad almost immediately, for his health. He cherished no kind feelings whatsoever towards my lord: he even talked wildly of bringing an action against him. Mr. Clapperly dissuaded him from so foolish a proceeding, and ventured to say that my lord had behaved towards the usurper with positive magnificence.

So my Lady Lowestoft thought, and wondered at it. My lord waved a lofty hand. “I am Tremaine of Barham,” he said. “A lesser man might have shown meanness.”

“You are superb, Robert,” she told him.

“Certainly,” he said.

===Chinese===
人们纷纷登门，向巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）道贺，告诉他，他的爵位主张——这一层他们向来就觉得是真的——如今已顺利证明属实，他们个个欢喜不尽。他带着惯常的微笑接待这些访客，至于说他给了堂亲伦斯利（Rensley）一笔极为可观的赠产，他只谦辞不居。这消息是怎么传出去的，谁也不知道，因为伦斯利对此分明一字不提。伦斯利几乎立刻便出国去了，说是为了调养身体。他对勋爵大人没有半分好感：甚至气头上扬言要告他一状。克拉珀利先生（Mr. Clapperly）劝住了他这桩蠢行，还斗胆进言，说勋爵大人对待那位僭夺爵位的人，实在是宽宏得无以复加。

洛斯托夫特夫人（My Lady Lowestoft）也作如是想，还为之啧啧称奇。勋爵大人高高地一摆手。「我乃巴勒姆的特雷梅因（Tremaine of Barham），」他说。「差一等的人，兴许就小家子气了。」

「您气派极了，罗伯特（Robert）。」她对他说。

「那是自然。」他说。

===Original===
In due course my lord took possession of his house in Grosvenor Square, and travelled down to Barham for a day or two, to warn the servants there of his coming later with guests. To his friends he announced that he did but await the advent of his children to proceed in state to the Court.

If he had been sought out before he was now inundated with invitations from all sides. He spent not a single evening alone: either he went out, or he gave select card-parties in his own house. A great many mammas courted him blatantly in expectation of the arrival of his son; Mr. Devereux told his friend Belfort that since that aunt of his showed every promise of being immortal he had a good mind to try his luck with the Honourable Prudence Tremaine. Charles Belfort opined that she would have a squint, or a face scarred by smallpox. He said that with the exception of Letty Grayson all heiresses were ill-favoured. Mr. Belfort had been very much put out by the defection of Peter Merriot, and could still talk of little else. He had no interest, he said, in my Lord Barham’s children.

It was not many days before a post-chaise, piled high with baggage, came to the house in Grosvenor Square, and drew up before the door. A slight young gentleman sprang out, followed by a French valet. One of my lord’s servants opened the door to this young gentleman, and inquired politely who he might be. The young gentleman said briskly: “My name’s Tremaine. I must suppose I am expected.”

===Chinese===
过了些时日，勋爵大人接收了格罗夫纳广场（Grosvenor Square）的宅邸，又下乡去巴勒姆小住一两日，知会那边的仆人，说他随后要携宾客前来。他向朋友们宣布，自己只等一双儿女到京，便要摆足仪仗，进宫朝觐。

从前还不过有人登门求见，如今四面八方的请帖已把他淹没。他没有一个晚上是独自消磨的：要么出门赴会，要么在自家宅子里开精选的小牌局。一大帮做母亲的明目张胆地巴结他，一心指望他儿子的到来；德弗罗先生（Mr. Devereux）告诉他的朋友贝尔福特（Belfort），既然他那位姑母方方面面都显出长命百岁的势头，他很有心去向尊贵的普鲁登丝·特雷梅因（the Honourable Prudence Tremaine）碰碰运气。查尔斯·贝尔福特则断言，她保准是个斜眼，再不然就是一张出过天花的麻脸。他说，除了莱蒂·格雷森（Letty Grayson），天下的女继承人没一个生得好看。彼得·梅里奥特（Peter Merriot）的弃他而去，叫贝尔福特先生懊恼不已，至今开口闭口还是这一桩。他说，巴勒姆勋爵的儿女，他毫无兴趣。

没过几天，一辆驿车堆着高高的行李，来到格罗夫纳广场的宅邸，在门前停稳。一位清瘦的年轻绅士跳下车来，身后跟着一名法国贴身男仆。勋爵大人的一名仆人替这位年轻绅士开了门，客客气气地请教他尊姓大名。年轻绅士脆生生地答道：「我姓特雷梅因。想必府上是在等我吧。」

===Original===
Indeed, it seemed so, for there was at once a bustle made. The numerous valises and boxes were brought into the house; a footman came bowing to inform Mr. Tremaine that his lordship was unfortunately out, but should be sent for in a trice, to White’s.

Mr. Tremaine refused this offer. Having drunk a glass of excellent Burgundy, brought by yet another footman, he announced his intention of setting forth himself in search of his father. Faith, one must face everyone sooner or later; then a’ God’s name let it be at once!

One of the lackeys at White’s escorted Mr. Tremaine to the cardroom, and stood for a moment by the door looking round for my lord. Robin paused beside him, holding his hat under his arm, and his handkerchief and snuffbox in the other hand. Several people looked up, wondering who the handsome young stranger might be. Mr. Belfort, dicing with Devereux and Orton, said:﻿—“Gad, that’s a devilish modish wig! Who is it?”

===Chinese===
看来确实如此，宅子里顿时忙乱起来。大大小小的行囊箱笼纷纷抬进屋来；一个男仆躬身前来禀报特雷梅因先生，勋爵大人不巧外出，但可即刻派人往怀特俱乐部（White's）去请。

特雷梅因先生谢绝了这番好意。又一名男仆端来一杯上好的勃艮第酒，他饮罢，宣布要亲自出动，去寻他的父亲。说真的，人早晚总要见人的；那么看在上帝份上，索性现在就见！

怀特俱乐部的一名听差把特雷梅因先生引到牌室，在门边站了片刻，环视着寻找勋爵大人。罗宾（Robin）在他身旁站定，帽子夹在臂弯里，另一只手拿着手帕和鼻烟盒。好几个人抬起头来，纳闷这位俊俏的年轻生客是谁。贝尔福特先生正同德弗罗和奥顿（Orton）掷骰子，说道：「天哪，好一顶时髦得要命的假发！那是哪位？」

===Original===
Sir Raymond looked round and met Robin’s eyes. “I don’t think I know him,” he said hesitatingly. “Yet﻿—there’s something faintly familiar in his face.”

Mr. Devereux put up his glass. “ ’Pon my soul, Bel, that’s a monstrous pretty fashion of lacing he has to his coat! A prodigiously modish young buck, I protest!”

At the next table Mr. Troubridge said:﻿—“Who’s the stranger? I seem to have seen that face before. A handsome boy, and carries himself well. A little arrogant, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
雷蒙德爵士回过头来，正与罗宾的目光相接。「我想我不认识他，」他迟疑地说。「不过——他这张脸隐隐有些眼熟。」

德弗罗先生举起单片眼镜。「我以灵魂起誓，贝尔（Bel），他外衣上那身镶绦的式样可真够漂亮的！好一个时髦透顶的浮华子弟，我敢说！」

邻桌上，特拉布里奇先生（Mr. Troubridge）说道：「那位生客是谁？我好像见过这张脸。俊俏的后生，仪态挺拔。也许有点儿傲气。」

===Original===
Certain, Robin carried himself well, and had his trim figure well set off by a marvellously cut coat of dark blue cloth. He appeared to have been travelling, for he wore top-boots, highly polished, on his small feet, and a sword at his side. His coat was heavily laced with gold, tight across the shoulders and at the waist, and spreading them into wide skirts, silklined, the cuffs very large and turned back almost to the elbow to show a profusion of Mechlin ruffles. His waistcoat, a dozen men of fashion noted at once, was of the very latest style; the lace at his throat was arranged to fall in cascades down his chest, and there was a sapphire pin glinting in it. His wig, at which Mr. Belfort, an expert in these matters, had exclaimed, must have come direct from Paris; the hat under his arm was richly edged with finest point. His blue eyes were cool; his mouth, though delicately curved, was firm enough; when he turned that arrogant profile towards Mr. Troubridge that gentleman said with greater emphasis than before:﻿—“Gad, yes! A remarkably handsome boy. A pity he is not taller.”

The lackey had perceived my lord over by the window, and pointed him out now to Robin. Robin went forward between the tables, and stood at his father’s elbow. “Sir﻿—”

My lord was playing picquet with my Lord March. He looked round and exclaimed. “My Robin!” He threw down his cards and sprang up. “My son!” he said joyously.

===Chinese===
确实，罗宾仪态挺拔，一身匀称的体格，又被一件剪裁精妙绝伦的藏青呢外衣衬托得益发好看。看样子他刚下旅途，因为一双小脚蹬着擦得锃亮的长筒马靴，腰间还佩着剑。他的外衣镶满金绦，肩头和腰身收得紧窄，往下展成宽大的裙式下摆，衬着绸里；袖口极大，翻折得几乎到肘，露出一叠叠繁盛的梅克林花边褶饰。他的马甲——十来位时髦人物一眼便看在眼里——正是最时新的式样；喉间的花边理成一叠叠小瀑布，直泻胸前，其间闪着一枚蓝宝石别针的微光。他那顶假发——此道行家贝尔福特先生已为之惊呼过了——准是直接从巴黎来的；臂下夹着的帽子，又镶着一圈顶精细的针绣花边。他的蓝眼睛是冷冷的；嘴唇虽弯得秀气，却相当坚毅；待他把那傲慢的侧脸转向特拉布里奇先生，那位先生说得比方才更笃定了：「天哪，正是！好个出挑的俊俏后生。可惜个子不够高。」

那听差已望见勋爵大人在窗那边，此刻指给罗宾看。罗宾从牌桌间穿行上前，站到他父亲的肘边。「先生——」

勋爵大人正同马奇勋爵（Lord March）打皮克牌。他回过头，失声惊呼。「我的罗宾！」他掷下手中的牌，霍地站起来。「我的儿子！」他喜不自胜地说。

===Original===
Robin stood bowing deeply before his father. “I’ve but this instant arrived, sir.” His lips brushed the back of my lord’s hand punctiliously. “I found you from home, and came to seek you here. You permit?”

My lord clasped his arm. “And I am from home when my Robin arrives! My Lord March, you will allow me to present to you my son?”

“So this is your son, is it, Barham?” My lord nodded in a friendly fashion to the grave young gentleman bowing so gracefully before him. His lordship was not, after all, so very far removed from Robin’s age, but he had the manner of a man of forty. “A very pretty youth, Barham. And are you just come from France, Tremaine?”

===Chinese===
罗宾站在父亲面前，深深躬身行礼。「我这才刚到，先生。」他一丝不苟地把嘴唇轻触勋爵大人的手背。「我见您不在家，便寻到这儿来了。您容许么？」

勋爵大人紧握住他的手臂。「我的罗宾到时，我竟偏偏不在家！马奇勋爵，可容我把犬子引见与您？」

「这就是令郎了，是么，巴勒姆？」马奇勋爵朝那位在自己面前行礼行得如此潇洒的、神情庄重的年轻绅士友好地点了点头。说到底，马奇勋爵的年纪同罗宾相去并不很远，作派却已是一副四十岁人的沉稳。「好俊俏的一个年轻人，巴勒姆。特雷梅因，你是刚从法国回来么？」

===Original===
“Just, sir.”

“I dare swear you have all the latest fashions at your fingertips then. Is it true they are wearing earrings in Paris?”

“I have occasionally seen them, sir. At balls a single earring is considered in some circles de rigueur.”

===Chinese===
「正是，先生。」

「那我敢打赌，最时新的花样你准是样样门儿清。巴黎那边当真时兴戴耳环么？」

「偶尔见过，先生。舞会上，有些圈子把单戴一只耳环奉为必不可少的规矩。」

===Original===
By this time nearly everyone in the cardroom had realized that the modish stranger must be my lord’s long looked-for son. Sir Raymond Orton said that it accounted for the familiarity of his face, and went to be introduced.

My lord presented his son with justifiable pride, and had the satisfaction of seeing him borne off to dice at Orton’s table. Mr. Belfort and Mr. Devereux received him with kindness, and made him welcome. He protested that he had no right to be in the club at all, but was told that that was nonsense. In a day or two he would of course be made a member. He was found to be well-versed in the ways of the world, and could tell an entertaining tale. Mr. Belfort enrolled him promptly in the numerous ranks of his intimates.

On his way out of the room Mr. Troubridge paused to lay a hand on Robin’s arm. “Barham’s son?” he said. “To be sure, we have all been most anxious to see Robin Tremaine.”

===Chinese===
这时，牌室里几乎人人都已明白，这位时髦生客必是勋爵大人盼了许久的儿子。雷蒙德·奥顿爵士（Sir Raymond Orton）说，怪不得那张脸透着眼熟，便过去请人引见。

勋爵大人带着当之无愧的骄傲把儿子一一引见，心满意足地看着他被半请半拉地弄到奥顿那一桌掷骰子去了。贝尔福特先生与德弗罗先生和和气气地接待他，热诚地接纳他。他推说自己根本无权踏进这俱乐部，却被告知这是傻话。过一两天，他自然会被吸纳为会员。众人发觉他深谙世故人情，又讲得一手有趣的轶闻。贝尔福特先生当即把他收进了自己那一长串密友的行列。

出牌室的时候，特拉布里奇先生驻足把一只手搭上罗宾的手臂。「巴勒姆的儿子？」他说。「可不是，我们大伙儿一直眼巴巴地盼着见罗宾·特雷梅因呢。」

===Original===
Robin rose to his feet, a hand on the back of his chair. “You are very kind, sir.”

“And have you brought your sister?” smiled Mr. Troubridge.

Robin’s brows rose. “My sister came over some time before me, sir. She is the guest of my Lady Enderby, at Dartrey.”

Mr. Molyneux, overhearing, gave a soundless whistle. So that was the charming visitor Tony had written about? Gad, but Tony had all the luck!

===Chinese===
罗宾一手扶着椅背，站起身来。「您太抬爱了，先生。」

「那令姐呢，也一道带来了么？」特拉布里奇先生含笑问。

罗宾扬了扬眉。「家姐比我早过来些时日，先生。她如今在达特里（Dartrey）做恩德比夫人（Lady Enderby）的宾客。」

莫利纽克斯先生（Mr. Molyneux）无意间听见，无声地吹了声口哨。原来那就是托尼（Tony）信里写过的那位可人的客人？天哪，好运全叫托尼占去了！

===Original===
Robin very soon left the club in company with his father. My lord had presented him to everyone: several people said that they had thought his face vaguely familiar from the first, and were sure it must have been his likeness to his father. Robin bowed, and suppressed an inward smile.

Once outside the club my lord became more rapturous still. “You are perfect, my Robin! Perfect! There is not a soul will suspect. You had no trouble?”

“None, sir. And you?”

“How should I, my son? Need you ask?”

===Chinese===
罗宾不久便随父亲一同离开了俱乐部。勋爵大人已把他向所有人一一引见：有几位说，打一开头就觉得他这张脸隐约眼熟，如今拿准了，那必是像他父亲的缘故。罗宾躬身致意，按下了心头的笑意。

一出俱乐部，勋爵大人愈发喜不自胜。「你真是完美无缺，我的罗宾！完美！没有一个活人会起疑。你一路没遇上麻烦吧？」

「没有，先生。您呢？」

「我会有什么麻烦，我的儿子？这还用问么？」

===Original===
“I suppose not. Am I to understand, sir, you are in very sooth Tremaine of Barham?”

My lord smiled. “My Robin, confess you have doubted me!”

“Yes, sir. I do not know that I am to be blamed.”

“Certainly you are to be blamed. You who have known me from your cradle!”

“For that very reason, sir, I doubted.”

===Chinese===
「也是。那我可否这样理解，先生——您当真就是巴勒姆的特雷梅因？」

勋爵大人微笑了。「我的罗宾，招了吧，你疑心过我！」

「是的，先生。只是我不知道这该怪我。」

「当然该怪你。你可是打襁褓里起就认识我的！」

「恰恰是为了这个缘故，先生，我才疑心。」

===Original===
“Ah, you should have had faith in me, my Robin!”

“I had, sir﻿—in your ingenuity.”

My lord shook a finger. “I saw from the outset that you doubted. I might have convinced you. I chose rather to confound you, as I do now.”

Robin blinked. “Let me have a plain answer, sir. Is this all a trick, or are you Tremaine?”

“Of course I am Tremaine,” said his lordship, with a calmness more convincing than all his heroics.

===Chinese===
「唉，你本该对我怀有信心才是，我的罗宾！」

「我有的，先生——对您的足智多谋。」

勋爵大人摇了摇一根手指。「我打一开始就看出你在疑心。我本可以说服你的。我却宁可叫你惊愕失色，一如眼下。」

罗宾眨了眨眼。「请给我一句明白话，先生。这究竟是场把戏，还是您真是特雷梅因？」

「我当然是特雷梅因。」勋爵大人说道，那份平静，比他所有的一切豪言壮举都更有说服力。

===Original===
Robin turned his head to stare. He drew a deep breath. “Give me time, sir. You have certainly confounded me. I confess, I thought it a trick.”

My lord laughed in gentle triumph. “I am always an unknown quantity, my Robin. You should have thought of that. But if I were not in very truth Tremaine of Barham﻿—which I am﻿—I should stand precisely where I stand today. Therein lies my greatness. Believe it!”

“Oh, I do, sir. I’m of the opinion you might be King of England if you choose.”

My lord considered this. “It is possible, my son,” he said seriously. “I do not say that it would be altogether beyond my powers. But there would be difficulties﻿—great difficulties.”

“Lord, let’s remain content as we are!” said Robin, alarmed. “I’m satisfied, sir.”

“I told you, and you would not believe it, that we had come to the end of our wanderings!”

===Chinese===
罗宾转过头去，直勾勾地望着他。他深深吸了一口气。「容我缓缓神，先生。您确实叫我惊愕失色了。我招认，我原先只当那是场把戏。」

勋爵大人带着温和的得意笑了。「我一向是个未知数，我的罗宾。这一点你早该想到。不过，纵然我当真不是巴勒姆的特雷梅因——而我正是——今日我立足之地，也会分毫不差。我的伟大正在于此。信了吧！」

「哦，我信，先生。依我看，只要您愿意，英格兰国王您也做得。」

勋爵大人把这话掂量了一番。「也未尝不可能，我的儿子，」他一本正经地说。「我不能说这就全然超出了我的能力。只是会有难处——天大的难处。」

「老天，咱们还是安于现状吧！」罗宾惶恐地说。「我心满意足了，先生。」

「我早告诉过你，我们漂泊流离的日子到头了，可你就是不肯信！」

===Original===
“I doubt I shall wake up soon,” said Robin.

He was present at a dinner party my lord gave that night, and my lord’s guests decided that he was a charmingly mannered young man. My lord said afterwards:﻿—“You will be almost as great a success as your father, my Robin!”

“Impossible, sir,” said Robin, stirring the dregs of punch in the big silver bowl.

“I do not deny it,” said my lord. His eyes rested fondly on his son’s fair face. “Tomorrow, Robin, you will go down to Dartrey and bring my Prudence to Barham.”

Came a slight frown. “The next day, an it please you, sir. I’ve affairs of my own tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「我看我八成很快就要从这梦里醒过来了。」罗宾说。

当晚勋爵大人设宴，他在席间作陪；席上宾客一致断定，他是个风度翩翩的可喜青年。事后勋爵大人说：「你日后几乎要同你父亲一样大出风头了，我的罗宾！」

「万万不能，先生。」罗宾说着，搅动大银钵里潘趣酒的残淀。

「我不否认。」勋爵大人说。他慈爱的目光停在儿子俊美的脸上。「明日，罗宾，你下乡去达特里，把我的普鲁登丝接到巴勒姆来。」

他眉头微微一蹙。「若蒙您允准，后日吧，先生。明日我有几件自己的事要办。」

===Original===
These affairs took him, on the next day, to the house of Sir Humphrey Grayson. Sir Humphrey received him in his library, and looked somewhat at a loss. “Mr. Tremaine?” he said. Robin bowed. “I have to suppose﻿—my Lord Barham’s son?” Again Robin bowed. “Er﻿—you have business with me?” Sir Humphrey was puzzled.

Robin looked straightly at him. “Sir Humphrey, may I ask what you may perhaps think an impertinent question?﻿—Are you in your daughter’s confidence?”

“Fully, sir.” Sir Humphrey was a little stiff.

“She has perhaps mentioned to you a man calling himself *l’Inconnu*?”

Sir Humphrey started. “Sir?”

“I am he,” said Robin quietly.

===Chinese===
次日，这几件事便把他带到了汉弗莱·格雷森爵士（Sir Humphrey Grayson）府上。汉弗莱爵士在书房里接待了他，神情有些无所适从。「特雷梅因先生？」他说。罗宾躬了躬身。「那我得这样设想——是巴勒姆勋爵的公子？」罗宾又躬了躬身。「呃——您找我有事？」汉弗莱爵士满腹疑惑。

罗宾直视着他。「汉弗莱爵士，可否容我问一个您或许觉得冒失的问题？——令嫒的心事，您全然知情么？」

「全然知情，先生。」汉弗莱爵士的回答有点生硬。

「她或许向您提起过一个自称 *l'Inconnu*（无名氏）的人？」

汉弗莱爵士猛地一震。「您说什么？」

「正是在下。」罗宾平静地说。

===Original===
For a moment Sir Humphrey could find no words. This slim boy﻿—that magic swordsman Letty raved about! No highwayman, no outlaw, as he had feared, but a Viscount’s son and heir! “You?” he gasped. “You are the man who saved my daughter? Barham’s son! You will excuse me, sir; I am completely taken aback! Are you indeed my daughter’s mysterious champion?”

“A grander term than I should have chosen, sir. I am the Unknown who killed Markham, certainly. But I beg you won’t mention it.”

“You are he! Sir, you must let me take your hand! I owe you more than I can ever hope to repay. Indeed, I scarcely know how to thank you, for words seem to be inadequate!”

Robin broke in, flushing. “Sir Humphrey, you will agree that gratitude is out of place when I tell you that I love your daughter. I am here today to ask you for your permission to pay my addresses to her in form.”

===Chinese===
汉弗莱爵士一时间找不出话来。这个清瘦的少年——就是莱蒂念叨得神魂颠倒的那位神奇剑客！不是路劫匪，不是亡命徒——全然不是他担心的那种——而是一位子爵的嗣子！「你？」他倒吸一口气。「你就是搭救小女的那个人？巴勒姆的儿子！您多包涵，先生；我实在是惊得措手不及！您当真就是小女那位神秘的守护骑士？」

「这称呼比我自个儿会挑的隆重多了，先生。杀了马卡姆（Markham）的那个无名氏，确实是在下。只是恳请您千万别声张。」

「真是您！先生，务必容我握一握您的手！我欠您的恩情，一辈子也报答不尽。说实在的，我简直不知如何道谢才好，言语都显得太苍白了！」

罗宾涨红着脸插了进来。「汉弗莱爵士，等您听我说出我爱您的女儿，您也会同意，道谢的话就大可不必了。我今日登门，是恳求您俯允我正式向她求婚。」

===Original===
Sir Humphrey wrung his hand anew. His feelings were almost too much for him. At one moment he was under the gloomy conviction that the late appalling scandal had ruined his Letitia forever; at the next a brilliant match for her was proposed to him. Her position in the world would be honourable beyond his wildest hopes, and no one would dare to talk scandal of the prospective Viscountess of Barham. He gave Robin to understand that he had a father’s blessing and suggested that he should send Letty to him at once.

Robin begged him, with a dancing smile, to lose no time in so doing. Sir Humphrey went off with quite a jaunty step.

In a few moments the door opened again to admit Letty. She was dressed all in primrose taffety, with a ribbon through her curls. Her father had not told her who waited to see her; there was a wondering expression in the brown eyes, and she looked doubtfully at Robin.

He stood in the middle of the room, watching her, and said no word. The puzzled gaze ran over him slowly; a little hand stole to Letty’s breast and her eyes widened. The slight, strong figure was surely familiar. She saw him put a hand to his pocket, and bring out a string of pearls. On his little finger was a gold ring cunningly wrought.

===Chinese===
汉弗莱爵士再一次紧握他的手。他的心绪激荡得几乎承受不住。前一刻，他还沉在阴郁的定见里，认定新近那桩骇人听闻的丑闻已把他家莉蒂西娅（Letitia）的名声毁了个干净；下一刻，一门耀眼的亲事便当面提了出来。她日后在世间的地位，将体面得远超他最大胆的期许，再没有人敢对未来的巴勒姆子爵夫人飞短流长。他赐给罗宾一位父亲的祝福，并提议即刻把莱蒂给他唤来。

罗宾含着跃动的笑意，恳请他千万莫要耽搁。汉弗莱爵士迈着格外轻快的步子去了。

不多时，门又开了，迎进来莱蒂。她穿一身樱草黄的塔夫绸，鬈发间穿了一根缎带。父亲没有告诉她候着的是谁；那双棕色眼睛里带着纳罕的神情，将信将疑地望着罗宾。

他立在房间中央，望着她，一语不发。那困惑的目光缓缓地在他身上逡巡；一只小手悄悄移上莱蒂的胸口，她的眼睛越睁越大。这清瘦而结实的身影，分明好生眼熟。她看见他一只手伸进衣袋，取出一条珍珠项链。他的小指上，戴着一枚打造得精巧入微的金戒指。

===Original===
“It is you!” she said, little above a whisper. Then she saw the fascinating smile, and the pearls held out to her, and she came forward in a stumbling run. “Oh, you have come at last!” she said, on a sob, and found herself in his arms. The pearls dropped unheeded to the floor.

“I have come as I told you I should and you know what I demand!” he said in her ear. “Letty, will you marry me?”

She breathed a shy yes; she was clinging to him, and she put up her face to be kissed. All dreams had come true for her.

The arms tightened about her. “Ah, you wonderful girl!” Robin said. “You do not even know my name!”

===Chinese===
「是你！」她说，声音比耳语高不了多少。随即她看见了那摄人的微笑，看见了朝她递来的珍珠，便跌跌撞撞、连跑带颠地迎上前去。「哦，你到底来了！」她带着一声呜咽说，人已在他怀里。珍珠项链掉在地板上，谁也顾不上理会。

「我说过我会来，我这不来了么，你知道我要的是什么！」他在她耳边说。「莱蒂，你肯嫁给我么？」

她怯生生地吐出一个「肯」字；她偎在他身上，仰起脸来待他亲吻。她所有的梦，都成真了。

那双手臂把她搂得更紧了。「啊，你这奇妙的姑娘！」罗宾说。「你连我的名字都还不知道呢！」

===Original===
“I love you.”

“I worship you,” Robin said, and lifted one of her hands to his lips. “Do you love me enough to forgive, Letitia?”

His voice sounded anxious. She pulled his hand to her own lips. “Forgive you! I have nothing, nothing to forgive you!”

“Ah, but you have!” He put her gently from him. “Look at me! Look at me well, Letty!”

She was blushingly rosily, and dropped shy eyelids. He said more insistently:﻿—“Look into my face, Letitia, and tell me then if you have nothing to forgive.”

The long lashes fluttered upwards; the brown eyes were misty. “What is it you mean?” Letty asked.

===Chinese===
「我爱你。」

「我崇拜你。」罗宾说着，把她的一只手举到唇边。「你爱我，爱得足以原谅我么，莉蒂西娅？」

他的嗓音听去发急。她把他的手拉到自己唇边。「原谅你！我没有什么、没有什么要原谅你的呀！」

「啊，有的！」他轻轻把她从身前推开些。「看着我！好好看着我，莱蒂！」

她双颊红霞飞起，羞答答地垂下眼帘。他愈发急切地说：「看着我的脸，莉蒂西娅，然后再告诉我，你是不是当真没有什么要原谅。」

长睫毛扑闪着扬起来；棕色的眼睛蒙着一层水雾。「你这话是什么意思？」莱蒂问。

===Original===
“You don’t know me? You don’t recognize me? Look at me well, child?”

She stared full into his face, blankly at first, and then with dawning astonishment. “But﻿—but﻿—oh no, you could not be!”

“Could not be what?”

“Another brother﻿—another brother of Kate Merriot’s,” she ventured. “You﻿—it is the eyes﻿—and the nose﻿—and﻿—”

“I am not her brother,” Robin said. “Try again, Letty. You come near the truth.”

She fell back a pace. “You are not﻿—oh, you cannot be﻿—no, no, how could you be?”

===Chinese===
「你不认得我？你认不出我？好好看着我，孩子？」

她直勾勾地盯进他的脸，起初一片茫然，继而惊异渐醒。「可是——可是——哦不，你不可能是！」

「不可能是什么？」

「另一个哥哥——凯特·梅里奥特（Kate Merriot）的又一个哥哥，」她试着说。「你——是这双眼睛——还有这鼻子——还有——」

「我不是她的哥哥。」罗宾说。「再猜猜，莱蒂。你离真相不远了。」

她退后了一步。「你不是——哦，你不可能是——不，不，你怎么可能是？」

===Original===
“I am Kate Merriot,” Robin said, and waited, his eyes on her face.

Letty was as pale now as she had been rosy. “You﻿—you? A woman? You acted﻿—But it can’t be! Kate was a woman!”

He shook his head; he was no longer smiling.

“Oh!” cried Letty. “Oh, the things I must have said﻿—” She broke off in distress.

“I swear on my honour you said naught to Kate you would not have said to a man!” he said quickly.

Letty was staring at him in amazement. “It was not fair!” she said. “You might have told me!”

===Chinese===
「我就是凯特·梅里奥特。」罗宾说，随即静候着，目光落在她脸上。

莱蒂此刻的脸色，白得同方才的红润恰成一对。「你——你？女的？你原来是在演——可这不可能！凯特分明是个女的呀！」

他摇了摇头；他脸上已没了笑意。

「哦！」莱蒂叫道。「哦，我从前必定说过不知多少话——」她难过地说不下去了。

「我以名誉为誓，你对凯特说过的话，没有一句是你不会对一个男人才说的！」他赶忙道。

莱蒂惊愕地瞪着他。「这不公平！」她说。「你本可以告诉我的！」

===Original===
“Will you let me explain?” he asked. “Won’t you hear me?”

Letty came nearer. “Yes, please explain,” she said in a small tearful voice. “But﻿—but I wish you had trusted me!”

He held out his hand, and she put hers into it. “I wish I had, Letitia. But I had been schooled to tell no secrets. And this one had my life at stake.”

Her lips formed an O. “Tell me!” she begged. “You know I forgive you anything. And I would never, never betray you.”

“Beloved!” He caught her to him. “I hardly dared to think that you could forgive so hateful a deception.”

===Chinese===
「你肯容我解释么？」他问。「你不愿听我说么？」

莱蒂凑近了些。「好，请你解释吧。」她用细细的、含哭腔的声音说。「可是——可是我但愿你当初信得过我！」

他伸出手去，她便把自己的手放进了他的掌心。「我也但愿当初如此，莉蒂西娅。可我从小受的调教，是绝不泄露秘密。而这一桩，押上的是我的性命。」

她的双唇嘟成了一个 O 形。「讲给我听吧！」她央求道。「你知道我什么都能原谅你。而且我永远、永远不会出卖你。」

「心爱的！」他把她一把搂进怀里。「我几乎不敢设想，你竟能原谅这么可恨的一场欺瞒。」

===Original===
She hung her head. “You forget﻿—you are the Unknown hero,” she confided shyly.

“There’s very little of the hero about me, child; I’m an escaped Jacobite.”

Her head came up; her eyes sparkled. “And I thought it romantic to elope with that odious Markham!” she cried. “Tell me all about it, please!”

At that Robin went off into a peal of laughter. She was surprised. “Why, you did not suppose I should mind, did you?” she inquired.

“I ought to have known,” Robin said, and swept her off her feet. “My darling, my name is Robin, and I’m an adventurer! Will you still marry me?”

===Chinese===
她垂下了头。「你忘了——你是那位无名氏英雄。」她羞答答地吐露。

「我身上实在没多少英雄气概，孩子；我是个亡命在外的詹姆士党人。」

她一下子抬起头来；眼睛亮晶晶的。「我还只当同那个可恶的马卡姆私奔才算罗曼蒂克呢！」她叫道。「求你把这一切都讲给我听！」

这话惹得罗宾朗声大笑起来。她吃了一惊。「哎呀，你总不至于以为我会介意吧？」她问道。

「我早该知道的。」罗宾说着，一把将她拦腰抱起。「我的宝贝，我的名字叫罗宾，我是个冒险家！你还肯嫁我么？」

===Original===
“I like your name, and I should love to be an adventuress,” said Letty. “May I be one?”

“Alack, you are more like to be a Viscountess,” Robin said, and sat down with her on his knee.

The tale took some time in the telling, and it left Letty wide-eyed and amazed. When she heard that Peter Merriot was Prudence Tremaine, she gasped, and gasped again. At the end for a while she could only bewail the fact that she had not known it all before.

“And Tony knew? *Tony?*”

“My dear, it was Fanshawe rescued her from the hands of the Law,” Robin said. “He carried her off to his sister, and I’m off to fetch her tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
「我喜欢你的名字，我也乐意做个女冒险家。」莱蒂说。「我可以做么？」

「唉，你多半要做子爵夫人呢。」罗宾说着，坐下来把她抱在膝上。

这段故事讲了些时候才讲完，听得莱蒂双眼圆睁、惊叹连连。听到彼得·梅里奥特就是普鲁登丝·特雷梅因，她倒抽了一口气，又倒抽一口气。讲完之后有一阵子，她只顾哀叹，自己竟没能早些知道这一切。

「连托尼都知道？*托尼*？」

「亲爱的，正是范肖（Fanshawe）把她从官府手里救出来的。」罗宾说。「他把她带到了他妹妹那儿，我明日便动身去接她。」

===Original===
Letty stammered a little. “T-Tony tied up those m-men? T-Tony stopped the c-coach? Why-why﻿—”

“He’s not so stolid as you thought,” teased Robin. “The truth is he has an ambition to marry her.”

“Oh, and I thought he wanted to marry you!” Letty cried. “And all the while he knew, and﻿—oh, ’tis the most amazing thing I ever heard! It is wonderful, Robin! I am very glad, for I like Tony vastly. But your sister to play the man.﻿—She must be monstrous brave and clever!”

“Like her brother,” bowed Robin. “For myself I have a weakness for a fairy-like creature with brown eyes, but I confess Sir Anthony is fortunate. My Prue’s a dear creature.”

“And﻿—and you deceived me grossly!” Letty said, but she did not sound angry. “Goodness, will your sister ever forgive me? ’Twas my fault she was taken by the Law for I told those odious men you had brown hair and were of medium height! But I never, never thought they would seize on Mr.﻿—I mean, on your sister. Robin, are you sure you are quite safe now?”

===Chinese===
莱蒂有点结巴起来。「托——托尼捆住了那几个男——男人？托——托尼拦下了马——马车？怎——怎么会——」

「他不像你想的那样木头木脑。」罗宾打趣道。「实情是，他正一门心思要娶她呢。」

「哦，我还只当他想要娶你呢！」莱蒂叫道。「原来他从头到尾都知道，还有——哦，这是我平生听过的最叫人惊奇的事了！真是太妙了，罗宾！我高兴极了，因为我顶喜欢托尼。可让你姐姐来扮男人。——她必定胆大得惊人，聪明得也惊人！」

「随她弟弟。」罗宾躬身道。「至于我自己，我一向偏爱生着褐眼睛的、仙子似的人儿，不过我承认，安东尼爵士实在有福气。我的普鲁（Prue）是个人见人爱的可人儿。」

「还有——还有你可把我骗苦了！」莱蒂说，但听口气并不恼。「天哪，你姐姐还会原谅我么？她被官府抓走，全是我的不是，因为我告诉那些可恶的家伙，说你是褐色头发、中等身材！可我万万、万万没想到，他们会抓走那位先生——我是说，抓走你姐姐。罗宾，你如今确信自己彻底安全了么？」

===Original===
“Quite sure, child. Robin Lacey is no more. Here is only Robert Tremaine.”

“And no one would ever guess you were Kate,” Letty said. “Even I did not guess until you made me look at you, and then I could not credit it. Oh Robin, Robin, I knew you would come again, but I have been so miserable! There has been the horridest scandal and aunt is dreadfully cross.”

“But now,” Robin said, holding her close, “it is for me to see that you are happy ever after. And I will see to it.”

“I am happy,” Letty said into his shoulder. A thought came to her; she lifted her head, and said in a voice of unholy glee:﻿—“And aunt shall see that I am not in the least ruined for life! She will look very silly when she knows I am to be a Viscountess one day!”

===Chinese===
「彻底确信，孩子。罗宾·雷西（Robin Lacey）已经不复存在。这儿站着的，只有罗伯特·特雷梅因。」

「而且谁也决计猜不到你就是凯特。」莱蒂说。「连我都没猜出来，直到你逼着我好好看你，我才敢相信。哦罗宾，罗宾，我知道你还会回来的，可我一直苦得不行！外头闹了顶顶可怕的丑闻，姑母又气得要命。」

「不过如今，」罗宾搂紧了她，说道，「保你从此幸福到老，这件事归我管了。我一定办到。」

「我幸福。」莱蒂贴着他的肩窝说。一个念头涌上心头；她抬起头，用一种不怀好意的欢天喜地的声调说：「还有，得叫姑母瞧瞧，我这辈子一点儿也没被毁掉！等她知道我日后要做子爵夫人，她那副样子一定傻透了！」

## XVIII The Large Gentleman Is Awake / XVIII 大块头绅士醒了

===Original===
There would be cards at Fanshawe’s house, Prudence guessed; a fair number of young bucks might be counted on to be present; and her frustrated duel with Rensley must be sure of receiving notice.

She chose, at random, a coat of peach satin from her wardrobe, and found a fine waistcoat embroidered in silver to wear with it. Robin came to dredge powder on to her brown locks, and was busy with hot irons for a while. Coaxing a rolling curl into place, Robin said:﻿—“Leave early, and have no private talk with Fanshawe. It’s my belief it’s a quixotic gentleman with no other mind than to step between a callow youth and death. But it’s as well to have a care.”

Prudence agreed to the first part of this speech, but held her peace for the rest. No use in alarming Robin, but she felt there might be more in the large gentleman’s mind than her brother guessed. She waited patiently for Robin to finish tying the black ribbon in her neck, and rose afterwards to be helped into her coat. Her glance strayed to the mirror, and showed satisfaction. Faith, she made a neat young gentleman. Who should think more? She slipped a ring on to her finger, and her snuffbox into one of the great pockets of her coat. Her stockings seemed to her to be rolled too loosely above the knee; she bent to rectify the fault; gave a final pat to the ruffles about her throat, and sallied forth to the waiting chair.
===Chinese===
范肖（Fanshawe）府上少不了要设牌局，普鲁登丝（Prudence）猜想着；到场的准有一大批纨绔少年；而她那场被搅黄的与伦斯利（Rensley）的决斗，保管逃不过众人的议论。

她从衣橱里随手拣了一件桃红色缎面外套，又找出一件绣银线的精致马甲来配它。罗宾（Robin）过来给她的棕发扑粉，又拿着热卷发钳忙活了一阵。一面把一绺卷发拢得服服帖帖，罗宾一面说：「早点儿告辞，别同范肖单独密谈。依我看，那是位堂吉诃德式的绅士，除了插到一个乳臭未干的小子和死亡中间去，再没有别的念头。不过小心些总归没错。」

普鲁登丝依了这话的前一半，后一半却只字不提。惊动罗宾没有用处，可她觉得，大块头绅士心里盘算的，兴许比她弟弟猜的多。她耐心等罗宾替她系好颈前的黑缎带，随后起身让人伺候着穿上外套。她的目光溜到镜子上，流露出满意之色。说真的，她扮成个俊俏利落的年轻绅士。还想怎么样呢？她把一枚戒指套上手指，把鼻烟盒塞进外套一只大口袋。她觉得长筒袜在膝上卷得松了些，便弯下腰去把这毛病纠正了；又对着颈间的褶边最后拍了两下，这才出门上那候着的轿椅去。

===Original===
The house in Clarges Street was strangely quiet. As she gave her hat and cloak into the servant’s care she listened for sound of voices, but none came. The lackey went before her to the door of Sir Anthony’s library, flung it wide, and sonorously called her name.

Sir Anthony was standing alone before the fireplace, where a small wood fire burned. There was no one else in the room. He came forward to greet Prudence, took her hand a moment, and asked a jovial question. She answered in kind, and realized with his next words that she was to be his only guest.

“I’ve positively no entertainment to offer you, excepting a hand at picquet after dinner,” smiled Fanshawe. “I feel I invite you under false pretences, but you’ll forgive me.”
===Chinese===
克拉吉斯街（Clarges Street）的宅邸静得出奇。她把帽子和外氅交到仆人手上照管时，侧耳去捕捉人声，却一点也没有。听差在前头引路，到得安东尼爵士书房门前，把门大大推开，朗声通传她的名号。

安东尼·范肖爵士独自站在壁炉前，炉膛里燃着一小堆柴火。房间里再没有别人。他上前迎接普鲁登丝，握了一会儿她的手，快活地问了一句什么。她也照样快活地答了，而他接下来的一句话便让她明白：今晚她是他唯一的客人。

「除了饭后一局皮克牌（picquet），我实在拿不出一点消遣来款待您。」范肖微笑道，「我自觉是名不副实地把您请来的，还望您海涵。」

===Original===
“Why, I’m pleased to have it so, sir!” There was not much truth in that, but one must say something of the sort, she supposed. She paused. A word must be said also of his strange behaviour of yesterday, since it concerned her so nearly. There was not a tremor in her voice as she spoke: nothing but a mixture of amusement and some reproof. “I have a quarrel with you, Sir Anthony. You must be aware of it.”

He pulled forward a chair for her, and himself stood leaning with his broad shoulders against the mantelshelf. “Faith, not I,” he answered. “Have I offended you?”

One of her long fingers played with the fob of her snuffbox. She looked up tranquilly into the gentleman’s inscrutable, good-humoured countenance. “Well, sir, Mr. Devereux is of the opinion I might call you out,” she said, and the twinkle was in her grave eyes.

“God forbid, little man! What have I done to incur this wrath?”
===Chinese===
「哎呀，这样再合我意不过了，爵士！」这话里实在没几分真，可这种场合总得说上这么一句，她心想。她顿了顿。他昨天那番古怪行径，也须提上一句，毕竟这事同她切身相干。她开口时嗓音没有一丝震颤：只有几分打趣，掺着几分责备。「我同您有一笔账要算，安东尼爵士。您心里一定有数。」

他为她拖过一把椅子，自己则端着宽阔的肩膀，倚在壁炉架上。「说真的，我没有。」他答道，「难道我哪里冒犯了你？」

她修长的一根手指拨弄着鼻烟盒的链坠。她安详地抬眼，望进这位绅士那张高深莫测、一团和气的脸。「是这样的，爵士，德弗罗（Devereux）先生认为，我大可以约您出去较量一场。」她说，严肃的眼睛里闪着一点狡黠。

「上天不容，小个子！我做了什么，招来这雷霆之怒？」

===Original===
“You must know, sir, that I had an engagement this morning to meet Mr. Rensley out at Grey’s Inn Fields. In this I’m baulked by Sir Anthony Fanshawe. I can’t pretend to be pleased.”

She had the feeling she was being watched all the time. He smiled a little, and made a slight bow. “Oh, I cry your pardon, Mr. Fire-Eater. But your complaints were better addressed to Rensley than to me.”

Prudence said coolly:﻿—“You may be very sure Mr. Rensley will hear from me just so soon as he leaves the surgeon’s care.” It seemed to her that the straight brows rose in momentary surprise. She went on. “Charles is of the opinion I can’t meet the man, but for myself I conceive that so far from considering myself debarred from fighting him after this insult I have the more reason. If Charles won’t act for me﻿—faith, his sense of propriety in these matters is prodigious!﻿—may I call on you, sir?” This was something of a bold move, to be sure, but by the time Mr. Rensley was recovered there would be no Mr. Merriot in town, she believed.

“I’m of Belfort’s opinion, little man,” Sir Anthony said slowly. “You are exempt from the obligation of meeting Rensley.”
===Chinese===
「您有所不知，爵士，今儿早上我本有个约会，要同伦斯利先生去格雷律师学院广场（Grey's Inn Fields）外头碰面。这件事叫安东尼·范肖爵士给搅黄了。我没法装出高兴的样子来。」

她觉得自己自始至终被人注视着。他微微一笑，浅浅欠了欠身。「噢，恕我失言，喷火先生（Mr. Fire-Eater）。只是您这些抱怨，说给伦斯利听，比说给我听更对路些。」

普鲁登丝冷冷地说：「您尽管放心，伦斯利先生一脱离外科大夫的看护，我就会递话给他的。」在她看来，那两道挺直的眉毛霎时惊异地扬了扬。她接着说下去。「查尔斯（Charles）认为我不能再同此人交手了，可依我自己的道理，受了这场羞辱之后，我非但不觉得自己被免除了同他一斗的义务，反倒更有一斗的理由。倘若查尔斯不肯替我当副手——说真的，他在这类事情上的体面感可真了不得！——我能转而请您么，爵士？」不得不承认，这步棋下得有些大胆，不过她相信，待到伦斯利先生伤愈之时，城里早就没有什么梅里奥特（Merriot）先生了。

「我站在贝尔福特（Belfort）一边，小个子。」安东尼爵士缓缓地说，「你已经免除了同伦斯利交手的义务。」

===Original===
“By your leave, sir. I think the choice rests with me.” She looked up with an assumption of displeasure. “Next time I trust there will be nothing to hinder our meeting,” she said.

“Myself, for instance?” Sir Anthony put up his glass. “I believe I don’t repeat myself.”

She bowed and let it go at that. A servant came to announce dinner, and Sir Anthony led the way into the dining-room at the back of the house.

There were wax candles in wrought holders on the table, and silver winking in the golden light. Two chairs were set, and two places laid, with wine in cut-glass decanters, shining covers, and fine white napery.
===Chinese===
「恕不敢苟同，爵士。我认为这选择权在我手里。」她抬眼望去，装出一副不豫之色。「只盼下一回，再没有什么能拦阻我们会面了。」她说。

「比方说，我本人？」安东尼爵士举起单片眼镜。「我相信自己从不来第二回。」

她躬了躬身，便不再深究。一名仆人前来禀报晚宴已备，安东尼爵士在前引路，进了屋子后部的餐室。

桌上锻饰烛台里立着几支蜡烛，银器在金黄的光里眨眼。设了两把椅子，铺了两副席位，雕花玻璃醒酒瓶里盛着酒，还有亮晶晶的菜罩和细白的上等桌布。

===Original===
They sat down, Sir Anthony at the head of the small table, and Prudence on his left. Dishes were presented to her; she made a fair meal, and the talk ran merrily. Sir Anthony spoke of a visit to Newmarket, and begged Prudence’s company. When she paused before making reply he said provocatively:﻿—“You daren’t say me nay this time, Peter. Remember my displeasure on another such occasion.”

She suspected him of teasing her and looked up smilingly. “What, am I supposed to fear that, sir?”

Sir Anthony was busy with the carving of a chicken, but he found time to meet the challenge in the grey eyes with a look quizzical and humorous. “Don’t you, little man?”

Well, if the truth be told, one did fear it. But what was the gentleman’s drift? “I take that to be a reflection on my courage,” she said gaily. “I believe I’ve no cause to fear you.”
===Chinese===
两人落座，安东尼爵士坐小桌首席，普鲁登丝在他左手。菜肴一道道端到她面前；她像样地吃了一顿，言谈甚欢。安东尼爵士说起要去纽马基特（Newmarket）一趟，恳请普鲁登丝作伴同往。她沉吟着未即作答，他便撩拨似的先说了：「这一回你可不敢对我说不了吧，彼得（Peter）。记得上一回这种时候，我的不痛快。」

她疑心他是在打趣自己，含笑抬眼。「怎么，我还该怕这个不成，爵士？」

安东尼爵士正忙着切分一只鸡，却仍腾出工夫，迎住那双灰眼睛里的挑衅，回以一瞥亦谑亦谐的目光。「你不怕么，小个子？」

唉，说句实话，人是怕的。可这位绅士话里的用意何在呢？「我只当这话是在贬损我的胆量了。」她快活地说，「我想我没有缘由怕您。」

===Original===
“You never can tell,” Sir Anthony answered. “I might lose patience with so fugitive and reserved a youth. Then have you naught to fear?”

Was this a threat, perchance? No, for the large gentleman was smiling with the same good-humour. “Oh, am I to be called out?” she wondered.

“Acquit me of child murder. But I might refuse to scare away the wolf﻿—a second time.”

She sipped the Burgundy in her glass, and frowned a little, “Ah!” She set down the half-empty glass, and her host filled it again. It was the second time. “You lead me to suppose, sir, that what you did yesterday was in the nature of wolf-scaring?”
===Chinese===
「这可说不准。」安东尼爵士答道，「对这么一个行踪无定、守口如瓶的少年，我说不定会失了耐性。到那时候，你就什么都不怕了么？」

这莫非是威胁？不是，因为大块头绅士正带着一如既往的好脾气微笑呢。「哦，这难道要闹到决斗么？」她心里纳闷。

「别把戕害孩童的罪名加到我头上。不过，吓退那匹狼的差事，下一回我兴许就撒手不管了。」

她抿了一口杯中的勃艮第酒（Burgundy），微微皱起眉头，「啊！」她放下那半杯酒，主人又给斟满了。这已是第二回。「您这话使我以为，爵士，您昨天所作所为，性质上是属于吓狼的了？」

===Original===
“Would you call it that?” Sir Anthony filled his own glass very leisurely. “I had thought it more in the nature of disabling the wolf.”

“If you like. Then what I suspected was truth indeed?” She looked steadily at him, with some dignity in her glance.

“That depends, young man, on what your suspicions were.”

“I thought, sir, that you had intervened﻿—quite incomprehensibly﻿—on my behalf.”
===Chinese===
「你愿意这么称呼它么？」安东尼爵士慢条斯理地给自己斟酒。「我倒觉得，它更近于废掉那匹狼。」

「随您怎么想。那么，我所疑心的果真是实情了？」她定定地望着他，目光里带着几分尊严。

「那要看你的疑心是什么了，年轻人。」

「我原以为，爵士，您插了手——全然令人费解——而且是站在我这一边。」

===Original===
“But why incomprehensibly?” inquired Sir Anthony.

This was something of a check. “Well, sir, I believe I am not, after all, just out of the nursery, though it pleases you to think so. I’m grateful for the kindliness of the action, but﻿—frankly, Sir Anthony, I had rather be given the chance to prove my mettle.”

There came a fleeting look of admiration into the eyes that rested so enigmatically on her face, but it was so transient an expression that she doubted she had been mistaken. “I compliment you, boy. But prove your mettle on one nearer your own age.”

She bowed, and for form’s sake sipped at her wine again. A dish of nuts was pushed towards her; she chose one and cracked it without having recourse to the silver crackers in the dish. A boy’s trick, and she hoped the large gentleman noted it well.
===Chinese===
「可为什么令人费解？」安东尼爵士问道。

这一问问得她有些招架不住。「好吧，爵士，我相信我终究不是才出育儿室的娃娃，尽管您乐意这么想。这番举动的好意，我心存感激，可是——恕我直言，安东尼爵士，我宁肯有人给我一个证明胆气的机会。」

那双神秘莫测地停在她脸上的眼睛里，掠过一丝赞赏，可那神情转瞬即逝，她倒疑心是自己看岔了。「我佩服你，孩子。不过要证明胆气，找一个同你年纪相仿的吧。」

她躬了躬身，碍着礼数又抿了一口酒。一盘坚果推到她面前；她拣了一颗，没去动盘中那副银制坚果夹，只用手指便捏开了。这是男孩子的小把戏，她只盼大块头绅士把这看得分明。

===Original===
The indolent voice continued. “Though to be sure I’d an idea your mettle had been proved already. You’ve had an engagement before this.”

She was peeling the nut, and her fingers did not falter, though she was taken by surprise. What was he at now, pray? She looked up inquiringly, but had sense enough to commit herself to nothing.

“Some duel when you sustained a wound in the shoulder,” said Sir Anthony.

She was at a momentary loss, and knew herself closely scrutinized. Recollection of the night when she was set on by Mohocks returned to her. She remembered the excuse manufactured on the spur of the moment for Belfort’s edification. “True, Sir Anthony, but that took place abroad.”
===Chinese===
那副懒洋洋的嗓音继续道：「不过说实在的，我原以为你的胆气早就证实过了。这样的约会，你从前就有过一回。」

她正剥着那颗坚果，虽是猝不及防，手指却纹丝不乱。他如今又在算计什么，请问？她抬眼投去探询的一瞥，好在尚有分寸，什么也不曾应承。

「某一场决斗，你在里头肩上挂了彩。」安东尼爵士说。

她一时语塞，又明知自己正被人细细端详。她想起遭摩霍克匪徒（Mohocks）围袭的那个夜晚，也想起自己为让贝尔福特深信不疑、当场现编的那套说辞。「是真的，安东尼爵士，不过那一场发生在国外。」

===Original===
“Like so many of your experiences,” nodded Sir Anthony, and again picked up the decanter. “But you don’t drink, my dear boy.”

She thought she drank a deal too much of this heavy Burgundy, and deplored the absence of claret. Once more her glass was filled. To refuse it would give food for suspicion in these days of hard drinking. She swallowed some of the deep red wine, was aware of a lazy glance upon her, and emptied the glass recklessly. God send she kept a sober head on her shoulders! If there was to be more of it the next glass must go down her arm.

“But we drift from the point,” Sir Anthony said genially. “We were talking of Newmarket, and, as I remember, I queried an assertion on your part, child, that you’d no fear of me.”
===Chinese===
「同你的许多经历一个样。」安东尼爵士点头道，又拿起了醒酒瓶。「可你不喝酒啊，我亲爱的孩子。」

她觉得自己这烈性的勃艮第酒已喝得太多，只叹席上偏偏没有红葡萄酒（claret）。她的杯子又被斟满了。在这人人豪饮的年月，推杯不饮是要惹人起疑的。她咽下几口深红的酒液，觉察到一道懒洋洋的目光落在自己身上，索性一横心，把整杯都干了。上帝保佑，但愿她还能保住一颗清醒的头脑！倘若再来这么几杯，下一杯就得顺着她的胳膊倒下去了。

「可咱们扯远了。」安东尼爵士和颜悦色地说，「咱们方才在谈纽马基特，还有——我记得——我对你的一个断语提出过疑问，孩子：你说你不怕我。」

===Original===
“Why, what should I fear in you?” Prudence asked, and chuckled, “You tell me you won’t call me out, and I’m able to breathe again.”

Sir Anthony’s mouth relaxed into a smile of real amusement. “I do verily believe, young man, that you’d meet me with perfect sangfroid.”

“Oh, as to that, sir, I might know some serious nervous qualms. I’m to understand you’re accounted something of a master of the small sword.”

“You’ve been misinformed. Do you ever have nervous qualms I wonder?”
===Chinese===
「哎呀，您身上有什么可怕的？」普鲁登丝问，咯咯一笑，「听您说自己不会约我决斗，我才算又能喘上气来了。」

安东尼爵士的嘴角松开，绽出一个真正被逗乐了的笑。「我的确相信，年轻人，真到了要同我交手的那天，你准会带着十足的镇定自若。」

「哦，那可难说，爵士，我兴许会正经八百地心慌腿软呢。据我理解，人家都把您算作使小剑（small sword）的一位行家。」

「那是误传了。我倒纳闷，你可曾也有过心慌意乱的时候？」

===Original===
Her ringers closed round the stem of her wineglass; she was looking at the ruby liquid sparkling in it. “Often, sir. Why should you suppose me cast in the heroic mould?”

“I’d a notion you’d a vast deal of courage, my friend,” placidly replied Fanshawe.

“Good Gad, sir, why? Because I would fight Rensley?”

“That, and some other things.” Sir Anthony drained his glass, and refilled it, glancing at the untouched wine in the glass Prudence still held.
===Chinese===
她的手指拢住酒杯的杯脚，望着杯中闪闪发亮的红宝石色酒液。「常有的，爵士。您凭什么把我当成英雄模子里铸出来的人呢？」

「我有个印象：你的胆量大得惊人，我的朋友。」范肖不动声色地答道。

「天哪，爵士，为什么？就因为我要同伦斯利决斗？」

「因为那个，也因为些别的事。」安东尼爵士饮尽杯中酒，重新斟满，眼角瞥了瞥普鲁登丝仍端在手里、一口未动的那杯。

===Original===
He selected a nut from the dish, and became busy with the cracking of it. Now was her moment, while his eyes were bent on his plate. Prudence raised her glass to her lips, as though to toss off the whole; there was a quick practised turn of the wrist, over in a flash, and the contents of her glass were sent down her arm.

But quicker even than her own movement, Sir Anthony leaned forward. His hand shot out, and the hard fingers closed round her wrist. Relentlessly her arm was borne down: down till the glass she held emptied its dregs on to the floor.

She made no effort to break free; perhaps she breathed a little faster. The fingers were clamped still about her wrist; Sir Anthony was looking down at her hand, watching the wine trickle down her arm, and drip on to the carpet.

She sat perfectly still; her eyes were calm, even meditative, resting on Fanshawe’s face. She had lost some of her colour, and the lace at her bosom rose and fell rather quickly, but other signs of alarm there were not.
===Chinese===
他从盘中拣了颗坚果，忙着去夹它。时机来了，此时他的眼睛正对着自己的盘子。普鲁登丝把酒杯举到唇边，仿佛要一饮而尽；手腕利落熟练地一转，一闪而过，杯中之酒便已顺着她的胳膊泻了下去。

可比她的动作还要快，安东尼爵士倾身向前。他的手倏地探出，坚硬的手指扣住了她的手腕。她的手臂被无情地往下压：一压到底，直压到她握着的杯子把残酒泼尽，尽数洒上了地板。

她不作半分挣脱；兴许呼吸微微急了些。那几根手指仍钳在她的腕上；安东尼爵士垂目看着她的手，看着酒液顺着她的手臂蜿蜒而下，一滴一滴落上地毯。

她端坐不动；目光平静，甚至若有所思，停在范肖的脸上。她颊上的血色褪去了几分，胸前的蕾丝起伏得颇急，但其他惊惶的形迹，却一概没有。

===Original===
It seemed an age before her wrist was released. At last the merciless fingers left it, and Sir Anthony sat back in his chair. She brought her hand up, and set the glass down on the table. In a detached manner she noticed that her hand did not shake, and was vaguely pleased.

The large gentleman’s voice broke in on her reflections. “There is no Borgia blood in my veins, Peter Merriot.”

There was some sternness in the tone. Her left hand came mechanically to cover the maltreated wrist; the marks of the gentleman’s fingers still lingered. “I did not suppose it, sir.”

Sir Anthony rose, pushing back his chair. He walked to the window and back, and the grey eyes followed him. He stopped, and looked down at Prudence; there was gravity in his face, but no anger, she thought. His words gave her a slight start. “My dear, I wish you could find it in your heart to trust me,” he said.
===Chinese===
仿佛隔了一个世纪，手腕才被放开。那几根无情的手指终于离了开去，安东尼爵士靠回椅背。她抬起手来，把杯子搁回桌上。她神思恍惚地注意到，自己的手竟不发抖，心里泛起一阵说不清的欢喜。

大块头绅士的声音打断了她的浮想。「我的血管里没有波吉亚（Borgia）家的血，彼得·梅里奥特。」

话音里带着几分严厉。她的左手下意识地拢上来，覆住那只吃了苦头的手腕；那位绅士指头的印痕还留在上头。「我没有这么想过，爵士。」

安东尼爵士推开椅子，站起身来。他走到窗边，又踱了回来，那双灰色的眼睛一路相随。他停住脚，低头望普鲁登丝；他脸上有郑重之色，却没有怒容，她想。可他的话叫她微微一怔。「亲爱的，我但愿你能打心底里信我一回。」他说。

===Original===
’Deed, but trust was there, in her heart, but how tell him?

“I’ve had suspicions of your secret since the first evening you dined with me here,” he went on. “Of late I have been as certain as a man may be of so wild a masquerade.”

So much for Robin, and for my Lady Lowestoft, scornful of his perspicacity. Well, she had had fears of this. But not even she had realized how much the sleepy gentleman saw. Egad, what must he think of her? The colour rose at the thought. She lifted her eyes; it did not occur to her to try evasion. “I would trust you willingly, Sir Anthony,” she said in a still, calm voice. “I have not liked the lies I have told, and the great lie I have acted.” She put a hand up to her neckcloth; it was tight round her throat of a sudden. “But there is not only myself involved. If it were all to do again, I would do it.” A look of pride came into her face; her chin was up, but it sank after a moment. She looked down at the ring on her finger, and wiped the trickle of wine from her hand with a crumpled napkin.

“Will you tell me your name?” Sir Anthony said gently.
===Chinese===
诚然，信任分明就在那里，在她心里，可怎么对他说呢？

「打你头一个晚上在这里同我进餐起，我就疑心你的秘密了。」他接着说，「到了近来，对这么一场匪夷所思的化装戏，一个人所能有的几分笃定，我已尽数有了。」

罗宾，还有那位讥笑他眼力的洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft），如今还有什么可说。唉，这一层她原本就有过惧怕。可就连她，也不曾料到这位睡眼惺忪的绅士竟看去了这么多。天哪，他心里该把她想成什么样的人？念及此处，血色涌上她的脸。她抬起眼睛；她压根儿没想起要试什么遁词。「我情愿信您，安东尼爵士，」她用平稳安静的嗓音说，「我一直不喜欢我说过的那些谎，不喜欢我演下的这场弥天大谎。」她抬手去碰领巾；它蓦地箍紧了她的喉咙。「可这局里缠着的并不止我一个。倘若一切能重来一遍，我还是会这么做的。」骄傲之色浮上她的脸；她下巴扬着，可不多时又垂了下去。她低头看指上的戒指，拿一团揉皱的餐巾擦去手上淌下的酒痕。

「你愿意告诉我你的名字么？」安东尼爵士温言道。

===Original===
“It is Prudence, sir. In truth, I know no more. I have had many surnames.” There was no hint of bitterness in her voice, nor any shame. It was best the large gentleman should know her for the adventuress she was.

“Prudence?” Sir Anthony was frowning now. “So that is it!” he said softly.

She looked up, searching his face.

“You are not very like your father,” said Sir Anthony.

She gave nothing away in her expression, but she knew that he had very nearly the full sum of it.
===Chinese===
「是普鲁登丝，爵士。说实在的，我自己也只知道这么多。我用过的姓氏，数也数不清。」她的嗓音里没有一丝苦涩，也没有半分羞惭。让大块头绅士认清她本就是个女冒险家，这样最好。

「普鲁登丝？」安东尼爵士此刻皱起了眉头。「原来是这么一回事！」他轻声说。

她抬眼望去，细细端详他的脸色。

「你长得不太像你父亲。」安东尼爵士说。

她的神情不曾泄露分毫，可她知道，全部底细他已几乎尽收掌中。

===Original===
There fell a silence. “Prudence﻿ ﻿…” Sir Anthony repeated and smiled. “I don’t think you were very well named, child.” He looked down at her, and there was a light in his eyes she had never seen there before. “Will you marry me?” he said simply.

Now at last there came surprise into her face, on a wave of colour. She rose swiftly to her feet, and stood staring. “Sir, I have to suppose﻿—you jest!”

“It is no jest.”

“You ask a nameless woman, an adventuress to marry you? One who had lied to you, and tricked you! And you say it is no jest?”

“My dear, you have never tricked me,” he said, amused.

“I tried to do so.”
===Chinese===
