一阵沉默落了下来。「普鲁登丝……」安东尼爵士复诵了一遍，笑了。「我看你这名儿实在取得不大高明，孩子。」他低头看她，眼里有一道她从未见过的光。「你愿意嫁给我么？」他简单地说。

直到此刻，惊愕才终于涌上她的脸，一浪血色随之泛起。她倏地立起身，怔怔站着望他。「爵士，我只得认为——您是在说笑！」

「这不是说笑。」

「您求一个无名无姓的女人、一个女冒险家嫁给您？一个对您撒过谎、耍过花招的人！您还说这不是说笑？」

「亲爱的，你可从来不曾耍过我。」他说，只觉得有趣。

「我试过要耍您。」

===Original===
“I wish you would call me Tony,” he complained.

She had a tiny suspicion she was being punished. Sure, the fine gentleman would never ask her to be his wife in all seriousness. “You have the right to your revenge, sir,” she said stiffly.

He came round the corner of the table, and took one of her hands in his. She let it lie there resistless. “Child, have you still so little faith in me?” he asked. “I offer you all my worldly goods, and the protection of my name, and you call it a jest.”

“I’ve﻿—I’ve to thank you, sir. I don’t understand you. Why do you offer this?”
===Chinese===
「我倒希望你叫我托尼（Tony）。」他抱怨道。

她隐隐疑心自己这是在受罚。可不是么，这位风雅的绅士决计不会一本正经地求她做妻子。「您尽可以施行您的报复，爵士。」她僵僵地说。

他绕过桌角走来，把她一只手握进掌中。她任它绵软地留在那里。「孩子，你还是这样信不过我么？」他问，「我把我全部的身外之财奉献给你，还有我姓氏的庇护，你却说这是说笑。」

「我——我得谢谢您，爵士。我不明白您。您为什么要提出这个？」

===Original===
“Because I love you,” he answered. “Must you ask that?”

She raised her eyes to his face, and knew that he had spoken the truth. She wondered that he did not take her into his arms, and with a fine intuition realized the chivalry of this man who would take no advantage of her being alone in his house, and quite defenceless. She drew her hand away, and felt a hot pricking beneath her eyelids. “I cannot marry you, Sir Anthony. I am no fit bride for you.”

“Don’t you think I might be permitted to judge of that?” he suggested.

She shook her head. “You know nothing of me, Sir Anthony.”
===Chinese===
「因为我爱你。」他答道，「这难道还用问？」

她抬眼望向他的脸，知道他说的是真话。她奇怪他为什么不把她搂进怀里，又凭着一缕细腻的直觉，懂得了这个男人的骑士风度：他决不趁她孤身困在他府上、全无防备之时占她便宜。她把手抽了回来，觉出眼皮底下有一阵火辣辣的刺痛。「我不能嫁给您，安东尼爵士。我配不上做您的新娘。」

「你不觉得，这件事可以容我自己来判断么？」他提议道。

她摇了摇头。「您对我一无所知，安东尼爵士。」

===Original===
“My dear, I have looked many times into your eyes,” he said. “They tell me all I have need to know.”

“I﻿—don’t think so, sir,” she forced herself to say.

Her hand lay on the chair-back. He took it in his again, and carried it to his lips. “You have the truest eyes in the world, Prudence,” he said. “And the very bravest.”

“You don’t know me,” she repeated. “I have led the life of an adventurer; I am an adventurer﻿—a masquerader! I have no knowledge even of my true name. My father﻿—” She paused.
===Chinese===
「亲爱的，我朝你那双眼睛里望过许多回了。」他说，「它们把我需要知道的一切，都告诉我了。」

「我——不这么认为，爵士。」她逼着自己说出口。

她的手搭在椅背上。他再度把它握进手中，送到唇边一吻。「你有一双世上最真的眼睛，普鲁登丝。」他说，「也是最勇敢的。」

「您并不认识我。」她重复道，「我过的一直是冒险者的日子；我本人就是个冒险者——一个化装舞会者！就连我真实的姓名，我也一无所知。我父亲——」她停住了。

===Original===
“I take it your name may well prove to be a Tremaine,” he said, with a soft laugh.

“You’ve guessed my father, sir?”

“Why yes, it’s the remarkable old gentleman who claims to be the lost Viscount, I believe. You told me once your father would surprise me.”

“Did I, sir? Well, that is he. I think you are one of those who have little faith in his claim.”

“To say truth,” remarked Sir Anthony, “I care very little whether he proves to be Barham or not.”

“But I care, Sir Anthony. If he is Barham indeed, and I am thus a woman of birth noble enough﻿—” She found it was impossible to continue.
===Chinese===
「依我看，你的姓氏到头来很可能证实是特雷梅因（Tremaine）。」他含着一丝轻笑说。

「您把我父亲猜出来了，爵士？」

「正是，我相信，就是那位不同寻常的老先生，自称是失踪的子爵的那一位。你从前对我说过一回，你父亲会让我大吃一惊。」

「我说过么，爵士？嗯，正是他。我想，您是对他的继承之诉不怎么看好的那一类人。」

「说句实话，」安东尼爵士评道，「他到底证实是巴勒姆还是不是，我几乎毫不挂心。」

「可我挂心，安东尼爵士。倘若他当真是巴勒姆，我便这样成了一位门第足够高贵的小姐——」她发现这话实在难以为继。

===Original===
“Then you would marry me?” Sir Anthony prompted. “Is that it?”

She nodded. It was not in her nature to deny she cared for him.

“And do you know what you will do if he is not Tremaine of Barham?” inquired Sir Anthony conversationally.

She made a gesture of fatalism. “I shall be off on my adventuring again, sir.”

“You may call it adventuring if you please, but I believe I’m a staid creature. You will marry me just the same, you see.”
===Chinese===
「那么你就肯嫁我了？」安东尼爵士循循善诱，「是这么回事，对不对？」

她点了点头。要她否认自己心里有他，这有违她的天性。

「那你知道么，倘若他当不上巴勒姆的特雷梅因，你打算怎么办？」安东尼爵士像闲话家常一般问道。

她打了个听天由命的手势。「我就又得动身，去闯我的江湖了，爵士。」

「你乐意管它叫闯江湖也随你，不过我相信，我是个安分守己的人物。你照样会嫁给我的，你瞧着好了。」

===Original===
She smiled a little. “This is madness, sir. You will be glad one day that I said you nay.”

“And will you be glad, Prue?” he asked gravely.

“I shall be glad for your sake, sir.”

“My dear, I want to take you out of this masquerade of yours at once. There’s danger on all sides, and﻿—I love you.”
===Chinese===
她微微一笑。「这是疯话，爵士。有朝一日，您会庆幸我回绝了您。」

「那你会庆幸么，普鲁（Prue）？」他正色问道。

「为了您，我会庆幸的，爵士。」

「亲爱的，我要立刻把你从你这场化装戏里领出去。四下里处处是危险，而且——我爱你。」

===Original===
“Ah, do not!” she made swift outcry. “It’s not possible, sir. More depends on the masquerade than you know.”

“I believe I may guess. You’ve a brother took part in the late Rebellion, dressed now in woman’s clothes. His name is, I think, Robin.”

She looked wonderingly up at him. “Do you know everything, sir?”

“No,” he answered, smiling. “Not quite. Marry me, and put both your fortunes into my hands. I can help this Robin, maybe.”
===Chinese===
「啊，别说这个！」她急急地喊出声来。「这不可能，爵士。系在这场化装戏上的干系，比您知道的大得多。」

「我想我猜得出来。你有一位兄弟参与过那场叛乱，如今正穿着女人的衣裳。他的名字，我想，叫罗宾。」

她又惊又奇地仰头望他。「您什么都知道么，爵士？」

「不，」他含笑答道，「不尽然。嫁给我，把你兄妹两人的命运都交到我这双手里。这位罗宾，我兴许帮得上。」

===Original===
“Not even for that. I could not, sir. Grant me a little pride! You would be King Cophetua, but I’ve no mind to play the beggar-maid.”

He made no reply for a moment, but stood looking down at her. “I cannot force you to marry me,” he said at last.

“Sir Anthony﻿—I would have you marry a woman of whom you can be proud.”

“I have nothing but pride in you. In your courage, and in the quick wits of you. I have never known so wonderful a woman.”
===Chinese===
「便是为这个，也不能。我做不到，爵士。请容我留一分傲气吧！您就做科菲图亚王（King Cophetua），可我没心思去扮那乞女。」

他一时没有作声，只站着低头看她。「我不能强迫你嫁给我。」他终于开口。

「安东尼爵士——我但愿您娶的是一位您能引以为傲的女子。」

「我对你有的只是引以为傲。为你的勇气，也为你的机敏。我从不曾识得这样了不起的一位女子。」

===Original===
“You can have no pride in my birth, sir. I do not know what my father is; we have never known, for he loves to be a mystery. If this claim is true﻿—if he is indeed Tremaine of Barham﻿—ask me once more!” Her eyes were wet, but her mouth smiled resolutely.

“I am to wait, then! You deny me the right to protect you now?”

“You have me at your feet, sir,” she said unsteadily, “but I do deny you. I must.”

“You at my feet!” he said. “That is a jest indeed!” He let go her hand, and took a turn about the room. She watched him wistfully, and at last he spoke again. “Ay, you’ve pride,” he said. “Did that spring of low birth? You must needs cleave your own path, and take no help even from the man who loves you. You ask me to wait. I will wait, until this father of yours has settled his affairs. But when that day comes, and whatever the issue﻿—believe me I shall take you then, by force if need be, and carry you off to Church. Is it understood?”

She smiled mistily, and tried to shake her head. He laughed and there was no laziness either in his face or in his voice. “Better come to me willingly then,” he said, “for, by God, I shall have no mercy!”
===Chinese===
「您没法为我的出身骄傲，爵士。我不知道我父亲是什么人；我们从来就不知道，因为他天生爱做个谜。倘若这继承之诉属实——倘若他真是巴勒姆的特雷梅因——那就请您再问一遍！」她的眼睛湿润了，嘴角却决然含着笑。

「这么说，我就得等了！眼下你可要剥夺我保护你的权利了？」

「您已令我拜倒在您脚下了，爵士，」她声音发颤地说，「可我确实要回绝您。我必须如此。」

「你在我脚下！」他说，「这话才真是说笑呢！」他放开她的手，在屋里踱了一个来回。她怅然望着他，他终于又开口了。「是啊，你有傲气。」他说，「这傲气，是低微的出身里生出来的么？你偏要独自劈出自己的路来，连爱你的人的援手也不肯领受。你要我等。我就等，一直等到你这位父亲了结了他的事务。可等到那一天来临，无论结局如何——相信我，到那时我会来带你走，必要时便用强，把你抢去教堂成婚。听明白了么？」

她泪眼朦胧地笑了，还想摇一摇头。他朗声笑了，脸上和嗓音里再没有半分懒散。「那么，还是你自己乖乖地到我这儿来吧。」他说，「因为，上帝作证，我到时候决不留情！」

## VI The Polite World Receives Mr. and Miss Merriot / VI 上流社交界接纳梅里奥特兄妹

===Original===
My Lady Lowestoft made no idle boast when she declared that all the world might be seen at her rout that evening. The world, as she knew it, was the Polite Society of the day; and Polite Society chose to venerate her ladyship. She had the felicity of seeing her salons filled to overflowing. Downstairs there were refreshments laid out in the dining-room; angel cakes, and ratafie; strange French concoctions and some of the late Sir Roger’s best Burgundy; sweetmeats of every known variety and French champagne, sparkling in the glasses, to go with them. There was a cardroom also, spacious enough to hold some few tables with comfort. Those who wished might escape from the chatter and the scraping of the fiddles in the saloons above, to seek a little quiet diversion here with a dice-box. My lady was fond of all games of chance herself, but her duties as hostess kept her tonight in the main rooms, where people came and went, gathered into knots for conversation, separated again to greet a new arrival, or lent an indulgent ear to the fiddlers scraping away at the back of the room.

Robin, in his character of Miss Merriot, was kept near my lady. He had chosen to array himself in shades of rose pink. A necklace lay on the white skin of his chest, and a bracelet enclasped one rather sinewy arm. If there could be found aught whereat to cavil in his appearance it must be those arms. They lacked the dimpled roundness necessary for beauty. Elsewhere no fault could be detected. The fair hair was piled up on top of his head, lavishly powdered, and decorated with a jewelled ornament; the face below was pink and white as any girl’s, with blue eyes dreamy under delicately pencilled brows, and a nose many a reigning toast mighty envy. A black ribbon round the throat served to emphasise the creamy whiteness of the skin; the waist, thanks to John’s lacing, was trim enough, and the foot peeping from beneath the hem of a flowered petticoat sufficiently small to escape notice. Maybe it was fashioned on the large size for so dainty a lady, but a high heel disguised a possible fault.

There could be no fault found either in his deportment. Standing a little back from the crowd, Prudence watched him with a critical eye. He had several times before donned this woman’s garb, but never for so long a stretch. She had coached him to the best of her ability, but well as she knew him could still fear some slip. She had to admit knowledge of him was deficient yet. Sure, he might have been born to it. His curtseys were masterpieces of grace; the air with which he held out a hand to young gallants so consummate a piece of artistry that Prudence was shaken with silent laughter. He seemed to know by instinct how to flirt his fan, and how to spread his wide skirts for the curtsey. Apparently he might be left safely to his own devices. His sister withdrew her gaze from him, reflecting that she would give much to hear what he was saying to the beautiful Miss Gunning standing beside him. If the spirit of mischief did not carry him away there was naught to be feared in his bearing. Prudence turned away, and came upon my Lady Lowestoft, in gay talk with Mr. Walpole, who, since he lived so close, was naturally a late comer.

===Chinese===
洛斯托夫特夫人（my Lady Lowestoft）夸口说当晚她的盛大晚会上天下名流都将到场，这可不是一句空话。她所认识的这个世界，便是当日的上流社交界；而上流社交界又一心尊崇这位夫人。她有幸眼见自家厅堂宾客盈门，挤得水泄不通。楼下餐室里摆好了点心：天使蛋糕，拉塔菲亚甜酒（ratafie）；稀奇的法式调制吃食，还有几瓶已故罗杰爵士（Sir Roger）窖藏的上好勃艮第；各式各样叫得出名目的蜜饯糖果，配上法国香槟，在杯中晶晶冒泡，正好佐着吃。另有一间牌室，宽敞得舒舒服服摆得下好几张牌桌。嫌楼上客厅里人声嘈杂、提琴聒耳的客人，尽可以躲到这里来，摇摇骰盅，寻一点清静的消遣。各种碰运气的牌戏夫人自己都钟爱，可今晚女主人的一份职守把她拴在了正厅里——那里宾客川流不息，一簇簇聚拢来攀谈，又散开去迎一位新到的客人，或是带着纵容的耳朵，听屋后琴师们吱吱嘎嘎地拉着弓弦。

罗宾（Robin）顶着梅里奥特小姐（Miss Merriot）的名头，被留在夫人近旁。他给自己挑了一身玫瑰粉的色调。一条项链贴在白皙的胸口，一只手镯箍住一条相当筋结的手臂。若说他这副模样还能挑出什么刺来，必定就是那双手臂了：它们缺了美所必需的那种带浅涡的圆润。除此之外，找不出半点破绽。金发高高堆在头顶，扑足了粉，簪着一枚嵌宝头饰；底下那张脸粉白得同哪个姑娘的一样，蓝眼睛在描得精致的眉下如梦似幻，那只鼻子叫多少红极一时的名媛妒羡得要死。喉咙上一圈黑缎带，愈发衬出肌肤的乳白；腰身托约翰（John）系带子的功劳，也够纤窄了；从绣花裙裾底下探出来的一只脚，也小得不惹眼了。兴许对这么位纤娇的小姐来说，它天生偏大了些，好在一只高鞋跟把这点可能的毛病遮掩了过去。

他的仪态举止同样挑不出毛病。普鲁登丝（Prudence）站在人群稍后一点的地方，用挑剔的眼光守着他。这身女装他从前也穿过几回，却从不曾穿这么久。她已倾囊调教过他，可纵然对他知之再深，也还是怕他出个岔子。她不得不承认，自己对他的了解还很不够。真的，他简直像天生就该吃这碗饭。他的屈膝礼是优雅的杰作；朝年轻公子们伸出一只手去的那副风致，更精妙得是一件完璧的艺术品，普鲁登丝无声地笑了，笑得直发颤。他仿佛凭本能就知道怎样摇折扇，怎样铺开宽大的裙裾行礼。看来大可放心让他自己去施展了。当姐姐的把目光从他身上收回来，心里想着，若能听见他在同身旁那位美丽的冈宁小姐（Miss Gunning）说些什么，出多大代价都情愿。只要那股促狭劲儿别挟卷了他去，他的举止风度没什么可怕的。普鲁登丝转过身，正遇上洛斯托夫特夫人——她正同沃波尔先生（Mr. Walpole）谈笑得起劲；这位先生住得近，来得自然也就晚。

===Original===
My lady manoeuvred the elegant Mr. Walpole away from Prudence’s vicinity, and disposed of him at length to his dear friend Gilly Williams, who, with Mr. Selwyn, seemingly but half awake, stood talking by the fire.

My lady came rustling back to the door, for there were guests still ascending the stairs. To Prudence, under her breath, she said: “I take him away, so! Of an inquisitive disposition, my cabbage! You would not believe! I feared he might pry too close﻿ ﻿… Ah, madame!” She curtseyed to a new arrival, and, a moment later, was exchanging witticisms with my Lord March, that saturnine peer.

A gentleman but lately introduced to Prudence suggested a hand at picquet. She looked calmly at this gentleman and professed herself all readiness. It took her no more than a minute to reach the conclusion that she was to be a lamb for the fleecing. Well, the gentleman should see.

===Chinese===
夫人施展身手，把这位风雅的沃波尔先生从普鲁登丝近旁引开，末了把他打发给他的至交好友吉利·威廉姆斯（Gilly Williams）——此公正同看似只醒着一半的塞尔温先生（Mr. Selwyn）站在炉边叙话。

夫人窸窸窣窣地又回到门口去，楼梯上还有客人正往上走。她压低嗓音对普鲁登丝说：「我就这么把他引开了！天生一副爱打听的脾性，我的小卷心菜！你简直不会相信！我就怕他打听得过了头……啊，夫人！」她朝一位新到的女宾行了屈膝礼，片刻之后，又同马奇勋爵（Lord March）——那位神情阴郁的贵族——你一句我一句地斗起妙语来。

一位刚引见给普鲁登丝不久的绅士提议来一局皮克牌（picquet）。她平平静静打量了这位绅士一眼，满口应承下来。不出一分钟她就得出结论：自己要被当成一只待剪毛的羊羔了。好啊，这位先生只管等着瞧。

===Original===
There were several men in the cardroom, some few dicing, some talking idly beside, and one party engaged in a hand of lansquenet. Prudence sat down with Sir Francis Jollyot at a table away from the door, and assented placidly to his proposed stakes. They seemed large, but she had played for larger, and was in no wise perturbed.

“ ’Tis a game I’m devilish partial to,” Sir Francis observed. “You play it much, eh?”

“A little, sir,” Prudence said and displayed hesitation over the question of her discard. Across the table Sir Francis smiled in infinite good-humour. He had played with young gentlemen from the country before, and foresaw a profitable evening. When the game was over he condoled with Mr. Merriot on his ill-fortune, and proposed a fresh one. Prudence accepted most cordially. She perceived a greater skill at picquet in herself than in her smiling opponent. Played carefully this game of turning the tables on the wolf would be amusing. With no less hesitation in her demeanour, but with much less folly in her discards, she won the game. She was complimented on the cards she had held, and embarked upon the third encounter.

===Chinese===
牌室里已有几位男士，有几个在掷骰子，有几个在旁边闲谈，还有一桌人正打着一局兰斯克内牌（lansquenet）。普鲁登丝同弗朗西斯·乔利奥特爵士（Sir Francis Jollyot）在离门远些的一张桌旁落了座，对他提的赌注心平气和地应允了。数目瞧着不小，可她赌过更大的，毫不动声色。

「这牌戏我偏爱得要命。」弗朗西斯爵士搭话道。「您常玩么，嗯？」

「略会一点，爵士。」普鲁登丝说着，在换牌这桩事上露出一副踌躇不定的样儿。桌子对面，弗朗西斯爵士满面春风地笑了。乡下年轻绅士这一套他从前领教过，预见到一个有利可图的夜晚。一局打完，他好生慰藉了梅里奥特先生一通手气不佳，又提议再来一局。普鲁登丝热热乎乎地应承下来。她已瞧出，自己的皮克牌功夫比这位笑吟吟的对手高出一筹。小心打下去，这出反过手来耍弄恶狼的戏码会很有趣。她做派上的踌躇一分不减，换牌时的胡闹却少了许多，赢下了这一局。人家还恭维她一手好牌，她便又投入了第三场交锋。

===Original===
“A reverse!” commented Sir Francis gaily. “I hardly thought you would keep a guard to that Queen in the last hand, throwing the King of Hearts.”

The crease showed between Prudence’s brows. “Did I throw my King? You played out your cards so fast, you see, I scarcely﻿ ﻿…” She left the end of her sentence to be understood. Sir Francis thought that he did understand, and sorted his hand with a smile ill concealed.

There came a fresh arrival into the room, and paused a while in the open doorway. This gentleman was very large, with wide shoulders under a coat of maroon velvet, and a strong, handsome face. Under heavy lids his eyes fell on Prudence and rested there.

===Chinese===
「风水倒转了！」弗朗西斯爵士快活地点评道。「真想不到，方才那局您竟留着那张王后做防备，倒把红心国王扔了。」

普鲁登丝眉心蹙起一道细纹。「我把国王扔了么？您牌出得那么快，您瞧，我都来不及……」话的尾巴她留着让人自行领会。弗朗西斯爵士自以为领会了，理着手里的牌，笑容掩都掩不住。

又有一个人进了屋来，在敞开的门口驻足片刻。这位绅士身形十分魁伟，一件褐红色天鹅绒外套底下双肩宽阔，一张脸孔刚毅英俊。沉沉的眼睑底下，他的目光落到普鲁登丝身上，便在那里停驻下来。

===Original===
“Why, Fanshawe! I had thought you were out of town. Someone told me you had gone down to Wych End.” Mr. Troubridge, standing nearby, stepped closer to Sir Anthony, and offered his snuffbox. “What are you looking at? Oh, my Lady Lowestoft’s protégé! By name Merriot, and seemingly a pleasant youth. That face should captivate the ladies.”

“It should,” Sir Anthony replied. “Jollyot wastes no time, I see.”

Mr. Troubridge laughed. It was, after all, no concern of his. “Oh, trust Jollyot! By the way, young Apollo has a prodigious fine sister. Have you seen her? One of your fair beauties. She’s above stairs in the withdrawing room.”

===Chinese===
「咦，范肖！我还当你出城去了。有人告诉我你下威奇恩德（Wych End）去了。」站在近旁的特拉布里奇先生（Mr. Troubridge）朝安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）跨近一步，递上鼻烟盒。「你在瞧什么呢？哦，洛斯托夫特夫人提携的那位新秀！姓梅里奥特，瞧着是讨人喜欢的年轻人。那张脸保管迷得住太太小姐们。」

「保管会的。」安东尼爵士答道。「我看得出来，乔利奥特一点工夫也不肯浪费。」

特拉布里奇先生笑了。说到底，这与他不相关。「哦，乔利奥特还用得着人操心么！对了，那位年轻的阿波罗（Apollo）有位出落得极俊的妹妹。你见过没有？也算得你那些金发白肤美人里的一个。这会儿她在楼上休憩室里呢。」

===Original===
“I’ve been presented.” Still Sir Anthony’s eyes dwelt on the unconscious Prudence. “Up from the country, are they? Now, neither has the look of it. Our young gentleman yonder”﻿—very slightly he indicated Prudence with a movement of his quizzing-glass﻿—“has all the air of a town beau.”

“Very modish, to be sure. He’ll have need of keen town wits if he plays with Jollyot.” Mr. Troubridge smiled a little, and looked towards the picquet table.

Prudence sat sideways at it, an arm laid along it, and one shapely leg stretched out before her. She wore a coat of dull gold brocade, with the skirts very full and stiffly whaleboned, and the great cuffs turned back to the elbow. There was much foaming lace at throat and wrists, and a jewelled buckle was placed above the black ribbon that confined her powdered locks in the nape of her neck. She was looking at the cards held in one hand, her face expressionless. There was a patch set at the corner of the firm mouth, and one high up on the cheekbone. Her other hand, with a glowing ring on it, lay lightly on the arm of her chair. As though conscious of the gaze upon her, she looked up suddenly, straight at Sir Anthony. A tinge of colour rose in her cheeks; involuntarily she smiled.

===Chinese===
「已经引见过了。」安东尼爵士的目光仍旧凝在那浑然不觉的普鲁登丝身上。「他们是乡下上来的，是么？可依我看，两人谁都没有乡下相。我们那位年轻绅士」——他抬了抬单片眼镜，极轻微地朝普鲁登丝示意了一下——「浑身上下都是城里翩翩公子的气派。」

「时髦得很，这没得说。可他要是同乔利奥特打牌，就少不得城里人那副灵敏的头脑。」特拉布里奇先生微微一笑，朝皮克牌桌望过去。

普鲁登丝侧身坐在牌桌前，一条手臂搭在桌沿，一条线条漂亮的长腿朝前伸着。她穿一件暗金色织锦外套，下摆撑得极满，衬着鲸骨硬挺挺的，宽大的袖口翻到肘弯。喉头和腕间堆着层层雪白翻卷的蕾丝，束住颈后扑粉头发的黑缎带上方，别着一枚嵌宝的带扣。她望着单手执着的牌，脸上毫无表情。那张坚毅的嘴角贴着一枚俏斑，颧骨上方高处还贴着一枚。另一只手戴着一枚莹亮的戒指，轻轻搭在椅子扶手上。仿佛觉察到落在自己身上的目光，她猛然抬头，直直望向安东尼爵士。双颊浮起一抹红晕；她不由自主地笑了。

===Original===
“Oh, do you know him?” asked Troubridge, surprised.

“We were introduced above stairs,” Sir Anthony answered, with a fine disregard for the truth, and went across the room to Prudence’s side. “Well met, my dear boy.” His hand pressed on Prudence’s shoulder to prevent her rising. “No, do not permit me to interrupt.”

At the sound of that lazy, pleasant voice a faint frown crossed Sir Francis’ face. He acknowledged Sir Anthony’s greeting only by a curt nod, and declared a point of five.

Sir Anthony stood still behind Prudence’s chair, and in silence watched the play through his eyeglass. The stakes had been raised at each new game; at the end of this one Sir Francis was most strangely a heavy loser. Either the young sprig from the country had played the game a-many times before, or else the Providence who guides the hands of novices had exerted herself most prodigiously on Mr. Merriot’s behalf. Sir Francis was disinclined to believe Mr. Merriot an adept: he had not the manner of it.

===Chinese===
「哦，你认识他？」特拉布里奇讶异地问。

「我们在楼上引见过了。」安东尼爵士答道，全然不把实情放在心上。说罢他穿过屋子，走到普鲁登丝身边。「幸会，我亲爱的孩子。」他一只手按住普鲁登丝的肩头，不许她起身。「不，别让我搅了你们的局。」

听见这副懒洋洋又悦耳的嗓音，弗朗西斯爵士脸上掠过一丝浅浅的不豫。对安东尼爵士的招呼，他只以一个生硬的点头作答，随即声明了一门五张同花。

安东尼爵士在普鲁登丝椅后站定，一言不发，隔着单片眼镜静静观牌。赌注一局一局往上加；到这一局打完，弗朗西斯爵士竟莫名其妙地输了个惨重。要么是这乡下嫩苗子从前打过不知多少回，要么就是那位专司引领新手之手的上天，为梅里奥特先生破格大大地出了一场力。弗朗西斯爵士不肯相信梅里奥特先生是行家：他没那副行家的做派。

===Original===
Sir Anthony moved at last, and spoke before Jollyot could suggest yet a fourth game, “Will you take a hand with me, Merriot?”

“I should be pleased, sir,” Prudence swept the little pile of guineas to one side.

There was nothing for Sir Francis to do but to go elsewhere. He gave up his seat to Fanshawe, and trusted he might have an evening with Mr. Merriot some time in the near future.

“Why, sir, I shall count myself fortunate,” said Prudence.

===Chinese===
安东尼爵士终于挪动了，赶在乔利奥特还没来得及提议第四局之前开了口：「梅里奥特，肯同我打一局么？」

「乐意之至，爵士。」普鲁登丝说着，把那一小堆几尼拨到一边。

弗朗西斯爵士别无良策，只好往别处去。他把座位让给范肖，只盼近日哪一天还能同梅里奥特先生再消磨一个晚上。

「哎呀爵士，那真是我天大的造化。」普鲁登丝说。

===Original===
Sir Francis moved away to a group of men by the window. Prudence turned to find Sir Anthony shuffling the pack. “Will you name the stakes, sir?” she said.

“What you will,” Sir Anthony replied. “What were they with my friend, Jollyot?”

She told him indifferently enough.

“Do you make it a rule to play for so large a sum?” blandly inquired Sir Anthony.

“I make it a rule, sir, to play for whatever sum my opponent suggests,” was the quick answer.

===Chinese===
弗朗西斯爵士挪到窗边一群男士那边去了。普鲁登丝回过头，见安东尼爵士正在洗牌。「请您定赌注吧，爵士？」她说。

「随你意。」安东尼爵士答道。「同我那位朋友乔利奥特打的是多大？」

她淡漠地把数目告诉了他。

「你惯常都赌这么大的数目么？」安东尼爵士和颜悦色地问道。

「我的规矩是，爵士，对手提多大，我便赌多大。」回答来得干脆利落。

===Original===
The heavy lids lifted for a moment, and she saw the grey eyes keen. “You must needs have faith in your skill, Mr. Merriot.”

“In my luck I have, Sir Anthony.”

“I felicitate you. I will play you for the half of Jollyot’s stakes.”

“As you please, sir. Will you cut?”

===Chinese===
沉沉的眼睑抬了一瞬，她瞥见那双灰眼睛锐利逼人。「想必你对自己的牌技怀着十足的信心，梅里奥特先生。」

「我对自己的运气，安东尼爵士。」

「那我恭贺你。我同你打，就赌乔利奥特那个数目的一半。」

「悉听尊便，爵士。您切牌么？」

===Original===
It would not do to show a change of front now that the large gentleman had watched her at play with Sir Francis. Prudence fumbled a little at the cards, and displayed a beginner’s uncertainty. Sir Anthony seemed to be engrossed with his own hand, but as she hesitated once more over the five cards of her discard he glanced up, and drawled: “Oh, spare yourself the pains, my dear boy! I am no hawk.”

Prudence fenced cautiously; she was not quite sure what the gentleman would be at. “The pains of what, sir?”

“Of all this dissimulation,” said Sir Anthony, with a disarming smile. “I must suppose you were taught to play picquet in your cradle.”

Almost she gasped. It seemed as though John had reason when he said that large gentleman was awake for all his sleepiness. She laughed, and forebore to evade, judging her man with some shrewdness. “Nearly, sir, I confess. My father has a fondness for the game.”

===Chinese===
大块头绅士既已看过她同弗朗西斯爵士交手，这会儿再改换旗鼓就不像话了。普鲁登丝摆弄着牌，稍显笨拙，拿出一副生手拿不准的样儿。安东尼爵士似乎全神贯注在自己手里那副牌上，可待她对着该换的五张牌又踌躇起来时，他抬眼一瞥，慢悠悠地说：「哦，省省这份力气吧，我亲爱的孩子！我可不是鹰。」

普鲁登丝谨慎地闪避着；她吃不准这位绅士意欲何为。「省哪门子力气，爵士？」

「这一路的装模作样啊。」安东尼爵士说，脸上挂着一个叫人无法设防的微笑。「我只好认定，你还在襁褓里的时候，就有人教你打皮克牌了。」

她差点倒抽一口气。看来约翰说得不差：那位大块头绅士别看睡眼惺忪，其实是醒着的。她笑了，把对面这人掂量了个八九分，便不再闪避其词。「差一点真是那样，爵士，我承认。家父就好这一门牌戏。」

===Original===
“Has he indeed?” said Sir Anthony. “Now, what may have induced you to play the novice with my friend Jollyot, I wonder?”

“I have been about the world a little, Sir Anthony.”

“That I believe.” Leisurely Sir Anthony looked at the three cards that fell to his minor share. “It seems you lost no feathers in that bout.”

She laughed again. “Oh, I’m an ill pigeon for plucking, sir! I declare a point of five.”

===Chinese===
「当真么？」安东尼爵士说。「那我倒纳闷了，是什么叫你在我朋友乔利奥特跟前扮起生手来了？」

「我在世上闯荡过一程子，安东尼爵士。」

「这我相信。」安东尼爵士慢条斯理地看着分归他那小份的三张牌。「方才那一局，你似乎一根羽毛也没折掉。」

她又笑了。「哦，我可是只拔不着毛的鸽子，爵士！我声明一门五张同花。」

===Original===
“I concede it you, my fair youth.”

“A quarte may perhaps be good?”

“It depends, sir, on what heads it.”

“The King, Sir Anthony.”

===Chinese===
「让与你了，我漂亮的小伙子。」

「一副四连张兴许算数？」

「那要看它打头的是什么牌了，先生。」

「国王打头，安东尼爵士。」

===Original===
“No good,” Sir Anthony said. “I hold a quarte to the Ace.”

“I am led to believe, sir, that three Kings won’t serve?”

“Quite right, my dear boy; they must give way to my three Aces.”

This was all in the grand manner. Prudence chuckled. “Oh, I’ve done then! My lead, and I count six, sir.”

===Chinese===
「不顶用。」安东尼爵士说。「我手里有一副爱司打头的四连张。」

「听您这话音，先生，三张国王也派不上用场么？」

「说得一点不错，我亲爱的孩子；它们只好让位给我的三张爱司。」

这一番应对，全是雍容大度的做派。普鲁登丝咯咯笑了。「哦，那我认栽了！该我出牌，我记六分，爵士。」

===Original===
The hand was played. As the cards were gathered up Sir Anthony said: “I take it so shrewd a youth stands in no need of a friendly warning?”

Certainly the enigmatic gentleman was developing a kindness for her. “You’re very kind, sir. I do not know why you should be at this trouble for me.” It was spoken with some warmth of gratitude.

“Nor I,” said Fanshawe indolently. “But you are not﻿—in spite of those twenty years﻿—of a great age, and there are plenty of hawks in town.”

Prudence bowed. “I shall take that to heart, sir. I have to thank you.”

===Chinese===
这一手打完了。收拢牌的时候，安东尼爵士说：「我料想，这么精明的一位年轻人，总用不着旁人好心提个醒吧？」

这位谜一样的绅士对她显然正生出一份好感来。「您太好了，爵士。我不明白，您何苦为我这般费心。」这话里带着几分感激的热忱。

「我也不明白。」范肖懒洋洋地说。「不过你年纪不算大——纵然已经二十岁了——而这城里，鹰可是多得很。」

普鲁登丝躬了躬身。「这话我一定记在心里，爵士。多谢您了。」

===Original===
“Pray do not. Plucking pigeons has never been a favourite pastime of mine﻿ ﻿… Well, I concede your point, but I claim a quinte and fourteen Queens, besides three Kings. Alack for a spoiled repique! Five played, sir.”

The game came presently to an end. “Very even,” said Sir Anthony. “Do you care to honour me at a small card-party I hold on Thursday evening?”

“Indeed, sir, mine will be the honour. On Thursday and in Clarges Street, I think?”

Sir Anthony nodded. He beckoned to a lackey standing near, and sent him to fetch wine. “You will drink a glass with me, Merriot?”

===Chinese===
「请别谢。拔鸽子毛从来算不上我心爱的消遣……好吧，你那门同花我认了，可我认领一副五连张，外加一手十四分的四张王后，还有三张国王。唉，可惜一记瑞皮克（repique）满贯坏在了上头！打过五张了，先生。」

这一局不多时便见了分晓。「势均力敌。」安东尼爵士说。「星期四晚上我办一场小小的牌会，肯否赏光？」

「哪里的话，爵士，承蒙抬举的是我。是星期四，在克拉吉斯街（Clarges Street）吧，我没记错？」

安东尼爵士点点头。他招手唤来侍立近旁的一个仆役，打发他去取酒。「陪我喝一杯，梅里奥特？」

===Original===
“Thank you, a little canary, sir.”

The wine was brought; one or two gentlemen had wandered towards the table, and stood now in converse there. Sir Anthony made Mr. Merriot known to them. Prudence found herself pledged to ride out next morning in the Park with a chubby-faced young gentleman of a friendly disposition. This was the Honourable Charles Belfort, who combined a passion for dice with almost phenomenal ill-luck, but managed to remain cheerful under it.

“Well, Charles, what fortune?” Sir Anthony looked up in some amusement at the young profligate.

“The same as ever. It always is.” Belfort shook his head. “Bad, very bad, but I have a notion that my luck will turn tomorrow, at about eight o’clock.”

===Chinese===
「多谢，来一点加那利酒（canary），爵士。」

酒取来了；有一两位绅士踱到桌边，正站在那儿叙话。安东尼爵士把梅里奥特先生引见给他们。普鲁登丝发觉自己已应承下来，明早同一位面庞圆润、脾性和善的年轻绅士到公园骑马。这位便是尊贵的查尔斯·贝尔福特（Charles Belfort），此公一手热着掷骰子，一手握着几乎神乎其神的坏运气，偏还能在这坏运气底下乐乐呵呵。

「怎么样，查尔斯，手气如何？」安东尼爵士抬眼看着这个浪荡的年轻人，眼里带着几分好笑。

「还是老样子。从来都是老样子。」贝尔福特摇了摇头。「糟，糟得很。不过我有个预感，明天早上八点上下，我的运势就要转了。」

===Original===
“Good Gad, Bel, why at eight?” demanded Mr. Molyneux.

The Honourable Charles looked grave. “Angels told me so in a vision,” he said.

There was a shout of laughter.

“Nonsense, Charles, they were prophesying your entry into a sponging house!” This was my Lord Kestrel, leaning on the back of Fanshawe’s chair.

===Chinese===
「老天爷，贝尔（Bel），为什么偏是八点？」莫利纽克斯先生（Mr. Molyneux）追问道。

尊贵的查尔斯神色郑重起来。「天使们在异象里告诉我的。」他说。

哄堂大笑。

「胡说，查尔斯，他们是在预言你进债户拘留所呢！」说话的是凯斯特雷尔勋爵（Lord Kestrel），他正倚在范肖的椅背上。

===Original===
“You see how it is, sir”﻿—Belfort addressed himself plaintively to Prudence.﻿—“They all laugh at me, even when I tell them of a visitation from heaven. Irreligious, damme, that’s what it is.”

There was a fresh outburst of mirth. Through it came Sir Anthony’s deep voice, full of friendly mockery. “You delude yourself, Charles: no angel would visit you unless by mischance. Doubtless a sign from the devil that he is about to claim his own.” He rose, and picked up his snuffbox. “Well, Merriot, I must do myself the pleasure of making my bow to your sister. Upstairs, when I was there, she was surrounded.”

“I’ll lead you to her, sir,” said Prudence readily. “At nine in the morning, Mr. Belfort: I shall be with you.”

===Chinese===
「您瞧瞧这光景，先生」——贝尔福特哀哀切切地把话头递给普鲁登丝——「他们全都笑话我，哪怕我讲的是上苍的显灵降谕。没有敬畏之心，妈的，就是这么回事。」

又爆出一阵哄笑。笑声里透出安东尼爵士低沉的嗓音，满是善意的揶揄。「你自欺欺人呢，查尔斯：除非是阴差阳错，没有哪个天使会光顾你。那八成是魔鬼打的信号，说他就要来认领他的东西了。」他站起身，拿起鼻烟盒。「好了，梅里奥特，我得赶紧去向你妹妹致意，也算讨我自己一个乐子。方才我上楼那会儿，她正叫人团团围住呢。」

「我给您领路，爵士。」普鲁登丝爽快地说。「明早九点，贝尔福特先生：我一定奉陪。」

===Original===
Sir Anthony went out on Mr. Merriot’s arm. Halfway up the broad stairway he said: “It occurs to me you may be in need of a sponsor at White’s, my dear boy. You know you may command me. May I carry your name there?”

So she was to become a member of a club for gentlemen of quality? Egad, where would it all end? No help for it: the large gentleman overwhelmed one. She accepted gracefully, and then with a hesitancy not unpleasing in a young man looked up into the square face, and said diffidently:﻿—“I think you go to some trouble for me, Sir Anthony. From all I have heard I had not thought to find so much kindness in London.”

“There are any number would do the same, boy﻿—my friend Jollyot, for instance. But you had better take me for sponsor.”

“I do, very gladly, sir.”

===Chinese===
安东尼爵士挽着梅里奥特先生的手臂出了门。走到宽阔楼梯的半中央，他说：「我忽然想到，你在怀特俱乐部（White’s）兴许正需要一位引荐人，我亲爱的孩子。你知道的，尽管差遣我。可否容我把你的名字递进去？」

这么说，她竟要跻身一家上流绅士的俱乐部了？哎呀，这一切到哪儿才算个头？没法子：这位大块头绅士的盛情教人无从招架。她优雅地应承下来，随即又带着一股在年轻人身上并不讨嫌的迟疑，抬眼望进那张方正的脸，讷讷地说——「我想，您为我是有些费心了，安东尼爵士。凭我听到的一切，我原不曾指望在伦敦寻到这么多善意。」

「乐意效劳的人多着呢，孩子——比方我那位朋友乔利奥特。不过，你的引荐人还是挑我更妥当。」

「我正是这个意思，而且万分乐意，爵士。」

===Original===
They came into the withdrawing room, where the crowd had dwindled somewhat. Robin was easily found, talking to an exquisite of advanced years. From the looks of it he was receiving some extravagant compliments. Prudence could not but applaud inwardly the pretty modesty of the downcast eye, and the face slightly averted.

Over his fan Robin saw them. He rid himself of his elderly admirer with some adroitness, and came rustling forward. “My dear, I vow I am nigh to swooning from fatigue!” he told Prudence. He swept a curtsey to Sir Anthony, and flashed him a dazzling smile. “Give you good even, sir. I saw you a while back, but there was such a press of people then!”

Sir Anthony’s lips just brushed Robin’s hand. “All gathered about Miss Merriot,” he said gallantly.

“What, with the beautiful Miss Gunning in the same room? Fie, sir, this is flattery! Peter, of your love for me, procure me a glass of negus.”

===Chinese===
两人走进休憩室，那边的人潮已消退了些。罗宾一眼就能找到：他正同一位上了年纪的花花公子说话。看那光景，他领受的是些天花乱坠的恭维。普鲁登丝忍不住在心里喝彩：那低垂的眼帘、微微偏侧的脸蛋，一派娇羞可人的谦逊。

罗宾从折扇上方望见了他们。他颇见机巧地脱开那位年老的倾慕者，窸窸窣窣地迎上前来。「亲爱的，我发誓，我简直要累晕过去了！」他对普鲁登丝说。他朝安东尼爵士盈盈一屈膝，又送他一个耀眼的笑。「祝您今晚安好，爵士。方才我就瞧见您了，可那会儿人围得里三层外三层！」

安东尼爵士的嘴唇在罗宾手背上轻轻一擦。「还不都聚到梅里奥特小姐身边来了嘛。」他殷勤地说。

「什么，美丽的冈宁小姐可同在一间屋里呢！呸，爵士，这分明是恭维话！彼得（Peter），瞧你疼我的份上，去给我弄杯尼格斯热酒（negus）来。」

===Original===
Prudence went away to execute this command; Robin sat down with Sir Anthony upon a couch. When Prudence returned with the wine it seemed as though a good understanding had been established between them. Robin looked up brightly. “Sir Anthony tells me he is to steal you from me on Thursday, my Peter. Thus are we poor sisters imposed upon!”

“I want also to sponsor your Peter at White’s, ma’am,” Sir Anthony said, smiling. “Thus still more are you imposed upon.”

“Oh, these clubs! This means I shall see nothing of the creature.” Miss Merriot put up her fan to hide her face from Sir Anthony, in feigned indignation. So, at least, it appeared, but behind the fan that mobile eyebrow flew up for Prudence’s benefit, and the blue eyes brimmed with laughter. It was done in a trice, and the fan shut again with a snap. “Your kindness to Peter is much greater than your consideration for his poor sister, sir!” she rallied Fanshawe.

===Chinese===
普鲁登丝领命走开了；罗宾同安东尼爵士在一张长榻上坐下。等普鲁登丝端着酒回来，两人之间似乎已经结下了不错的谅解。罗宾亮晶晶地抬起头来。「安东尼爵士告诉我，星期四他要把你从我身边偷走了，我的彼得。我们这些可怜的做妹妹的，就是这样受人摆布！」

「我还想把你的彼得引荐进怀特俱乐部呢，小姐。」安东尼爵士微笑着说。「于是你就更受摆布了。」

「哦，这些个俱乐部！这么一来，我算要见不着这个人影儿了。」梅里奥特小姐举起折扇遮住脸，不让安东尼爵士瞧见，装出一副嗔怪模样。至少看着是这样，可扇子后头，那道灵活的眉毛朝普鲁登丝飞快地扬了扬，一双蓝眼睛盛满了笑意。一瞬间的工夫就演完了，扇子又啪地一声合拢。「您对彼得的这份好意，可比对他可怜妹妹的体恤多得多了，爵士！」她这样打趣范肖。

===Original===
“Why, as to that I offer my apologies, ma’am. I stand somewhat in both your debts.”

“Ah, let’s have done with that!” Prudence said quickly. “There is no debt that I know of.”

“Well, let us say that what you are pleased to call my kindness is naught but a seal to what I hope is a friendship.”

“I’m honoured to have it so, sir,” Prudence said, and felt the colour rise, to her annoyance.

The large gentleman had a mind to befriend her, and there was no help for it. And was one glad of it, or sorry? There was apparently no answer to the riddle.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，说到这个，我向您告罪，小姐。你们两位的情，我都多少欠着一些呢。」

「啊，这话快别说了！」普鲁登丝赶紧说。「据我所知，没有什么债。」

「好吧，那我们不妨这样说：你乐意称作我的好意的，无非是给一段交情落下的印信——但愿那真是一段交情。」

「能这样，我很荣幸，爵士。」普鲁登丝说，随即只觉血往脸上涌，不由得暗恼自己。

这位大块头绅士是打定了主意要同她交好，而她拿他毫无办法。那么，该为此欢喜呢，还是为此懊恼？这个哑谜显然无解。

## XXIX The Ride Through the Night / XXIX 夜驰

===Original===
Shoulder to shoulder, galloping over silent fields in the light of the moon, Prudence and Sir Anthony passed through the country unseen and unheard. There was little said; the pace was too fast, and Prudence too content to talk. This then was the end, in spite of all. The large gentleman swept all before him, and faith, one could not be sorry. Several times she stole a look at that strong profile, pondering it; once he turned his head and met her eyes, and a smile passed between them, but no words.

It seemed she was very much the captive of his sword; there could be nothing more to say now, and, truth to tell, she had no mind to argue.

She supposed they were off to his sister, but the way was unfamiliar to her. The gentleman seemed to know the country like the back of his hand, as the saying was; he eschewed main roads and towns; kept to the solitary lanes, and ever and anon led her ’cross country, or turned off through some copse or meadow to avoid a village, or some lone cottage on the road. There would be no one to tell of this mad flight through the dark hours; no man would have seen them pass, nor any hear the beat of the horses’ hoofs racing by.

Sure, they seemed to be the only people awake in all England. The failing daylight had gone hours since; there had been a spell of darkness when they rested their horses in a walk along a deserted lane; and then the moon had risen, and there was a ghostly pale light to show them the way, and the trees threw weird shadows along the ground. There might be heard now and then the melancholy hoot of an owl, and the chirp and twitter of a nightjar, but all else was hushed: there was not so much as a breath of wind to rustle the leaves on the trees.

===Chinese===
肩并着肩，在月光下飞驰过一片片寂静的田野，普鲁登丝（Prudence）与安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony）穿乡越野，无人得见，无人得闻。两人难得说什么话；马速太快，普鲁登丝也太过心安，无意言语。这便是结局了，任凭此前种种波折。这位大块头绅士所向披靡，说真的，谁也犯不着为此难过。她几次三番偷眼觑那道坚毅的侧影，暗自思量；有一次他偏过头来，正遇上她的目光，两人之间掠过一丝笑意，却谁也没有开口。

看来她是彻头彻尾做了他剑下之俘；如今再没有什么可说的了，而且说老实话，她也无意争辩。

她猜他们这是投奔他妹妹去，可这条路她并不认得。这位绅士对乡野地势仿佛了如指掌——正如俗话所说；他避开大道与城镇，专拣僻静的小径走，时不时引她越野穿行，要不就拐进一片矮林或草场，绕开村庄，或是路上孤零零的村舍。这趟暗夜里的疯狂驰驱，往后没有人能说与人听；没有人见他们经过，也没有人听见疾驰而过的马蹄声。

当真，他们仿佛是整个英格兰仅有的两个醒着的人。暮色褪尽已有好几个钟头；方才一段漆黑里，他们缓辔而行，沿一条荒僻小径遛马歇力；随后月亮升起来，洒下一片鬼魅似的惨白微光，替他们照路，树木在地上投下幢幢怪影。时不时听得见猫头鹰凄凉的啼叫，还有夜鹰的啁啾呢喃，除此之外万籁俱寂：连一缕能吹得树叶沙沙作响的风都没有。

===Original===
They saw squat villages lying darkly ahead, swung off to skirt them round, seeing occasionally the warm glow of a lamp-lit window, and reached the road again beyond. Once a dog barked in the distance and once a small animal ran across the road in front of them, and the mare shied and stumbled.

There was a quick hand ready to snatch at the bridle. Prudence laughed, and shook her head, bringing the mare up again. “Don’t fear for me, kind sir.”

“I need not, I know. Yet I can’t help myself.”

The moon was high above them when they reined in to a walk again. Prudence was helped into her greatcoat; the horses drew close, and the riders’ knees touched now and again.

===Chinese===
他们望见黑黢黢蹲伏在前方的村庄，便拨转马头绕行，偶尔瞥见一扇灯烛通明的窗口透出暖光，绕过村去，再回到路上。有一回远处狗吠了一声；又有一回，一只小兽从他们马前窜过马路，母马惊得一个趔趄。

一只手眼疾手快，霎时便来拢住笼头。普鲁登丝笑了，摇一摇头，把母马重新带稳。「别替我担心，好心的爵士。」

「用不着担心，这我知道。可我管不住自己。」

待到再度收缰改为缓行时，月亮已高悬头顶。普鲁登丝由他伺候着穿好大衣；两匹马偎近了些，两位骑手的膝盖时不时碰到一处。

===Original===
“Tired, child?” Sir Anthony’s free hand came to rest a moment on hers.

Faith, it was a fine thing to be so precious in a man’s eyes. “Not I, sir. Do you take me into Hampshire?”

“Be sure of it. I’ll have you under my sister’s wing at last.”

Prudence made a wry face. “Egad, I wonder what she will say to me?”

There was a little laugh. “Nothing, child. She’s too indolent.”

===Chinese===
「累了吧，孩子？」安东尼爵士空着的那只手覆上她的手，停了片刻。

说真的，在一个男人眼里这般金贵，是件美妙的事。「不累，爵士。您这是要带我去汉普郡（Hampshire）？」

「这还用说。我总算能把你安置到我妹妹的羽翼底下去了。」

普鲁登丝做了个苦脸。「哎呀，真不知她会怎么说我？」

他轻笑了一声。「什么也不会说，孩子。她太懒了。」

===Original===
“Oh, like Sir Anthony Fanshawe﻿—upon occasion.”

“Worse. Beatrice is of too ample a girth to indulge even in surprise. Or so she says. I believe you will like her.”

“I am more concerned, sir, that she may be pleased to like me.”

“She will, don’t fear it. She has a fondness for me.”

“I thank you for the pretty compliment, kind sir. You would say you may order her liking at your will.”

===Chinese===
「哦，就像安东尼·范肖爵士——偶尔犯一回懒。」

「更糟。比阿特丽斯（Beatrice）腰围太可观，连吃惊这份消遣都享用不起。至少她自己是这么说的。我相信你会喜欢她。」

「我更放心不下的，爵士，是她肯不肯屈尊喜欢我。」

「她会的，别怕。她疼着我呢。」

「多谢您这句动听的恭维，好心的爵士。您这是想说，她的好恶也任凭您一手调遣。」

===Original===
“You’re a rogue. I would say she will be prepared to like you from the outset. Sir Thomas follows her lead in all things. It’s a quaint couple.”

“Ay, and what are we? Egad, I believe I’ve fallen into a romantic venture, and I always thought I was not made for it. I lack the temperament of your true heroine.”

There was a smile hovering about Sir Anthony’s mouth. “Do you?” he said. “Then who, pray tell me, might stand for a true heroine?”

“Oh, Letty Grayson, sir. She has a burning passion for romance and adventure.”

===Chinese===
「你是个小滑头。我是说，她保管会一开始就打定主意喜欢你。托马斯爵士（Sir Thomas）事事都随她引领。是别致的一对儿。」

「是啊，那我们又算什么呢？哎呀，我看自己八成是卷进了一桩浪漫奇遇，可我一向以为自己天生不是这块料。我缺真正女主角的那副脾性。」

安东尼爵士嘴角浮起一丝笑意。「是么？」他说。「那么请教，谁当得起真正的女主角？」

「哦，莱蒂·格雷森（Letty Grayson）呀，爵士。她对浪漫和奇遇怀着炽热的痴情。」

===Original===
“Which Madam Prudence lacks. Dear me!”

“Entirely, sir. I was made for sobriety.”

“It looked excessively like it﻿—back yonder in the coach,” said Sir Anthony, thinking of that shortened sword held to poor Matthew’s throat.

“Needs must when the old gentleman drives,” said Prudence, smiling. “I should like to breed pigs, Sir Anthony, I believe.”

===Chinese===
「而这恰恰是普鲁登丝小姐没有的。哎呀呀！」

「半点也没有，爵士。我天生是安分守己的命。」

「瞧着可真像啊——方才在那辆马车里的时候。」安东尼爵士说，心里想着那柄抵在可怜的马修（Matthew）喉咙上的截短佩剑。

「老先生赶车，由不得人不坐呀。」普鲁登丝含笑说。「说起来，我倒是想养猪来着，安东尼爵士，真的。」

===Original===
“You shall,” he promised. “I have several pigs down at Wych End.”

The chuckle came, but a grave look followed. “Lud, sir, it’s very well, but you lose your head over this.”

“An enlivening sensation, child.”

“Maybe. But I am not fit to be my Lady Fanshawe.”

===Chinese===
「保管叫你养上。」他应许道。「威奇恩德（Wych End）家里我就养着好几头猪。」

她咯咯一笑，随即神色又郑重起来。「老天，爵士，这一切自然再好不过，可您为这桩事昏了头啦。」

「昏头是一种叫人精神一振的滋味，孩子。」

「也许吧。可我不是当范肖夫人（my Lady Fanshawe）的料。」

===Original===
The hand closed over her wrist; there was some sternness in the pressure. “It is when you talk in that vein that I can find it in me to be angry with you, Prudence.”

“Behold me in a terror. But I speak only the truth, sir. I wish you would think on it. One day I will tell you the tale of my life.”

“I’ve no doubt I shall be vastly entertained,” said Sir Anthony.

“Oh, it’s very edifying, sir, but it’s not what the life of my Lady Fanshawe should be.”

===Chinese===
那只手扣住她的手腕；力道里透着几分严厉。「普鲁登丝，偏偏就是你说这种话的时候，我才能打心眼里生得起你的气。」

「瞧我把您吓得魂不附体。可我说的句句是实话，爵士。但愿您肯细细想想。哪天我把我的生平讲给您听。」

「想必我定会听得津津有味。」安东尼爵士说。

「哦，那故事颇有教益，爵士，只是范肖夫人的日子不该是那样过的。」

===Original===
“Who made you judge of that, child?”

She laughed. “You’re infatuated, sir. But I’m not respectable, give you my word. In boy’s clothes I’ve kept a gaming-house with my father; I’ve escaped out of windows and up chimneys; I’ve travelled in the tail of an army not English; I’ve played a dozen parts, and﻿—well, it has been necessary for me often to carry a pistol in my pocket.”

Sir Anthony’s head was turned towards her. “My dear, will you never realize that I adore you?”

She looked down at her bridle hand; she was shaken and blushing like any silly chit, forsooth! “It was not my ambition to make you admire me by telling you those things, sir.”

===Chinese===
「谁封你做这个裁判官了，孩子？」

她笑了。「您是鬼迷心窍了，爵士。可我并非体面人，这话我可以向您担保。穿着男装，我陪着父亲开过赌馆；我从窗户里逃过命，顺烟囱爬上去过；我尾随过一支并非英格兰的军队辗转奔波；我扮过一打角色，还有——唉，口袋里揣把手枪，于我是家常便饭。」

安东尼爵士把头朝她转过来。「亲爱的，你难道永远明白不过来，我是爱慕你的？」

她低头看自己执缰的那只手；她心旌摇荡，脸红得活脱脱像个傻丫头，真是出息！「我说这些，可不曾指望借它们赢得您的爱慕，爵士。」

===Original===
“No, egad, you hoped to make me draw back. I believe you don’t appreciate yourself in the least.”

It was very true; she had none of her father’s conceit; she had never troubled to think about herself at all. She raised puzzled eyes. “I don’t know how it is, Tony, but you seem to think me something wonderful, and indeed I am not.”

“I won’t weary you with my reasons for holding to that opinion,” said Sir Anthony, amused. “Two will suffice. I have never seen you betray fear; I have never seen you lose your head. I don’t believe you’ve done so.”

Prudence accepted this; it seemed just. “No, ’tis as Robin says: I’ve a maddening lack of imagination. The old gentleman tells me it is my mother in me, that I can never be in a flutter.”

===Chinese===
「不曾，哎呀，你是指望叫我知难而退。我看你压根儿不把自己的好处当一回事。」

这话半点不假；她没有父亲那份自负；她从不曾费心琢磨过自己。她抬眼，满目困惑。「我说不清怎么回事，托尼（Tony），可您仿佛把我当成什么了不得的人物，其实我当真不是。」

「我不拿我抱定这看法的种种道理来烦你了。」安东尼爵士说，只觉有趣。「两条足矣。我从未见你露过惧色；我从未见你失过方寸。谅你也不曾失过。」

普鲁登丝认下了这话；听着公道。「是啊，正像罗宾（Robin）说的：我这人缺乏想象力，缺乏得叫人光火。老先生告诉我，这是我随了母亲，永远不会心慌意乱。」

===Original===
Sir Anthony leaned forward, and took the mare’s bridle above the bit; the horses stopped, and stood still, very close together. An arm was round Prudence’s shoulder; the roan’s reins lay loose on his neck. Prudence turned a little towards Sir Anthony, and was gripped to rest against a broad shoulder. He bent his head over hers; she had a wild heartbeat, and put out a hand with a little murmur of agitation. It was taken in a firm clasp: for the first time Sir Anthony kissed her, and if that first kiss fell awry, as a first kiss must, the second was pressed ruthlessly on her quivering lips. She was held in a hard embrace; she flung up an arm round Sir Anthony’s neck, and gave a little sob, half of protest, half of gladness.

The horses moved slowly on; the riders were hand-locked. “Never?” Sir Anthony said softly.

She remembered she had said she could never be in a flutter. It seemed one was wrong. “I thought not indeed.” Her fingers trembled in his. “I had not before experienced﻿—*that*, you see.”

He smiled, and raised her hand to his mouth. “Do I not know it?” he said.

===Chinese===
安东尼爵士俯身向前，在嚼口上方攥住母马的笼头；两匹马站定下来，紧紧偎在一处。一条手臂环上普鲁登丝的肩头；菊花青马的缰绳松松垂在颈子上。普鲁登丝微微朝安东尼爵士侧过身，便被搂紧，靠上一副宽阔的肩膀。他的头俯罩住她的头；她一颗心狂跳起来，伸出手去，低低呢喃了一声，满心激动。那只手被牢牢握住：安东尼爵士第一次吻了她，而这初吻若说像天下初吻一样落偏了，第二记便不容分说，紧紧压上她颤动的双唇。她被牢牢箍在怀抱里；她扬起一条手臂，环住安东尼爵士的脖颈，低低啜泣了一声，一半是推拒，一半是欢喜。

两匹马缓缓前行；两位骑手的手紧紧交握。「从不会？」安东尼爵士轻声说。

她想起自己说过，她永远不会心慌意乱。看来人也会有说错的时候。「我原以为当真不会呢。」她的手指在他掌中颤动。「那种滋味——*那个*，您明白，我从前不曾领教过。」

他微微一笑，抬起她的手送到唇边。「我还会不知道么？」他说。

===Original===
The grey eyes were honest, and looked gravely. “You could not know it.”

The smile deepened. “Of course I could know it, my dear. Oh, foolish Prue!”

It was all very mysterious; the gentleman appeared to be omniscient. And what in the world was there to amuse him so? She gave a sigh of content. “You give me the happy ending I never thought to have,” she said.

“I suppose you thought I was like to expose you in righteous wrath when I discovered the truth?”

===Chinese===
那双灰眼睛坦诚无欺，神色庄重。「您不可能知道的。」

笑意更深了。「我当然可能知道，亲爱的。哦，傻普鲁（Prue）！」

这一切真是玄之又玄；这位绅士仿佛无所不知。可究竟又是什么事让他这样乐不可支？她心满意足地叹了口气。「您给了我一个我从不曾奢望的幸福结局。」她说。

「我猜，你以为我一查明真相，便会义愤填膺地揭发你？」

===Original===
“Something of the sort, sir,” she admitted.

“You’re an amazing woman, my dear,” was all he said.

They rode on in silence, and quickened presently to a canter. “I want to rest you awhile,” Sir Anthony said. “Keep an eye for a likely barn.”

“The horses would be glad of it.” Prudence bent to pat the mare’s neck.

===Chinese===
「多少是这么盘算的，爵士。」她承认道。

「你真是个叫人惊叹的女人，亲爱的。」他只说了这一句。

两人默默骑行，不多时便催马小跑起来。「我要让你歇一会儿。」安东尼爵士说。「留神找一座合用的谷仓。」

「马儿们也求之不得。」普鲁登丝俯身拍拍母马的脖颈。

===Original===
They were in farmland now; it was not long before they found such a barn. It lay by some tumbledown sheds across a paddock, where a little rippling stream separated field from field. The farm buildings were hidden from sight by a rise in the ground; they rode forward, past what was left of a haystack, and dismounted outside the barn.

It was not locked; the door hung on rusty hinges, and inside there was the sweet smell of hay.

Sir Anthony propped the door wide to let in the moonlight. “Empty,” he said. “Can you brave a possible rat?”

Prudence was unbuckling her saddle-girths. “I’ve done so before now, but I confess I dislike ’em.” She lifted off the saddle and had it taken quickly from her.

===Chinese===
眼下他们已到了农耕乡；不多时便寻着了这么一座谷仓。它在围场对面几间塌败的棚屋旁，一条淙淙的小溪在那里隔开一垄垄田地。农舍场院让一处隆起的高坡遮得看不见；他们策马上前，经过一堆干草垛剩下的残垛，在谷仓外下了马。

谷仓没有上锁；门吊在锈蚀的合页上，里头一股干草的清香。

安东尼爵士把门撑得大开，好放进月光。「空的。」他说。「万一有耗子，你敢不敢招架？」

普鲁登丝正解着鞍带。「从前也招架过，不过不瞒您说，我腻味那东西。」她卸下马鞍，那鞍子立刻叫人从她手里接了过去。

===Original===
“Learn, child, that I am here to wait on you.”

She shook her head, and went on to unbridle the mare. “Attend to Rufus, my lord. What, am I one of your frail, helpless creatures then?”

“You’ve a distressing independence, on the contrary.” Sir Anthony removed the saddle from the roan’s back, and led him into the barn. For the next few minutes he was busy with a wisp of straw, rubbing the big horse down.

Prudence went expertly to work on her mare, and stood back at last. “It’s warm enough here,” she remarked. “They’ll take no hurt. When they’ve cooled we’d best take them down to the stream. Lord, but I’m thirsty myself!”

===Chinese===
「学着点儿，孩子，我此来就是专司伺候你的。」

她摇头，继续去解母马的笼头。「照管您的鲁弗斯（Rufus）去吧，我的爵爷大人。怎么，我就成了您那些娇怯怯、弱不禁风的小东西中的一个了？」

「恰恰相反，你这股子独立劲儿真叫人伤脑筋。」安东尼爵士从菊花青马背上卸下马鞍，牵它进谷仓。接下来几分钟，他拿一小束干草，忙着给这匹高头大马上上下下地擦蹭。

普鲁登丝熟练地拾掇自己的母马，末了退后一步。「这儿够暖和。」她说。「它们冻不着。等汗消了，咱们最好把它们牵到溪边去。老天，我自己倒渴得慌！」

===Original===
Sir Anthony threw away the wisp of straw. “Come then. There’s naught but my hands to make a cup for you, alack.”

But they served well enough. They came back at length to the barn, and found the horses lipping at a pile of hay in the corner. A bed was made for Prudence. “Now sleep, my dear,” Sir Anthony said. “You need it, God knows.”

She sank down on to the sweet-smelling couch. “What of yourself, sir?”

“I’m going to take the horses down to the stream. Be at ease concerning me. What, must you be worrying still?”

===Chinese===
安东尼爵士扔掉那束干草。「那就来吧。只可惜没有别的，我这双手只好凑合给你当杯盏。」

好在这双手倒也使得。他们终于回到谷仓，只见两匹马正撮着嘴唇，吃角落里的一堆干草。有人给普鲁登丝铺好了一个铺位。「睡吧，亲爱的。」安东尼爵士说。「你缺觉缺得厉害，天知道。」

她颓然躺倒在清香扑鼻的草铺上。「那您自己呢，爵士？」

「我要把马牵到溪边去。别惦记我。怎么，你还非得牵挂不休不成？」

===Original===
She lay back with her head pillowed on her folded greatcoat, and smiled up at him. “A pair of vagabonds,” she said. “Faith, what have I done to the elegant Sir Anthony Fanshawe? It’s scandalous, I protest, to set you at odds with the law.”

Sir Anthony led the horses to the door. “Oh, you must always be thinking you had the ordering of this!” he said teasingly, and went out.

When he brought the horses back her eyes were closed, and she had a hand slipped under her cheek. Sir Anthony took off his greatcoat, and went down on his knee to lay it gently over her. She did not stir. For a moment he stayed, looking down at her, then he rose, and went soft-footed to the door, and paced slowly up and down in the moonlight. Inside the barn the horses munched steadily at the armful of hay he had given them. There was silence over the fields; the world slept, but Sir Anthony Fanshawe stayed wakeful, guarding his lady’s rest.

===Chinese===
她仰面躺下，头枕着叠好的大衣，朝他嫣然一笑。「一对流浪汉。」她说。「说真的，我都把这位风雅的安东尼·范肖爵士弄成什么样子了？竟害得您与王法作起对来，我郑重抗议，这实在不成体统。」

安东尼爵士把马牵到门口。「唉，你总以为这一切都是你一手安排的！」他打趣了一句，走了出去。

等他把马牵回来，她双眼已经阖上，一只手垫在脸颊底下。安东尼爵士脱下自己的大衣，单膝跪下，轻轻给她盖上。她一动不动。他停留片刻，低头端详她，随后起身，蹑手蹑脚走到门口，在月光里缓缓地来回踱步。谷仓里，两匹马不紧不慢地咀嚼他抱给它们的那抱干草。田野上一片沉寂；世人都睡了，安东尼·范肖爵士却醒着，守着他心爱的姑娘安眠。

## XXII Tortuous Methods of My Lord Barham / XXII 巴勒姆勋爵的迂回手段

===Original===
Robin preserved the light manner, but he had begun to chafe at his petticoats. Faith, the old gentleman seemed to do nothing and there were rumours current now that Rensley, as soon as he was able to leave his room, meant to bring a case against his would-be cousin. Robin had small mind to go on playing the lady indefinitely. He believed the Black Domino remained in Letty’s memory, but he had little chance of seeing her as the days passed. She was out driving, or she was visiting, or even she was indisposed. When he did meet her she was abstracted, and volunteered no confidences. There were shadows under her eyes: her aunt said it was no wonder, since nowadays she was seldom in bed before midnight; Robin dared to hope a Black Domino had induced this wistfulness.

Prudence thought nothing at all of it; she was rather preoccupied with her own affairs, and showed but slight interest even when Robin spoke of John’s new behaviour. Robin became aware of the frequent absences of his faithful henchman, and receiving only evasive replies to a sharp question or two, immediately suspected activity on the part of my Lord Barham. Prudence said placidly that it was very possible she thought they were like to know all soon enough.

She was right: in a short while my lord came to pay a morning visit in Arlington Street, and having rapturously kissed my Lady Lowestoft’s hands, requested the favour of some private talk with his son.

My lady opined mischief to be brewing, shook a playful finger, and went off most obligingly.

===Chinese===
罗宾（Robin）照旧端着那副轻松做派，可他已渐渐对这身裙子生出不耐。说真的，老先生似乎毫无动作，市面上如今倒有了传言，说伦斯利（Rensley）只等能出得房门，便要与那冒牌堂亲对簿公堂。罗宾可无意把这小姐一角无休无止地演下去。他相信黑衣多米诺（Black Domino）还留驻在莱蒂的记忆里，可日子一天天过去，他却难得见上她一面。她不是驾车出游，便是出门访客，再不然就是身子欠安。真见了面，她也心不在焉，绝口不吐半点心事。她眼下隐隐挂着两团青影：她姑母说这不足为奇，如今她难得在午夜前上床；罗宾竟敢指望，这份怅惘正是那位黑衣多米诺引出来的。

普鲁登丝（Prudence）对此全不上心；她自己的事已够她操心，就连罗宾说起约翰（John）近来一反常态的举止，她也兴味寥寥。罗宾察觉出他那位忠心耿耿的跟班频频不见人影，尖锐地追问了一两句，只换来闪烁其词的回答，立时疑心这是巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）在那头动作频频。普鲁登丝心平气和地说，这大有可能；依她看，他们不出多久便什么都会知道的。

她说得不错：没过多久，勋爵大人便到阿灵顿街（Arlington Street）来做上午的拜访，喜孜孜地吻过洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft）的双手，随即请求赏个方便，同他儿子私下叙谈。

夫人料定又有什么祸事在酝酿，顽皮地摇了摇一根手指，便极其识趣地走开了。

===Original===
Robin turned one of the bracelets on his arm, and shot a quick look at his father. “Well, sir?”

My lord dusted his sleeve with a lace handkerchief. “I come, my Robin, at last. There is work on hand for you, my son.”

“God be praised for that! Do I come out of these petticoats, sir?”

“For a little, son, for a little only! Patience! I unfold a miracle.”

“I’m all attention, sir. Let me hear it.”

===Chinese===
罗宾转着手臂上的一只手镯，朝父亲疾扫一眼。「怎么说，先生？」

勋爵大人用花边手帕掸了掸袖子。「我来了，我的罗宾，总算来了。手头有一桩差事要派给你，我的儿子。」

「那真要感谢上帝！我能从这身裙子里脱身了么，先生？」

「只脱得一时，孩子，仅仅一时！耐着性子！我这便揭晓一桩奇迹。」

「我洗耳恭听，先生。说来吧。」

===Original===
My lord sat down by the window. There was a gleam in his eyes Robin knew full well, and the smile curling his lips was one of reflective pleasure. By the signs my lady was right, and there was mischief brewing indeed. “My son, I see the end of the road. It becomes plain at last. I arrange all with wonderful subtlety. You may say that I pull a string here, and a string there, and the puppets move.”

“Lord, sir! Am I one of your puppets?”

“But, of course, my Robin!” said his lordship affectionately. “I set the stage for you to play the hero. You shall thank me.”

“Shall I, sir? It’s a part I’m not in the habit of playing, that of hero.”

“I assign to you a role the most romantic,” announced my lord. “Certainly you shall thank me.”

“Well, let me hear it, sir. You become interesting.”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人在窗旁落座。他眼中那点光亮罗宾再清楚不过，唇边漾起的笑意也是一番回味无穷的愉悦。照这些迹象看，夫人说得没错，确有祸事在酝酿。「我的儿子，我已望见这条路的尽头。事情终于豁然开朗。我把一切都运筹得精妙绝伦。你不妨说，我在这里牵一根线，在那里牵一根线，木偶们便都动了起来。」

「老天，先生！我莫非也是您的木偶之一？」

「当然是，我的罗宾！」勋爵大人满怀慈爱地说。「我搭好戏台，让你来演英雄。你会感谢我的。」

「会么，先生？英雄这个角色，我可没有演惯。」

「我派给你的是一个最最罗曼蒂克的角色。」勋爵大人宣布。「你必定会感谢我的。」

「好吧，说来听听，先生。您变得有意思起来了。」

===Original===
“I become dangerous, Robin﻿—dangerous as only I can be. I am Nemesis, no less! And you﻿—you are the instrument to my hand. You shall rescue a lady, and kill the villain.”

“Out, sword!” said Robin flippantly. “You hold me entranced, sir. Who is the lady?”

My lord looked surprised. “Who but the lady of your heart, my son? Do I arrange so clumsily?”

Robin stiffened. The flippancy left him, and he spoke crisply. “What’s this?”

“I kiss my fingers to her!” My lord made a gesture very French. “She is ravishing!”

“Who?”

===Chinese===
「我这是变得危险了，罗宾——危险得唯有我方能做到。我就是涅墨西斯（Nemesis），不折不扣！而你——你便是我手中称手的工具。你将搭救一位小姐，再宰了那个恶棍。」

「出鞘吧，宝剑！」罗宾轻飘飘地说。「您可把我迷住了，先生。那位小姐是谁？」

勋爵大人面露讶色。「除了你心坎上的那位小姐还能是谁，我的孩子？难道我运筹得这般笨拙？」

罗宾绷紧了。轻佻之态倏然敛去，他清脆利落地问：「这是怎么说？」

「我为她吻我这两根指尖！」勋爵大人打了个十足法国派头的手势。「她真教人心醉神驰！」

「谁？」

===Original===
My lord’s eyes widened reproachfully. “Why, Letitia, of course; I should not arrange for you to rescue another. Did you﻿—it is really possibly that you thought I did not know? My son, my son, you grieve me, positively you grieve me!”

“Accept my apologies, sir. I suppose you know everything. But what’s this talk of rescues, and who’s your villain?”

“Gently, my hothead, gently! You shall know all. You will rescue her tomorrow night; the villain is my poor blundering friend of Munich days.”

“What! Markham again! You’re mad, sir; he would never dare a second time, nor she consent.”

“You discount my influence, Robin. Remember that she and Markham too are my puppets.”

Robin got up rather quickly. “What devilry’s this? Be plain with me, if you please, sir!”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人睁圆了眼睛，目光里尽是责备。「自然是莉蒂西娅（Letitia）了；我总不至于运筹着让你去救旁人。莫非——你竟当真以为我不知道么？我的儿子，我的儿子，你叫我伤心，千真万确叫我伤心！」

「谨向您告罪，先生。想必您无所不知。可这搭救之说是怎么回事，您的恶棍又是哪位？」

「慢慢来，我的急性子，慢慢来！你自会知道一切。明晚你就去救她；那恶棍么，便是我那可怜又专会闯祸的慕尼黑旧友。」

「什么！又是马卡姆（Markham）！您疯了，先生；他决不敢再来第二回，她也断不会点头。」

「你低估了我的影响力，罗宾。记住，她和马卡姆也都一样是我的木偶。」

罗宾腾地起身。「这是耍的什么鬼把戏？请您对我说个明白，先生！」

===Original===
My lord put the tips of his fingers together. “She elopes with my Munich friend tomorrow evening, from Vauxhall Gardens, whither she is bound.”

“She elopes!” Robin was thunderstruck. “And you tell me you arrange it!”

“Certainly,” said my lord. “It is entirely my doing. I am to be congratulated.”

“Not by me, sir,” said Robin, and there was an edge to the words.

“Even by you, child. You shall at last appreciate me. Sit down and all shall be told you.”

Robin sank back into his seat. “Go on, sir. I suppose one of us must be mad. Why have you arranged﻿—if indeed you have﻿—a thing so criminal?”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人把指尖对指尖地合在一处。「明晚她要同我那位慕尼黑朋友私奔，从沃克斯豪尔花园（Vauxhall Gardens）走——她本来就要赴那儿去。」

「私奔！」罗宾如遭雷击。「而您竟对我说，这是您运筹安排的！」

「正是。」勋爵大人说。「这完完全全是我的手笔。该受道贺的是我。」

「我可不来道贺，先生。」罗宾说，话音里带着刺。

「偏你也要道贺，孩子。你到底会赏识我的。坐下，一切都讲给你听。」

罗宾跌回椅中。「讲下去，先生。依我看，我们两个总有一个是疯了。您为什么运筹出——倘若当真是您运筹的——这么一桩伤天害理的事？」

===Original===
My lord reflected. “It seemed the most poetic justice,” he explained. “It is really exquisitely thought of.” He swung one foot, and smiled sweetly down at the silver buckle. “Nemesis!” he sighed. “My Munich friend thought me of so small account: I don’t forgive that. He conceived that he could bend me﻿—me, Tremaine of Barham!﻿—to his paltry will! He dared﻿—you shudder at such temerity﻿—he dared to use threats to me! He sees me as a cat’s-paw. Almost I can find it in my heart to pity him. But it was an impertinence.” He shook his head severely.

“Markham knows something of you?” Robin was frowning. “That letter?”

My lord raised his eyes. “My son, you have a little of my swiftness of apprehension. He had that letter of which I told you. How he came by it I do not know. I admit it freely: I do not know. It is entirely unimportant, or I should have found out. He brought it to my rooms. He demanded money.” His lordship laughed at the thought. “He was very clever, no doubt, but he did not know that he had chosen a man of supernatural parts for adversary. He showed me my own letter; he told me he knew me for Colney, and I am sure he expected to see me in a palsy of fear.”

A smile flitted across Robin’s face. There was a light in his eyes which made his resemblance to his father very strong. “I dare swear he was disappointed, sir.”

“I fear so, I fear so, my Robin. And was I afraid? Was there fear beneath my sangfroid? No, my son! There was a relief quite enormous. At last I knew where my letter was to be found. I do not fear the danger I can see. My Munich friend﻿—his manners appal me; I am aghast at such a lack of polish!﻿—had delivered himself into my hands.”

“Lord, the man’s a fool!” said Robin. “But, troth, he doesn’t know you, sir!”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人沉吟了一下。「这在我看来，是最富诗意的一场公道。」他解释道。「实在是想得精妙入骨。」他悠荡着一只脚，甜甜地俯视着鞋上的银扣微笑。「涅墨西斯！」他叹道。「我那位慕尼黑朋友竟把我看得这般无足轻重：这我不能原谅。他竟以为能让我——我，巴勒姆的特雷梅因（Tremaine of Barham）！——屈从于他那一文不值的心意！他竟敢——这样的孟妄你听了都要打颤——他竟敢对我出言威胁！他视我如一只火中取栗的猫爪子。我几乎要打心底里生出几分怜悯他了。可这终究是一桩僭慢。」他严峻地摇了摇头。

「马卡姆知道您的某些底细？」罗宾皱起眉头。「是那封信？」

勋爵大人抬起眼来。「我的儿子，你多少承了我一点颖悟如疾的本事。他手里握着的，正是我同你说过的那封信。他如何到手，我不知道。我坦然承认：我不知道。这事全然无关紧要，否则我早已查个水落石出。他把信带到我的住处。他索要银钱。」勋爵大人想到这里笑了。「他无疑自以为很高明，却不知道他挑来做对手的，是一位身怀超凡才具的人物。他把信拿给我看；他说他识得我就是科尔尼（Colney），我敢说他满心以为会看见我吓得瘫作一团。」

一抹笑意掠过罗宾的脸。他眼中闪起的光芒，使他与他父亲像到了十分。「我敢打赌，他失望透了，先生。」

「恐怕如此，恐怕如此，我的罗宾。那我当时怕了么？我那副泰然自若底下可藏着惧意？没有，我的儿子！我心头只有一股天大的宽慰。我总算知道那封信到哪儿去寻了。我从不惧怕看得见的危险。我那位慕尼黑朋友——他的做派叫我不寒而栗；如此缺乏教养，实在令我骇然！——已经把自己送进了我的掌心。」

「老天，这人是个傻瓜！」罗宾说。「不过老实讲，他不认识您呀，先生！」

===Original===
“No one knows me,” said my lord austerely. “But might he not have descried that in my bearing which speaks greatness? No, he was absorbed in the admiration of his own poor wits. I descended to crush one infinitely inferior to me, and he could not even appreciate the manner in which it was done. I could wish him worthier of my enmity. Observe, my son, the deficiencies in his intelligence! He thought to obtain a promise in writing from me to pay him untold gold on the day when I am acknowledged to be Tremaine of Barham!”

“H’m!” said Robin. “An optimistic gentleman. And you said?”

“I had to open his eyes. I dispelled the illusion. A plan so subtle that almost it took my breath away formed itself in my brain. You remember, my son, those papers I told you I held?”

“Good God!” said Robin. His father began seriously to alarm him. “I remember.”

“There was one written by﻿—you would never guess﻿—that foolish Humphrey Grayson. A trifle: half promises which he never fulfilled. But enough for my purpose.”

===Chinese===
「无人识得我。」勋爵大人凛然说道。「可他难道看不出，我举止之间自有一股陈述伟大的气象？不，他光顾着欣赏自己那点可怜的机巧了。我纡尊降贵，去碾死一个远不如我的人，他竟连这手段的高明都领会不了。我真希望他更配做我的仇敌一点。你瞧瞧，我的儿子，他的智力有多少欠缺！他竟想叫我立下一纸凭据，许诺在世人公认我为巴勒姆的特雷梅因之日，付给他不知其数的黄金！」

「嗯哼！」罗宾说。「真是位乐观的绅士。那您怎么说？」

「我不得不开开他的眼。我把他的幻象驱散了。一个精妙得几乎让我自己都屏息的计划，在我脑中成了形。你记得吧，我的儿子，我告诉过你我握着的那几份文书？」

「我的上帝！」罗宾说。他父亲开始叫他当真惶恐起来。「我记得。」

「其中一份出自——你绝猜不着——那个糊涂的汉弗莱·格雷森（Humphrey Grayson）之手。区区一件小玩意：几条半截子的许诺，他后来一条也没兑过。但用于我的目的，已经尽够。」

===Original===
“Thunder an’ turf! Was Grayson in the Rebellion?” cried Robin.

“You may say he once toyed with the notion. It came to naught. He is one of those who waits to see which way the weathervane points. That silly letter I gave to Mr. Markham in exchange for my own, which I have since burned. Do you begin to appreciate the subtlety of my plan, Robin?”

“I’m very far from appreciating it, sir. Be a little plainer! Am I to understand that you gave Markham this paper so he might force Letty into marrying him?”

My lord nodded. “You have it pat, my son.”

Robin’s brow was black. “Do you ask my appreciation of this, sir? You think I shall admire so dastardly a plot? Good God, was there no other way of getting your letter back?”

===Chinese===
「雷公在上！格雷森也沾过那场叛乱？」罗宾叫道。

「可以说他一度拿这念头当过消遣。终归没成事。他是那种专候着看风向标朝哪边指、再行事的人。那封蠢信，我给了马卡姆先生，换回了我自己那一封——那封我后来就烧了。你可开始赏识我计划的精妙了么，罗宾？」

「我离赏识还差得十万八千里呢，先生。您说浅白些！难道我该理解为，您把这份文书给了马卡姆，好让他拿去胁迫莱蒂嫁给他？」

勋爵大人点点头。「你说得一字不差，我的儿子。」

罗宾面色阴沉。「您还指望我赏识这个么，先生？您以为我会佩服这么卑劣的一条毒计？老天爷，难道就没有别的法子把您的信弄回来？」

===Original===
“Oh, at least a dozen!” answered his lordship airily. “I rejected them all; they were too clumsy. And I want Markham out of the way, besides. He were far better dead. You will attend to that. Consider also that this way I present you to your lady in the guise of a hero. It is a tour de force, and as such﻿—irresistible to me!” He smiled benignantly. “Until now you are a woman in her eyes; she has no chance to fall in love with you. When you are disclosed a man she might even feel anger. But I arrange that you shall be her deliverer. In a word, I provide for your romance at the very moment of removing the last boulder from my own path. When I think on it, my son, I begin, faintly, to realize the extent of my greatness.”

For the life of him Robin could not help laughing. Faith, how like the old gentleman to choose a way so tortuous and intricate. But to place Letty in such a position﻿—to work so on her fears﻿—that was unpardonable. “I believe you mean well, sir, but I must censure your methods. I could have got that paper from Markham without drawing Letty in.”

“But how crude! how unworthy a scheme when placed beside mine!” protested my lord. “And you forget that I arrange this way the death of Markham. A person of such boorish manners is not fit to remain in the same world with me. You must perceive the truth of that.”

===Chinese===
「哦，少说也有一打！」勋爵大人轻飘飘地答道。「我尽数驳回了；它们都太拙劣。况且，我还嫌马卡姆碍事呢。他呀，还是死了干净。这个就交给你去办。你再想想，这样我便把你装点成英雄，呈到你那位小姐面前。此乃神来一笔，而神来之笔——于我是无从抗拒的！」他慈祥地微笑。「直到今日，你在她眼里始终是个女人；她无从对你萌生爱意。待到你显出男儿本相，她说不定还要生一场气。可我运筹安排，教你做她的救主。一句话，在我为自己搬开道上最后一块巨石的那一刻，也顺手备下了你的罗曼史。每念及此，我的儿子，我便开始隐隐领会我自己伟大的幅员。」

罗宾要笑，怎么也忍不住。说真的，专拣这么一条迂回曲折的路子走，老先生真是一如既往。可把莱蒂摆进这样的境地——这样在她恐惧上头做功夫——那是不可饶恕的。「我相信您用心不坏，先生，可您的手段，我不能不指摘。我原可以从马卡姆手里把那份文书取来，用不着把莱蒂牵扯进去。」

「可是何其粗糙！同我的计划并置一处，又何其不衬！」勋爵大人抗议道。「况且你忘了，我这一运筹，还一并安排下了马卡姆的死。举止这般粗野之人，不配与我同存于一个世界。这层道理你必然看得分明。」

===Original===
Whether Robin saw the matter in quite that light is doubtful. He was dwelling on Letty’s share of the plot, and he waxed more indignant still. “Markham held that letter over her poor little head? He was cur enough to work on her fears for her father? He forced her to agree to a fresh elopement? My God, sir, you need not be afraid that he will live many days longer!” He rose, and fell to pacing the room. His skirts rustled, and the big hoop swayed to a stride no woman would take. “Oh, I see my way! Without this I might well have hesitated to meet even an enemy of yours with the notion of killing him, sir! But this changes things; I grant you some subtlety, sir, but don’t ask me to approve a plan that involves my Letitia so damnably. When do they fly?”

“Tomorrow. That I have from our inestimable John. Change of horses has been ordered at Barnet. The strangeness of the hour made the discovery of the rest simple. Letitia goes in a party to Vauxhall Gardens. What easier to be lost in the crowd there? It will be some time before her absence is even noticed. It is really quite clever of my Munich friend to think of Vauxhall. You, my Robin, may stop them with little inconvenience to yourself on Finchley Common. You will, of course, be masked. I leave the details to you. I make no doubt you will arrange all to my satisfaction.”

Robin paused in his pacing. “I shall.” His eyes were alight. Concern for Letty had faded a little before the sheer joy of battle. He stretched his arms exultantly. “Ah, to feel a sword in my hand again!” he said, and made an imaginary pass in the air. “Not pistols﻿—no, no, that would be clumsy. Am I not your son?”

===Chinese===
罗宾是否也这样看这桩事，颇可怀疑。他一心想着莱蒂在这局棋里的份子，火气越发大了。「马卡姆把那封信悬在她可怜的小脑袋顶上？他竟下作到拿她父亲安危去吓唬她？他逼她应允再一次私奔？我的上帝，先生，您不必担心他还能多活几天！」他站起身，在屋里踱起步来。裙裾窸窣作响，宽大的裙撑随着大步摆荡——那样的步子没有一个女人迈得出来。「哦，我心里有数了！要不是有了这一节，先生，即便对上您的仇敌，要我存着杀他的念头去会他，我原还难免掂量！可如今局面不同了；我承认您运筹得有几分精妙，先生，可别指望我赞许一条把我莉蒂西娅如此作践的计划。他们几时动身？」

「明日。这话是我从我们那位功德无量的约翰嘴里得来的。巴尼特（Barnet）换马的事已经吩咐下去了。动身时辰的蹊跷，使查清其余缘由变得容易。莉蒂西娅要随一班人去沃克斯豪尔花园。在那边人潮里走失，还有比这更便当的么？过上好一阵子，才会有人觉察她不见了。我那位慕尼黑朋友能想到沃克斯豪尔，实在相当聪明。而你，我的罗宾，可以到芬奇利公地（Finchley Common）上拦截他们，自己不受多少劳顿。你自然要蒙上面罩。细节我留给你料理。我毫不怀疑，你会把一切运筹得令我满意。」

罗宾收住脚步。「我会的。」他眼里燃着光。在临阵纯粹的欢畅面前，对莱蒂的挂虑淡了些许。他扬眉吐气地舒展双臂。「啊，又叫一柄剑握在手里了！」他说着，朝空中虚晃了一剑。「不要手枪——不，不，那就笨拙了。我岂不是您的儿子么？」

===Original===
My lord became enthusiastic. “You are, my Robin! I perceive some shadow of myself in you. Remove my Munich friend! Do not unmask: you shall remain a mystery to your Letitia for a little while yet, but not for long! Remove me this Markham from the path, and you shall see me go swiftly forward to the promised goal. I am Tremaine of Barham!”

Robin looked sceptical. “Are you, sir! I wonder!”

“So too did Markham,” said my lord. “So do you all, and there is only one who knows the truth concerning me. It affords me infinite amusement. I say nothing: all shall soon be disclosed.” He picked up his hat. “I leave you to your plans, my Robin. See you do not bungle them. There must be no suspicion of your identity. Play the highwayman, and take John along with you. But I don’t interest myself in the petty details; you will think on them at your leisure. By my reckoning, and you will hardly question it, they should leave Vauxhall at nine in the evening, maybe later. It does not signify. Au revoir, my son! I wish you, though there is not the need, all success!” He waved his hand and was gone next instant, through the doorway into the hall beyond. There he came upon his daughter returned from a morning’s ride with Charles Belfort. He tapped her cheek with one indulgent finger, and said gaily: “My Prudence! You come too late to hear the tale of my achievement. You will find your brother in amaze.”

“Lord, sir!” said Prudence placidly, and watched him go out. She was chuckling a little: he had always the effect of making her laugh. She went into the room he had left, and found Robin biting his fingers in meditation. “What’s the old gentleman so pleased about now, Robin?” she asked. “Is there work for us afoot?”

He looked up, appraising her. “Have you a mind to it? It’s to be rescue, and slaughter, child!”

“You shock me,” said Prudence, sitting down upon the table’s edge. “Count on me; you will need me belike. What’s toward?”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人热切起来。「正是，我的罗宾！我在你身上窥见了我自己的几分影子。替我除掉那位慕尼黑朋友！莫要揭面罩：在你那位莉蒂西娅跟前，你还得再当一阵子谜，可当不长了！替我把这马卡姆从道上搬开，你便将看着我大步流星，直奔那既定的目标。我便是巴勒姆的特雷梅因！」

罗宾满面狐疑。「您是么，先生！我倒要打个问号！」

「马卡姆从前也这般想。」勋爵大人说。「你们个个都这么想，而识得我真相的，天下只有一人。这给我无穷的乐趣。我什么也不说：一切不久自会揭晓。」他拾起帽子。「我把你留给你的计划了，我的罗宾。切记莫办砸了。万不可教人起半点疑心，疑到你的身份。扮拦路的强盗去，把约翰也捎上。至于琐碎细节，我不操心；你自会在闲暇时细细思量。依我推算——你总不至于要质疑——他们该在晚间九点离开沃克斯豪尔，兴许更晚。那无关紧要。再会，我的儿子！我祝你——虽说并无必要——旗开得胜！」他一挥手，转眼便出了门，穿过门道进了外间大厅。在那儿，他遇见女儿同查尔斯·贝尔福特（Charles Belfort）晨骑归来。他纵容地伸出一根手指点点她的脸颊，喜洋洋地说：「我的普鲁登丝！你来迟一步，没赶上听我这桩功业的故事。你去看你弟弟吧，他正惊奇不置呢。」

「老天，先生！」普鲁登丝平平静静地说，目送他离去。她忍不住轻笑：他总有法子逗她发笑。她走进他刚离开的屋子，见罗宾正咬着手指出神。「老先生这会儿又为什么事这样得意呀，罗宾？」她问。「有咱们要办的差事了么？」

他抬眼，把她掂量了一番。「你可有意么？这回是搭救，加杀伐，孩子！」

「你吓煞我了。」普鲁登丝说着，在桌子边上坐下。「算我一个；你少不得要用我。有什么名堂？」

## XXX Triumph of Lord Barham / XXX 巴勒姆勋爵的凯旋

===Original===
Speculation concerning the result of my Lord Barham’s coming meeting in Grosvenor Square was in abeyance. The strange flight of the Merriots formed the topic of every conversation in Polite Circles. It was a seven-days’ wonder, and society was greatly put out to think it had received this couple with open arms. It was felt that my Lady Lowestoft had been very much to blame, and quite a number of people who heard my lady’s lamentation felt a glow of superiority. They had a comfortable conviction that they would never have been so foolish as to invite such a chance-met pair to stay. One or two persons had an odd idea that they had heard my lady say she was acquainted with the Merriots’ father, but when they mentioned this my lady was positively indignant. *Voyons*, how could she have said anything of the sort when she had never set eyes on the elder Mr. Merriot? She had been most grossly deceived; no one could imagine how great was the kindness she had shown the couple; she had had no suspicion of foul play. When she heard that Mr. Merriot was taken by the law for the killing of Gregory Markham she was so shocked, so astonished, she could scarcely speak. And then, next morning, to find Kate flown, and a horse gone from her own stables﻿—oh, she was prostrated. The affair was terrible﻿—she believed she would never recover.

It seemed like it indeed. Society grew tired of hearing her on this subject, for she could talk of naught else. And where had the Merriots gone? Who were the men who snatched Peter from the coach? One had undoubtedly been the servant, John, but who was the other? The unfortunate gaolers swore to a man of gigantic size, but no one paid much heed to that. It was the sort of exaggeration one would have expected.

Sir Anthony Fanshawe heard of it down at Dartrey, and took the trouble to write to his friend Molyneux. He protested he could not believe young Merriot was the villain this affair showed him to be. He was inexpressibly shocked by the news, but he felt sure some explanation must sooner or later be forthcoming. He ended by telling his friend that he had some notion of extending his stay with my Lady Enderby, since her ladyship had with her a most charming visitor.

Molyneux chuckled over this, and told Mr. Troubridge that Beatrice Enderby was once more trying to foist an eligible bride on to poor Tony.

===Chinese===
关于巴勒姆勋爵（Lord Barham）即将在格罗夫纳广场（Grosvenor Square）举行的那场会面的结果，种种揣度一时都悬了起来。梅里奥特兄妹的离奇出逃，成了上流社交界一切谈资的话题。这是一桩足足轰动了一星期的新鲜事，而社交界一想到自己竟曾张开双臂款待过这一对，个个懊恼不迭。众人觉得洛斯托夫特夫人（Lady Lowestoft）难辞其咎，听过夫人哀叹的人里，颇有几位心头升起一股优越感。他们舒舒服服地笃信，自己断不会蠢到邀请这样一对萍水相逢的男女上门做客。有一两个人模模糊糊记得，曾听夫人说过她同梅里奥特家的父亲相熟，可这话一提，夫人简直气愤不过。*Voyons*，她连老梅里奥特先生的面都不曾见过，怎么可能说过这种话？她被人蒙骗得苦了；她对这对男女何等仁至义尽，谁也想象不到；她对奸诈勾当毫无察觉。听说梅里奥特先生因格雷戈里·马卡姆（Gregory Markham）那条人命官司被官府拿问，她又是震惊，又是骇异，几乎说不出话来。紧接着第二天早晨，又发现凯特（Kate）不翼而飞，自家马厩里还少了一匹马——哦，她整个人都垮了。这场变故可怕极了——她相信自己永远缓不过来了。

看来的确像缓不过来了。社交界听她翻来覆去念叨这件事，都听腻了，因为她嘴里再装不下别的题目。再说，梅里奥特兄妹到底去了哪里？把彼得（Peter）从马车上劫走的又是些什么人？其中一个分明是那个仆人约翰（John），可另一个是谁？那两个倒霉的押送看守赌咒发誓，说那人块头大得像巨人，只是没谁把这话太当回事。这等夸张，原在人人的意料之中。

安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）在达特里（Dartrey）乡下听到了消息，特地不嫌烦劳，给朋友莫利纽克斯（Molyneux）写了封信。他申言自己无法相信小梅里奥特当真是这桩案子所显示的那种恶棍。这消息令他震惊得无以言表，但他确信，迟早总会有一番说明水落石出。信末他告诉朋友，他有几分打算在恩德比夫人（Lady Enderby）府上多盘桓些时日，因为夫人府上正有一位顶顶迷人的客人。

莫利纽克斯看信直乐，告诉特拉布里奇先生（Mr. Troubridge），说比阿特丽斯·恩德比（Beatrice Enderby）又在往可怜的托尼（Tony）跟前硬塞一位门当户对的新娘了。

===Original===
In the meantime there could be found no trace of the fugitives. They had vanished, and no man saw the way they went. Nor did any man see the way John returned, for he came secretly and looked quite different. The black hair had changed to a grizzled brown crop; the black brows became sandy, and the ugly mole beside his nose had vanished. It was not to be expected that the Merriots’ swarthy servant wore a wig, darkened his brows and lashes, and affixed a seeming mole to his face. Nor could it cross anyone’s mind that an old servant of my lord’s, who had been in waiting on the young Tremaines should have any connection with the Merriots’ lackey. Such a notion occurred to no one, more especially since it appeared that more than once my lord had warned my Lady Lowestoft that she should not trust too much in her youthful visitors.

People could not help admiring my lord’s perspicacity. He shook his head at my lady, and said only: “Ah, Thérèse!”

Whereupon my lady put a handkerchief to her eyes, and confessed that she had been wrong in her estimation of the Merriots, and my lord right. It became known that my lord had warned her many times; he had suspected something to be amiss from the first.

For three days everyone had theories to put forward, and exclamations to make, but on the fourth day interest veered round again to my lord’s claim.

===Chinese===
与此同时，这对遁走的亡命者却寻不出半点踪迹。他们销声匿迹了，没有人瞧见他们走的是哪条路。也没有人瞧见约翰是怎么回来的，因为他此番回来得隐秘，模样也全然变了。一头黑发换成了斑白的褐色短发；两道乌黑的眉毛变成了沙色，鼻子旁边那颗难看的痣也无影无踪。谁也不会想到，梅里奥特家那个面色黧黑的仆人，竟是戴着假发、把眉毛睫毛染深、还在脸上粘了一颗假痣。更不会有人起念，勋爵大人的一名老仆——昔日侍奉特雷梅因家（Tremaine）年轻一辈的那位——会同梅里奥特家的听差有什么干系。这样的念头谁也不曾有过，尤其是因为，据说勋爵大人早已不止一回提醒过洛斯托夫特夫人，叫她别太信得过那两位年轻客人。

众人不由得不叹服勋爵大人的先见之明。他朝夫人摇了摇头，只说了一句：「唉，泰蕾丝（Thérèse）！」

夫人闻言举起手帕按住眼睛，承认自己看梅里奥特兄妹是走了眼，而勋爵大人看的没错。于是人人都知道了：勋爵大人警醒过她许多回；他从第一天起就疑心有诈。

一连三天，人人都有高见要发表，都有惊叹要发出；到第四天，众人的兴致又转回到勋爵大人的爵位主张上来了。

===Original===
My lord was to meet the lawyers and his cousin at Grosvenor Square, and he would give conclusive proof of his identity.

Mr. Rensley, with his arm still in a sling, awaited the issue with not unjustifiable impatience. The family lawyers, Clapperly and Brent, were the first to arrive: young Mr. Clapperly brought old Mr. Clapperly, long since retired from the lists; and Mr. Brent brought a grave clerk, and many documents.

Mr. Brent rubbed his hands together and murmured over a list he held. He desired to know whether a Mrs. Staines, and a Mr. Samuel Burton had arrived.

Mr. Rensley stared at that. “Burton?” he echoed. “Do you mean my lodge-keeper?”

Mr. Brent coughed. “Let us say, sir, the lodge-keeper at Barham. You know we said we would not be﻿—er﻿—controversial.”

===Chinese===
勋爵大人将在格罗夫纳广场同律师们和他的堂亲会面，届时他要拿出决定性的凭证，证明自己的身份。

伦斯利先生（Mr. Rensley）胳膊还吊着悬带，等候着这场了断，那份急不可耐倒也不无来由。族中聘请的律师克拉珀利与布伦特两位最先到场：小克拉珀利先生（young Mr. Clapperly）带来了早已退出律界的老克拉珀利先生（old Mr. Clapperly）；布伦特先生（Mr. Brent）则带来一位神情肃穆的书记员和许多文书。

布伦特先生搓着双手，对着手里的一张单子低声核点。他想知道一位斯坦斯夫人（Mrs. Staines）和一位塞缪尔·伯顿先生（Mr. Samuel Burton）到了没有。

伦斯利先生听得一愣。「伯顿？」他跟着念了一遍。「您是说我的那个门房看守？」

布伦特先生咳了一声。「我们就这么说吧，先生，巴勒姆的门房看守。您知道，咱们讲好了措辞上不——呃——起争议。」

===Original===
Mr. Rensley said something under his breath, at which Mr. Clapperly frowned. “Why should he arrive?” he asked brusquely.

“The claimant, sir, desired it. Also Mrs. Staines, who is, I believe, Burton’s sister.”

“I know nothing about her,” Rensley answered. “Has that impostor bribed them to recognize him?”

Young Mr. Clapperly, a man of some forty years, begged Mr. Rensley to moderate his language. Mr. Brent assured Rensley that my lord had not set eyes on either Burton or his sister since his arrival: both brother and sister were as mystified as he was himself.

Shortly after this the couple arrived, and were ushered into the big library.

===Chinese===
伦斯利先生压着嗓子嘀咕了句什么，听得克拉珀利先生直皱眉。「叫他来做什么？」他没好气地问。

「是那位主张人的意思，先生。还有斯坦斯夫人——我相信她是伯顿的妹妹。」

「那女人我一无所知。」伦斯利答道。「莫不是那冒牌货塞了钱，买他们来认他？」

小克拉珀利先生——一位四十来岁的绅士——请伦斯利先生措辞收敛些。布伦特先生向伦斯利担保，勋爵大人抵达以来，从未同伯顿或他妹妹打过照面：这兄妹俩眼下的茫然，同他伦斯利自己一模一样。

不多时，这一对儿到了，被引进那间大书房。

===Original===
Burton was a stockily-built man of middle age, sandy-haired and blue-eyed; his sister was rather older, a respectable-looking woman, who dropped a shy curtsey to Rensley, and another to the lawyers. She was given a chair by the table, and sat down on the extreme edge of it, with her brother beside her.

“Three o’clock,” said young Mr. Clapperly, consulting a large watch. “I think we said three, sir?”

A coach was heard to drive up, as though in answer. In a few minutes the door opened to admit my Lord Barham, my Lord Clevedale, and Mr. Fontenoy.

My lord swept a magnificent leg to the assembled company. “I am late!” he exclaimed. “I offer a thousand apologies!”

“No, sir, no, almost to the minute,” Mr. Brent told him.

===Chinese===
伯顿是个敦实的中年汉子，沙色头发，蓝眼睛；他妹妹年纪略长些，是位看着本分体面的妇人，朝伦斯利怯生生地屈膝一福，又朝律师们行了一福。有人在她桌边摆了把椅子，她只坐了椅子最边上一沿，哥哥坐在她旁边。

「三点钟。」小克拉珀利先生掏出一只大怀表看了看。「我没记错的话，咱们约的是三点，先生？」

话音未落，就听得一辆马车辘辘驶到门前，像是应声而至。几分钟后，房门开处，巴勒姆勋爵偕克利夫代尔勋爵（Lord Clevedale）、丰特努瓦先生（Mr. Fontenoy）一同进来。

勋爵大人朝满座宾朋潇洒地一甩腿，行了个漂亮至极的大礼。「我来迟了！」他扬声道。「谨奉上一千个歉意！」

「不迟，先生，不迟，几乎分秒不差。」布伦特先生告诉他。

===Original===
Mr. Rensley was looking with dislike upon my lord’s companions. My lord addressed him at once. “You scowl upon my friends, cousin. But you must remember that I have the right to bring whom I will to this interview.” He turned to Mr. Clapperly. “Is that not so?”

“Oh, perfectly, sir! There can be no objection. Pray, will you not be seated, gentlemen?”

They were grouped about a table that stood in the middle of the room. My lord sat at the end of the table, with old Mr. Clapperly opposite to him. My lord produced his snuffbox, and unfobbed it. “And now my cousin Rensley wants to put some questions to me,” he said gently. “There is no reason why I should answer any of them. I stand proved already Tremaine of Barham. You have tried to find that I stole my papers, and you have failed, gentlemen. I condole with you. Let me hear your questions; I shall endeavour to satisfy you.”

There was an uncomfortable air of strain in the room; my lord was too much master of the situation. Rensley sat on Mr. Clapperly’s right hand, and scowled at the table. Mr. Clapperly had begged him to leave all to his men of business, and he had agreed to hold his peace. He did not look at my lord; the sight of that smiling countenance enraged him to the point of desperation.

Mr. Fontenoy preserved his prim severity; my Lord Clevedale lounged beside the old gentleman, and was frankly agog with curiosity. Burton and his sister sat together on one side of the table, and appeared to be rather bewildered.

===Chinese===
伦斯利先生正厌恶地打量着勋爵大人的同伴。勋爵大人当即冲他发了话。「您对我的朋友们怒目而视呢，堂亲。可您得记得，这场会面，我有权爱带谁来便带谁来。」他转向克拉珀利先生。「我说的可对？」

「哦，完全没错，先生！这没有任何可反对的。诸位先生，请入座吧？」

众人围着屋子中央的一张桌子坐定。勋爵大人坐在桌子一端，对面是老克拉珀利先生。勋爵大人掏出鼻烟盒，从表坠链上解了下来。「如今，我的堂亲伦斯利要向我提几个问题，」他温言道，「而我全然没有义务回答其中任何一个。我巴勒姆的特雷梅因（Tremaine of Barham）之身，已然立证在案。诸位曾试图查明我的文书系偷窃而来，诸位失败了，先生们。我谨向诸位志哀。有什么问题只管道来；我将竭力使诸位满意。」

屋里弥漫着一种叫人不自在的紧绷气氛；勋爵大人把局面拿捏得太死了。伦斯利坐在克拉珀利先生右手边，只管冲着桌子瞪眼。克拉珀利先生求过他，一切交给几位办案的律师料理，他也应承了缄口不言。他不朝勋爵大人看；那张笑吟吟的脸只要入眼，便足以把他激怒到发狂的地步。

丰特努瓦先生端着他那副拘谨的严正；克利夫代尔勋爵懒洋洋地靠在老先生身旁，那满脸按捺不住的好奇，坦率得毫不掩饰。伯顿兄妹一同坐在桌子一侧，瞧着颇为茫然。

===Original===
Mr. Brent signed to his clerk, who brought forward a leather case. Mr. Brent opened this, and produced a slip of paper. It seemed to have been cut from a letter, for it was closely written over. “Perhaps, sir, you would be good enough to tell us if you recognize this writing,” he said courteously, and gave the slip to the clerk, who carried it to my lord.

My lord put out a white hand to receive it. He glanced at it, smiled, and gave it back. “Certainly,” he said. “It is my father’s hand.”

Mr. Rensley shot a quick look at him, and bit his lip.

“Thank you, sir,” bowed Mr. Brent. “And these?”

My lord took three other such slips. One he handed back at once. “My brother. Pray take it away.” He frowned over the second and shook his head. “I have not the smallest notion,” he said calmly. “I doubt whether I have ever seen it before.” He turned to the third, and spent some time over it. “I am inclined to think that this must be my Aunt Susanna,” he said.

===Chinese===
布伦特先生朝他的书记员递了个眼色，书记员捧上一只皮匣。布伦特先生打开匣子，取出一张纸条。看样子是从一封信上裁下来的，上面写得密密麻麻。「劳驾，先生，请您告诉我们，可认得这笔迹？」他客客气气地说，把纸条交给书记员，由书记员呈到勋爵大人面前。

勋爵大人伸出一只白皙的手接过。他扫了一眼，微微一笑，递了回去。「当然认得。」他说。「这是家父的手笔。」

伦斯利先生疾速瞟了他一眼，咬住了嘴唇。

「多谢您，先生。」布伦特先生欠身道。「那么这几张呢？」

勋爵大人接过另外三张同样的纸条。头一张他立刻递了回去。「家兄的手笔。请把它拿开吧。」第二张他皱着眉端详，摇了摇头。「我半点也摸不着头脑。」他心平气和地说。「我疑心从前根本没见过它。」轮到第三张，他琢磨了好一阵。「我倾向于认为，这必定是我苏珊娜姑母（Aunt Susanna）的手笔。」他说。

===Original===
“Inclined, sir?”

“Inclined,” nodded my lord. “I never received a letter from her in my life that I can remember. But I perceive the word Toto. My respected aunt, when I knew her﻿—and I do trust she’s dead?﻿—had a small dog of that name. A yapping, petted little brute of a spaniel. Mr. Fontenoy would remember.”

Mr. Fontenoy nodded. The lawyers exchanged glances. If this were indeed an impostor he knew a deal about the family of Tremaine.

“But the second letter, sir?”

My lord raised his brows. “I told you, did I not? I do not know the hand at all.” He put up his glass and looked at it again. “Very ill-formed,” he remarked. “No, I know no one with such an undistinguished hand.”

===Chinese===
「倾向于，先生？」

「倾向于。」勋爵大人颔首道。「我这辈子还不曾收到过她一封信，至少我记得不曾。可我瞧见了托托（Toto）这个词。我那位受人尊敬的姑母——在我认识她的时候——我由衷盼着她已经过世了？——养过一只小狗就叫这名字。一只汪汪乱叫、娇生惯养的西班牙猎犬小畜生。丰特努瓦先生该记得的。」

丰特努瓦先生点了点头。两位律师交换了一个眼色。此人倘若当真是冒牌货，那他对特雷梅因家的事，知道的也着实太多了。

「那么第二封信呢，先生？」

勋爵大人扬起眉毛。「我说过了，不是么？这笔迹我压根不认识。」他举起单片眼镜又看了一遍。「写得歪歪扭扭，不成体统。」他品评道。「不，我不认识谁的字如此平庸无奇。」

===Original===
Mr. Rensley reddened angrily and opened his mouth to speak. Mr. Brent put up a hand to silence him. “Is it not a little strange that you should not know the writing of the man you claim as cousin, sir?” he asked.

My lord was aghast. He looked at Rensley. “Good gad, cousin, is it yours indeed? I have been guilty of a breach of manners! I am desolated to have passed such a stricture on your hand.”

“You do not answer me, sir,” Mr. Brent pointed out.
