My lord turned to him. “I crave your pardon. But does it need an answer? I thought I had made the situation between the Tremaines and the Rensleys clear to all. It is not in the least strange that I should not recognise the hand. I had never seen it before.”

Mr. Brent bowed in a noncommittal manner, and drew a miniature from the case before him. “Do you know this face, sir?”

===Chinese===
伦斯利先生气得涨红了脸，张口就要说话。布伦特先生抬手止住他。「您竟认不出这位您自称堂亲的先生的笔迹，先生，这难道不有些蹊跷么？」他问道。

勋爵大人大惊失色。他望向伦斯利。「我的老天，堂亲，这竟是您的手笔？我犯下了一桩失礼之罪！对您的字下了那样的苛评，我懊丧欲绝。」

「您还没有回答我的话，先生。」布伦特先生指出。

勋爵大人转向他。「请您恕罪。可这难道需要回答么？我原以为，特雷梅因家同伦斯利家之间的情势，我早已向诸位交代清楚了。我认不出这笔迹，丝毫不足为奇。我从前从来没有见过它。」

布伦特先生不置可否地欠了欠身，从面前的皮匣里取出一幅微型画像。「您认得这张脸么，先生？」

===Original===
“I ought to,” said my lord. “But do put it away again, dear sir! I’ve not the smallest wish to gaze upon my late brother’s image.”

Old Mr. Clapperly gave a dry cackle of laughter. Young Mr. Clapperly looked reproachful, and said: “I believe, gentlemen, we cannot regard that as conclusive. The late Viscount was well known. Show him the other one.”

My lord held a miniature of a dark lady at arm’s length, and surveyed it critically. “When was this done?” he inquired. “It quite fails to convey an impression of her charm.”

“You know the face, sir?”

“Dorothea,” said my lord. “At least, so I suppose, but it is very bad. More like my aunt Johanna. There is a far better portrait of her in the gallery of Barham.” He showed the miniature to Mr. Fontenoy. “You knew my sister, sir. Do you agree that this does her less than justice?”

===Chinese===
「我理当认得。」勋爵大人说。「不过快把它收起来吧，亲爱的先生！瞻仰先兄的遗容，我没有半点兴致。」

老克拉珀利先生发出一声干笑。小克拉珀利先生面带责备之色，说道：「我想，诸位先生，这一项算不得决定性的凭证。已故子爵的容貌是人人熟识的。把另一幅拿给他看。」

勋爵大人把一幅黑发美人的微型画像伸直了手臂端着，挑剔地审视。「这是什么时候画的？」他问道。「她的风韵，它全然没能传出分毫。」

「您认得这张脸么，先生？」

「多萝西娅（Dorothea）。」勋爵大人说。「至少我猜想是，不过这画糟透了。倒更像我的乔安娜姑母（aunt Johanna）。巴勒姆的画廊里有一幅她的像，比这强得多。」他把画像递到丰特努瓦先生眼前。「您是认识我妹妹的，先生。您可同意，这幅画实在委屈了她？」

===Original===
“Miss Tremaine had certainly more animation than is shown here,” Mr. Fontenoy answered.

My lord gave back the miniature. There was a gleam in his eye. “But why not produce a picture of myself?” he suggested.

Mr. Fontenoy, and old Mr. Clapperly looked sharply. Rensley said triumphantly:﻿—“You make a slip there, my clever gentleman! There is no picture of you!”

My lord smiled. “No? And does my friend Mr. Fontenoy agree with that?”

Mr. Fontenoy said nothing. My lord tapped the lid of his snuffbox. “What of the sketch that was taken of me when I was eighteen?” he asked softly.

===Chinese===
「特雷梅因小姐确比这上面画的神采要生动得多。」丰特努瓦先生答道。

勋爵大人交还了画像。他眼里闪过一丝亮光。「可为什么不拿一幅我本人的画像出来呢？」他建言道。

丰特努瓦先生和老克拉珀利先生目光倏地一凛。伦斯利得意扬扬地说：「聪明的先生，您这可走了嘴了！根本没有您的画像！」

勋爵大人微微一笑。「没有么？我的朋友丰特努瓦先生也认同此说么？」

丰特努瓦先生一言不发。勋爵大人轻叩鼻烟盒的盒盖。「我十八岁那年画的那幅素描像，怎么说？」他轻声问道。

===Original===
It was plain Rensley knew nothing of this; equally plain was it that my lord had impressed the two eldest people present. “It is true that there was once such a portrait, sir,” said old Mr. Clapperly. “But it exists no longer.”

“You may be right,” said my lord politely. “It is a long time since I left England. But perhaps you have not looked for it in the right place.”

“We have searched both in this house, and at Barham, sir. It is not to be found.”

“I see that I must assist you,” smiled my lord.

There was an alert look in Mr. Brent’s face. “Indeed, sir, and do you know where this likeness is to be found?”

===Chinese===
明摆着，伦斯利对此一无所知；同样明摆着的是，勋爵大人已折服了在场两位最年长的人。「从前确有过这样一幅画像，先生。」老克拉珀利先生说。「只是如今已不复存在了。」

「您或许没错。」勋爵大人彬彬有礼地说。「我离开英格兰已经很久了。不过，诸位也许没有找对地方。」

「这座宅子和巴勒姆，两处我们都搜遍了，先生。它无处可寻。」

「看来我只好亲自助诸位一臂之力了。」勋爵大人含笑说。

布伦特先生脸上现出警觉的神色。「当真，先生，您知道这幅画像到哪里寻么？」

===Original===
“I hope so, Mr. Brent. But do not let us be rash. If the likeness is still where I hid it, then I can find it.”

Mr. Fontenoy lost some of his primness. Everyone was staring eagerly at my lord. “Where you hid it, sir?”

“Where I hid it,” repeated my lord. “Now I have overheard you to say, Mr. Fontenoy, that young Robert Tremaine was a romantic youth. It is very true! Years have not dulled the edge of my romantic fervour.” He laid down his snuffbox on the table before him, and his strangely compelling eyes swept the room. “They have only sharpened a brain that was always acute, gentlemen. You cannot fail to have observed a forethought in me that excites the admiration. I had it even as a boy.” He smiled benignantly. “Such a contingency as the present one I dimly expected, even in those far-off days. I saw that the day might come when I might desire to prove my identity. The romantic boy, Mr. Fontenoy, hid a picture of himself in this very room, to serve as a proof if ever he should need one.”

“In this room!” ejaculated my Lord Clevedale, looking round.

“Certainly,” said my lord. “That is why I chose this room today.” He rose. “Tell me, cousin, are you a great reader?”

===Chinese===
「我希望如此，布伦特先生。不过咱们不可鲁莽行事。倘若那画像还在我当年藏它的地方，那我就能找到它。」

丰特努瓦先生的拘谨褪去了几分。人人都急切地盯着勋爵大人。「您藏过它，先生？」

「我藏过它。」勋爵大人重复道。「我可是无意中听见您说过的，丰特努瓦先生——年少的罗伯特·特雷梅因（Robert Tremaine）是个满脑浪漫的青年。千真万确！岁月磨钝不了我浪漫热忱的锋芒。」他把鼻烟盒搁在面前的桌上，那双奇异地令人无从抗辩的眼睛扫视全场。「岁月只是把一颗向来敏锐的头脑磨得愈发锋利了，诸位先生。诸位不可能不曾察觉，我身上有一种令人叹服的未雨绸缪。我还是个孩童时便已具备。」他蔼然一笑。「像今天这样的万一之局，早在那些久远的岁月里，我便影影绰绰地料到了。我看出或许会有那么一天，我需要证明自己的身份。于是那个浪漫的孩子，丰特努瓦先生，就在这间屋子里，藏下了自己的一幅画像，以备他万一有朝一日用得着凭证。」

「就在这间屋子里！」克利夫代尔勋爵脱口喊道，四下张望。

「正是。」勋爵大人说。「所以我今天才挑了这间屋子。」他站起身。「告诉我，堂亲，您是个博览群书的读书人么？」

===Original===
“No, I am not,” said Rensley curtly.

“Nor was my brother,” said his lordship. “I thought of that at the time. My father was much addicted to the works of Shakespeare but I believe he had no Latin.”

“What’s all this to do with it?” Rensley demanded uneasily.

My lord’s glance travelled to the top shelf of the books that lined the room. “Do you ever chance to take down the works of the poet Horace, cousin?”

“No, I do not, and I don’t see﻿—”

===Chinese===
「不是。」伦斯利简慢地说。

「先兄也不是。」勋爵大人说。「当年我便想到了这一层。家父酷嗜莎士比亚（Shakespeare）的著作，不过我相信他不通拉丁文。」

「这跟画像有什么相干？」伦斯利不安地追问。

勋爵大人的目光移向满壁藏书最上层的书架。「堂亲，您可曾碰巧取下过诗人贺拉斯（Horace）的著作？」

「没有，从不取，而且我不明白——」

===Original===
“Nor did my brother, I am convinced,” said my lord. “I thought it was safe﻿—wonderfully safe, and wonderfully neat. I admire my own astuteness.” He met the puzzled eyes of my Lord Clevedale. “A great pity to have no knowledge of the humanities,” he said. “It is an estimable advantage. Had you been familiar with the Odes of Horace, cousin﻿—but you are not. But take them down now: it is never too late to begin. Over in that corner, on the top shelf you will find the first volume, elegantly bound in tooled leather, the covers clasped by wrought hasps.”

“Pray, sir, what’s your meaning?” Mr. Brent asked.

“Why, is it not plain?” said my lord. “I ask my cousin to pull the steps to that corner, and to take down the Odes of Horace. Let him open the clasps, and turn to the Fifth Ode.”

“You speak in riddles, sir.”

“But the riddle will very soon be answered, sir, if my cousin will do as I say. The first volume and the Fifth Ode. It will be most enlightening.”

===Chinese===
「先兄也从不取，我深信不疑。」勋爵大人说。「我当年便料定它万无一失——稳妥至极，又干净利落至极。我钦佩我自己的精明。」他迎上克利夫代尔勋爵困惑的目光。「不通人文学问，实在是一桩大大的憾事。」他说。「那是一份可敬的优势。堂亲，您若熟悉贺拉斯的《颂歌》——可惜您不熟。不过现在就去把它们取下来吧：开卷总不嫌迟。就在那边的角落，最上层的书架上，您会找到第一卷：压花皮面装帧得雅雅致致，封皮以锻制的书扣扣拢。」

「请教，先生，您这话是什么意思？」布伦特先生问。

「嗨，这还不明白么？」勋爵大人说。「我请我的堂亲把梯凳拖到那个角落，取下贺拉斯的《颂歌》。让他打开书扣，翻到第五首颂歌。」

「您这是在打哑谜，先生。」

「不过这哑谜很快就会揭晓，先生——只要我的堂亲肯照我说的做。第一卷，第五首颂歌。届时必将大有启迪。」

===Original===
Rensley went impatiently to the shelves. “Mountebank! What am I to find there?”

“The missing sketch, my dear Rensley, of course.”

“What!” Mr. Clapperly looked up. “You put it there, sir?”

“I don’t believe it!” Rensley said, and went quickly up the ladder. He found the book, and pulled it out. A moment he fumbled with the clasps. The leaves parted naturally at the Fifth Ode. Mr. Rensley stood staring down at the book.

Every head was turned his way. “Is it there?” demanded Mr. Clapperly.

===Chinese===
伦斯利不耐烦地走向书架。「江湖骗子！我在那儿能找到什么？」

「当然是那幅失踪的素描，我亲爱的伦斯利。」

「什么！」克拉珀利先生抬起头来。「是您把它放在那儿的，先生？」

「我不信！」伦斯利说着，快步登上梯凳。他找到了那本书，把它抽了出来。他对着书扣摸索了一阵。书页在第五首颂歌处自然而然地摊开了。伦斯利先生站在那里，低头瞪视着那本书。

每一颗头都转向了他那边。「在里面么？」克拉珀利先生追问。

===Original===
“You were told of this!” Rensley burst out, and flung the book violently to the ground. A drawing fluttered across the room, and was pounced on by Mr. Fontenoy.

Instantly everyone save my lord went to peep over Mr. Fontenoy’s shoulder. “It is certainly Robert Tremaine,” Mr. Fontenoy said. He looked from it to my lord. “And there is﻿—a likeness.”

“Why, damme, sir, the eyes and nose are exact!” cried Clevedale.

Mrs. Staines ventured to speak. “ ’Deed, sir, but you have a look of Master Robert.”

“My good Maggie, you ought to know that I am Master Robert,” said his lordship. “I perfectly remember you.”

===Chinese===
「你们事先把这事透给他了！」伦斯利勃然发作，把那本书狠狠掼在地上。一幅画飘飘荡荡掠过屋子，被丰特努瓦先生一把扑住。

霎时间，除勋爵大人外，人人都凑到丰特努瓦先生肩后去看。「这确实是罗伯特·特雷梅因。」丰特努瓦先生说。他的目光从画像移到勋爵大人身上。「而这——确有几分相像。」

「嗨，妈的，先生，这眼睛鼻子分毫不差！」克利夫代尔叫道。

斯坦斯夫人壮着胆子开了口。「可不是嘛，先生，您真有几分罗伯特少爷（Master Robert）的模样。」

「我的好玛吉（Maggie），你本该知道，我就是罗伯特少爷本人。」勋爵大人说。「我可清清楚楚记得你呢。」

===Original===
She stared. “You do know my name, sir. But your lordship will pardon me﻿—it is so long ago, and you’ve changed, my lord.”

“So it would appear,” said his lordship. “I said I should satisfy you, gentlemen.”

“Pardon, sir,” Mr. Brent interposed. “It seems a proof certainly. But we must not forget that you might have been told of this.”

“How?” inquired my lord. “No one but myself knew of it.”

“I am assuming, sir, for the moment, that you are not Tremaine.”

===Chinese===
她怔怔地瞪着他。「您真知道我的名字，先生。只是求大人恕罪——年头太久远了，您也变了模样，大人。」

「看来确实如此。」勋爵大人说。「我说过我会让诸位满意的，诸位先生。」

「恕我插一句，先生。」布伦特先生插话道。「这看来确是一项凭证。但我们不可忘了，这件事也可能是有人透露给您的。」

「怎么个透法？」勋爵大人问道。「除了我本人，无人知晓此事。」

「我且作个假设，先生——眼下姑且假设您不是特雷梅因。」

===Original===
“An impertinence,” said my lord. “But I suppose I must forgive it. Pray continue. The legal mind is very wonderful.”

“And if﻿—I only say if, sir﻿—you are not Tremaine, you might have heard this from the man himself.”

My lord looked at him in blank astonishment. It was Clevedale who spoke. “Lord, what in the plague’s name would Tremaine tell such a secret for?”

“It is a possibility, my lord: I do not say a probability.”

“This is all quite ridiculous,” said my Lord Barham. “Moreover I am becoming weary of it. I bring you papers, and you say I stole them. I show you where I hid my own portrait, years ago, and you say I was told of it. I show you a ring, and you say I stole that. What a pity it is I have no birthmarks! Or would you say that I had stolen them as well? It is a very good thing that I brought my friend Mr. Fontenoy. And here is Mr. Clapperly as well may remember a little about me.”

===Chinese===
「好一记无礼。」勋爵大人说。「不过我想我必须宽恕它。请继续。法律头脑真是妙不可言。」

「而倘若——我只是说倘若，先生——您不是特雷梅因，这件事您就可能从那位正主儿本人嘴里听说来的。」

勋爵大人一脸茫然的惊愕望着他。开口的是克利夫代尔。「老天，特雷梅因究竟犯的什么疯，要把这样的秘密说给人听？」

「这是一种可能性，勋爵大人：我并没有说它有多大的成算。」

「这一切实在荒唐透顶。」巴勒姆勋爵说。「而且我开始厌倦了。我给诸位拿来文书，诸位说我偷的。我指给诸位看多年前我藏自己画像的地方，诸位说我是听来的。我给诸位看一枚戒指，诸位说那也是偷的。真可惜我身上没有胎记！要不然，诸位是不是还要说，连胎记也是我偷来的？幸而我把我朋友丰特努瓦先生带来了，这是极好的事。还有这位克拉珀利先生，兴许也能记得我的一些旧事。」

===Original===
“Vividly, sir.” Mr. Clapperly inclined his head.

“Then I am sure you will remember the circumstances of my departure, all those years ago?”

“I do, sir.”

“Then I beg you will correct me if I should err in my tale. It is quite short.” He offered snuff to Clevedale. “My own mixture, Clevedale. You will like it. Well, gentlemen, you know that I was never at one with my father: he could not appreciate the genius that was in me. I disliked my brother only less than he disliked me. He hated me, I believe, but he would *not* have chosen to set you in my shoes, cousin, in spite of it. He was, after all, a Tremaine. I was no doubt a wild youth. I can remember incidents here and there﻿—but no matter. I overspent my allowance with amazing regularity. I shall be careful to put no limits to my son’s income. Then I committed the indiscretion of falling in love with a lady called Maria Banstead. She was the daughter of a farmer.”

“Near Barham,” nodded Mr. Clapperly.

===Chinese===
「历历在目，先生。」克拉珀利先生颔首道。

「那么您必定还记得，那么多年以前我离家的种种情由了？」

「记得，先生。」

「那么倘若我把这段经历讲错了一处，就恳请您指正。它不长。」他向克利夫代尔敬鼻烟。「我自己配的方子，克利夫代尔。你会喜欢的。好吧，诸位先生，你们知道，我同家父从来不是一条心：他鉴赏不了我身上蕴藏的天才。先兄嫌恶我，我对他的嫌恶，也不过比他对我的少那么一点。我相信他是恨我的，但即便如此，他也绝不会宁可让您来顶我的位置，堂亲。他到底是一位特雷梅因。我当年无疑是个放浪的少年。零零碎碎的事我还记得几桩——不过无关紧要。我超支津贴，超得规律惊人。往后我儿子的进项，我要留心不设任何界限。再后来我犯下一桩失检，爱上了一位名叫玛丽亚·班斯特德（Maria Banstead）的小姐。她是一位农场主的女儿。」

「就在巴勒姆附近。」克拉珀利先生点头道。

===Original===
My lord looked ironically across at him. “Your memory fails you, sir. Not in the least near Barham. She lived at Culverly, on the estate of my aunt Johanna’s husband. I was, I admit, young, and possibly hotheaded. But I have never regretted my marriage. An incomparable creature! I led her a sad dance I fear me. I eloped with her secretly, and went to France, just as soon as I heard that I had been thrown off by my indignant family. That is my story, gentlemen. Is it true?”

It was admitted to be true. My lord indicated the clerk with a wave of his hand. “Tell your clerk, Brent, to call my man in. He is in the hall.”

“Certainly, my lord. Go, Fawley.” It was the first time he had addressed my lord by his title and Rensley flushed as he heard him.

The clerk went out, and a moment later John stood in the doorway.

===Chinese===
勋爵大人讥诮地朝他那边望过去。「您的记性不济了，先生。丝毫不在巴勒姆附近。她住在卡尔弗利（Culverly），在我乔安娜姑母的丈夫的产业上。我承认，我那时年轻，兴许还火爆性子。可我对我的婚姻从无悔意。一位举世无双的可人儿！只怕我是领着她跳了一场够呛的舞。一听说我已被义愤填膺的家人扫地出门，我便同她秘密私奔，去了法兰西。这就是我的故事，诸位先生。属实么？」

众人承认它属实。勋爵大人挥手朝那名书记员示意。「布伦特，叫您的书记员去把我的人请进来。他在门厅里。」

「遵命，勋爵大人。去，福利（Fawley）。」这是他头一回以爵衔称呼勋爵大人，伦斯利听得耳中，涨红了脸。

书记员出去了，片刻之后，约翰站在了门口。

===Original===
Everyone looked towards him, since it seemed he had been called for some special purpose. But my lord’s eyes were on Mrs. Staines’ face. “He does not change much with the passing of time, I believe,” he said.

Mrs. Staines was staring. The colour left her face, and she put up a hand to her ample bosom. “Johnny!” she faltered. “Oh, dearie, dearie, am I dreaming?”

Burton was incredulous. “It’s never our John!” he gasped. “Bless my soul, but it is really yourself, John?”

“Ay, it’s me,” John said grimly and sustained the shock of having his sister cast herself on his chest. “Well, Maggie, how do you, eh? Remember where you are, lass!”

Mrs. Staines was quite oblivious of her surroundings. “Oh, Johnny, to think of you come back to us after all these years! Snakes, and I scarcely knew you, dearie, you’ve grown so grey! Sam, do you know your brother?”

===Chinese===
人人都朝他望去，因为看样子，他是为着某种特殊缘故被唤进来的。可勋爵大人的眼睛却望着斯坦斯夫人的脸。「我想，这个人任凭岁月流转，模样是不大变的。」他说。

斯坦斯夫人正怔怔地望着。血色从她脸上褪去，她抬起一只手按住自己丰满的胸脯。「约翰尼（Johnny）！」她声音发颤。「哦，亲爱的，亲爱的，我是在做梦么？」

伯顿不敢相信。「这哪会是咱们的约翰！」他倒抽一口气。「我的天老爷，这当真是你本人么，约翰？」

「哎，是我。」约翰绷着脸说，硬生生受住了妹妹扑上他胸前的这一撞。「哎，玛吉，你近来可好，嗯？记着点儿这是在什么场合，丫头！」

斯坦斯夫人全然忘了身在何处。「哦，约翰尼，隔了这么多年，你竟回到我们身边来了！天哪，我险些儿认不出你，亲爱的，你头发白成这样了！萨姆（Sam），你认得出你哥哥么？」

===Original===
Mr. Samuel Burton gripped Mr. John Burton’s hand. “Well, John!” was all he could find to say.

“Did you ever learn to master the bay mare?” John asked grinning.

It appeared to be an old jest. Samuel shook with laughter. “Lordy, John, to think you’d remember that! Ay, I was naught but a stripling then, and the mare the tricksiest piece﻿—well, to think you’d remember!”

Surprise had held the others spellbound, but Mr. Brent recovered himself. “Mrs. Staines, do you recognise this man?”

“Oh, the legal mind!” murmured my lord.

===Chinese===
塞缪尔·伯顿先生紧紧攥住约翰·伯顿先生的手。「哎，约翰！」——他只寻得出这一句。

「那匹枣红母马，你后来学会驾驭了么？」约翰咧嘴笑着问。

看来这是句老玩笑话。塞缪尔笑得直打颤。「老天爷，约翰，亏你还记得这个！可不是，那会儿我不过是个半大小子，那匹母马又是头一等刁钻的畜生——唉，亏你还记得！」

这一惊非同小可，把旁人都钉在了原地，布伦特先生却先回了神。「斯坦斯夫人，您认得这个人么？」

「哦，这法律头脑！」勋爵大人低声感叹。

===Original===
“Why, of course I do, sir! It’s our John, who went off years ago soon after Master Robert.” She turned again to her brother. “And you’ve been with him all the time! Eh, and we never thought of it! But you was always saying you’d be off to Americky to try your fortune, Johnny, and we made sure you’d gone there.”

Mr. Brent put a question no one thought needful. “Is this gentleman Viscount Barham?” he said.

John looked scornful. “Ay, of course he is,” he answered. “Is there ever another would have that nose but a Tremaine?”

“You have been with him all these years?”

“I have, sir, and a pretty dance he’s led me.” John smiled grimly at my lord. “Many’s the time I’ve told his lordship I’d be off home again. But we Burtons have always served Tremaine.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，当然认得，先生！这是咱们的约翰呀——那么多年前，罗伯特少爷走后不久，他也跟着走了。」她又转向哥哥。「原来这些年你一直跟着他！哎，我们竟从没想到这一层！可你从前总念叨要去美利坚闯运气来着，约翰尼，我们都拿准你上那儿去了。」

布伦特先生提出了一个谁都觉得多此一问的问题。「这位先生是巴勒姆子爵（Viscount Barham）么？」他说。

约翰一脸不屑。「是，当然是。」他答道。「除了特雷梅因家的人，天下还能有谁生得那样一只鼻子？」

「这些年您一直跟着他？」

「跟了一路，先生，而他领我跳的这场舞，可真够瞧的。」约翰朝勋爵大人绷着脸一笑。「不知多少回，我同大人说，我要卷铺盖回家了。可咱们伯顿家的人，向来伺候的都是特雷梅因家。」

===Original===
There was a long silence. Mr. Brent was slowly putting his papers together; Mr. Clapperly smiled knowingly at his son; Rensley stood staring at the floor.

“Cousin,” said my lord. “I trust you are at last satisfied.”

“There is no more to be said, my lord,” said old Mr. Clapperly.

My lord picked up his hat. “In that case I will take my leave of you. I should like my house at the end of a week, if you please. Brent, you will make the arrangements necessary, and put my terms before Mr. Rensley. I hope he will not find me ungenerous. Clevedale, your arm!”

===Chinese===
接下来是长长一阵沉默。布伦特先生慢慢收拾着他的文书；克拉珀利先生朝儿子会心一笑；伦斯利站在原地，两眼瞪着地板。

「堂亲，」勋爵大人说，「我相信您终于满意了。」

「再没有二话可说了，勋爵大人。」老克拉珀利先生说。

勋爵大人拾起帽子。「既然如此，我便向诸位告辞。劳驾，一周之后我要收还我的宅邸。布伦特，必要的安排由您去办，把我的条件开列给伦斯利先生。但愿他不至于觉得我出手吝啬。克利夫代尔，把手臂借我！」

## XVI Unaccountable Behaviour of Sir Anthony Fanshawe / XVI 安东尼·范肖爵士的反常行径

===Original===
Sir Anthony was partaking of a solitary breakfast when Mr. Belfort was announced. He looked up genially from a red sirloin as the Honourable Charles came in, and offered him a share of the meal.

“Breakfasted an hour since,” said Mr. Belfort briskly. “But I don’t mind taking some of that ale.”

Sir Anthony pushed it towards him. “You’re very energetic, Charles,” he remarked. “Why this ungodly hour for a visit?”

“Well, I’ve had business to attend to, y’see,” said Mr. Belfort, nodding mysteriously. “But that’s not what I’m come upon. It’s about that grey mare, Tony.”

“My dear Charles, I really cannot talk horse flesh so early in the morning.”

“Oh, come now!” protested Mr. Belfort. “It’s past nine, man! The fact of the matter is, Orton offers me a hundred guineas for her, but I told him she was more than half promised to you. But if you think she’s not up to your weight﻿—”
===Chinese===
安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）正独自用着早餐，仆人来报贝尔福特（Belfort）先生到。尊贵的查尔斯（Charles）走进来时，他从一份煎得红嫩的牛里脊上抬起头，和颜悦色地招呼他分享这顿饭。

「一个钟头前就用过啦。」贝尔福特先生爽利地说。「不过再来点那麦芽酒，我不推辞。」

安东尼爵士把酒推给他。「您可真勤快，查尔斯。」他说。「怎么拣这个不像话的钟点来拜访？」

「呃，我有些事要料理，您瞧。」贝尔福特先生神秘兮兮地点着头。「可我不是为那个来的。是为那匹灰母马，托尼（Tony）。」

「我亲爱的查尔斯，一大早的，我实在谈不动牲口行情。」

「嗳，别这样！」贝尔福特先生抗议道。「都过九点了，老兄！其实是这么回事：奥顿（Orton）出一百几尼买她，可我告诉他，她多半已经许给您了。不过，您要是觉得她驮不动您的分量——」

===Original===
“I have a fancy for her,” said Sir Anthony. “I’ll give you Orton’s price.”

“Good God, man, no! If you want the mare she’s yours at the figure we named!” cried Mr. Belfort, horrified. “Burn it, I’m not a demned merchant, Anthony!”

They embarked straightway on a friendly wrangle. A compromise was reached at last, and Mr. Belfort disappeared into his tankard. When he emerged a thought seemed to strike him. “I say, Tony, there is no doubt as to young Merriot’s courage, is there?” he inquired.

“None that I know of. Why do you ask?” Sir Anthony was watching a fly hover over the sirloin.

“Oh, no reason!” Mr. Belfort answered, mighty offhand.

Sir Anthony regarded him thoughtfully. “He gives you some cause for doubting his courage?” he said, with just enough show of interest to demand an answer.
===Chinese===
「我看中她了。」安东尼爵士说。「奥顿出什么价，我照价给。」

「天哪，老兄，使不得！您要这匹马，照咱们原先说定的价钱牵去便是！」贝尔福特先生大惊失色地嚷道。「见鬼，我又不是个该死的买卖人，安东尼！」

两人当即展开一场友好的讨价还价。最后各让一步，达成妥协，贝尔福特先生便一头扎进他的大酒杯里去了。待他再冒出头来，像是忽然想起一桩事。「我说，托尼，小梅里奥特（Merriot）的胆量没什么可怀疑的吧，有么？」他探问道。

「据我所知，没有。为什么这么问？」安东尼爵士正瞧着一只在牛里脊上空盘旋的苍蝇。

「哦，没为什么！」贝尔福特先生答道，一副满不在乎的样子。

安东尼爵士若有所思地端详他。「他给了您什么由头，叫您怀疑起他的胆量？」他说，那份关切表露得恰到好处，教人不能不答。

===Original===
“My dear fellow, not in the least! It was only that I thought﻿—But the thing’s a secret. Mum’s the word, y’know!”

“Really?” Sir Anthony returned to the contemplation of the fly. “Some weighty matter, I must suppose.”

“Why, as to that, it’s kept close only for fear of Miss Merriot’s getting to hear of it. Never do at all!”

Sir Anthony’s fingers played with the ribbon that held his eyeglass. “Do you mean,” he said slowly, “that someone has called Merriot out?”

“As a matter of fact, Tony, that’s it,” said Mr. Belfort confidentially.

There was a short silence. “Who is the warlike challenger?” Sir Anthony asked.
===Chinese===
「我亲爱的伙计，半点也没有！只是我原以为——可这事是个秘密。守口如瓶，您是知道的！」

「是么？」安东尼爵士回去继续端详那只苍蝇。「想必是件了不得的大事。」

「呃，说到这个，瞒得这么紧，只是怕传进梅里奥特小姐的耳朵。那可万万使不得！」

安东尼爵士的手指拨弄着系单片眼镜的缎带。「您的意思是，」他慢悠悠地说，「有人向梅里奥特下了战书？」

「不瞒您说，托尼，正是这么回事。」贝尔福特先生推心置腹地说。

短短一阵静默。「哪位好战的挑战者？」安东尼爵士问。

===Original===
“Rensley. Molyneux thinks it’s a scandal, and so ’tis if you consider it. However, he was all for a fight, so what was there to be done?”

“Rensley! Dear me!” Sir Anthony’s eyes showed nothing but a mild surprise. “And Merriot refused the challenge, did you say?”

“No, no!” Mr. Belfort was shocked. “Nothing of the sort! Good God, man, no! Though I will say that for a moment I’d a notion he was going to rat. But I was quite wrong, Tony: he took up Rensley’s challenge mighty coolly.”

Sir Anthony rose, and walked to the mirror that hung above the fireplace and became busy with the rearrangement of his neckcloth. “Then what, Charles, gave you the reason to doubt his mettle?” he asked.

“Oh, nothing in the world, I give you my word! Only that I’d an idea this morning that he didn’t relish the affair overmuch. I have the whole thing arranged: I’m acting for him, y’see, and saw Jessup at my rooms a couple of hours since. Between the two of us we had it all fixed as snug as you please for tomorrow, out at Grey’s Inn Fields, and I was off at once to let young Merriot know.”

“And he didn’t seem to be so delighted with the arrangements as you’d expected?”
===Chinese===
「伦斯利（Rensley）。莫利纽克斯（Molyneux）说这是桩丑闻，细想起来也确是。可他一门心思要打，那还有什么法子？」

「伦斯利！哎呀呀！」安东尼爵士的眼里只露出一丝淡淡的讶异。「这么说，梅里奥特回绝了战书，是么？」

「不，不！」贝尔福特先生大为震惊。「绝无此事！天哪，老兄，不是的！不过我倒要说，有一阵子我还当他要临阵脱逃了。可我全想岔了，托尼：他接下伦斯利的战书，冷静得很。」

安东尼爵士站起身，走到壁炉上方悬着的镜子前，着手重新整理起领巾来。「那么，查尔斯，又是什么叫您起了疑，疑心他的胆量？」他问。

「哦，什么事也没有，我向您担保！只是今天早上我隐约觉得，他对这桩事不怎么来劲。全盘事都由我安排停当了：您知道，我当他的副手，两个钟头前还在我寓所见了杰瑟普（Jessup）。我们俩一合计，把事情办得要多熨帖有多熨帖，定在明天，地点在格雷律师学院广场（Grey's Inn Fields），我随即动身去知会小梅里奥特。」

「他听了这些安排，似乎不像您原先指望的那样欢天喜地？」

===Original===
“Well, he was precious quiet over it﻿—but there’s nothing in the world against him, Tony. Lord, he’s like you, I dare swear, and takes no pleasure in aught until he’s breakfasted.”

“Very possibly,” agreed Sir Anthony, and came away from the mirror.

The Honourable Charles took his gay leave of him, and went off to inform Sir Raymond Orton that the grey mare was bespoken.

For some time after he had gone Sir Anthony remained standing in the middle of the room, staring with supreme vacancy at the opposite wall, and the portrait of his grandfather which hung there. Then he went across to his writing table, and sat down to it, and with great deliberation drew a sheet of paper towards him. He dipped a quill in the inkpot, and inscribed some half a dozen lines on it, signing his name at the end with a bold flourish. He read over what he had written and dusted the paper with sand. It was sealed up with a wafer, and a big blot of red wax, and placed in one of the drawers of the desk. Sir Anthony rose, called for his hat and his cane, and sallied forth into the street.

He went leisurely to White’s, and found there a sprinkling of people, early in the day though it was. He sat down with a journal by the empty fireplace. Various people came and went, amongst them Mr. Merriot, with whom Sir Anthony exchanged a pleasant word or two. He said nothing about the prospective duel, but hoped Mr. Merriot would dine with him on the following evening. Prudence accepted, placid enough to all outward appearances, but she bore a sinking heart in her breast. The night had brought no good counsel, and with the morning had come the Honourable Charles, who seemed to her of a sudden, a cheerful young brute. She had small hope of keeping her appointment with Sir Anthony, but it would not do to let the large gentleman suspect that. She showed a faint desire to escape from him, and went out presently with Mr. Devereux, who desired her advice in the choosing of a flowered waistcoat.

Sir Anthony returned to his paper, and did not look up again until a laughing voice said: “Oh, he’s gone off to take a lesson from Galliano! Belfort held out for swords, and of course Rensley wanted pistols.”
===Chinese===
「唔，他听了倒是出奇地安静——不过这可算不得他什么把柄，托尼。老天，我敢说他跟您一个样，早饭没下肚，对什么都提不起兴致。」

「多半是。」安东尼爵士应道，从镜子前走开。

尊贵的查尔斯兴冲冲地辞别了他，跑去通知雷蒙德·奥顿爵士，说那匹灰母马已经许了人了。

他走后，安东尼爵士还在屋子中央立了好一阵，神思一片彻底的空茫，凝望着对面的墙壁，和墙上挂着的祖父画像。随后他踱到写字台前坐下，从容不迫地拉过一张纸。他往墨水池里蘸了鹅毛笔，在纸上写下六七行字，末了笔锋豪放地一挥，签下自己的名字。他把写就的信从头读了一遍，往纸上撒了吸墨的细沙。信以一枚干胶封签封口，再滴上一大团红火漆，放进了写字台的一个抽屉。安东尼爵士起身，唤人取来帽子和手杖，出门上街去了。

他慢悠悠地踱到怀特俱乐部（White's），虽说时辰尚早，里头已稀稀落落坐了些人。他在空壁炉边落座，取过一份报纸。人来人往，其中有梅里奥特先生，安东尼爵士同他愉快地寒暄了一两句。对那场悬在头上的决斗，他只字不提，只盼梅里奥特先生明晚来与他共进晚餐。普鲁登丝（Prudence）应承下来，外表平静得无可挑剔，胸中却揣着一颗往下沉的心。这一夜没带来什么高明的主意，随清晨一道登门的，是那位尊贵的查尔斯——在她眼里，他陡然成了个兴冲冲、没心没肺的小畜生。她自觉赴安东尼爵士之约的希望渺茫，可这千万不能让那位大块头绅士瞧出端倪。她淡淡做出一点想脱身的样子，不多时便随德弗罗（Devereux）先生出了门——那位正想请她参谋着挑一件绣花马甲。

安东尼爵士回到他的报纸上，直到一个含笑的嗓音响起，才再度抬头：「哦，他找加利亚诺（Galliano）上课去啦！贝尔福特咬定要用剑，伦斯利嘛，自然巴不得用手枪。」

===Original===
The heavy eyes lifted. It was Sir Raymond Orton who had spoken. He made one of a small group standing at the other side of the fireplace. Mr. Molyneux was there, and Mr. Troubridge, and young Lord Kestrel.

Mr. Troubridge took snuff. “It is not one’s business,” he remarked, “but one wonders that Rensley could find no one nearer his own age.”

My lord looked perplexed. “What’s that? Merriot said something about Rensley’s manners, you know.”

“You are perfectly right, Troubridge,” said Mr. Molyneux, preserving his air of disapproval. “Rensley’s sore﻿—small blame to him﻿—over all this pother of the claim, and he was out to pick a quarrel with someone by way of venting his spleen. Well, I’m glad young Merriot stood for the small sword: Rensley’s killed his man with the pistols.”

Sir Anthony put away his journal, and went to join the group. “He would not appear to have too great a faith in his skill with the small sword,” he remarked.

Orton looked scornful. “He’s skilled enough to account for young Merriot, I should have supposed. Only Devereux spread it about that Merriot was deadly with the weapon, and has some Italian tricks up his sleeve. So off goes our friend for an hour’s practice with Galliano.”
===Chinese===
那双惺忪的眼睛抬了起来。说话的是雷蒙德·奥顿爵士。壁炉另一侧站着小小一群人，他是其中之一。莫利纽克斯先生在，特拉布里奇（Troubridge）先生在，还有年轻的凯斯特雷尔勋爵（Lord Kestrel）。

特拉布里奇先生取了一撮鼻烟。「这本不干旁人的事，」他说，「可旁人免不了纳闷，伦斯利怎么就找不着一个年纪同他相仿的。」

凯斯特雷尔勋爵一脸困惑。「这话怎么讲？要知道，梅里奥特可是数落过伦斯利的礼数。」

「您说得完全对，特拉布里奇。」莫利纽克斯先生说，脸上依旧挂着一副不以为然的神气。「伦斯利为这桩继承之诉掀起的轩然大波憋着一肚子火——这也怨不得他——存心找个人寻衅，好出一出这口恶气。唉，幸亏小梅里奥特坚持用小剑：论手枪，伦斯利可是毙过人的。」

安东尼爵士收起报纸，走过去加入那一群。「看来，他对自己的小剑功夫，信心不见得太足。」他说。

奥顿满脸不屑。「照我看，要料理小梅里奥特，他的功夫绰绰有余。偏是德弗罗四处放风，说梅里奥特使剑狠辣，袖子里还藏着几手意大利绝招。于是咱们这位朋友便跑去加利亚诺那儿，加练一个钟头。」

===Original===
“It smacks to me of some qualms,” said Lord Kestrel, with a look of distaste. “Now Merriot’s gone off to look at waistcoats, as cool as you please.”

“Rensley will be in a devilish rage when he finds the secret’s out, and the whole world knows he went off to get Galliano to show him a cunning pass or two!” grinned Orton. He nodded to Sir Anthony. “Farraday went to wait upon him, and his man let it out. He deserves to be well roasted for playing such a shabby trick.”

“Well”﻿—Sir Anthony smiled pleasantly on the group﻿—“I’m bound for Galliano’s myself to arrange for some practice. I may stumble upon the gentleman. Give me your company, Molyneux.”

“What, are you purposing to fight a duel?” said Troubridge, laughing.

“No, my dear Troubridge, no, but I like to keep my wrist in practice. Come and have a bout with me.”

There was some raillery, for Sir Anthony was known to be a peaceable man. In high good humour, and in the expectation of entertainment to be gained from confronting Rensley at the fencing master’s, not only the two invited, but Orton also, and my Lord Kestrel decided to accompany Sir Anthony. They would bait Mr. Rensley a little, and take a turn with the foils. It would be an agreeable way of spending the morning.
===Chinese===
「我闻着这里头有股心虚的味儿。」凯斯特雷尔勋爵说，面带嫌恶。「再瞧梅里奥特，人家看马甲去了，要多自在有多自在。」

「等伦斯利发觉秘密漏了底，全城都知道他跑去找加利亚诺讨教一两手刁招，他不气个半死才怪！」奥顿咧嘴笑道。他朝安东尼爵士点点头。「法拉迪（Farraday）上他府上拜访，他的仆人漏了口风。使出这种下作的把戏，活该让人好生揶揄一番。」

「这样啊——」安东尼爵士朝众人愉快地一笑——「我本人正要去加利亚诺那儿安排点练习。说不定能撞上那位先生。陪我同去，莫利纽克斯。」

「怎么，您是打算去打一场决斗么？」特拉布里奇笑着说。

「不是，我亲爱的特拉布里奇，不是，我只是喜欢让手腕常练着不生疏。来同我过上几招。」

众人起了一阵哄笑，因为谁都知道，安东尼爵士是个出名的好好先生。一想到上剑术大师那儿当面会一会伦斯利，有热闹可看，人人兴高采烈：不光应邀的两位，连奥顿和凯斯特雷尔勋爵也决定陪安东尼爵士同去。他们要稍稍撩拨撩拨伦斯利先生，再花剑走上几回合。这么打发一个上午，倒不失为一桩乐事。

===Original===
The little Italian had a room over the shop owned by a purveyor of rappee, in the Haymarket. The small party was soon arrived there, and climbed the stairs to the first floor. There was some laughter and a deal of light talk. Signor Galliano’s servant came to the head of the stairs, drawn by the sudden noise, and requested the gentlemen to have the goodness to wait only a moment in the chamber behind the fencing-room. There was a gentleman with the good signor.

“Oh, we know all about that, Tino!” said my Lord Kestrel jovially, and pushed by to the door of the front room.

Tino expostulated feebly, but it seemed there was no gainsaying these merry gentlemen.

My lord opened the door, and affected a start of surprise. “Good gad, Rensley! You here?”

Mr. Rensley was putting on his coat, and looked up with a very genuine start. In the middle of the floor the little Italian instructor stood leaning on his foil, and beaming with pleasure upon these new visitors. He descried the large form of Sir Anthony Fanshawe, and flourished the foil joyously. “Aha, saire! Aha! You come to me to learn the newest passes, eh? I have one for you, and you may call it *Le Baiser de la Morte*. I teach it to you, for you have very nearly the soul to appreciate it.” His foil darted out to touch my Lord Kestrel lightly over the heart. “For you, milor, no! Ah, no! It is for ze vey few﻿—you may say for zose initiate in ze art of ze duello. You I teach a better management of ze feet.” He frowned fiercely upon Sir Raymond, but his little eyes twinkled. “I instruct zis bad Saire Raymond not to be ze bull at ze gate, hein?”
===Chinese===
这位小个子意大利人的房间，设在海马克特（Haymarket）一家鼻烟铺的楼上。一小行人不多时便到了那里，拾级登上二楼。一路笑声不断，闲话滔滔。加利亚诺先生的仆人被这骤起的喧闹引到楼梯口，恳请诸位绅士劳驾，先到剑室后面的小间里略候片刻：好先生正陪着一位客人。

「哦，那桩事我们全知道，蒂诺（Tino）！」凯斯特雷尔勋爵乐呵呵地说，从他身边挤过去，直奔前室的门。

蒂诺有气无力地劝阻，可这几位兴冲冲的绅士，看来是断然拦不住的。

凯斯特雷尔勋爵推开门，故作一惊。「老天爷，伦斯利！您在这儿？」

伦斯利先生正在穿外衣，抬头一惊，这一惊却货真价实。屋子中央，小个子意大利教习拄着他的花剑站定，满面春风地迎着这批不速之客。他一眼望见安东尼·范肖爵士那高大的身形，欢欢喜喜把手中的花剑舞了个花。「啊哈，先生！啊哈！您来找我学最新的招式，嗯？我有一手给您，您不妨叫它 *Le Baiser de la Morte*（死亡之吻）。我教给您，因为您的魂儿，差不多够格领受它了。」他的花剑倏地探出，轻轻点在凯斯特雷尔勋爵的心口。「您嘛，爵爷，不行！啊，不行！这一手是留给极少数人的——您可以说是留给那些参透了决斗艺术之门径的人。您，我教您把两只脚驾驭得更像样。」他冲雷蒙德爵士狠狠皱眉，一双小眼睛里却闪着光。「我管教这位坏雷蒙德先生，教他别学那头撞门的蛮牛，嗯？」

===Original===
“Oh, come now, Gally, it’s not so bad as that, surely!” protested Orton blinking.

“It is worse, my frien’. It is of a vileness! For Mistaire Troubridge, I take him sedately, aha? Mr. Molyneux not come to play wiz Galliano. He favours ze English school, which is just nozing at all. Mistaire Rensley he wastes my time too. *Sapristi*, but it is again ze bull at ze gate! I kill him a sousand times. Ten sousand times!”

Galliano was a privileged person, and his strictures and familiarities were received with mirth, and mock contrition. My Lord Kestrel went over to the window seat, and flung himself down upon it, demanding to be shown the *Baiser de la Morte*. Sir Anthony looked with great interest through his glass at Mr. Rensley. “Well, well!” he said. “And have you been acquiring the Kiss, Rensley?”

“Bacchus! You accuse me of a sacrilege ze mos’ infamous!” cried Galliano. “I teach him only to keep ze head cool on ze shoulders. I sink he go to fight a duello. I sank ze gods I have not to see it. It would wring ze heart! Me, I am an artis’.”

My lord said with a wicked look in his eye: “I’d no notion you were taking lessons of old Galliano, Rensley.”
===Chinese===
「嗳，得了吧，加利（Gally），没糟到那个地步吧，真的！」奥顿眨着眼抗议道。

「更糟，我的朋友。糟透了！至于特拉布里奇先生，我教他四平八稳，啊哈？莫利纽克斯先生不来跟加利亚诺玩。他偏爱英国派，那一派简直什么都不是。伦斯利先生呢，也白白糟蹋我的工夫。*Sapristi*，又是那头撞门的蛮牛！我杀他一千次。一万次！」

加利亚诺是个享有特权的人物，他的刻薄话和没大没小，众人照单全收，报以欢笑和佯装的悔罪。凯斯特雷尔勋爵踱到窗座上一躺，吵着要见识见识那记 *Baiser de la Morte*。安东尼爵士隔着单片眼镜，饶有兴味地打量伦斯利先生。「哎呀呀！」他说。「这么说，您已经学得那记死亡之吻了，伦斯利？」

「Bacchus！您这是指控我犯了天下最不赦的渎神罪啦！」加利亚诺嚷道。「我只教他把脑袋在肩膀上放凉些。我估摸着，他是要去打一场决斗。我谢天谢地，幸亏不必亲眼看。那要绞碎人的心！我嘛，我是个艺术家。」

凯斯特雷尔勋爵眼里闪着一丝促狭，说道：「我还不知道您正在老加利亚诺门下上课呢，伦斯利。」

===Original===
“I have now and then an hour with him,” Rensley answered, and seemed in some anxiety to be gone.

But Sir Raymond Orton leaned casually against the door. “Now and then being when there’s a fight brewing, eh, Rensley my buck?”

“Really, Orton! Is it a jest belike?”

“The most famous one, Rensley, and spreading all over the town.”

Sir Anthony spoke to Galliano. “We’d a mind to have the foils out, Gally, but I suppose you have Mr. Merriot coming to you?”

“I do not know any Mistaire Merriot,” said Galliano positively. “I am at Saire Anthony’s disposal. Why should I have an appointment wiz a Mistaire I don’ know?”
===Chinese===
「我隔三差五同他练上一个钟头。」伦斯利答道，一副巴不得赶紧脱身的焦灼。

可雷蒙德·奥顿爵士懒洋洋地倚在了门边。「隔三差五，敢情是每逢有架要打的时候吧，嗯，我神气的伦斯利老弟？」

「真是的，奥顿！这也算得上玩笑？」

「天字第一号的玩笑，伦斯利，眼下正传遍全城呢。」

安东尼爵士对加利亚诺开了口。「我们本想取出花剑来练，加利，不过我想，您这儿该有位梅里奥特先生要来吧？」

「我压根不认得什么梅里奥特先生。」加利亚诺斩钉截铁地说。「我听凭安东尼先生差遣。我为什么要同一位素不相识的先生有约在身？」

===Original===
“Oh, I thought ’twas a new fashion to take a lesson before a meeting!” said Sir Anthony idly twirling his eyeglass. “Now I see it is only Mr. Rensley’s fashion. But what a disappointment for him to have this new pass withheld! Can’t you teach him your *Baiser*, Gally?”

The Italian looked quickly from one face to the other. Some mischief he could smell in the air, and all his sharp little brain was on the alert. “I do not try to teach him *Baiser*. You﻿—yes, I will show. But I do not show Mr. Rensley, nor you, milor, nor Saire Raymond eizer.”

“You’ve no heart, Gally; positively you’ve none,” Sir Anthony told him. “Have a little pity on poor Rensley!”

Mr. Rensley stood still beside Sir Raymond. He had shut his mouth hard, but his eyes smouldered. Mr. Molyneux was looking curiously at Fanshawe, but my lord, by the window, watched Rensley and chuckled. It was a jest he could appreciate.

“You don’t apprehend the matter,” Sir Anthony went on persuasively, still twirling his glass. “Here’s Rensley feels he must let some blood﻿—not his own, of course﻿—and hits on the very man. That’s to say, it seemed so﻿—one of your youthful sprigs from the country. Ideal, you perceive. But the devil was in it that the sprig was held to have some cunning tricks of fence﻿—possibly your *Baiser*, Gally; who knows? Naturally poor Rensley’s monstrous put out over it, and what else should he do but fly to our friend Galliano? And you fail him, Gally! It’s unkind in you, upon my word it is. Poor Rensley will be forced to withdraw from the engagement, I fear me.”
===Chinese===
「哦，我还当临上阵前先上一课，是新时兴的规矩呢！」安东尼爵士说着，懒洋洋地转着他的单片眼镜。「如今我明白了，这只是伦斯利先生一个人的时兴。可是，这记新招偏偏扣着不让他学，他该多失望啊！您就不能把您那记 *Baiser* 教给他么，加利？」

这意大利人的目光在一张张脸之间飞快地逡巡。空气里飘着一股算计的味道，他嗅出来了，那颗精明的小脑袋全然警戒起来。「那记 *Baiser*，我不想教他。您——可以，我愿意演示。可我不演示给伦斯利先生，也不给您，爵爷，还不给雷蒙德先生。」

「您可真没心肝，加利；千真万确，一点也没有。」安东尼爵士对他说。「多少发点善心，可怜可怜伦斯利吧！」

伦斯利先生一动不动地僵立在雷蒙德爵士身边。他双唇紧闭，眼里却闷着暗火。莫利纽克斯先生正好奇地打量范肖，窗边的凯斯特雷尔勋爵却盯着伦斯利，低声窃笑。这个玩笑，他欣赏得来。

「您没能领会这桩事的关节。」安东尼爵士依旧转着单片眼镜，循循善诱地往下说。「这位伦斯利，觉得自己非得放一点血不可——当然，放的不是自己的血——于是一眼挑中了正主。也就是说，看上去如此——你们乡下来的嫩苗子之一。您瞧，再理想不过了。可恶就恶在，这棵嫩苗子据说身怀几手刁钻的剑招——兴许就是您那记 *Baiser*，加利；谁知道呢？可怜的伦斯利自然烦闷欲狂，他还能怎么样，只好飞奔来投咱们的朋友加利亚诺？结果您让他扑了空，加利！您这可不厚道，我向您担保，真不厚道。我怕可怜的伦斯利，只得退出这场决斗了。」

===Original===
The chuckle died on my Lord Kestrel’s lips; Sir Raymond looked round quickly. Mr. Rensley took two steps towards Sir Anthony, and spoke in a voice barely controlled. “Will you be good enough to explain these remarks, Sir Anthony?” he demanded.

Sir Anthony turned slowly to face him. Mr. Rensley was by no means a small man, but the lazy eyes looked down at him. Sir Anthony stopped twirling his glass, and though he smiled still it was not his usual genial expression, but on the contrary a smile rather disdainful, and with the hint of sternness behind it. “Certainly, Mr. Rensley. But I should have thought my meaning was plain enough. No doubt you have your reasons for not wishing to comprehend it.”

Rensley reddened. “This is not the first time you’ve sneered at me, Sir Anthony!”

“Nor the last, Rensley, unless the colour of your coat should change.”

“You make your meaning quite plain, I thank you, sir! You choose to think me a coward because I chance to take an hour’s practice here today.”

“You have it quite wrong, my good Rensley,” said Sir Anthony imperturbably. “I choose to think you a coward because you forced a quarrel on a man well nigh young enough to be your son.”
===Chinese===
凯斯特雷尔勋爵唇边的窃笑僵住了；雷蒙德爵士霍然环顾。伦斯利先生朝安东尼爵士跨出两步，开口时嗓音勉强克制着。「可否劳您把方才这些话解释清楚，安东尼爵士？」他质问道。

安东尼爵士缓缓转过身来，面对着他。伦斯利先生绝非小个子，可那双慵懒的眼睛却是居高临下地看着他。安东尼爵士停住转动的单片眼镜，嘴上虽还挂着笑，却不是平日那副和蔼的神情，恰恰相反，那是一抹颇为轻蔑的笑，笑意后头隐隐透着严厉。「当然可以，伦斯利先生。不过我原以为，我的意思已经够明白了。您不愿领会它，想必自有您的道理。」

伦斯利涨红了脸。「您讥诮我，这可不是头一回了，安东尼爵士！」

「也不会是最后一回，伦斯利，除非您那件外套的颜色换一换。」

「您把意思挑得够明白了，多谢您了，先生！您存心把我当懦夫，就因为我今天碰巧在这儿练了一个钟头。」

「您可全想岔了，我好心的伦斯利。」安东尼爵士不动声色地说。「我把您当懦夫，是因为您把一场衅端强加给一个年轻得几乎做得了您儿子的人。」

===Original===
Under his breath Sir Raymond gave the dueller’s “Sa-sa!” The jest had of a sudden taken an ugly turn, and what in the fiend’s name ailed Fanshawe to be picking a quarrel in this fashion?

Rensley spoke between shut teeth. “May I ask what concern it is of yours, sir?”

Sir Anthony’s eyes were hard and scornful. “Make no doubt, sir, I can readily understand your anxiety for me not to make it my concern.”

Troubridge laid his hand on Sir Anthony’s arm. “Tony﻿—” he began, expostulating.

His hand was removed. “In a moment, Troubridge.”

Mr. Rensley’s fingers sought the hilt of his sword. “I know how to take that, Sir Anthony. You shall have all the taste of my mettle you require, and maybe some more beside. Be pleased to name your seconds.”
===Chinese===
雷蒙德爵士压着嗓子，喝了声剑客临阵的「萨萨（Sa-sa）！」玩笑陡然转出凶险的路数，范肖到底撞了什么邪，要这样寻衅挑架？

伦斯利从咬紧的牙缝里挤出话来。「敢问这与您何干，先生？」

安东尼爵士的目光冷硬而轻蔑。「您尽管放心，先生：您急切地不愿让这事同我相干，这份急切，我完全体谅。」

特拉布里奇把手搭上安东尼爵士的手臂。「托尼——」他开口劝阻。

那只手被拂开了。「稍待片刻，特拉布里奇。」

伦斯利先生的手指摸上了剑柄。「这记话我知道怎么接，安东尼爵士。我的胆量，您要尝多少，尽管尝，兴许还额外饶上几分。请费心指定您的副手。」

===Original===
Sir Anthony looked round the room. “Why, here are enough for us both,” he said. “I will take Mr. Molyneux and Mr. Troubridge for mine. I make no doubt my Lord Kestrel, and Orton there will be charmed to serve you.”

Mr. Molyneux jumped. “Good Gad, Fanshawe, what’s this?”

“I’ll choose my own friends, I thank you, sir! You shall hear from them.” Mr. Rensley strode to the door but was checked by Sir Anthony’s voice.

“Not so fast, not so fast! It is for me to name the time and the place. What place could be better than this, and what time half so suitable as the present?”

Kestrel’s eyes danced. Fanshawe had undoubtedly taken leave of his senses, but this promised to be a rare morning’s work. “You can count on me, Rensley,” he struck in.

“Nothing, be sure, would please me more, Sir Anthony,” Rensley answered, “but I have a meeting with your protégé tomorrow and your quarrel must wait on his.”
===Chinese===
安东尼爵士环视屋内。「哎呀，咱们两边的人手，这儿现成就够了。」他说。「我这边，就请莫利纽克斯先生和特拉布里奇先生。我毫不怀疑，凯斯特雷尔勋爵和那边的奥顿，定会乐于为您效劳。」

莫利纽克斯先生跳了起来。「老天爷，范肖，这是演的哪一出？」

「我自己的朋友自己挑，多谢您了，先生！回头自会有人去寻您交涉。」伦斯利先生大步朝门口走去，却被安东尼爵士的声音拦下。

「慢来，慢来！时间地点，该由我来定。哪里的地方比这里更好，哪个时候比眼前更合适？」

凯斯特雷尔的眼睛发亮。范肖无疑是魂儿离了窍，可这一上午，注定有一场难得的好戏。「算我一个，伦斯利。」他插话道。

「放心，再没有比这更叫我称心的了，安东尼爵士。」伦斯利答道，「只是明天我先同您的被保护人有约，您这场争执，只好排在他那场后头。」

===Original===
“Really, Tony, you must﻿—”

“Give me leave, Molyneux.” A hand was raised to enjoin silence. “I don’t wait on young Merriot’s pleasure, Rensley.”

“In this instance, sir, you will find you must.”

Sir Anthony smiled. “You must think me a much bigger fool than I am, Mr. Rensley.”

“I doubt it, sir!” There was a bite to the words.

“Oh, but you do, my good Rensley, if you suppose that I do not perfectly understand the meaning of this refusal of yours to meet me now.”
===Chinese===
「说真的，托尼，您千万——」

「容我一句，莫利纽克斯。」一只手扬起，示意噤声。「我可不等小梅里奥特拣日子，伦斯利。」

「这一回，先生，您会发现非等不可。」

安东尼爵士微微一笑。「您必是把我当成个天大的傻瓜了，伦斯利先生，比实情大得多。」

「这我倒要怀疑了，先生！」话里带着刺。

「哦，您确实是这么想的，我好心的伦斯利——倘若您以为，您此刻拒不同我交手的用意，我竟没有看个通透。」

===Original===
“And what is the meaning, sir?”

Sir Anthony pointed his long cane at Rensley, and answered in a voice of indulgent scorn. “Oh, you will prove your mettle on young Merriot to the satisfaction of the world, and I shall hear next that you sustained some slight hurt in that encounter for which the surgeon prescribes a foreign clime.” He shrugged his broad shoulders. “No, no, Rensley, it won’t serve!”

Mr. Rensley’s hand shook on his sword hilt, but it was not from fright. “To hell with your insinuations!” he cried. “You’d say I fear to meet you, eh?”

“I say, Mr. Rensley, that you dare not meet me now or at any time,” Sir Anthony replied, to the astonishment of his friends. His hand came up, and he struck Mr. Rensley lightly across the mouth with the glove he held.

There was a choked oath, and the rasp of steel scraping against the scabbard. Mr. Rensley’s sword was out.

Galliano leaped in with his foil raised. “Ah, ah! Put up ze sword! Put up, I say! You go to make a scandal of me, ze pair of you!” he cried.
===Chinese===
「那用意是什么，先生？」

安东尼爵士用长长的手杖点着伦斯利，答话的口吻满是纵容的轻蔑。「哦，您会在小梅里奥特身上，向全天下证明您的胆气，跟着我便要听说，您在那场较量里受了点轻伤，外科大夫开了方子，说非去外国的气候里将养不可。」他耸了耸宽阔的肩膀。「不，不，伦斯利，这一套不顶用！」

伦斯利先生按在剑柄上的手抖个不停，却不是吓的。「你那些含沙射影，统统见鬼去吧！」他嚷道。「你是想说我怕同你交手，嗯？」

「我要说的是，伦斯利先生：此刻也好，任何时候也好，你都不敢同我交手。」安东尼爵士答道，他的朋友们无不为之愕然。他的手扬起，用手中握着的手套，在伦斯利先生嘴上轻轻抽了一记。

一声哽在喉头的咒骂，一阵钢刃蹭过鞘口的刺响。伦斯利先生的剑出了鞘。

加利亚诺高举花剑抢身上前。「啊，啊！收剑！我说，收剑！你们俩这是要闹出一桩砸我招牌的丑闻！」他喊道。

===Original===
“I will fight you here and now, Sir Anthony!” thundered Mr. Rensley, and flung his hat and cane aside.

There came a gleam into the grey eyes. “Give us houseroom, Gally,” said Sir Anthony. “What a pity neither of us had time to acquire the Kiss!”

“Anthony, you’re surely mad!” Mr. Molyneux’s voice was urgent in his ear.

“I was never more sane, believe me,” Sir Anthony assured him, coming out of his coat. “Lock the door, Gally.” He tucked up his ruffles. “There’s a letter in my desk, Molyneux, in case﻿—. You’ll find it.”

“Fanshawe, I do beseech you﻿—”

“Pray don’t, my dear fellow; it’s quite useless. Gally, my friend, help me to pull off these boots, of your compassion.”
===Chinese===
「我现在就当场同你打这一场，安东尼爵士！」伦斯利先生雷霆般怒喝，把帽子和手杖甩到一边。

那双灰眼睛里掠过一道亮光。「给我们腾腾地方，加利。」安东尼爵士说。「可惜咱们俩谁都没来得及学那记死亡之吻！」

「安东尼，你准是疯了！」莫利纽克斯先生的声音在他耳边急切地响着。

「我这辈子没这么清醒过，相信我。」安东尼爵士向他担保，一面褪下外衣。「把门锁上，加利。」他挽起袖口的花边。「我书桌里有一封信，莫利纽克斯，万一——。您会找到的。」

「范肖，我恳求你——」

「千万别，我亲爱的朋友；全然无益。我的朋友加利，发发慈悲，帮我脱掉这双靴子。」

===Original===
The Italian pulled them off for him, but he looked up with a worried face. “What comes to me over zis, hein? You make me a scandal, Saire Anthony!”

“Have no fear, Gally; there will be no scandal.”

Sir Raymond Orton came punctiliously forward to meet Mr. Molyneux, and swords were measured. Mr. Molyneux said, over the business:﻿—“It should be stopped, Orton. Fanshawe’s mad.”

“Stark mad!” agreed Orton cheerfully. “But it’s famous sport, after all, and there’s no stopping them now. My man’s itching to be at it. Are we ready?”

There was a formal salute, and the blades came together. In a moment there was no sound in the room save the clash and scrape of steel, and the pad-pad of stockinged feet on the wood floor. The seconds stood with drawn swords in their places; little Galliano, still holding his buttoned foil, sat in the window seat and watched with quick eager eyes. Several times he frowned; once he nodded in swift approbation.
===Chinese===
意大利人替他把靴子脱了下来，抬起头来，却满面愁容。「这摊子事算到我头上，算怎么一回事，嗯？您要给我惹出一桩丑闻了，安东尼先生！」

「别怕，加利；不会有丑闻。」

雷蒙德·奥顿爵士一板一眼地迎上前来，与莫利纽克斯先生相见，双方比量了剑。莫利纽克斯先生一面照章办事一面说：「这事该拦下来，奥顿。范肖疯了。」

「疯得通透！」奥顿乐呵呵地应和。「不过说到底，这终归是场头等的好戏，如今谁也拦不住了。我那边那位正手痒难耐呢。都预备好了么？」

两人依礼举剑一敬，剑刃随即咬合到了一处。顷刻间，屋内再无别的声息，只有钢铁的磕撞与刮擦，和穿袜的脚掌踏在木地板上的嗒嗒声。两名副手按剑各守其位；小个子加利亚诺仍握着那柄圆头花剑，坐在窗座上，一双急切敏锐的眼睛瞧着。他皱了几回眉；有一回倏地点头称赏。

===Original===
It was hard fighting, for one man had unbearable insults to avenge, and the other’s whole mind and will were bent on disabling his adversary. Very soon it was clear to see which was the better man. Rensley’s thrusts were savage indeed, and his attack full of fire, but his passes went wide, and more than once it seemed to the onlookers that Sir Anthony held him at his mercy. The big man, who was yet so curiously light on his feet, was playing with Rensley, and slowly the men standing by realized that he was making for just one spot, and would be satisfied with no other.

The end came quickly. Rensley saw an opening, and lunged forward. There was a scurry of blades, a lightning thrust, and Rensley went staggering back, with a hand caught to his right arm.

The seconds sprang in; Galliano clapped delighted hands; Sir Anthony stood back, and wiped his wet sword. A red stain was spreading over Mr. Rensley’s shirt, and his right arm hung useless.

Galliano skipped into the middle of the room. “Bravo, bravo!” he exclaimed. “I taught you zat pass! I, Girolamo Galliano!”

“Curb your enthusiasm, my friend,” Sir Anthony advised him.

Galliano tossed up his arms. “Ensusiasm! Bah, it was bad, bad﻿—all of it! You English you do not understand ze art! But just once or twice zere was a pass I might myself have make! Do not flatter yourself! You cannot fence: not even you, Saire Anthony!”
===Chinese===
这是一场恶斗：一方怀着忍无可忍的羞辱要雪耻，另一方的全副心神与意志，都用在废掉对手的战斗力上。孰优孰劣，很快就看得分明。伦斯利的突刺委实凶悍，攻势一团烈火，可他的剑招处处落空，旁观者不止一次觉得，安东尼爵士随时可以取他性命。这位大块头绅士，脚下却轻巧得出奇，这会儿正拿伦斯利耍弄着；立在一旁的人们渐渐看出，他只认准了一个部位下手，别的部位一概不能叫他满足。

结局来得很快。伦斯利觑出一个破绽，挺身直刺。剑刃一阵错乱疾响，一道电光似的突刺，伦斯利踉跄着连退几步，一只手死死捂住了右臂。

两名副手抢身上前；加利亚诺欢喜得直拍手；安东尼爵士退后一步，拭净了剑上的血。一团红斑正在伦斯利先生的衬衫上洇开，他的右臂无力地垂了下来。

加利亚诺蹦到屋子中央。「好哇，好哇！」他欢呼道。「那一招是我教您的！我，吉罗拉莫·加利亚诺（Girolamo Galliano）！」

「收敛收敛您的热情吧，我的朋友。」安东尼爵士劝他。

加利亚诺双臂往上一扬。「热情！呸，糟透了，糟透了——从头到尾！你们英格兰人，不懂这门艺术！不过有那么一两招，便是我亲自使出来，也不过如此！少给自己脸上贴金！你们不会击剑：连您也不例外，安东尼先生！」

## XXVII Violence on the King’s High Road / XXVII 王家大道上的暴行

===Original===

Having caught a glimpse of the sober coach’s equally sober pace, John had little doubt of reaching London far ahead of it. The mare was fresh; she desired nothing better than a good gallop. John left the road for the fields, and gave her her head.

It was a shortcut. He would pass the coach without the men on it seeing him, and could join the road again further on. Then for my Lord Barham, with all possible speed, and back again to hold Master Robin in check.

John could see no way out of the present dilemma, but he never saw the way in any crisis: he could only obey instructions. He had not the smallest doubt that my lord would at once perceive a way. The greatest anxiety, once my lord was informed, must be Master Robin’s behaviour.

John knew quite enough of this young gentleman to picture all manner of foolhardy deeds. Certain, he must hasten back to Richmond with all speed.

The mare was covering the ground in a long, easy gallop. John came on to a cart-track he had been making for, and turned down it. In a little while the cart-track joined the road; John reined the mare into a canter, easing her for a space. A strip of close turf bordered the road; he pressed on to it, and the mare, nothing loth, quickened to the gallop again.

John began to consider the time. Judging by the long shadows it was nearly dusk, and Mistress Prue must not be left to spend the night in captivity. And where should he find my lord at this hour? There came a worried look into the square face: John foresaw much waste of time spent in search of his master. Unconsciously he pressed his knees closer to the mare’s flanks. He was well ahead of the coach, but there was not a moment to be lost.

===Chinese===

望见那辆其貌不扬的马车，连步子也同样迈得不紧不慢，约翰（John）便颇有把握，定能远远抢在它前头赶到伦敦。母马精力正足；再没有比撒开四蹄好好狂奔一程更合它心意的了。约翰离开大路折进田野，任它撒欢去了。

这是一条近道。他能赶过那辆马车而不叫车上的人瞧见，到前头再重新并上大路。接着便去寻巴勒姆勋爵（my Lord Barham），越快越好，然后再赶回来拴住罗宾少爷（Master Robin），不叫他妄动。

眼下的困局，约翰看不出一条出路；可无论碰上什么危难，他从来不曾看出过出路：他只会奉行吩咐。勋爵大人立时便能想出法子，这一点他毫不起疑。勋爵大人一旦得讯，最叫人悬心的，便该是罗宾少爷的行止了。

这位年轻绅士的脾性，约翰领教得足够多了，尽可以想见种种亡命的莽事。毫无疑问，他必须火速赶回里士满（Richmond）。

母马舒展着长长的步子，一路从容地掠过地面。约翰踏上他一路奔着要去的那条车辙道，顺道拐了进去。不多时，车辙道并上大路；约翰收缰让母马改作小跑，缓它一缓。大路旁镶着一条齐整的草皮带；他催马踏上草皮，母马正巴不得，重新放开步子又狂奔起来。

约翰开始盘算时辰。看那长长的日影，天已近黄昏，总不能让普鲁小姐（Mistress Prue）在拘押之中过夜。可这个时辰，他又上哪儿去寻勋爵大人呢？他那张方脸上浮起一层忧色：约翰预见到，寻主人的这一趟，少不得大把耗时候。他不觉把双膝往母马肋侧又夹紧了些。他已远远赶在马车前头，可依旧一刻也耽误不得。

===Original===

The road turned a corner; there was a horseman in sight, trotting along the strip of turf towards John. John pulled the mare in a little, anxious to attract no attention, and she slackened to a canter.

He would have passed this other rider without a glance, but of a sudden the big roan horse was pulled across his path, barring the way, and he heard the voice of Sir Anthony Fanshawe.

“Well, my man? Well? Whither away so fast?”

The mare had been brought perforce to a standstill. John looked into that handsome, lazy face, and spoke urgently. “Let me pass, sir. I must get to his lordship.”

The eyes were keen and searching. “Yes?” said Sir Anthony. “And wherefor?”

===Chinese===

大路拐过一道弯；望得见一个骑马的人，正沿着那条草皮带小跑着迎约翰而来。约翰把母马稍稍收了收，唯恐惹人注意，它便放缓成了小跑。

他本打算眼皮都不抬一下便与这骑马人错身而过，谁知那匹高大的菊花青马猛地横到他马前，拦住去路，他听见了安东尼·范肖爵士（Sir Anthony Fanshawe）的嗓音。

「喂，朋友？怎么？匆匆忙忙往哪儿去呀？」

母马被逼得站定了。约翰望着那张俊朗而慵懒的脸，急切地开口。「请让我过去，爵士。我得赶到老爷那儿去。」

那双眼睛却锐利起来，究诘地望定他。「哦？」安东尼爵士说。「那又是为了什么呢？」

===Original===

“It’s Miss Prue!” John said in an agony of impatience. “She’s taken by the Law for the killing of Mr. Markham! Now will you let me pass, sir?”

The large hand on the bridle had tightened; the indolent air was gone. “Less than ever, my man. When was she taken? Come, let me have the whole story, and quickly!”

“She’s on the road now, sir, behind me! I must get to my lord.”

“We won’t trouble his lordship,” said Sir Anthony. “This is my affair.”

John looked doubtful. The large gentleman had a masterful way with him, but John was inclined to trust to no one but my lord. He waited.

===Chinese===

「是普鲁小姐！」约翰急得火烧火燎。「她叫执法的拿了，说是杀了马卡姆（Markham）先生！这下您肯放我过去了吧，爵士？」

搭在笼头上的那只大手扣紧了；那副慵懒神气一扫而空。「比先前更不能放了，朋友。她是什么时候被拿的？来，把事情原原本本讲给我听，要快！」

「她这会儿就在大路上，爵士，就在我后头！我得去报老爷。」

「我们就不去惊动他老人家了。」安东尼爵士说。「这事归我管。」

约翰面露迟疑。这位大块头绅士自有一股说一不二的气派，可约翰心里，还是只肯信老爷一个。他便等着。

===Original===

Sir Anthony passed his riding whip absently down the neck of his horse. His eyes looked straight ahead, and they were frowning. After a moment he turned his head, and spoke. “Yes, I think we might compass it, John,” he said placidly. “Have you a mind to a fight?”

John smiled grimly. “Try me, sir! You’ll stop the coach?”

Sir Anthony nodded. “I hope so. How many men?”

“Two inside﻿—naught to fear from them. There’s the coachman on the box, and a man with him.”

“Four.” Sir Anthony was unperturbed. “Possibly a pistol in the coach.”

“There’d be one in the holster, maybe. But Miss Prue’s inside and she has all her wits, sir.” John looked at the large gentleman in some awe. From the first he had felt respect for Sir Anthony, but he had not thought that he would undertake such a lawless venture as this quite so calmly. John was of the opinion that he might well be a good man in a fight, provided his size did not make him slow.

===Chinese===

安东尼爵士心不在焉地用马鞭顺着马颈一路抚下去。他双眼直望前方，眉心蹙着。过了一会儿，他转过头，开了口。「行，我想这事儿咱们办得成，约翰。」他平平静静地说。「你想不想打一架？」

约翰狞然一笑。「您试试我吧，爵士！您要把马车截下来？」

安东尼爵士点头。「但愿截得下来。有几个人？」

「车厢里两个——不足为惧。车夫座上有车夫，身边还带着一个人。」

「四个。」安东尼爵士面不改色。「车厢里兴许还有一支手枪。」

「枪套里兴许有一支吧。不过普鲁小姐在车厢里，她的机变一样不缺，爵士。」约翰望着这位大块头绅士，颇怀几分敬畏。打一开头他就敬重安东尼爵士，却没料到他接下这等目无法纪的险事，竟这般心平气和。约翰的看法是：真动起手来，这人八成是把好手，只消他那一身块头别拖累得他手脚迟缓。

===Original===

Sir Anthony came down out of the saddle, and produced his handkerchief. “Have you a muffler, my man? Cover your face to the eyes, and pull your hat well over your nose.”

John loosened the cloth at his neck. “There’s enough of it for two, sir. You’d best wear your greatcoat.” His glance rested expressively on Sir Anthony’s fine cloth coat.

Sir Anthony was unstrapping it from the saddle. He was handed a half of John’s generous neckcloth, and proceeded to arrange it to cover the lower half of his face. The greatcoat was buttoned up, and the sword-hilt pulled through the placket. “I’ve pistols,” Sir Anthony said, “but I don’t want to make this a killing matter. Break yourself a thick stick: it should suffice.”

“Give me one of your barkers, sir. I’ll do as I did when we held up Mr. Markham﻿—fire over the coachmen’s heads. It frightens them so they think they’re killed.”

“My dear good man, do you want every cottager running from miles round to see what the noise means? Threaten a shot if you like, but on no account fire. It is understood?”

===Chinese===

安东尼爵士翻身下鞍，掏出了手绢。「你有围巾么，朋友？把脸遮到眼睛底下，帽子也拉低，好好压住鼻梁。」

约翰解松颈间的布巾。「这一条够咱俩分，爵士。您最好把大衣穿上。」他的目光意味深长地落在安东尼爵士那件讲究的呢料外衣上。

安东尼爵士正把它从鞍后解下来。约翰那条宽大的领巾分了一半递到他手里，他便动手缠裹，遮住半张脸。大衣扣拢，剑柄从衣衩里抽了出来。「手枪我有，」安东尼爵士说，「可我不想把这趟营生闹出人命来。你去折一根粗棍子：尽够用了。」

「借我一支您的手枪（barker），爵士。我就照上回截马卡姆先生那样办——朝车夫头顶上放枪。那动静吓得他们只当自己已经死了。」

「我亲爱的好朋友，你是想把方圆几英里的庄户人都招来瞧瞧这枪响是什么名堂么？要吓唬着放一枪，随你，只是万万放不得。听明白了？」

===Original===

“Ay, sir,” said John, abashed. He went off to find a likely cudgel in the little spinney close at hand. Returning presently with a rough stick of ash, he ventured a piece of information. “Miss Prue has her sword-stick, sir. I saw to that. They don’t know it, but she does, and she knows how to use it, too.”

Sir Anthony smiled a little. Ay, she would know, that cool, daring bride of his. He mounted again, and pushed forward to the spinney. “We’ll lie in wait here. It’s as lonely a stretch of road as there is. Now attend to me a moment, John. You can do as you’re told?”

John, reining in under the trees beside the large gentleman, nodded assent. It was in his mind that there were few who would care to refuse to do Sir Anthony’s bidding.

“I am going to take your mistress down to my sister in Hampshire. I shall want the mare for her, but I’ll throw her up before me on the roan until we’re out of sight. We stop as soon as possible to mount her, and I fear me, John, you will have to walk back to Richmond. You’ll tell Mr. Robin what I’ve done, and get him out of the place as soon as may be. Let him know I have his sister safe, and be urgent with him to fly.” He paused; John nodded. “As to my chaise:﻿—You’ll send it on to my Lady Enderby’s. I’d best give you a note for my man.” Out came tablets: Sir Anthony scrawled a few lines, and gave them to John. “You are to be understood to have carried a message to me. Naturally I don’t stay with my Lady Lowestoft when Mr. Merriot is not there to play host. I’ve told my man that I may break my journey at the house of a friend, a little out of the way, so there will be naught to wonder at when I don’t join my chaise at the stage tonight. It’s clear?”

“Ay, sir.” John bestowed grudging praise. “You’ve a fine head on your shoulders, sir.”

===Chinese===

「是，爵士。」约翰说，讪讪的。他跑去近旁那片矮林（spinney）里，找一根合手的棍子。不多时拿着一根白蜡木粗棍回来，斗胆进上一句情报。「普鲁小姐随身带着剑杖（sword-stick）呢，爵士。这是我留心安排的。他们不知道，她可知道，而且还知道怎么使。」

安东尼爵士微微一笑。是啊，她自会知道，他那位冷静、大胆的新娘子。他重新上马，催到矮林跟前。「咱们就在这儿设伏。再没有哪段路比这儿更僻静了。且听我嘱咐一句，约翰。叫你做什么，你做得到么？」

约翰在树底下、大块头绅士身旁收住马，点头应承。他心里琢磨着，肯推拒安东尼爵士吩咐的人，天下可没有几个。

「我打算把你家小姐带下去，送到汉普郡（Hampshire）我妹妹那儿。母马我要留给她骑，不过先把她带上菊花青马坐我身前，直到脱开众人的视线。我们尽早找地方停下给她换骑，只是恐怕，约翰，你就得走回里士满了。你把我干的事告诉罗宾先生（Mr. Robin），尽快把他从那地方弄出去。让他知道他妹妹安然在我手里，还要极力催他快逃。」他顿了顿；约翰点头。「至于我的旅行马车：——你把它打发到恩德比夫人（my Lady Enderby）府上去。我最好给你一张便条，交给我那仆人。」记事本掏了出来：安东尼爵士潦草涂了几行，交给约翰。「要叫人明白，你是来给我送过信的。梅里奥特先生（Mr. Merriot）不在那儿尽地主之谊，我自然也不便在洛斯托夫特夫人（my Lady Lowestoft）府上留宿。我已告诉我的仆人，我或许要在一位朋友家中途落脚，那地方略微偏僻，所以今夜我不到驿站与马车汇合，也没什么可怪的。清楚了么？」

「是，爵士。」约翰难得地夸了一句。「您肩膀上这颗脑袋，可真灵光，爵士。」

===Original===

“I don’t aspire to my Lord Barham’s genius, but I believe I have my wits. As for your mistress﻿—why, you may trust her to me. If she has woman’s clothes with her bring them to Dartrey in Hampshire as soon as may be. She’s done with this masquerade.”

“H’m!” said John. “Mistress Prue has a will of her own, sir, I’d make bold to tell you.”

“I know it full well, my man. But I too have a will.”

John did not doubt this: the large gentleman looked as though he would carry all before him. Well, it was a man worthy of Miss Prudence, and certain, she would come to no harm while she was in his charge. John said no more, but sat still under the trees, awaiting the coming of the coach.

There was not long to wait. The clip-clop of the horses plodding stolidly along was heard, and the creak and rumble of the coach. Came a scrape of steel beside John: Sir Anthony’s sword was out, and Sir Anthony’s hand was tight on the bridle. John took a good grip on his cudgel, and awaited the word of command.

===Chinese===

「我不敢企及巴勒姆勋爵的天才，不过我想，我还有几分头脑。至于你家小姐——唔，你尽管把她托付给我。她若随身带了女子衣裳，尽早送到汉普郡的达特里（Dartrey）来。这场化装戏，她演到头了。」

「哼！」约翰说。「恕我斗胆奉告一句，爵士，普鲁小姐可是自有主见的。」

「这个我清楚得很，朋友。可我也有我的主见。」

这话约翰不怀疑：这位大块头绅士瞧那架势，俨然所向披靡。也罢，这是个配得上普鲁登丝小姐（Miss Prudence）的男子汉，而且毫无疑问，她在他看护之下，断吃不了亏。约翰不再多话，只静静坐在树下，等那辆马车到来。

没有等多久。几匹马闷头赶路的嘚嘚蹄声传了过来，还有马车的吱呀与轱辘声。约翰身畔响起一声钢刃出鞘的刮擦：安东尼爵士的剑出了鞘，安东尼爵士的手紧紧扣住笼头。约翰把棍子攥牢，只等号令。

===Original===

“Take them in a charge,” Sir Anthony said, and pulled the muffler up over his mouth.

The coach rounded the bend in the road; Sir Anthony drove in his heels hard, and the big roan bounded forward, snorting indignantly. The mare, fidgeting all this time, needed no spur to follow suit; together the two horsemen came thundering down upon the staid equipage journeying so placidly along.

John followed the large gentleman’s lead as best he might, but he had to admit he lacked that consummate horsemanship. Ahead of the chestnut mare a few paces Sir Anthony came down the centre of the road in a cloud of dust. It seemed as though he must crash full into the horses drawing the coach. So at least the coachman thought. This unfortunate individual had a sudden vision of two horsemen bearing down upon him at a mad, runaway pace, and instinctively dragged his own startled horses up, and tried to get to the side of the road. On and on came the first horseman, nearer, and perilously nearer. Then, even as the frightened men on the box thought collision inevitable, the roan, held so far on a straight course by an iron hand and an insistent knee, swerved off to the right, and was forced back almost upon his haunches, and held rigidly.

The coach horses were plunging in fright; the coachman had all he could do to hold them. Seeing Sir Anthony swing to the right of the coach, John, all the time on his heels, wrenched the mare to the left. Sir Anthony’s sword flourished horribly near the men on the box; instinctively the one nearest to it shrank from it, throwing himself sideways against his companion, who lurched, still pulling at his reins, towards John.

===Chinese===

「冲锋拿下他们。」安东尼爵士说，把围巾拉上来罩住口鼻。

马车拐过路弯；安东尼爵士靴跟狠狠一磕，那匹高大的菊花青马愤然喷着响鼻，纵身前扑。母马这一向本就躁动难安，不用鞭策便跟着冲了出去；两个骑手一道，朝着那辆正安安稳稳赶路的规规矩矩的车驾，雷霆万钧地压了下来。

约翰竭力循着大块头绅士的引领走，却不得不承认，自己缺了那份炉火纯青的骑术。在栗色母马前头几步远，安东尼爵士裹在一团尘土里，顺着大路正中直压下来。瞧那态势，简直要一头撞进拉车的马群里去。至少车夫是这么想的。这个倒霉的家伙眼前骤然出现两个骑手发疯也似的脱缰扑来，本能地把自己那几匹受惊的马硬生生拽住，往路边挪。头一个骑手还是一个劲儿地逼近，近了，近得愈发凶险。就在车夫座上那几个吓破了胆的人以为相撞在所难免的当口，那匹一直被一只铁腕和一双不依不饶的膝头钳在直线上的菊花青马，倏地朝右一偏，几乎被逼坐上后腿，僵立住了。

拉车的马吓得连连腾跳；车夫拼了全力才按得住它们。眼见安东尼爵士朝马车右侧荡开，约翰一路紧咬不放，把母马猛拽向左边。安东尼爵士的剑在车夫座那几个人跟前可怖地翻飞；离剑最近的那一个本能地缩身避让，整个侧身撞在他同伴身上，那人一手仍死拽着缰绳，朝约翰这边一个趔趄。

===Original===

The vicious tug brought one of the horses up on its hind legs; confusion reigned between the traces; John brought the nervous mare in close, rose in his stirrups, and struck hard and true. The coachman crumpled where he sat, and came sliding to the floor of the box; his companion caught desperately at the loose rein as the horses plunged forward. The animal that had reared up became entangled in one of the traces, and the confusion was complete.

Inside the coach Prudence’s two gaolers were taken entirely by surprise. Such an unheard of thing as an attack on a vehicle of the Law, in daylight, and only a few miles out of town did not occur to them as a reason for this sudden stop and commotion.

The apologetic man opposite Prudence, who had abstained carefully from looking at her till now, brought his eyes round to her, and said with inspiration: “Ah! We’ve gone over a pig. That’ll be it.”

Prudence said nothing at all, but her hand slid to that innocent-looking cane beside her, and closed round its head. She still leaned back in her corner, and there was nothing either in her pose or in her expression to tell her captors that every nerve and muscle in her body was taut and ready.

===Chinese===

这一记狠拽，拽得其中一匹马前半身人立而起；挽索之间登时乱了套；约翰把紧张的母马带近，从马镫上直起身来，一击又狠又准。车夫瘫坐在原处，顺势滑倒在车夫座的板上；马群往前一扑，他的同伴没命地去抓那根松脱的缰绳。那匹人立起来的马缠进了一根挽索里，这场乱子就此闹了个十足。

车厢里，普鲁登丝的两名押送官全然措手不及。光天化日之下、出城不过几英里，竟有人袭击执法官家的车辆——这等闻所未闻的事，压根儿没往他们脑子里去过，成了这场骤停与骚乱的原由。

普鲁登丝对面那位满脸歉意的先生——此前他一直小心克制着不朝她看——这时把眼睛转了过来，灵机一动说道：「啊！咱们碾着一口猪了。准是这么回事。」

普鲁登丝一言不发，手却滑向身旁那根看似无害的手杖，扣住了杖头。她仍旧靠坐在自己那角，姿态也罢、神色也罢，都拿不出半点东西告诉押她的人：她周身每一根神经、每一块肌肉，都已绷紧待发。

===Original===

“That’s no pig, Matthew!” said the leader of the two. “We’ve run into another coach belike.” He got up as he spoke, and let down the window. Even as he thrust his head out a great roan horse seemed to spring up from nowhere, and a huge man astride it bent in the saddle and wrenched open the coach door. The result was inevitable. The officer of the Law lost his balance, caught at the door frame to save himself, and received a blow from Mr. Merriot which sent him sprawling head foremost down on to the road.

No sooner had she caught a glimpse of the large figure astride the roan than all Prudence’s air of languor left her. The sword was out of the stick in a flash, and the carved handle caught the chief officer shrewdly between the shoulders. She did not pause to see what befell this unfortunate; she had given him all that was needed to send him tumbling out of the coach. She had one foot on the floor of the coach, and one knee on the seat, and swooped round upon poor Matthew almost before his companion’s misfortunes had reached his intelligence. He gasped out “Lordy, Lordy!” and clapped a hand to his pocket. And there it stayed, for as he turned his head to face his prisoner he found the point of a peculiarly murderous-looking sword about an inch from his nose. Mr. Merriot’s arm was drawn back in readiness to thrust; Mr. Merriot’s grey eyes were fixed on him with an expression in them which made Mr. Matthew goggle with dismay.

“Put your hands up! Quick, or I thrust home through your gullet,” said Prudence tersely.

The apologetic gentleman had never had such an experience in all his life. His arrests had never been interfered with in this unpleasant fashion, and he did not know what to do. There was a pistol in his pocket, but his hand had not reached it, and with that sword-point so close he had no intention of groping further for it.

===Chinese===

「那可不是猪，马修（Matthew）！」两人中领头的说。「多半是撞上别的马车了。」他一边说一边起身，摇下车窗。他脑袋才探出去，一匹高大的菊花青马仿佛凭空蹿起，鞍上一个魁伟的汉子俯下身，一把拽开了车门。结局在所难免。这位执法官失了平衡，慌忙抓住门框想稳住身子，却吃了梅里奥特先生一记，头朝下栽到路上，摔了个四仰八叉。

一望见那具跨坐在菊花青马上的魁伟身形，普鲁登丝周身的慵懒登时散了个干净。剑从杖中倏然抽出，雕花杖头不偏不倚，正敲在主管警官两肩之间。这位倒霉蛋落得个什么下场，她没停下看；要叫他跌出车厢，这一下尽够了。她一脚踩着车厢底板，一膝跪上座位，几乎不等同伴的祸事传进可怜的马修脑子，便猛转身扑了过去。他倒抽着气喊出「哎呀老天爷！哎呀老天爷！」，一手拍向口袋。那只手就此钉在了那儿，因为他一偏头去面对他押的犯人，便发现一柄模样格外凶悍的剑，剑尖离他的鼻子约莫一英寸。梅里奥特先生的手臂已然后引，蓄势待刺；梅里奥特先生那双灰眼睛定定盯在他身上，眼里那副神情，瞧得马修先生惊惶地瞪圆了眼珠。

「举起手来！快，不然我一剑捅穿你的嗓子。」普鲁登丝简短地说。

这位满脸歉意的先生活了半辈子，从没有过这样的经历。他办差拿人，从没叫人用这般不像样的方式搅过局，一时竟不知如何是好。口袋里是有一支手枪，可他的手还没够着；剑尖又逼得这么近，他也无意再往深处摸了。

===Original===

The point touched his throat. “Hands up!” Prudence said, and made as if to shorten her arm for the thrust.

Matthew’s hands were raised in shaking haste; Matthew’s eyes were riveted to Mr. Merriot’s face, and Matthew’s lips formed the words: “Don’t now, sir! don’t. It’s﻿—it’s a hanging matter, and there was no offence meant to your worship. It was all dooty, sir!”

Sir Anthony’s great bulk blocked the door as he sprang lightly up into the coach. He was a fearsome figure, with the muffler concealing the lower half of his face, his hat drawn over his eyes, and the heavy cloak making him to look even larger than he really was. Matthew began to tremble violently, and rolled a beseeching eye from him to Prudence.

“Right pocket. A pistol,” Prudence said, still holding the sword to Matthew’s throat.

===Chinese===

剑尖触到了他的喉咙。「举起手来！」普鲁登丝说，作势收臂便要刺出。

马修双手颤颤地仓促举过头顶；马修的两眼钉在梅里奥特先生脸上，马修的嘴唇吐出了话：「别介，先生！别介。这——这可是要上绞架的罪过，小人无意冒犯大人。这全是职责所在呀，先生！」

安东尼爵士轻身一纵跳上马车，伟岸的身躯堵死了车门。他这副形象煞是骇人：围巾遮着半张脸，帽子压到眼上，沉重的斗篷衬得他比实际还要庞大。马修剧烈地哆嗦起来，一只求告的眼睛从他身上滴溜转到普鲁登丝身上。

「右口袋。一支手枪。」普鲁登丝说，剑仍旧抵着马修的喉咙。

===Original===

There was a deep low laugh, which sounded like a death-knell to poor Matthew; the gigantic newcomer bent and slipped a hand into the pocket indicated. The pistol was soon stowed away in that voluminous greatcoat; to Matthew’s relief the sword point was slightly withdrawn.

Sir Anthony’s voice was full of amusement. “Now, fellow, I’m afraid we must truss you up a little,” he said. “Your muffler’s the very thing.” The shapely hand divested Matthew of his muffler and neckcloth without ceremony. He offered no resistance. He was twisted round, and in a trice his wrists were bound tightly behind his back with his own neckcloth, and Mr. Merriot was winding the muffler round his ankles in a most efficient manner. He was dumped down upon the back seat, and the next moment both the giant and Mr. Merriot had jumped down from the coach.

Prudence pushed the sword back into its stick and looked round wonderingly. The chief gaoler was lying bound at the side of the road; the coachman was groaning and swearing on the floor of the box, as he came to his senses; his mate was clinging desperately to the reins, with a noble attention to duty, and trying, unsuccessfully, to keep one eye on his horses and the other on John, who sat astride the mare, the roan’s rein in his bridle hand, and a pistol in the other. The coach-horses appeared to be hopelessly entangled in the traces, and the coachman, by the looks of it, would be unable to do anything but hold his head for some time to come.

Sir Anthony hoisted his second captive into the coach and shut the door on him. Under the brim of his hat his eyes were dancing. The one unhurt and unbound man would have his hands full with the frightened horses for quite a considerable time.

===Chinese===

一声低沉的笑，听在可怜的马修耳中宛如丧钟；那新来的巨汉俯下身，一只手滑进了指明的口袋。手枪很快收进那件肥大的大衣；叫马修松一口气的是，剑尖稍稍撤了回去。

安东尼爵士的嗓音里满是笑意。「好了，伙计，恐怕得把你稍稍捆一捆。」他说。「你这条围巾正合适。」那只漂亮的手毫不客气，扒下了马修的围巾和领巾。他没有反抗。他叫人拧过身子，转眼间两只手腕便用他自己的领巾反剪着捆了个结实，梅里奥特先生又正以十足高效的手段，把围巾往他脚踝上缠。他被撂倒在后座上，下一刻，巨汉和梅里奥特先生都已跳下了马车。

普鲁登丝把剑推回杖中，讶然四顾。主管押送官被捆着躺在路边；车夫正在车夫座的板上悠悠醒转，哼哼有声，骂骂咧咧；他的同伴死死攥着缰绳，那份恪尽职守着实可敬，正徒劳地试着一只眼睛盯马、另一只眼睛盯约翰——约翰跨坐在母马上，执缰的那只手里还攥着菊花青马的缰绳，另一只手握着一支手枪。拉车的马看样子已在挽索里缠得没了指望，而那位车夫，瞧那光景，往后好一阵子除了捂着自己那颗脑袋，怕是什么也干不成了。

安东尼爵士把第二个俘虏拎进车厢，关门把他关在了里头。帽檐底下，他一双眼睛灼灼发亮。那唯一没受伤也没挨捆的人，光是对付那几匹惊马，就有得他好一阵子手忙脚乱。

===Original===

Sir Anthony moved to the roan’s side, and swung himself up, taking the bridle from John. He gathered it up short in his left hand, and reached down his right to Prudence. “Put your foot on mine,” he said, “and up with you!”

She flung her coat up first, sent her sword-stick spinning into the ditch, and stretched up her hand to clasp Sir Anthony’s. She came neatly up into the saddle before him, and got her leg over without fuss. A strong arm girdled her about, and the roan, stamping and sidling, was given his head. In a few minutes they were in the spinney, trotting briskly through, then out in the open fields, with the road lost to sight.

“My dear, my dear, you’re surely mad!” she said, but her fingers clasped his. “You should not﻿—you should not, Tony﻿—for me!”

Came only a little laugh from behind her, and a tightening of the hold about her waist.

“Lord, your unfortunate horse!” said Prudence. “I believe I’m no featherweight.”

===Chinese===

安东尼爵士挪到菊花青马旁，翻身上了鞍，从约翰手里接过笼头。他把缰绳收短攥在左手，右手向下探给普鲁登丝。「把脚踩在我脚上，」他说，「上来吧！」

她先把外衣甩上去，又把剑杖甩得打着旋飞进沟里，伸手上去握住安东尼爵士的手。她干净利落地升上他身前的马鞍，不慌不忙地跨过了腿。一条有力的手臂把她拦腰一箍，菊花青马顿着蹄、侧着步，缰绳撒开了。不多时他们进了矮林，快步小跑穿林而过，随后出到空旷的田野里，大路已望不见踪影。

「亲爱的，亲爱的，你准是疯了！」她说，手指却扣紧了他的。「你不该——你不该，托尼（Tony）——为了我！」

回应她的，只有身后一声轻笑，和箍在她腰间的那道手臂一紧。

「老天，你那匹倒霉的马！」普鲁登丝说。「我看我可算不得轻飘飘的分量。」

===Original===

“He’ll bear us both for as long as I need,” Sir Anthony said. “We bear southwards, John, and leave you by Easterly Woods.”

“Ay, sir,” John answered, pulling the muffler down from his face.

Prudence turned her head, and smiled at his stolid countenance. “Tell Robin, John. Oh, but how he would have delighted in this.”

“I’m like to find him bent on some madness,” grunted John.

Easterly Woods came into sight; in a few minutes they were under the spreading beeches, and the horses were pulled up.

===Chinese===

「我要用多久，它就把我们俩驮多久。」安东尼爵士说。「我们取道往南，约翰，走到伊斯特利树林（Easterly Woods）把你放下来。」

「是，爵士。」约翰答道，把围巾从脸上扯了下来。

普鲁登丝回过头，朝他那副木讷的面孔嫣然一笑。「讲给罗宾（Robin）听，约翰。哦，他要是在场，不定多乐呢。」

「我怕就要撞见他一头扎进什么疯事里去了。」约翰咕哝道。

伊斯特利树林望得见了；不多时他们已在枝叶披拂的山毛榉底下，马也收住了。

===Original===

Sir Anthony sprang down and lifted Prudence from the saddle. She had an odd delight in this masterful treatment of her, though she could have come down easily enough by herself. For a moment as he held her she looked down into his eyes, and saw them alight with laughter, and something else, more deep than that. She was set lightly on her feet, and for an instant caught his hands in hers. Then she turned and pulled her coat from the roan’s saddle.

“You remember, John?”

“Yes, sir.” John was holding the mare in readiness for Prudence.

She came to him, and took the bridle. She had very little doubt of her destination now. “You’ll keep Robin safe, John?”

“Ay, trust me, mistress.”

===Chinese===

安东尼爵士跃下马，把普鲁登丝从鞍上抱了下来。她本可以自己轻轻松松下得来，对这番霸道十足的对待，心头却有种说不出的欢喜。他抱着她的那一刻，她低头望进他的眼睛，看见里面笑意盈盈，还有别的什么，比笑意更深。她被轻轻放到地上，有那么一瞬，双手握住了他的双手。随后她转过身，把外衣从菊花青马鞍上拽了下来。

「你还记得吧，约翰？」

「记得，爵士。」约翰牵着母马，已经给普鲁登丝备好了。

她走到他跟前，接过笼头。自己这趟要去往何处，她如今已没有多少疑问了。「你会护得罗宾周全的，约翰？」

「是，信我，小姐。」

===Original===

The twinkle crept up. “What, will you leave me to the large gentleman?”

“I will,” said John, and exerted himself to say more. “And I’d not wish you in better hands, Miss Prue. You’ll do as he says, and come to no harm. Up with you!”

She put her foot into his hand, and was flung up into the saddle. Beside her Sir Anthony sat on the roan again.

“Good luck to you, sir,” John said. “You don’t need to fret over Master Robin, mistress.”

===Chinese===

眼底的亮光悄悄爬了上来。「怎么，你们要把我丢给那位大块头绅士？」

「是要丢给他。」约翰说，又费了把劲多说出一句。「普鲁小姐，您这是落在再好不过的手里了。您照他说的做，保管平安无事。上马吧！」

她把脚踏进他的手掌，被一把托上马鞍。安东尼爵士已重新跨上菊花青马，在她身侧。

「祝您顺利，爵士。」约翰说。「罗宾少爷的事您就不必挂心了，小姐。」

===Original===

“Get him away tonight,” Sir Anthony said, and reached down his hand. “It was a good rough and tumble, John.”

John flushed unwontedly, and after a moment’s hesitation gripped the outstretched hand. “It was, sir. Goodbye, sir.”

The roan was pressed forward to the mare’s side; together they moved forward through the wood.

===Chinese===

「今晚就把他弄走。」安东尼爵士说，伸手向下探来。「这一场乱仗，打得痛快，约翰。」

约翰难得地红了脸，迟疑片刻，握住了那只伸来的手。「打得痛快，爵士。再见，爵士。」

菊花青马被催到母马身旁；两骑并辔，一同穿林而去。
